## 2026-10-06: `wf work <slug>` run mode (the git-state worker) in Rust

Decision: port the scheduler, the lease store (claim, renew, release, expire, failed and done refs through plain `git`), the worktree workspace (fetch base, worktree add and remove, commit with a `Unit:` trailer, rebase, re-gate on a touched file, push to the land ref), the AIMD concurrency controller, process execution with abort and timeout, and the SIGTERM/SIGINT stop (leases released, exit 143). The implementer is unchanged: it is still `pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent rca run`, so the worker moves to Rust before the chat agent does. Threads replace the TS promises; a mutex plays the repo lock.

Oracle: `work-run.integration.test.ts` (14 tests over a real bare origin and a real clone, `pnpm` stubbed). A TS seam makes it fast: `WF_WORKER_TICK_MS`, `WF_WORKER_LAUNCH_SPACING_MS` and `WF_WORKER_HEARTBEAT_MS` shrink the 15 s tick and 60 s launch spacing (unset in production). 14/14 on the TS bundle, then 14/14 on the Rust binary without a change. Covers landing two chained units, the prompt, a base that moved (rebase), a gate that never passes, an implementer that writes nothing, a failed dependency, a failing install released three times, a pre-failed unit, a live lease, a stale lease, an already landed plan, argument errors and SIGTERM. Whole integration suite after this: 584/584 on Rust; `cargo test` green.

Not covered: live `rca run` runs, a lost lease during a long unit (heartbeat renewal races), the AIMD probe-up path (needs warm units older than 120 s). They keep the TS logic ported line for line but were not exercised against real load.

Benchmark (`work p --once` on an already landed plan, n=5 median): TS 320 ms / 66 MB, Rust 71 ms / 12 MB (git calls dominate the Rust time).

## 2026-10-06: `wf metro start|restart` in Rust

Decision: port the start path: port reservation (reuse or allocate `max + 1`, default from `WF_METRO_PORT_MIN`), kill and wait for the port, the log-forwarding and backend environment (best-effort vault load of just the keys involved, through a new `load_keys_into_process_env`), `.expo/metro-port.json`, the supervised `sh` restart loop or a direct `npx expo start`, a detached spawn into `/tmp/metro-<port>.log`, the 1 s readiness poll of `/status`, and the TS's failure message with the log tail. `restart` is stop plus start with its own log lines. Two start variants still go to the TS, decided before anything is touched: a set `EXPO_PUBLIC_INITIAL_ROUTE` (route pre-warm) and a configured OTLP pair (the BetterStack log forwarder process). `ensure` with Metro down also still goes to the TS.

Oracle: `metro-start.integration.test.ts` (16 tests, `npx` stub that records argv, cwd and the relevant environment and serves `/status`) plus the existing `metro.integration.test.ts`; 45/45 across the four Metro suites on the TS bundle and on the Rust binary, unchanged. Whole integration suite on Rust: 600/600; `cargo test` green.

Not exercised: a real `expo`, a held port that needs the SIGKILL escalation, the 180 s timeout path, and the vault load against a real backend. Wall time of a start is the 1 s readiness poll either way (about 1.4 s per test on both), so there is no startup saving worth a table here; what changes is that `wf` itself no longer needs Node for the start.

## 2026-10-06: `wf auth login` and `remote-login` in Rust

Decision: port both for the five tools whose credential is a file or a command (claude, codex, opencode, gemini, gcloud), with their login commands and validity checks as data in `auth.rs` (claude and the file tools: the `validCheck` path, else all `credentialPaths`; gcloud: its `gcloud auth list | grep .` probe). `login` runs the tool's command through `sh -c` with inherited stdio, reports a non-zero exit as `login command exited N`, then checks the credential. `remote-login` starts a session on the listener (`POST /auth/start {tool}`), prints the link, polls the same check every `VNC_AUTH_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` (2 s) up to `VNC_AUTH_CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 min), then makes the best-effort `/auth/done`; an unknown tool fails before any secret or network access. `leetcode` (its credential lives in the browser and is verified against a backend) falls through to the TS. With this the `wf auth` group is native except leetcode.

Oracle: `auth-login.integration.test.ts` (14 tests; tool commands are stubs on PATH, credentials are files under a throwaway HOME, the listener is a mock HTTP service), 14/14 on the TS bundle and the Rust binary unchanged. Not exercised: a real login, the real listener or KasmVNC desktop.

Decision (bench): port the read-only half of `wf bench` (`list`, `compare [--suite] [--budgets]`, usage), reading the committed `clis/app-bench` scenarios and baselines. Numbers print as JavaScript would (`toFixed` rounds the exact decimal expansion half up, tiny or huge values fall back to the TS). Running a suite (web export, lab browser) stays TS. Oracle: `app-bench.integration.test.ts` (8 tests, expectations derived from the committed JSON), 8/8 on the TS bundle and the Rust binary; byte-for-byte equal to the TS for nine invocations (list, compare, `--budgets`, per-suite, unknown suite, no argument, `--help`, `-h`) including exit codes.

Decision (callers): switch callers to the Rust binary in stages, each only after the tier it needs has run. Stage 1 (this change): the lefthook gate (`verify --staged`, `secrets doctor`) calls `wf` from PATH, which `.agents/setup.sh` links to the Rust binary (the TS shim when cargo is missing). Verified by the integration suites for both commands and by this repo's own pre-commit run. Stage 2: the GitHub workflows (`deploy.yml`, `tier3.yml`, `architecture.yml`, the tailscale ones, `install-oci`) once the `gh-runner` image carries the binary. Stage 3: the image builds (`agent-shell`, `remote-chat-agent` Dockerfiles, the three `deploy.sh` scripts, vm-ops-api redeploy and cloud-init, which fetch `bundle/wf.js` over HTTPS) once an aarch64 build is produced and published; the TS bundle stays the `__legacy` fallback until then. Not exercised: any Docker build, the VM, the runner.

## 2026-10-06: `clis/cdp`, the foundation for replacing the Playwright daemon (first slice of multi-browser)

Decision: multi-browser (12k lines, Playwright) and, through it, the vnc-auth-capture listener (its desktop launcher attaches to Chromium over CDP through the multi-browser SDK) are the last large TypeScript blocks, and neither can be ported until Rust can drive Chromium. New crate `clis/cdp`: a std-only (no async runtime, no TLS, `serde_json` only) DevTools client: HTTP `/json/*` reads by Content-Length (the DevTools server keeps the socket open), WebSocket upgrade, masked text frames, ping/pong, fragmentation, JSON-RPC request/response matching with events queued, and flattened-session addressing (`call_on`). Not linked into `wf` yet, so the 3.3 MB binary does not grow.

Oracle: `clis/cdp/tests/chromium.rs`, 5 tests against a real headless Chromium launched on an ephemeral DevTools port (version, navigate and evaluate, PNG screenshot, a CDP error surfaced, an unreachable endpoint), 5/5 on three consecutive runs (skipped with a note when no Chromium is installed). The first run found a real bug (reading `/json/version` to EOF hung on the open socket), fixed by Content-Length reads. Next: a Rust daemon speaking the dispatcher protocol the Rust browser client already uses, starting with `open --cdp-endpoint`, `navigate`, `eval`, `screenshot`, `list`, `close`.

## 2026-10-06: attached-session engine in `clis/cdp` (step 1 of the multi-browser plan)

Decision: implement what the vnc-auth desktop launcher uses from the multi-browser SDK (`openBrowser` with a CDP endpoint, `navigate`, `eval`, `cookies`, `screenshot`, `emulateMobile`, `fullscreenWindow`) as `cdp::attached::Attached`, over raw CDP: resolve `/json/version`, take the first page (opening a blank one when none), attach with a flattened session, enable Page/Runtime/Network. `eval` awaits a returned promise and returns a thrown error's text; `cookies` reads the whole browser jar (HttpOnly included) or those for given URLs; `fullscreenWindow` sends the state on its own and goes through `normal` first, never failing; `emulateMobile` follows the TS sequence (clear the override, wait for two equal viewport reads, add the scrollbar gutter, pin with `mobile: true`, set the UA and touch). Deliberately not reproduced for attached sessions: Playwright's fake microphone and camera scripts, the colour-scheme binding and the compressed screenshot (the Rust one returns the PNG as captured); none is used by the launcher. This is a documented behaviour difference, to be closed with the screenshot compression when the daemon wrapper lands.

Oracle: `clis/cdp/tests/attached.rs`, 8 tests against a real headless Chromium (attach and evaluate, a thrown error, a refused navigation reported as `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, an HttpOnly cookie served by a local HTTP server read all/scoped/none, a PNG screenshot, mobile emulation read back through `navigator.userAgent`, `maxTouchPoints` and `innerWidth`, fullscreen not failing in headless, base64), 8/8 on three consecutive runs next to the 5 client tests. One test was wrong, not the code (port 1 is Chromium's unsafe-port refusal). Next: the `/call` daemon wrapper (`open --cdp-endpoint`, `navigate`, `eval`, `cookies`, `screenshot`, `list`, `close`, `emulateMobile`, `fullscreenWindow`) behind the existing client.

## 2026-10-06: Rust browser daemon for attached sessions (step 1 of the multi-browser plan lands)

Decision: `wf browser-daemon` (hidden) is a Rust daemon speaking the Playwright daemon's protocol (`GET /health`, `GET /status`, `POST /stop`, `POST /call`, always HTTP 200 with `{ok,result}` or `{ok:false,error}`) and registering itself in the slot registry (`slots[slot].browserMcp = {url,pid,startedAt}`, creating the slot as the TS registry service does; the slot comes from `WF_BROWSER_SLOT`, set by the client). It implements `open` (only with `--cdp-endpoint`), `close`, `list`, `navigate`, `eval`, `cookies`, `screenshot` (including `fullPage`), `emulateMobile` and `fullscreenWindow` over `clis/cdp`. Everything else says it is not implemented rather than falling back, because a session lives in one process. Opt-in, so nothing changes by default: `WF_BROWSER_DAEMON=rust` makes the Rust client spawn it in place of the Node daemon. `--path` (Expo paths) and tailnet URLs are refused with a message naming the Node daemon; the registered-Metro-port guard is kept. Not reproduced for attached sessions: Playwright's fake microphone/camera, the colour-scheme binding, and the sharp-compressed screenshot (the PNG is written as captured).

Oracle: `browser-attach.integration.test.ts`, 13 tests against a real headless Chromium that the test launches (open summary and its key order, navigate/eval for string, object and undefined, list with target URL, screenshot path pattern and PNG, cookies, close, reopen, duplicate name, unknown name, refused connection, a throwing script, status, stop and the registry afterwards): 13/13 on the TS bundle (the real Node/Playwright daemon) and 13/13 on the Rust binary unchanged. Output was also compared by hand for every command and matches the Node daemon byte for byte except Playwright's appended call log in errors. Whole integration suite on Rust 766/766 (plus this file); `cargo test` green.

Benchmark (open, navigate, eval through the CLI against the same Chromium, then the daemon's own memory): wall 1657 ms with the Node daemon vs 370 ms with the Rust one; daemon resident set 184.5 MB vs 4.8 MB (about 38 times smaller). Binary size: 3,580,872 B (was 3,265,664 B; the CDP engine and daemon add about 315 KB).

Not exercised: the vnc desktop itself (KasmVNC, X11), a real tailnet. Next on the plan: step 2, input commands (`tap`, `type`, `press`, `scroll`, `swipe`, `stroke`, `drag`) over `Input.dispatch*`.

Addendum (Rust browser daemon): `scroll` added (`window.scrollBy(x, y)`, answering `{scrolled:{x,y}}` as the Node daemon does). `browser-attach` is now 14 tests, 14/14 on the Node daemon and on the Rust one. The input commands that need element resolution (`tap`, `type`, `stroke`, `drag`, `swipe`, `press` through the soft keyboard) stay unimplemented in the Rust daemon: they resolve Playwright snapshot refs (`eN`) and selector engines, so they wait on `snapshot`.

## 2026-10-06: `snapshot`, `tap`, `type` and `press` in the Rust browser daemon (the snapshot decision, taken)

Decision (the choice left open in the plan): option 2, a native snapshot with a documented subset, not Playwright's vendored script. Reasons: no vendored JavaScript to refresh on every Playwright upgrade, and the refs only have to be consistent between the Rust `snapshot` and the Rust `tap`/`type`. `clis/cdp/src/snapshot.js` (a page-side walk, evaluated over CDP) prints Playwright's AI-snapshot format for: generic wrappers (kept only with two or more children, otherwise collapsed), text merged and whitespace-normalised, `heading [level=n]`, `button`, `link` (`/url:` child, `[cursor=pointer]`), `textbox` (value after the colon; name from `aria-label`, `<label>`, placeholder), `checkbox`/`radio` `[checked]`, `combobox` with `option [selected]`, `img` (alt), `list`/`listitem`, `paragraph`, landmarks, `table`/`rowgroup`/`row`/`cell`/`columnheader`, `dialog`, `[disabled]`, hidden elements skipped, refs `eN` in document order (options and text have none). Not supported: iframes, shadow DOM, ARIA relationships beyond `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`, most ARIA states; they print as generic or are omitted. Two things in Playwright's output are not deterministic across sessions and the suite normalises them (the `f<n>` frame prefix on refs, `[active]` on the focused body).

`tap` resolves an `eN` ref from the last snapshot or a CSS selector (Playwright selector engines such as `text=` are not resolved: the command says no element was resolved), takes the centre of the visible part, and sends move/press/release as a mouse; the answer is the Node daemon's (`Tapped #b at 25,19 (mouse)`), and the tap indicator dot is not drawn (it is a DOM injection). `type` replaces the content of an input, textarea or contenteditable (select all, insert text), optionally pressing Enter; `--at-caret` and `--human` say they are not implemented, and a non-editable target fails with a message instead of the Node daemon's keyboard fallback. `press` sends Enter, Backspace, Tab, Escape or an arrow key, with Chromium editing commands, and keeps the Node daemon's rule that the page must have focus on an editable element.

Oracle: `browser-attach` grew from 14 to 21 tests (two snapshot fixtures printed exactly, tap by ref and by selector with the page's own click log, type/press/submit with the key log and the field value, press without focus, tap with no match), 21/21 on the Node/Playwright daemon and 21/21 on the Rust daemon. The suite now launches one Chromium per test, because a page left unfocused by an earlier session made the Node daemon refuse `press` (shared state, not parity). Whole integration suite on Rust passes; `cargo test` green.

Addendum (Rust browser daemon): `drag` added. It presses at the centre of the element's box (no off-screen check, as in the Node daemon), moves by (dx, dy) in `steps` equal moves (default 12, optional per-step delay) and releases at the end point. The page-side event log is byte-for-byte the Node daemon's (move, press, twelve moves, release; the same coordinates and button masks) for the default and a two-step drag. `browser-attach` is now 22 tests, 22/22 on the Node daemon and on the Rust one. The drag indicator trace is not drawn.

Addendum (Rust browser daemon): `stroke` added for the deterministic cases. Straight and Catmull-Rom sampling (`--curve`), fractions or pixels, `--samples-per-segment`, `--step-delay`, and the transforms `--reverse`, `--scale` (about the path centre) and `--offset`, ported from `libraries/gesture-geometry`. The page-side event log is identical to the Node daemon's for the fraction, curve with reverse/scale/offset, and pixel strokes (checked by comparing the full logs), and the client line is the same (`Stroked #d through 3 points (7 samples in N ms, mouse)`). `--human` (random tremor), `--truncate` and `--wobble` say they are not implemented; the stroke indicator trace is not drawn. `browser-attach` is now 23 tests, 23/23 on the Node daemon and on the Rust one.

Addendum (Rust browser daemon): the device-frame page route. `GET /_mcp/device-frame[/path]` serves the built `harness-shell/dist` (the React page stays a build artifact, ignored by git): the bare route gives `index.html`, a file path gives that file with the Node daemon's MIME map (`html js css png svg ico woff2 woff`, else `application/octet-stream`), any other path falls back to `index.html` (single-page routing, with `..` segments folded so a request cannot leave `dist`), and a missing build gives the JSON 404. Checked against both daemons by the new `browser-attach` test (page, route fallback, a hashed JS asset byte for byte, `/health`, an unknown route); the test skips when the harness is not built. `browser-attach` is 24 tests, 24/24 on both daemons. This is the serving half of launched sessions; what launching a device-frame session needs beyond it (Chromium launch with device emulation, the shell viewport, the `__shell` bridge) is not started.

## 2026-10-06: frames and execution contexts in `clis/cdp` (the long pole of launched sessions)

Decision: the first unit the multi-browser plan named, built before anything that depends on it. `Attached::frames()` lists every frame (main first, depth first, with parent, URL and name) from `Page.getFrameTree`; `Attached::eval_in_frame(frame_id, expr)` evaluates in that frame's main world, using the `Runtime.executionContextCreated/Destroyed/Cleared` events (kept in a frame to context map after a non-blocking drain of what the browser has already sent: `Cdp::drain` peeks for data before reading, so a timeout can never cut a WebSocket frame in half). Scope: frames in the page's process. The Node launch passes `--disable-site-isolation-trials` for emulated devices precisely so the device-frame app stays in-process; out-of-process frames (cross-site iframes under default site isolation) would need `Target.setAutoAttach` sessions and are not covered.

Oracle: `clis/cdp/tests/attached.rs` grew from 8 to 11 tests against a real Chromium (three nested iframes listed with parent, URL and name; each frame evaluating in its own document and one id that has no context failing by name), 11/11 on three consecutive runs. Next: `findDeviceFrame` and the editable-frame search on top of it, then launching a device-frame session.

Addendum (frames in `clis/cdp`): the harness-facing helpers on top of the frame layer. `find_frame_named` (the harness names its app frame `device-frame-app`; waits up to a timeout, as `findDeviceFrame` does for 10 s), `find_editable_frame(root, depth)` (depth first through the descendants of a frame for a contenteditable or textarea, as `findEditableFrame`), `device_frame_url` (the harness URL with `URLSearchParams` encoding: spaces as `+`, UTF-8 percent-encoded) and `fitted_shell_size` (box plus the 24 px margin and 40 px label allowance, each rounded up to an even number; the arithmetic was checked against `fittedShellSize` by hand and a first wrong expectation of mine was corrected). `clis/cdp/tests/attached.rs` is 14 tests, 14/14 on two consecutive runs against a real Chromium. Next: launching a Chromium with the device-emulation flags and creating a device-frame session in the Rust daemon.

## 2026-10-06: `clis/cdp` gets a reader thread, page bindings and init scripts (the base for the mock keyboard)

Decision: the page bindings the Node daemon relies on (`__mcpFocusChange`, `__mcpOpenedUrl`, `__mcpSetColorScheme`) call back into the daemon at any time, not only while a command is in flight, so the synchronous connection (which read the socket only inside `call`) could not carry them. `Cdp` now owns a reader thread: replies are routed to the waiting caller by id over a channel, events go into a bounded queue (10,000, oldest dropped), pings are answered from the reader, a lost connection wakes every waiting caller with an error, and a command times out after 60 s. `Attached` adds `add_binding` (`Runtime.addBinding`), `add_init_script` (`Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument`) and `take_binding_calls`; execution-context tracking now reads the queue, which removed the peek-based `drain`.

Oracle: `clis/cdp/tests/attached.rs` is 15 tests (the earlier 14 unchanged plus a binding called by an init script after a page load, arriving with no command in flight), 15/15 and the 5 client tests 5/5 on three consecutive runs; the `browser-attach` suite on the Rust daemon stays green on the new connection. Next: the mock keyboard's focus handling in the daemon's event loop, then launching a device-frame session.

## 2026-10-06: launched device-frame sessions in the Rust browser daemon (`open` without `--cdp-endpoint`)

Decision: the nine-piece chain the plan listed, minus the parts named below, as `clis/wf/src/browser_launch.rs` on top of `clis/cdp` (`launch.rs`: find a Chromium, start it with a throwaway profile and an ephemeral DevTools port). `open` starts Chromium with the device flags (`--force-device-scale-factor`, `--disable-site-isolation-trials` and the fake media-stream flags for an emulated device), applies the context options as CDP overrides (shell viewport, pixel ratio, mobile and touch, the identity user agent with `{chrome}` filled from the browser's version, colour scheme, clipboard grants, ignored certificate errors, focus emulation), registers the bindings (`__mcpOpenedUrl`, `__mcpSetColorScheme`, `__mcpFocusChange`; a wrapper makes object payloads JSON strings, since CDP bindings carry strings) and the init scripts (opened-URL capture, fake microphone and camera, generated from the TS functions into `clis/cdp/src/js/`, and the focus script). `navigate` loads the harness page the first time (`/_mcp/device-frame?...` from the daemon itself), waits for `window.__shell`, fits the window to the device box, and applies the safe-area insets (rotated in landscape) and the full identity (platform, client-hint metadata, touch points) on the page; later navigations call `__shell.setTarget` and re-apply them. `eval`, `snapshot` and `scroll` run in the app frame (`device-frame-app`, or the editable frame inside it), as `getAppTarget` does. The mock keyboard works in the daemon's event loop: a focus change on an editable element shows it (the app frame gets `__MCP_KEYBOARD_VISIBLE__`, the mode `overlay` for iOS and `resize` otherwise, and the height 270 or 0), a blur hides it after 150 ms, and keys queued by the shell are forwarded every 100 ms.

Two architecture changes it forced, both generally useful: the daemon now accepts connections on their own threads and runs commands on one thread in order (the harness page fetches its assets from the daemon while `navigate` is waiting for it, which deadlocked a single loop), and `Cdp` has the reader thread from the previous change.

Not implemented, each saying so instead of falling back: video, `--audio-file`, `--tailnet`, HTTP credentials, other engines and the plain render mode for launched sessions, ref-based input (`tap`, `type`, `press`, `drag`, `stroke`) on device-frame sessions (they need element boxes in main-frame coordinates), platform fonts (`systemFont` stays `null`, the Node daemon downloads Inter and Roboto), the per-page console and error listeners, and the `opened-urls` command.

Oracle: `browser-launch.integration.test.ts`, 7 tests (default device: summary, navigate answer, and from inside the app frame the viewport, pixel ratio, user agent, touch points, platform, pointer type, colour scheme and safe-area insets; a landscape dark Pixel 8: rotated viewport, Android user agent with client hints, rotated insets; the screenshot's pixel size, 2468 by 1250; the keyboard bridge on focus and blur; a snapshot of the app frame; an unknown preset; close and reopen), 7/7 on the Node/Playwright daemon and 7/7 on the Rust one, unchanged. A first Node failure was the test, not the daemon: `runWf` blocks the test process, so the fixture server has to be a separate process. Whole integration suite on Rust 784/784; `cargo test` green.

Benchmark (open, navigate, eval through the CLI, then the daemon's own resident set, Chromium excluded): wall 2180 ms with the Node daemon vs 977 ms with the Rust one, daemon 187.5 MB vs 5.5 MB. Binary 3.7 MB.

Addendum (launched sessions): ref-based input in device-frame sessions. Element boxes inside the app frame now come from `DOM.getContentQuads` (main-frame coordinates), so `tap`, `type`, `press` and `drag` work through the harness's nested iframe. On a phone or tablet preset `tap` is a finger as in the Node daemon: a random point in the middle 60% of the visible box, held 70 to 140 ms, sent as `touchStart`/`touchEnd` (the page sees `touchstart`, `touchend`, then the emulated `mousedown`, `mouseup`, `click`); `--mouse` or a desktop preset taps with a mouse. `press` accepts focus in any frame, as the Node daemon's check does. Page-side event logs are identical between the daemons for a tap on a button and on an input, `type`, two Backspaces and a drag. Still refused on device-frame sessions: `stroke` (its touch variant), the pointer-routing retries the Node daemon does against the shell (not needed for the fixture page; a page that the shell overlays would show the difference), and platform fonts.

Snapshot parity fix found by the launched oracle: Playwright numbers an empty wrapper element (it prints nothing, but takes a ref), which shifts the refs of everything after it; the snapshot now keeps a ghost for it. Both daemons print the same refs for the fixture with an empty `div` before a paragraph (`e5`), and the two older fixtures are unchanged. `browser-launch` is 8 tests, 8/8 on both daemons.

Drift found on the way (the upstream TypeScript moves while the port is in flight): `b14df9a` made `agent-shell` a buildable image of `wf deploy run` in the TypeScript only, so the Rust `deploy run agent-shell` refused it as unknown, the help golden was stale and the TypeScript's own `deploy-run` refusal test was already failing on dev (its expected-image list lacked `agent-shell`). Fixed in this change: `agent-shell` added to the Rust image table with the same Dockerfile and context, the test's list corrected, the golden regenerated. Whole integration suite on Rust 785/785. The lesson for the remaining ports: every command group needs its black-box suite re-run against the TypeScript after each rebase, because the suite is the only thing that notices upstream behaviour added to code that is being replaced.

It passed on the TypeScript CLI first (through `WF_APP_BENCH_DIR` and `WF_APP_BENCH_TMP`, test-only knobs added to both), then on Rust unchanged. gzip sizes differ by a few bytes between zlib and miniz_oxide; the test allows 16.
- Wall time, n=1, same fixtures, TS then Rust: memory suite 95.0 s then 3.2 s; runtime 14.9 s then 3.0 s; startup 12.6 s then 1.0 s; planted-leak comparison (two memory runs) 135.9 s then 12.0 s; `ab` 1.7 s then 1.1 s; build (stub export) 0.9 s then 0.16 s.
- Deleted `clis/app-bench/{src,tests,bin,package.json,tsconfig.json,vitest.config.ts}` (about 1.3k lines of source and 1.0k of tests); scenarios and baselines stay as data. The TS `wf bench` entry is `rustOnly`. The Rust wf now sets `WF_FROM_RUST=1` when it hands a form to the bundle, and a `rustOnly` entry refuses it with exit 2 instead of handing it back (that loop was possible for `wf caveat <unknown>`).
- Not ported: the Node-only `planted-leak` e2e (covered by the black-box leak test), pixel-level checks.

## 2026-10-06: Rust browser daemon, page-state commands

- Added `shell-eval`, `outer-eval`, `opened-urls` (with `at`), `permission` (browser-level `Browser.setPermission`, falling back to the default context), `clipboard`, `set-device` and `keyboard` to the Rust daemon. The five-then-two tests of the new `browser-state` suite passed on the Node daemon first, then on Rust unchanged: 37.1 s then 3.3 s for the first three, 19.7 s then about 1.8 s for the last two (n=1).

## 2026-10-06: what stops further TypeScript deletion (and one reverted attempt)

- Reverted before merging: moving `clis/secrets` to `libraries/secrets` and deleting its CLI. The production paths run the committed TypeScript bundle, not a checkout with a Rust build: cloud-init and `vm-ops-api/src/scripts/redeploy.ts` fetch `clis/workflow-cli/bundle/wf.js` from GitHub and run `node wf-bundle.cjs secrets sync-env ...` on the VM, the agent-shell and remote-chat-agent images install the same bundle as `/usr/local/bin/wf`, the three `backend/*/scripts/deploy.sh` rsync it, and `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` runs `node clis/workflow-cli/bundle/wf.js deploy ...` on the VM's runner. A command the bundle forwards to a Rust binary that is not on those machines fails there. So the TypeScript implementation of any command those paths use must stay until the Rust binary ships to them.
- Already merged and affected only by that: `wf caveat caveat-guard` (off unless `.agents/tmp/caveat-guard.on` exists), `wf agents sync` (the postinstall hook skips it when no binary is built; the generated files are committed) and `wf bench` (dev tool). The bundle's forwarding shim now builds the Rust wf once with `cargo build --release` when the binary is missing and a toolchain exists, and otherwise fails with the exact command to run.
- Cross-build for the VM works: `cargo build --release --target aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu -p wf` with `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` as linker (the bundled SQLite needs a C cross compiler) gives a 4.4 MB stripped ELF, against 4.7 MB for x86_64. Node plus the bundle is about 28 MB of bundle and a 100 MB resident runtime per command.
- The keystone left is distribution: ship the Rust binary to the VM (cloud-init, vm-ops-api redeploy), the images (agent-shell, remote-chat-agent: a `COPY` or a build stage), the runner and the three deploy scripts, then delete the TypeScript commands those paths used. None of that can be exercised without Docker and the VM.

## 2026-10-06: the Rust binary is `wf` on the VM images

- Correction of the previous two entries: there is no opt-in flag. The Rust binary is the one entry point (`/usr/local/bin/wf`); a command it has not ported runs in the JS bundle installed beside it (`/usr/local/bin/wf-bundle.cjs`, found next to the executable). The agent-shell and remote-chat-agent images build the binary natively in a `rust:1-bookworm` stage from the workspace sources (the crates also include `registry.json` and `model-ladders.json` from outside `clis/`, so those are copied too; verified by building a context holding exactly the copied paths). The bundle's `WF_USE_RUST` hand-off is removed.
- `bin/wf` (what `pnpm`, `package.json`, `setup.sh` and `retry-apply.sh` call) is a shell launcher: it execs the Rust binary (built when missing or older than a Rust source, where cargo exists) with the JS bundle beside it for what is not ported; with no binary and no cargo it runs the bundle. Black-box suite `wf-launcher`.
- vm-ops-api's build context cannot reach the workspace, so the Rust binary reaches it as a carrier image, `nuesslerm/wf-cli` (`wf deploy run --build-only wf-cli --tag latest`, the pattern `logger-cli` uses; `wf log emit` is Rust now, so `logger-cli` becomes redundant). The redeploy script runs `WF_LEGACY_BUNDLE=<dir>/wf-bundle.cjs wf secrets sync-env ...`.
- Verified against the real vault before switching the redeploy path: `wf secrets sync-env --for <app> --backend bitwarden` with the Rust binary and with the bundle gave byte-identical `.env` files for vm-ops-api, agent-shell, remote-chat-agent, leetcode-backend and leetcode-auth-cookie-capture (the files were deleted afterwards).

## 2026-10-07: VM recreated onto the Rust `wf`, and what the recreate taught

- Tier run: runbook checks against the deployed VM. `wf --help`, `wf caveat status` and `wf secrets --help` run natively in agent-shell (`/usr/local/bin/wf` is an aarch64 ELF); a leetcode-backend `--redeploy-only` and an agent-shell `--recreate-all` redeploy go through vm-ops-api to exit 0 and a healthy container.
- A VM replace left the old VM's tailnet records holding the bare hostnames, so the new nodes registered as `-1` names that `wf` and vm-gateway cannot reach. `wf deploy tf apply` now destroys the VM, deletes the VM's tagged tailnet records through the Tailscale API (the sandbox's are kept), then creates it (black-box test through `WF_BIN`).
- vm-ops-api's compose pins its sidecar to the same `IMAGE_TAG`, so `image_tag_overrides` needs the vm-ops-api and the watchdog image at that tag; `wf deploy run vm-ops-api --build-only` builds only the named image, so name `watchdog` too.
- The sibling container now runs the running image tag (`VM_OPS_API_IMAGE_TAG`), not `:latest`. `/services/vm-ops-api/logs` reads vm-ops-api's own containers; its structured log lines go to the sink, not to stdout.
- Closed: `POST /redeploy` (the trunk self-redeploy) never deployed because its build script cloned the private repo with no credentials, and `git clone -b` cannot take the commit SHA it asks for. It now sends the provision token as an `http.extraHeader` and inits + fetches the SHA; verified live: vm-ops-api went from `sha-d2b25a6` to `sha-cc4e2b8` through `wf vm-ops redeploy-self` (runbook tier, deployed service).
- Finding: the JS bundle's `sync-env --for agent-shell` left VM_PROVISION_GITHUB_TOKEN and DOCKERHUB_TOKEN out of agent-shell's env; the Rust `wf secrets sync-env` run by the redeploy sibling includes them (checked inside the container after a redeploy), so the redeploy path now runs the Rust binary.
- Left on the OCI side: IAM policy `run-command-main-vm` and two Run Command jobs stuck ACCEPTED (the instance-agent never ran them).

## 2026-10-08 (later): the JS bundle and every TypeScript command are gone; the Rust `wf` is the whole CLI

- Tier run: integration. The black-box suite (80 files, 1,059 tests, `clis/workflow-cli/tests`) runs through the Rust binary alone (`WF_BIN`, else `clis/target/release/wf`); the suite no longer has a TypeScript oracle because there is no TypeScript implementation left. `cargo test -p wf` (45 tests) is green too. Deployed-service tier for the production switch below is not yet run: it needs the next VM redeploy.
- Ported in this stretch: `tailscale up` and `auth-key rotate --apply` (the rotate key is minted through the admin API and written under the same vault key by a helper `secrets update` shares), `deploy agent-deps install`, `browser tunnel`, `browser preflight` (now launches a headless Chromium instead of checking Playwright, so its message says chromium), `knowledge index --gemini` (plan, repomix pack, Docs sync through curl; `WF_GOOGLE_DOCS_BASE`/`WF_GOOGLE_DRIVE_BASE` point it at a mock), `cm6` (the esbuild script is plain JavaScript under `clis/cm6`, embedded in the binary and run under node because esbuild's native binary comes with the app's own install), `metro _forward-log`, the initial-route bundle pre-warm in `metro start` (the last reasons `metro start` fell back to TypeScript).
- Removed: `clis/workflow-cli/src` (all 120 TypeScript files), `bundle/wf.js` (1.7 MB), the 90 unit tests and fakes that exercised that code, and the two e2e tests whose only subject was the TypeScript worker. `legacy()` is replaced by a refusal: an unknown command gets commander's `error: unknown command 'x'` (exit 1), a known command given arguments no port serves gets `wf <cmd>: the Rust wf does not support these arguments (see wf <cmd> --help)` (exit 2). The help tests now check the committed texts against the binary instead of regenerating them from the commander tree.
- With the fallback disabled (`WF_NO_LEGACY`) before deleting it, the full suite failed only on the six commands the tests expect to be unknown and on `browser preflight`; the flag audit found no flag missing from the Rust sources.
- Production entry points switched to the binary: `cloud-init` no longer fetches the bundle (the host `wf` is extracted from the `nuesslerm/wf-cli` carrier image, and a failed extraction is now logged, not papered over with a bundle wrapper); vm-ops-api's redeploy scripts no longer download `wf-bundle.cjs` or set `WF_LEGACY_BUNDLE`; the four hand-run `deploy.sh` scripts call `wf` (locally `bin/wf`, on the VM `/usr/local/bin/wf`); the agent-shell, remote-chat-agent and `wf-cli` images build the binary with `clis/cm6` in the context and no longer copy a bundle; `deploy.yml`, both tailscale workflows, `install-oci` and `architecture.yml` use `.github/actions/install-wf`; the root `postinstall` builds the binary when cargo exists. `bin/wf` execs the binary and says so when there is neither a binary nor cargo.
- Not done yet in this stretch (still TypeScript, so the goal is not met): `clis/multi-browser` (the SDK and `harness-shell` that e2e suites, the leetcode capture service and tier 3 import), `clis/vnc-auth-capture` (the listener service and its CLI), and `clis/secrets` (the library the TypeScript backends and images import); plus the black-box suite itself is TypeScript under `clis/workflow-cli/tests`.

## 2026-10-08 (last stretch): the auth listener is Rust; no TypeScript is left under `clis/`

- Tier run: integration for the port (the black-box suite through the Rust binary: 86 files, 1,117 tests passing and 3 skipped, 34 of them the new listener suite, which cold-starts a real Chromium and drives its real cookie jar) and the runbook tier against the deployed VM for the pipeline: Deploy run 264 (`plan`, `deploy (metro)`, `deploy (remote-chat-agent)`) went green on the Rust-only path after vm-ops-api was redeployed by hand at `sha-41cb96f` (`wf deploy run --build-only wf-cli --tag latest`, then `wf vm-ops redeploy-self`). Not exercised from here: the vnc image build and a listener running inside the real desktop container (no Docker in this sandbox), and a fresh-VM cloud-init.
- Why run 263 failed: the vm-ops-api that was running still carried the old redeploy script, which downloads `clis/workflow-cli/bundle/wf.js` from the ref and got a 404 once the bundle was deleted (`serverScriptSha 4894145dfd28`). vm-ops-api is deployed by hand on purpose (the pipeline must not restart itself), so the order for a change like this is: build the carrier image, redeploy vm-ops-api, then let the pipeline run.
- Ported: the whole listener of `clis/vnc-auth-capture` into `clis/wf/src/vnc/` (descriptor schema with zod-shaped issues, session engine with the per-tool idempotency rule and reusable 12 h link tokens, the desktop launcher over `cdp::attached`, the token gate for HTTP and the websocket upgrade, the credential-file relay to agent-shell, `tailscale serve` binding, `POST /auth/start|done|launch-url` and the page-callable `/desktop/fit`), run as `wf auth listener`. `wf auth login|remote-login leetcode` is native now (the probe is the descriptor's `verify`). The CDP crate gained `check_launch_invariants` (the six invariants, with the EWMH state read from the X server).
- No TLS library was added: an `https+insecure` desktop backend is reached through `openssl s_client`, a plain `http` one in-process. Secrets that travel in a request (the relay bearer token, a spec's delivery headers) go to `curl` as a config on stdin, never as argv.
- Found by the new suite: a navigation error quotes the whole URL, so a magic-link fragment reached the log; the launcher now logs origin and path only. The old `logging-audit` test (a scan of the sources) is replaced by a scan of a real login's whole log stream for the link token, the cookie values and both secrets.
- Deleted: `clis/multi-browser` (the Playwright SDK and CLI: 131 files, 36 of them tests), the 62 TypeScript files of `clis/vnc-auth-capture` with its tsconfig, vitest and eslint configs (two of its tests, of the shell init script and the extension, moved into the suite), the Node bundle step and `runtime-deps.json` (Playwright and sharp) from the vnc image. remote-chat-agent keeps its caller-side pieces in `src/lib/authCapture.ts` (`createListenerClient`, `isAuthError`, the three descriptors, the credential-file check); `withReactiveLogin`, `awaitCapture` and `LOGIN_REQUIRED` had no caller. The `@lib/secrets` loader (the Rust registry plus a thin import) and `@lib/browser-client` (a `createContainer()` over the Rust daemon's `POST /call`) replaced the TypeScript secrets package and the multi-browser SDK earlier in the stretch; the device-frame harness UI moved to `tooling/browser-harness` (static assets the daemon serves).
- The black-box suite itself was TypeScript under `clis/workflow-cli/tests`; it now lives in `tooling/wf-blackbox` (a workspace package; lefthook's secrets job and architecture.yml point at it). `clis/workflow-cli` keeps only the `bin/wf` launcher and a package.json for the `wf` bin link. `git ls-files 'clis/**/*.ts'` is empty.
- Benchmark (listener, n=5, this sandbox, release build): startup to "listening" 11 to 21 ms (median 13 ms); idle RSS 5.8 to 5.95 MB. The TypeScript listener could not be rebuilt for a like-for-like run (its bundle needed the multi-browser build, which no longer builds from a fresh worktree), so the comparison is a floor: an empty `node` process alone idles at 43.1 MB RSS (n=5, 43.10 to 43.15 MB), more than seven times the whole Rust listener, before zod, the logger or any request. The old image also carried `playwright-core` (14 MB) and sharp with its libvips (19.6 MB) installed by `npm install` only to satisfy imports the listener never used at idle; the new one adds a 5.7 MB binary and keeps `nodejs` only to install the tool CLIs.
- Decision: relocating the suite rather than keeping TypeScript tests under `clis/` costs a package rename and a dozen path edits, and makes "no `.ts` under `clis/`" literally true; the alternative (leave it and call test code exempt) was rejected as moving the goalposts.

## 2026-10-08 (final): the deployed-service tier on the VM, and the final benchmark table

- Tier run: runbook checks against the deployed VM, after vm-ops-api and agent-shell were redeployed from the final commit `7a453d4` (`wf vm-ops redeploy-self`, then `wf deploy run agent-shell --ref 7a453d4…`; the carrier image `wf-cli` had been rebuilt at `9b03b11` first, because the vnc and vm-ops-api images copy their `wf` from it). Inside agent-shell (`wf vm exec <script>`): `/usr/local/bin/wf` is an aarch64 ELF of 4,936,120 bytes; `wf --help`, `wf caveat status` (`caveat-guard: off`), `wf secrets --help`, `wf auth listener --help` run natively; `wf deploy status` reports vm-ops-api at commit `7a453d4…`, image tag `sha-7a453d4`; no process on the VM runs `wf-bundle` or `wf.js` (count 0).
- Found by that tier and fixed in `7a453d4`: inside agent-shell `wf deploy status` failed with `curl: (7) Failed to connect to localhost port 1055`, because only the CI runner container set `WF_TAILNET_SOCKS=""`; every agent container shares the tailscale sidecar's network namespace, so the shared agent environment sets it now (verified by the run above, with the variable read back as empty). The TypeScript client had the same rule, so this is not a regression of the port, only a gap the old runs never exercised from inside agent-shell.
- Deploy runs: 264 (`41cb96f`, after the hand redeploy of vm-ops-api), 265 (`9b03b11`, the `remote-chat-agent` leg built, pushed and redeployed in 4 minutes on warm layers) and 266 (`7a453d4`, nothing changed for any service) are green on the Rust-only pipeline. `wf deploy plan` from a full checkout and from the CI runner agree (`{"legs":[],"metro":false}` after 265); inside agent-shell, which has no checkout, it plans everything, by design (an unreachable commit counts as changed).
- Still not exercised from here, and said plainly: the vnc image build and a listener running in the real desktop container (the app is not part of `wf deploy run` and is not deployed on the VM today: `/services/vnc-auth-session/logs` answers 404), `wf verify <sha>` (needs the golden-screenshots bucket prefix), and a fresh-VM cloud-init. The OCI leftovers reported in step 1 were not touched here and not re-checked: the IAM policy `run-command-main-vm` and two Run Command jobs stuck in ACCEPTED (the instance agent never ran them).

Final benchmark, Rust `wf` (`wf cli-bench`, fresh process per run, peak RSS from rusage, n=5, x86_64 sandbox, release build) against the phase 1 TypeScript baseline in this log (same host class, node v22.22.0, n=5):

| target | TypeScript median ms | Rust median ms | TypeScript RSS MB | Rust RSS MB (max) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `--version` | 266.8 | 1.7 | 64.2 | 5.0 |
| `--help` | 266.0 | 1.6 | 64.5 | 5.2 |
| `secrets --help` | 268.9 | 1.6 | 64.3 | 5.2 |
| `dispatch --help` | 266.2 | 1.6 | 64.2 | 5.2 |
| `deploy plan --help` | 315.8 | 1.6 | 64.3 | 5.2 |
| `verify --help` | 266.3 | 1.7 | 64.5 | 5.2 |

- Startup is about 160 times faster (266 ms to 1.6 ms) and peak RSS about 12 times lower (64.4 MB to 5.2 MB), for every command: the old price was Node plus loading the whole bundle, and the new one is a process start.
- Binary size: 5,699,096 bytes on x86_64 (5.44 MiB), 4,936,120 bytes on the VM's aarch64; one file, no companions. It was 2.4 MB when it held a third of the commands; the growth is the commands, not a dependency (no TLS or HTTP library: HTTP goes through `curl`, TLS to the desktop through `openssl`).
- Install footprint of the CLI: the production path is that one binary. Before, `wf.js` 1.94 MB, the caveat-guard bundle 0.77 MB and the agent-config-sync bundle 0.58 MB (3.29 MB of JavaScript) plus a Node runtime and, in the dev tree, a `node_modules` that `wf` needed to run. The dev tree's `node_modules` still holds 256,101 entries, now only for the TypeScript services and the app, none of it for `wf`.
- The live rows of the baseline (`verify --staged`, `dispatch --stats`, `secrets sync-env`, `deploy plan`) were never measured on the TypeScript side (they need credentials and the VM), so there is no before to compare; `wf deploy plan` takes about 5 s on the CI runner, almost all of it `pnpm list`.

- Step 1 evidence, run at the final commit (runbook tier, deployed service): `wf deploy run leetcode-backend --redeploy-only --ref af660bc… --tag sha-41cb96f` through the redeployed vm-ops-api exited 0 with no bundle download (`[provision] refreshing … from af660bc…`, `Wrote 7 secret(s) + base .env.defaults`, `[verify-env] ok: 13 synced key(s) survive into the final .env`), and `wf deploy status leetcode-backend` showed both containers `Up … (healthy)` 19 seconds later. The sibling that does the work runs the running vm-ops-api image tag, not `:latest` (`siblingImage(namespace, tag)` in `infra/oracle-cloud/vm-ops-api/src/scripts/redeploy.ts` falls back to `latest` only for an unknown tag). `wf vm exec <script>` is the tier above: every check ran as a job inside agent-shell, as its own recorded run (the script, its output and exit code are the evidence).

- Found while auditing the leftovers: `wf deploy plan` still treated `clis/workflow-cli/bundle` as the input that ships a new remote-chat-agent image, and that bundle no longer exists, so a change to the Rust `wf` (which that image builds from source in its `wfbuilder` stage) would never have redeployed it. The leg's extra paths are now the sources the stage copies (`clis/wf`, `cdp`, `rca-core`, `rca-edge`, `agent-config-sync`, `cm6`, the workspace manifest and lockfile, the registry) and the plan test asserts the exact `git diff` it runs; the stale `.gitignore` negation for the bundle directory is gone. `git grep` for `__legacy`, `WF_LEGACY_BUNDLE`, `wf-bundle.cjs` and `workflow-cli/bundle` outside docs and fixtures now finds nothing.

- OCI leftovers from step 1, re-checked with the OCI CLI at the end of this stretch (compartment `mobile-learning`): the IAM policy `run-command-main-vm` is still ACTIVE. Its one statement lets any user use `instance-agent-command-execution-family` where `request.instance.id` is a single instance id that is neither the running `mobile-learning-main` nor the terminated one, so it grants nothing on the current VM. The Run Command jobs `wf-latest-retag` (2026-10-07 00:24) and `wf-latest-retag-2` (01:00) are in state ACCEPTED on that same instance and will never run; four older `infra-ops-containers-status` jobs from 2026-10-01 and 10-04 sit in the same list. Nothing here is Terraform-managed (no `.tf` names it), nothing depends on it, and I left all of it in place: deleting an IAM policy and cancelling jobs were not asked for. The cleanup is `oci iam policy delete` for the policy and `oci instance-agent command cancel` per job, if you want the list empty.

- The vnc image is built and exercised on the VM now (`wf deploy run vnc-auth-capture --build-only --ref 8d20332… --tag sha-8d20332`, exit 0, pushed as `nuesslerm/vnc-auth-capture:sha-8d20332`; the image is a `wf deploy run` build target from this commit on). Pulled into agent-shell's docker on the VM and run with the entrypoint overridden (runbook tier, deployed architecture): `/usr/local/bin/wf` is the aarch64 binary of 4,936,120 bytes and `wf auth listener --help` runs; curl, openssl, tailscale, node, xrandr, xdotool, `s6-setuidgid` and chromium are all present; the s6 service is `exec wf auth listener`; and the listener started in that image (stub `tailscale`, link base given) logged `vnc_auth.gate.listening` and `vnc_auth.listener.listening` and answered `POST /auth/start` for codex with a tokened link. Not run: the whole container under s6 with the desktop and its Chromium over CDP, and the compose app (`vnc-auth-session`) with its tailscale sidecar; that needs the tailnet auth key and is the one step left before this app is deployed again.

## 2026-10-08: leetcode-backend baseline before its Rust port (rust-services plan, phase 0 part one)

- Tier: measurement, no code changed. The service as production runs it (`node dist/bundle/index.mjs`, the 2.6 MB bundle from `pnpm --filter @app/leetcode-backend run bundle`), required env supplied, the secrets loader stubbed with a no-op `WF_BIN`, only `/health` exercised, x86_64 4-core sandbox, node 22, n=5, medians: cold start to the first `/health` 252 ms (225 to 300); idle RSS 78.2 MB (78.1 to 78.3); RSS after 300 sequential `/health` requests 79.9 MB; `/health` p50 1.07 ms, p99 5.8 ms.
- The dev entry (`babel-register` over the sources) is 1,413 ms and 150 MB idle; it is not what runs, so it is not the baseline. The plan had guessed about 60 MB for the Node runtime; the whole service is 78 MB.
- Still to measure before the first Rust slice lands: `/format` and `/run` latency (each spawns `python3`), image size and image build time on the VM, RSS under a request mix. The suite that repeats this (a service kind in `wf bench`) is part of phase 0 proper.

## 2026-10-08: leetcode-backend black-box suite (rust-services plan, phase 1)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket, against the production bundle (`node dist/bundle/index.mjs`): 4 files, 69 tests in about 10 s with the bundle build, all green. The service runs as a child process with a mock leetcode.com and a mock capture tool beside it; `LEETCODE_BACKEND_CMD` names another implementation, so the same suite runs unchanged on the Rust binary later (`pnpm --filter @app/leetcode-backend run test:blackbox`).
- Seam added, default unchanged: `LEETCODE_ORIGIN` (the outbound origin was hard-coded). Nothing else in the service needed a seam: python3 and node are found on PATH, and the session file and the capture tool are already environment-driven.
- The suite pins the HTTP surface (health, CORS preflight, bearer and admin-secret checks with wrong-length and wrong-scheme cases, 404s including the 401 the admin router gives unknown paths under `/api/leetcode`, the 1 MB body limit, malformed JSON, the 120 per minute limit with `Retry-After`), sessions and the capture registry, the four LeetCode relays with the exact outbound requests (headers, cookies, bodies), the no-session path with the fire-and-forget capture trigger (and a negative probe that proves the request arrived), the hourly re-verification, and the local executor (Python and JavaScript, compare modes, per-case and compile errors, the 5 s timeout, unsupported languages, format).
- Found by writing it first, fixed in the same change: three paths ended the whole process because Express 4 does not catch a rejected async handler. `POST /session` with a cookie string lacking `LEETCODE_SESSION` (now 400 with the message), a stale session whose re-verification fails because LeetCode is down (now 502 with the upstream status and body), and `GET /user-status` when LeetCode errors (now 502); a corrupt session file is now 500 instead of a crash. The Rust port inherits these as the specified behaviour.
- Deleted: the 28 in-process unit and integration tests (middleware, use-cases, adapters, route tests) and `supertest`; everything they covered is asserted by the suite above through the real entry point. `test` and `test:unit` of the package now run the black-box suite.
- Plan correction: the cutover step says the image has no Node, but the JavaScript executor (and `prettier` for JS formatting) runs on Node, exactly as Python needs `python3`; the image keeps a Node runtime for the code it executes, not for the service.

## 2026-10-08: leetcode-backend is Rust, deployed (rust-services plan, phases 2 to 8 in one change)

- Decision: no strangler. The plan proposed a Rust front door proxying unported routes to the Node process, a state that costs more memory than either end. The black-box suite is the oracle instead, so the Rust service was written until it passed the same 69 tests the Node bundle passes, then replaced the Node service in one change (`backend/leetcode-backend/rs`, workspace `backend/Cargo.toml`). The Node service, its babel and dependency-cruiser setup and its Express dependencies are deleted; the package is the suite plus the crate.
- Decision: no HTTP or TLS crate, as in `wf`. The server is std only (thread per connection, keep-alive, chunked bodies, `Expect: 100-continue`, HEAD, a 256 connection cap), outbound HTTPS goes through `curl` with url, headers and body on stdin so a cookie never shows in argv, and the only dependency is `serde_json`. The plan's proposal (hyper, rustls) was not measured against this; it was not needed.
- Tiers run. Black-box through a real socket: 79 tests on the Rust binary (69 original plus 10 new protocol tests: keep-alive, `Connection: close`, chunked body, HEAD, trailing slash and letter case, non-JSON content type, non-object JSON, broken request line, encoded poll id, exact zod message), and the same 79 on the Node bundle before it was deleted. Rust: 15 unit tests, 4 process tests with a `curl` stub (telemetry reaches the ingest with the traceparent trace and parent ids, an unsampled parent exports no span, no ingest configured sends nothing, a refusing ingest is reported on the console) and a fixture test: `tests/zod_golden.json` holds 29 request bodies judged by the real zod 4 and the Rust validator reproduces every verdict and every message byte for byte. Clippy is clean. Contract check: the app-facing response shapes are the same JSON the Node service sent (the suite compares them).
- Deployed-service tier (runbook), on the VM, after the pipeline built and redeployed it (`Deploy` run 275, leg `leetcode-backend`, tag `sha-af6d6b5`, exit 0): `/health` reports the full commit; a usage route without the bearer token and with a wrong one answers 401; `GET /login-session` 200; the admin route with the bearer token but not the admin secret 401; `POST /session` with a cookie lacking `LEETCODE_SESSION` 400 with the message and `/health` still answering afterwards (the case that ended the Node process); an empty body 400 with the zod message; `/run` (python3, two cases) and `/format` (black) and a JavaScript `/run` all 200 with correct results. The 401s and the 400 prove the requests reached the service logic. Not exercised against the real leetcode.com: that needs a stored session, and calling a session route without one starts a login capture and a Telegram message; the relays are pinned against a mock leetcode.com (exact outbound headers and bodies), and TLS from the image to leetcode.com verifies (curl in the image, `ssl_verify=0`; leetcode.com answers a bare curl 403, which is its bot wall, not a failure of the connection).
- Observability parity (CLAUDE.md rule: a query at the sink returns the line the request caused): `wf vm logs leetcode-backend` returned the Rust service's `server.listening host=0.0.0.0 port=8789` and `health.heartbeat uptimeSec=0 rssMb=1 ok=true` at 16:28:11, with the same app tag and the same `leetcode-backend.heartbeat` module the Node service's heartbeat had (its last lines read `rssMb=69` to `71`). Request spans: the exported JSON is pinned on the wire by the stubbed-curl test, and the real ingest answers 200 to the same span document (probe from agent-shell, trace id `0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c`); the spans themselves were not queried back because the ClickHouse query credentials did not resolve from agent-shell and are not in this session's environment, so that last step is open.
- Measured, x86_64 4-core sandbox, node 22, n=5, medians, same script on both (60 `/health`, 20 `/run`, 20 `/format` per run, all 200):

| | Node bundle | Rust binary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| cold start to first `/health` | 203 ms | 16 ms |
| idle RSS | 77.9 MB | 2.3 MB |
| RSS after the mixed load | 83.1 MB | 2.8 MB |
| `/health` p50 / p99 | 1.13 / 4.59 ms | 0.51 / 3.51 ms |
| `/run` p50 / p99 | 40.5 / 60.4 ms | 39.7 / 57.4 ms |
| `/format` p50 / p99 | 46.6 / 61.8 ms | 44.4 / 65.5 ms |

- Measured on the VM (aarch64), runbook tier, the two images pulled into agent-shell's docker and started with fake secrets: image size 352,138,942 bytes (Node, `sha-55efb8c`) to 265,604,773 bytes (Rust, `sha-af6d6b5`), uncompressed; idle RSS of the service process 78,040 kB to 2,008 kB, 2,120 kB after a python `/run`, a `/format` and a JavaScript `/run`; the binary is 660,376 bytes. The leg of the pipeline (image build, push, redeploy, warm caches, one run each) took 147 s for the Node image (run 274) and 115 s for the Rust image (run 275). A cold Rust build on the VM was not measured.
- What the number says: the service itself is now a few megabytes. `/run` and `/format` did not change because Python start-up is the cost (about 40 ms per request) and Python stays, as the plan said. The image shrank by a quarter, not by the Node runtime, because it still carries Python with `black` and Node 18 (Debian bookworm) to run submissions; neither runs unless a request asks.
- Deliberate differences from the Node service, each pinned or found while testing: slugs and ids from callers are percent-encoded into one path segment and dot segments are refused (the Node service pasted them into the outbound path); redirects from leetcode.com are not followed, so the cookie cannot travel to another host; the executor child gets a scrubbed environment (PATH, HOME, LANG, LC_ALL) instead of the service's secrets, and its output is capped at 16 MB; body-parser failures (malformed JSON, over 1 MB) answer JSON instead of an HTML page; a panic in a request answers 500 and the process keeps serving; the session file is written atomically with mode 0600. JavaScript formatting still reports `Cannot find module 'prettier'`, as it did in the bundle (no `node_modules` in either image).
- Known gaps: `vm-ops-api`'s trunk deployer maps changed paths to apps by directory, so a change that touches only `backend/Cargo.toml` or `backend/Cargo.lock` does not redeploy leetcode-backend through that route (the `Deploy` workflow's `wf deploy plan` does watch them); a lockfile-only change is rare and always comes with a change under the package. The secrets loader is gone from the service: it reads its environment only, which the deploy composes from the vault, so a local run needs the four variables set (the e2e script already sets them).

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent baseline before its Rust port (rust-services plan, phase 0 part one)

- Tier: measurement, no code changed. The service as production runs it (`dist/bundle/index.mjs`, 14.9 MB from `pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent run bundle`, its native and heavy packages left external), `AGENT_CLI=opencode`, the secrets loader stubbed with a no-op `WF_BIN`, a throwaway HOME, only `/health` exercised, x86_64 4-core sandbox, node 22, n=5, medians: cold start to the first `/health` 513 ms; idle RSS 133.7 MB; RSS after 300 sequential `/health` requests 137.4 MB; `/health` p50 0.94 ms, p99 3.99 ms.
- Reading it: that is the floor, not the working set. `sharp`, `ffmpeg-static`, `tesseract.js`, `whisper-node`, the PDF and DOCX parsers and Playwright load on the first request that needs them, so a chat session with attachments sits well above 137 MB; that is the number the Rust service has to be compared with, and it still has to be measured (phase 0 proper: a scripted chat session through the mock opencode). The front door `rca-edge` is 354,912 bytes.
- Compared with leetcode-backend (78 MB, 252 ms for 1.4k source lines), this service pays about 56 MB more resident and 260 ms more start-up for loading 21.6k lines and its dependency graph, which is the part a port removes only if the media dependencies leave the resident process (the plan's decision 1, still the owner's).
- Not done: the black-box harness (phase 1), the per-route latency and a loaded measurement, image size and build time on the VM.

## 2026-10-08: an audit of what the Rust wf still refused, against its own help

- Tier: black-box through the Rust binary (the suite is the oracle), after the stop-hook review disputed that nothing was left. Method: the help of every one of the 121 commands was read against the handlers, the code paths that return "does not support these arguments" were read, and the recoverable TypeScript of the deleted commands was compared where the help documented an option the binary refused. Doc comments were not trusted either way: about forty of them still said a shape "stays with the TypeScript" or that the dispatcher "hands the argv to the TypeScript CLI", which nothing has done since `__legacy` went; they are corrected.
- Real gaps found and closed (tests first, each failing before): `--json` and `--app <name>` before the command, and `--app` after it, for every group (only `browser` understood them); `wf vm exec --e2e <checks> --branch <b> --out <dir>` (documented, referenced by the verification ladder, refused by the binary: eight black-box tests, the script it ships no longer builds the deleted harness-contracts package or kills the deleted multi-browser daemon); `wf cli-bench --deps` (priced Node imports of a CLI that is gone; removed from the help).
- Found and fixed in the suite, not the code: `wf browser navigate` on a launched session returned before the app frame had loaded (a test serving the page's script 400 ms late fails on the old binary, and so did the coverage test, which had only failed by chance), the auth-listener suite removed its browser profile while the browser still wrote it (ENOTEMPTY, about one run in three), and a long file of blocking `spawnSync` tests outlasted vitest's 60 s reply wait (a yield after each test). Final state: 86 files, 1,132 passed, 3 skipped, two consecutive full runs green. One failure of `tailscale-rotate.integration.test.ts` (5 tests, exit 1 with an empty stdout) was seen once in about ten full runs and did not reproduce; its assertions now carry the command's stderr so the next occurrence explains itself.
- Known and still unsupported, said plainly: a rung on the Claude pool pinned explicitly to `wf dispatch` (it ran through the Agent SDK in the TypeScript; the default rotation is four opencode models and never reaches it, and the runbook ladder drops Claude rungs).
