## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent black-box harness, first slice (rust-services plan, phase 1 started)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the production bundle (`node dist/bundle/index.mjs`): 1 file, 21 tests, all green in about 12 s with the bundle build. `RCA_BACKEND_CMD` names another implementation, so the same suite will run on the Rust service (`pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent run test:blackbox`). The service starts with nothing but its environment: no opencode, no vault (a no-op `WF_BIN`), a throwaway HOME.
- What the slice pins, the layers every route shares: `/health` (status, `wsProtocolVersion`, commit); the origin policy (the configured origins with their trailing slash normalised, localhost on any port, `127.0.0.1`, the dev-bridge `metro-runner-*` hosts, and the refusals for an unlisted origin, a prefix lookalike and a public-domain metro-runner lookalike), with `Vary: Origin`, the method and header lists and the 86,400 s max age; preflights answered 204 for every path and not counted against the rate limit; the 120 per minute limit per caller, where the caller is the `X-Forwarded-For` address when the peer is loopback (the front door `rca-edge` relies on exactly that); 404, malformed JSON 400 and the 30 MB body limit 413; and the one secret in the service, `X-Bot-Secret` on `/api/worktree` (401 before the body is read, 400 with issue details after).
- Not yet covered, and the next slices in order: the small routes (`control`, `question`, `plan`, `runs`, `editStats`, `diff`, `vcs`, `sourceControl`, `filePreview`), then `chat`, `chatCompat` and the WebSocket layer against a mock opencode server, then the stores' on-disk formats. The existing 95 test files are untouched until a black-box test subsumes them.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent black-box harness, second slice (the small routes)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the production bundle: 2 files, 41 tests, all green (about 25 s with the bundle build); lint and typecheck of the package clean.
- Pinned in this slice: `GET /api/plan/:id` (a 302 to `/api/file/.bot-state/plans/<id>.md?chatId=&threadId=` keeping or emptying the query, 404 `Plan <id> not found`, 403 `Forbidden` for an id that climbs out of `FORK_DIR`) and `POST /api/plan/:id/annotate` (400 `No annotations`, 500 without a Telegram token); `POST /runs` (401 before the body is read, and 401 when no secret is configured at all, because a run endpoint fails closed; 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` with issues for a missing prompt, an empty cwd, an unknown cli and non-string models); `POST /api/edit-stats` (202 `{ok:true, stored:false}` with no table configured, 400 `invalid_edit_stat` with at most five issues); `POST /api/code-assist` (404 with mock mode off; with it on, 400 for a missing transcript, `unknown_intent`, and the frames of a known intent as server-sent events, one `event:` and one `data:` line per frame); and `POST /api/convert` (400 without a file, 415 for a type it does not read, 422 for a PDF or DOCX that is not one). Convert is one of the media routes: its success path needs the PDF and DOCX parsers and is left to the media decision.
- Still to cover before the Rust service can be written against the suite: `control`, `question`, `diff`, `vcs`, `sourceControl`, `worktree` creation, `filePreview`, `voiceEdit`, `tamilCheck`, then `chat`, `chatCompat` and the WebSocket layer with a mock opencode server.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent black-box harness, third slice (git and pull requests)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the production bundle: 3 files, 80 tests, all green (about 30 s); prettier and typecheck of the package clean. The git half runs against real repositories: a temporary working tree with a bare local `origin`, a second clone to move origin ahead, a real merge conflict. The pull-request half puts a stub `gh` first on the service's `PATH` that records its working directory and argv and replies from a file, so the suite sees exactly what the service asks `gh` and never touches GitHub.
- Pinned in `/api/vcs`: `X-Bot-Secret` 401 before the request is read; 400 `Missing required parameter: cwd` for all four routes and `Missing required field: message` for a missing or blank message (the cwd is checked first); `status` as `{branch, upstream, ahead, behind, files, clean}` with git's own ordering and untracked files last, `added` for a staged add even when edited again, `deleted`, `renamed` with git's `old -> new` text as the path, `conflicted`, ahead and behind counted against origin, a null upstream, and a null branch on a detached HEAD; `diff` unstaged by default, staged only for exactly `staged=true`, cut at 200,000 characters with `truncated`; `commit` that adds everything or only the named paths and returns the new HEAD, 409 `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`, 422 `GIT_FAILED` for an unknown path; `push` of the current or a named branch, `setUpstream`, 422 for a detached HEAD with that exact message and for a push origin refuses.
- Pinned in `/api/source-control`: 401 with `gh` never run, 400 for a missing cwd, a non-numeric number and each missing create field; `pr list --json <fields>` run in the given cwd with `--state` passed through as given except `all`, only the seven summary fields returned and a missing state read as `OPEN`; `pr view <n>`; `pr create --head --base --title --body` with an empty body by default and `--draft` last, the number read from the last output line (0 when it has none); 502 `SOURCE_CONTROL_FAILED` carrying `gh`'s stderr for a failing `gh`, and for output that is not JSON.
- Behaviour found and pinned as is, for the Rust service to reproduce or the owner to change deliberately: `git diff` outside a repository does not fail with "not a git repository" but prints the `--no-index` usage, so only the status and code of that 422 are pinned, not its text; a 422 for a commit failure other than "nothing to commit" carries an empty `error`, because the gateway prefers the empty stdout to stderr; `state` is not validated before it reaches `gh`.
- Still to cover: `control`, `question`, `diff` (the route), `worktree` creation, `filePreview`, `voiceEdit`, `tamilCheck`, then `chat`, `chatCompat` and the WebSocket layer with a mock opencode server, then the stores' on-disk formats.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent black-box harness, fourth slice (viewers and worktrees), and what it found

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the production bundle: 5 files, 123 tests, all green (about 40 s); prettier, eslint and typecheck of the package clean. Worktree creation runs real git: a bare local repository stands in for GitHub through git's `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` `insteadOf` rewrite of the `https://x-access-token:<token>@github.com/` URL the service builds, so the service's own clone command runs unchanged. The diff store is read from, and seeded into, the throwaway HOME, so the on-disk format is pinned too.
- Pinned in `/api/diff` and `/api/file`: the 503 without an https `AGENT_PUBLIC_URL`; registration (16 hex id, the URL with the trailing slash of the public URL trimmed, 400 `Missing diff text or sections`); the stored entry as `~/.local/state/opencode-telegram-bot/diffs.json` holds it (`id, title, rawDiff | sections, chatId as a string, threadId, cwd, createdAt`), seven-day expiry applied when the file is loaded, and a corrupt file starting empty; the page as html showing the title and file; `expand` (1-indexed, inclusive, clamped, a trailing empty line for a file ending in a newline, 400 for each bad parameter, 403 for a climb, 404 for a missing file); annotations (400, 500 without a Telegram token; the Telegram call itself is not exercised); the file preview (markdown rendered, source escaped in a `language-<ext>` block, 404, 403, 500 for a directory, 404 for a post that is not `/annotate`).
- Pinned in `/api/worktree`: 422 without a token; clone into `<base>/<slug(repo)>`, worktree at `<base>/<slug(repo)>-<slug(branch)>` on a new branch, a second branch from the same clone, `baseDir` in the body over the configured one, 409 when git refuses the path (two branches whose slugs collide), 422 for an existing branch or a missing repository; `DELETE /:name` (401, removal of a clean or a dirty checkout, 200 for a name that never existed, 400 for names that are not plain directory names).
- Found and not pinned, because the Rust service must not reproduce it: `POST /api/diff` needs no secret and stores the caller's `cwd`, and `expand` then returns lines of any file under it, so `cwd: "/"` plus `file=etc/hostname` returned the host's file (probed on the bundle). The containment check is also a bare `startsWith` without a separator. Recorded as the open gap `docs/gaps/rca-diff-expand-reads-any-file.md` with the fix; whether live clients send the secret when registering is not known from this repository, so the Node service was not changed.
- Found and pinned as it is: the worktree pre-flight probe asks the base directory, which is not a repository, so it never sees an existing worktree; asking twice for the same repo and branch answers 422 (`a branch named ... already exists`), not the 409 the route's comments promise, and 409 is reachable only when two branch names share a slug.
- Found in the Rust `wf` while regenerating the gap index: `wf knowledge index|stale|for` refused the repository's own `docs/gaps` with the unsupported-arguments message, because one frontmatter parse failure aborted the whole command. Fixed with two black-box tests (c4483f2). The ledger is still invalid: three gap records are `status: closed`, which the schema forbids, and the index cannot be regenerated until they are deleted. The deletion was refused by the session's permission classifier, so it waits for the owner.
- Still to cover: `control`, `question`, `voiceEdit`, `tamilCheck`, then `chat`, `chatCompat` and the WebSocket layer with a mock opencode server, then the stores' on-disk formats.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent black-box harness, fifth slice (chat, control and question against a fake opencode), and what it found

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the production bundle: 7 files, 151 passed and 6 skipped (about 55 s); prettier, eslint and typecheck clean. The new piece is `tests/blackbox/fakeOpencode.ts`, a fake `opencode serve` that speaks what the service's gateway speaks (`POST /session`, `PATCH /session/:id`, `POST /session/:id/prompt_async`, the `GET /event` server-sent stream, the v2 prompt, `/question/:id/reply`, `/find/file`), records every request and runs a per-test script of events once the service has subscribed. The chat, control and question routes are therefore pinned at both ends: the frames a client sees and the calls opencode receives. The service's own mock gateway is not used, so the Rust service needs none.
- Pinned in `/api/chat`: 400 `Invalid request body` for each bad body, 400 naming the available CLIs; the stream `session`, reply, `done` as server-sent events; the exact opencode call sequence of a new turn (create in the shared directory with the allow-everything ruleset, patch, prompt with the model, then the event stream for the request's cwd); session reuse across turns; `agent: "plan"`; model handling (sent, reported as `model_used`, replaced by `OPENCODE_DEFAULT_MODEL` when it belongs to another CLI); `thinking`, `tool` (running before done, no input or output) and `question` frames; `session.error` as an `error` frame. Reply text is compared joined, not chunk by chunk: the Node service ignores `message.part.delta` and sends the final part text as one chunk, and a streaming port may split it.
- Pinned in `/api/control` and `/api/question`: the 400s; for a session the service never ran, stop, queue and steer reach opencode as an abort and as deferred and immediate prompts for that very session; a question asked during a turn and one the service never saw are both answered with the same matrix of answers and a 204.
- Four bugs found, none pinned as the Node behaviour; each has a gap record and a test that runs only against another implementation (`RCA_BACKEND_CMD` set), each verified to fail on the Node bundle: concurrent turns in one directory receive each other's replies and every turn leaves its event subscription open (`rca-chat-event-stream-not-scoped-to-session`); a silent or failing opencode hangs the stream and the hard timeout never fires, and the control routes answer success when opencode is unreachable (`rca-chat-hangs-when-opencode-is-silent-or-down`); stop, queue and steer on a running turn address opencode with the thread id (`rca-control-stop-targets-thread-id`). One quirk is pinned as it is: a request's `cwd` scopes only the event stream, while the session is always created in the shared directory.
- The gap index `docs/known-gaps.md` is not regenerated for these records or the earlier one: `wf knowledge index` stops on three `status: closed` records that wait for the owner's decision to delete them.
- Still to cover: `voiceEdit`, `voiceTranscribe`, `voicePlan`, `tamilCheck`, the WebSocket layer, and the stores' on-disk formats; then the Rust service itself, which waits on the media-worker decision for the media routes only.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent media evaluation, Rust against Node on the same samples (rust-services plan, step 0 of the goal)

- Tier: measurement, no service code changed. Method as `wf cli-bench`: a fresh process per run, wall time and peak RSS from `wait4` rusage, n=5, medians, x86_64 4-core sandbox, Node 22, release builds with LTO. The Node side imports the very packages the service does (`sharp`, `shiki`, `pdfjs-dist`, `mammoth`, `tesseract.js`) through a script that copies the service's code paths; the Rust side is a scratch crate. Samples: an ink SVG and the glyph அ for the Tamil check, a 2-page reportlab PDF and a Chromium-printed PDF (two columns, a table, ligatures, Tamil), a python-docx file (heading, tab, line break, bullets, a table), a 900x260 OCR image, and `routes/vcs.ts` for highlighting.
- What the service really uses (the plan's "no small Rust equivalent" was wrong): the media surface is about 1.2k lines. `playwright`, `diff2html` and `pdf-parse` are not imported anywhere in `src` (dead dependencies, 5.0 + 13.0, 2.2 and 27.2 MB installed). Speech to text is already out of Node: `whisper-node` is only a vendored whisper.cpp build that the service runs as a binary behind an ffmpeg re-encode, or an HTTP sidecar, or Groq, so Rust spawns the same binaries and calls the same URLs. The markdown preview is a hand-written converter, not a library.

| capability | Node today (median ms / peak RSS) | Rust or native choice (median ms / peak RSS) | fidelity | choice |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tamil check raster (`sharp` + librsvg, 98 MB, install 17.9 MB of libvips) | 428 ms / 98.0 MB | `resvg` + `tiny-skia` + `image`: 76.5 ms / 18.8 MB | same 920x448 picture; 0.69% of pixels differ by more than 32/255, mean difference 0.8/255 (edge anti-aliasing); Tamil shaped correctly, both pictures sent to the owner | Rust crates |
| PDF text (`pdfjs-dist`, install 35.3 MB) | 282 ms / 119.8 MB (309 ms / 118.6 MB on the Chromium PDF) | `pdftotext` (poppler, 718 KB + libs): 10.8 ms / 15.6 MB; pure-Rust `pdf-extract`: 5.8 ms / 11.8 MB | plain PDF: the same words from all three. Chromium PDF: pdfjs splits ligatures and Tamil ("con fi rming", "த ம  ழ்"); `pdf-extract` keeps ligature code points and loses one Tamil vowel sign ("தம ழ்"); `pdftotext` is exact ("தமிழ் எழுத்து", "office, affluent") | `pdftotext` spawned from Rust (native binary, no Node), `pdf-extract` as the fallback when the binary is absent |
| DOCX text (`mammoth`, 85 MB, install 2.5 MB) | 253 ms / 84.7 MB | `zip` + `quick-xml`: 2.1 ms / 12.0 MB | byte-identical on the sample except a trailing paragraph break the Rust draft omits (one line to fix) | Rust crates |
| OCR (`tesseract.js`, 136 MB, install 43.2 MB of wasm) | 617 to 643 ms / 134 to 136 MB | `tesseract` binary: 223 ms / 37.3 MB; pure-Rust `ocrs` (12.8 MB binary + 12 MB models): 408 ms / 162 MB | tesseract binary: identical text; `ocrs` misread one character (`472:9` as `472;9`) and uses more memory than the Node it would replace | `tesseract` binary spawned from Rust (apt: about 7 MB of libraries and 15 MB of English data); `ocrs` rejected |
| Syntax highlighting (`shiki`, 117 MB) | 467 to 540 ms / 113 to 117 MB | `tree-sitter-highlight` with compiled-in grammars: 41 ms / 11.9 MB for TypeScript | 741 spans in 15 classes against shiki's 545 in 6 colours; the viewer's CSS maps classes to colours | `tree-sitter-highlight`. Tried and rejected: `syntect` default syntaxes have no TypeScript (it fell back to plain text); `syntect` with `two-face`'s extended set highlights it but loads 92 MB and takes 530 to 1,020 ms; Sublime's own TypeScript syntax does not load in `syntect` |
| Speech to text | whisper.cpp binary behind ffmpeg, an HTTP sidecar, or Groq | the same binaries and URLs, spawned or called with `curl` from Rust | unchanged by construction | no Node; ffmpeg startup is 54 ms / 44.8 MB per call as today |
| Multipart upload (`busboy`) | in Node | a small Rust parser | covered by the convert and voice black-box tests | Rust |

- Conclusion: no capability needs an on-demand Node worker. The decision the plan left to the owner resolves to Rust for every route, with three native binaries the image already carries or can add (`pdftotext`, `tesseract` and its English data, `ffmpeg`) and the existing whisper.cpp build; none of them is Node. The Node media packages total about 245 MB installed (`ffmpeg-static` 76.3, `tesseract.js-core` 43.2, `pdfjs-dist` 35.3, `pdf-parse` 27.2, `@img/sharp` 17.9, `whisper-node` 13.8, `playwright-core` 13.0, the rest small).
- Caveats said plainly: samples are small, so the Node figures include its start-up (about 80 MB for the runtime alone) as the service pays it only once per process, not per request; the per-request difference is therefore smaller than these per-process numbers, and the number that matters is the resident set of the whole service, measured in the pre and post benchmark records. Tamil text in PDFs is the one place pure Rust (`pdf-extract`) is worse than a native binary, which is why `pdftotext` is the primary.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent PRE-STATE benchmark, the Node service before any port (goal step 1)

- Tier: measurement, no service code changed. `pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent run bench:service` (`backend/remote-chat-agent/tests/bench/service.benchmark.test.ts`, commit `ef39dff`) with `BENCH_LABEL=pre BENCH_RUNS=5 BENCH_CALLS=10 BENCH_TURNS=20`; the full result is `docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-pre-node.json`. The service is the production bundle `node dist/bundle/index.mjs` (14,869,215 bytes, service source last changed in `9b03b11`), one fresh process per run, x86_64 4-core sandbox, Node 22, a fake opencode server and a fake STT sidecar, medians over 5 runs. Memory is `VmRSS` and `VmHWM` from `/proc/<pid>/status`. The same file measures the Rust service later by setting `RCA_BACKEND_CMD`.
- Start and idle: cold start to the first `/health` 503.4 ms; idle RSS 133.6 MB; RSS after 300 `/health` requests 139.5 MB; `/health` p50 1.0 ms.
- Per route, p50 over 10 calls (20 for the chat turn) and the resident set after the scenario, run in this order on one process:

| scenario | p50 | RSS after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chat turn (20 deltas, scripted opencode) | 121.6 ms | 178.5 MB |
| convert PDF (2 pages) | 12.4 ms | 228.3 MB |
| convert PDF (Chromium, two columns, Tamil) | 16.0 ms | 232.7 MB |
| convert DOCX | 61.7 ms | 293.8 MB |
| diff register and page (200 hunks) | 39.5 ms | 326.8 MB |
| file preview, markdown | 1.2 ms | 326.9 MB |
| file preview, TypeScript | 1.2 ms | 326.9 MB |
| Tamil check (raster, then the scripted vision model) | 479.9 ms | 491.2 MB |
| voice transcribe (fake STT sidecar) | 6.6 ms | 491.6 MB |

- Peak: 491.6 MB median over the runs (497.1 MB the largest). Reading it: the 134 MB idle floor grows to about 490 MB once every media path has run once, and it does not come back, because the heavy packages stay loaded: the Tamil check alone adds 165 MB (sharp and librsvg), the DOCX parser 65 MB, the PDF parser 50 MB, the diff viewer 33 MB. That resident growth, not the per-request time, is what the Rust service is compared on.
- Image size: the registry's compressed `nuesslerm/remote-chat-agent` image (arm64) is 441.1 MB for `sha-5aee168` and for `sha-9a91e81` (Docker Hub tags API). The uncompressed size and the container's memory on the VM were not read: `docker` is not reachable from agent-shell through `wf vm exec` (the image listing came back empty). The installed Node media packages alone are about 245 MB (`ffmpeg-static` 76.3, `tesseract.js-core` 43.2, `pdfjs-dist` 35.3, `pdf-parse` 27.2, `@img/sharp` 17.9, `whisper-node` 13.8, `playwright-core` 13.0).
- Limits stated: the voice route is measured against a fake STT sidecar, so it covers the upload and forwarding, not whisper.cpp or ffmpeg; the Tamil check's vision model is scripted, so its time is the raster plus the service's own work; the benchmark does not run two chat turns at once, because the Node service answers concurrent turns with each other's text (gap `rca-chat-event-stream-not-scoped-to-session`). The sandbox's other work was stopped while the five runs went.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 1 (HTTP layer and the shared pipeline)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the Rust binary (`pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent run test:blackbox:rust`): `http.blackbox.test.ts`, 21 of 21, the same file the Node bundle passes (151 passed and 6 skipped in the whole suite there). 11 Rust unit tests; clippy clean. The crate is `backend/remote-chat-agent/rs` (`rca-service`, workspace `backend/Cargo.toml`), std only apart from `serde_json`, like the leetcode-backend service.
- What it is: a thread-per-connection HTTP/1.1 server with keep-alive, chunked request bodies, a 30 MB body limit that answers 413 after draining (the client sees the status, not a reset), a streaming response body (chunked, for server-sent events) and an upgrade hook (for WebSocket); the CORS policy (listed origins with their trailing slash dropped, localhost on any port, the dev-bridge `metro-runner-*` hosts; an unlisted origin gets no allow headers; a preflight is answered 204 before the limiter); the 120 per minute fixed-window limiter keyed on the caller, where the caller is Express's `req.ip` with `trust proxy: loopback` (the front door passes `X-Forwarded-For`); the JSON body parsed for every path, so a malformed body is a 400 before routing; `/health`; and the worktree route's secret check and validation, which reproduces zod 4's issue text. Creating a worktree answers 501 until slice 2.
- Benchmark of this slice (`bench:service:rust` with `BENCH_ONLY=none`, n=5, the same harness as the pre-state record; `docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-rust-slice1.json`): cold start to the first `/health` 30.5 ms (Node bundle: 503.4 ms), idle RSS 2.4 MB (133.6 MB), RSS after 300 health checks 2.5 MB (139.5 MB), `/health` p50 0.41 ms (1.0 ms), artifact 462,080 bytes (14,869,215). Reading it: this is the floor of a service with one route, so the Node comparison is the floor against the floor; the loaded figures come with the media slices. The cold start is bounded by the harness's 25 ms polling.
- Duplication said plainly: `limiter.rs`, `log.rs` and the request and response code are adapted copies of the leetcode-backend crate's. They should become one shared crate once the streaming and upgrade needs of this service have settled; the leetcode-backend service was not touched.
- Next, in order: slice 2 (git, source-control, worktree and the viewers), slice 3 (the small routes and the inventory proxies, whose black-box tests are still to be written), slice 4 (the opencode client, the orchestration engine, chat, control and question), then media, the WebSocket layer, the Claude and Codex gateways, and the cutover.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 2 (git, pull requests, worktrees and the two viewers)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the Rust binary: `http`, `vcs`, `worktree` and `viewers`, 107 of 107 passing (`pnpm --filter @app/remote-chat-agent run test:blackbox:rust`), the same files the Node bundle passes (the viewers file there: 27 passed and 4 skipped, the 4 being the Rust-only security tests). 38 Rust unit tests; clippy clean. `/api/source-control` is the second half of `vcs.blackbox.test.ts`, run against a stub `gh` that records its argv.
- Ported: `/api/vcs` (status parsed from `git status --porcelain=v1 -b`, diff cut at 200,000 UTF-16 units, commit with or without paths, push with `-u`), `/api/source-control` (`gh pr list|view|create`, the seven summary fields, the last output line as the URL), `/api/worktree` create and delete (slug directories, clone or fetch, `git worktree add`, a best-effort `pnpm install`, 409 only when git names the path, the pinned quirk that a repeated request is git's branch collision), `/api/diff` (register, the seven-day `diffs.json` store read at start and written by rename, the annotatable page, expand, annotations to Telegram through `curl`) and `/api/file/*` (markdown through a port of `mdToHtmlFull`, other files as an escaped code block, annotate). The diff and file pages are the TypeScript's own HTML, CSS and script, extracted by a script that evaluates the template literals with placeholders and kept as assets (`rs/assets/`), so the browser sees the same page. Syntax highlighting is `tree-sitter-highlight` with the grammars compiled in (TypeScript, JavaScript, JSON, Python, Rust, Go, Java, C, C++, Ruby, Bash, CSS, HTML, YAML) emitting the same inline colours as the TypeScript's shiki theme; Markdown, XML, SQL, SCSS, Dockerfile and HCL fall back to escaped text for now (no compatible grammar crate tried yet).
- Deliberate differences from the Node service, each with a test that runs only against another implementation: registering a diff needs `X-Bot-Secret` (the Node service answered anyone, and `expand` then read any file under the caller's `cwd`, gap `rca-diff-expand-reads-any-file`), so whoever registers diffs (the Telegram bot, the app) must send it before the cutover, which is not known from this repository and is an owner check; `expand` and the file preview refuse a path whose real location, after symlinks, is outside its root and compare whole path components instead of a string prefix; the token is redacted from a failed clone's message; the JSON embedded in the pages escapes `<` so a title cannot close the script tag; a failed commit reports git's stderr when stdout is empty instead of an empty message. The unauthenticated page and expand stay open behind the unguessable id, as before.
- Benchmark of the slice (same harness, n=5, `rca-rust-slice2.json`): cold start 31.9 ms (Node 503.4), idle RSS 4.0 MB (133.6), diff register and page 9.5 ms p50 at 9.5 MB resident (39.5 ms; Node was at 326.8 MB by then, with every earlier scenario loaded), markdown preview 1.04 ms (1.2), TypeScript preview 0.70 ms (1.2); the whole process peaks at 9.9 MB. Artifact 14,269,840 bytes (462,080 before this slice): the grammars and `fancy-regex` are the growth, so the binary is now about the size of the Node bundle (14,869,215 bytes) though it needs no `node_modules` (about 245 MB of media packages alone); trimming the rarely used grammars is a later size decision, not made here.
- Next: slice 3 (small routes and the inventory proxies, whose black-box tests come first), then slice 4 (the opencode client, the orchestration engine, chat, control and question).

## 2026-10-08: the rtk pipe guard let every `cargo ... | tail` through

- Found when the owner asked why piped cargo commands were running during the remote-chat-agent port. `.agents/hooks/rtk-rewrite.sh` blocks a pipeline whose only job is to trim output (`<cmd> | head|tail|cat`) when `<cmd>` has an rtk form, but it took only exit code 0 of `rtk rewrite` as proof of one. The `cargo` rules answer exit 3 (a rewrite that asks for confirmation), and a head carrying `2>&1` answers exit 1 (rtk has no rewrite for a redirect), so the two commonest shapes of the session, `cargo test 2>&1 | tail -30` and `cd dir && cargo build | head`, passed untouched. Measured with the hook script fed each command: before, `ls | head` and `grep | head` blocked (exit 2), all three cargo shapes passed (exit 0); after, all five block with the rtk form named.
- Change: exit 3 counts as a rewrite, and `2>&1` is dropped from the head before it is asked. Still deliberately alone: a pipe into anything that consumes the bytes (`| grep`, `| wc -l`, `| jq`), a quoted head, a heredoc head, and an explicit `rtk ...` head. So `cargo build 2>&1 | grep -E "^error" | head` still passes; the answer to that is `rtk cargo build`, which already prints only errors.
- Tier: unit, with a stand-in `rtk` on PATH so the test needs no installed binary (`libraries/hook-runtime/tests/rtk-rewrite.test.ts`, 10 tests, the package's 87 pass). No token benchmark: the guard adds a block message and no tokens of its own, and the saving it protects is the one the 2026-09-07 measurement already records.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 4 (the orchestration engine, chat, control and question)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the Rust binary, the same files the Node bundle passes: 176 of 180 pass on Rust and none is skipped (the four that fail are the document-conversion routes, slice 5). `chat` 20 of 20 and `control` 14 of 14 include the six tests that run only against another implementation (concurrent turns do not cross, a finished turn closes its event subscription, a silent opencode ends in a retryable `hard_timeout`, a refused session create ends in an error frame, stop aborts opencode's own session, an unreachable opencode answers JSON errors); the Node bundle runs the same two files with those six skipped, 28 passed. 103 Rust unit tests; clippy clean.
- Ported: the engine over a store (`orchestration/{types,decider,projector,store,engine}.rs`: the decider and projector as pure functions with their unit tests, a file log compatible with `threads/<id>/events.ndjson`, a memory store, one lock around decide, append, project and notify, thread and shell subscriptions that send the snapshot or a replay before going live), the ingestion of runtime events into commands (`ingest.rs`, a port of `ingestion.ts` with its tool-output summaries), the translation of opencode's events (`translate.rs`, `opencode_backend.rs`: the gateway's event mapper, a message-part accumulator so an increment and the snapshot that repeats it are handed out once, and the user's echoed prompt dropped), the reactor (`reactor.rs`: one worker per thread while it has work), the per-thread ingestion loop (`ingestion.rs`) and the chat frames (`compat.rs`), then `/api/chat`, `/api/control/{stop,queue,steer}` and `/api/question/:id/reply`. Two engines as in Node: the chat stream's runs in memory and creates sessions in `RCA_DEFAULT_CWD`, the thread view's (control, question, the WebSocket slice) is on disk when `RCA_STATE_DIR` is set.
- What the four gap records needed, done: the event stream is opened before the prompt and read only for the thread's own session, one loop per thread serving every queued turn and closing its subscription when the last settles or the request gives up; a turn that cannot start (session create refused, prompt refused, stream refused) ends in an error session, so the stream says so; a hard timeout is enforced by the stream itself, not on the next event; the turn is marked running before the prompt goes out (so an interrupt is accepted), and control resolves the opencode session id of a chat thread before aborting or prompting.
- Differences from the Node service, said plainly: text arrives as the increments opencode sends (Node ignored them and forwarded each snapshot as a new message, which duplicates text for a provider that sends growing snapshots); a tool part starts once and completes once instead of once per update; a stream that ends before the turn settles is an error session where Node left the turn running; threads on disk are listed when the engine starts and a turn a crash left running is marked interrupted (Node's `start()` meant this but its index file is never written); a thread id that could name another directory is refused by the file store; a failed answer from opencode to a question reply is a 502 where Node answered 204; claude and codex report available when their executable is on `PATH` (Node probed the SDK package) but have no backend in this build yet, so a request for one is a 400; attachments answer 501 until the media slice. Not changed on purpose: read tools produce no activity in the thread view (the ingestion's tool set has no `file_read`), and a continuing chat turn's `cwd` is the thread's, not the request's.
- Test infrastructure fixed on the way: the fake opencode counted subscribers with `req.on('close')`, which Node 22 does not emit when a client goes away, so its count never fell (the test that a finished turn closes its subscription could not pass anywhere); it uses the response's `close` now. Its prompt script now starts once a subscription opened after the session was created exists, so a service that subscribes before prompting and one that subscribes after both get it. The chat test lists the available CLIs from `/api/agent-clis` instead of assuming claude is installed.
- Benchmark of the slice (same harness, n=5, `rca-rust-slice4.json`): cold start 30.8 ms (Node 503.4), idle RSS 4.5 MB (133.6), chat turn p50 106.5 ms at 6.3 MB resident (121.6 ms at 178.5 MB; the fake opencode's 100 ms delay before its script is in both), diff 9.2 ms, previews 0.99 and 0.64 ms, the whole process peaks at 12.1 MB. Artifact 14,995,376 bytes.
- Next: slice 5, the black-box tests for the media routes and then the Rust port of conversion, voice and the Tamil check; then the WebSocket layer.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 5 (voice, conversion, attachments and the Tamil check)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the Rust binary, the whole suite: 12 files, 245 tests, none skipped. The new files (`voice`, `convert`, `attachments`, `tamil`, in `routes` the document-conversion block) pass on the Node bundle with the Rust-only tests skipped and on Rust with them run (the whisper.cpp path with stand-in binaries, the recording written under the last segment of the client's filename, a picture sent to a model that can see). 156 Rust unit tests (the DSL is golden-tested against the TypeScript original through `rs/tests/golden/dsl.json`); clippy clean with `-D warnings`.
- Ported: the voice-edit DSL and planner (`voice/{dsl,one_shot,planner}.rs`: UTF-16 offsets, the lenient JSON repair, field-name drift, validation, block-body completion, a session held per model, prompt variant and agent, one retry on a malformed answer, abort on a spent budget), speech to text (`voice/stt.rs`: the faster-whisper sidecar and Groq through `curl`, whisper.cpp behind ffmpeg through the binaries `WHISPER_CPP_BINARY` and `FFMPEG_PATH` name), a multipart intake that replaces `busboy` (`multipart.rs`), `/api/voice-transcribe`, `/api/voice-plan` and `/api/voice-edit` with their latency rows, `/api/convert`, document text (`docs.rs`: PDF through `pdftotext`, DOCX through `zip` and `quick-xml`, OCR through the `tesseract` binary with the 300,000-byte guard), the chat attachment path (`attachments.rs`: text and source files, PDF, DOCX, a picture read by OCR and a vision model when the chat model cannot see, with the model capability cache), and the Tamil check (`tamil.rs`, `routes/tamil.rs`: `resvg` and `tiny-skia` for the two panels, `image` for the PNG, the Noto Sans Tamil face embedded in the binary).
- Gap records closed in the Rust service: a picture attached for a model that can see now reaches it beside the prompt (`rca-chat-native-image-attachment-dropped`: the file parts are filed under the turn's message id and taken by the reactor, so they never enter the event log), and the recording is written under the last segment of the client's `filename` (`rca-voice-filename-writes-outside-temp-dir`). Both stay open for the Node service until cutover.
- Differences from the Node service, said plainly: PDF text comes from `pdftotext`, so the image must carry poppler-utils, and the `pdf-extract` fallback the media evaluation named is not shipped (it would add `lopdf` and its parsers to every binary for a path the image never takes; a missing `pdftotext` is a 422 on `/api/convert` and "could not extract text" in a chat turn); an upload that is not multipart is a 400 where Node answered 500; error texts that came from a library (an SVG that does not parse, a model reply that is not valid JSON after the braces are found) are the Rust library's; the printed letter in the Tamil check always uses the app's face, where the Node image did not carry the font directory and fell back to whatever system fonts it had; a pad larger than 16 megapixels at 2x is drawn smaller instead of at full size.
- Benchmark of the slice (same harness, n=5, `rca-rust-slice5.json`, medians): cold start 30.3 ms (Node 503.4), idle RSS 5.5 MB (133.6). Per route, p50 and the resident set after the scenario, Rust against Node: convert PDF 14.7 ms / 6.2 MB (12.4 ms / 228.3 MB), convert PDF from Chromium 14.1 ms / 6.3 MB (16.0 ms / 232.7 MB), convert DOCX 1.3 ms / 6.6 MB (61.7 ms / 293.8 MB), Tamil check 158.1 ms / 44.4 MB (479.9 ms / 491.2 MB), voice transcribe through the sidecar 10.2 ms / 44.5 MB (6.6 ms / 491.6 MB). Two routes are slower at the median, and the reason is the design: converting a PDF spawns `pdftotext` and a transcription spawns `curl`, each about 8 ms of process start that the in-process Node did not pay (Node pays it back in memory: the resident set above is what each scenario left behind in one long-lived process). The whole process peaks at 56.2 MB at its worst run (44.5 MB median), almost all of it the Tamil check's 3.6 megapixel reference panel, which is not returned to the allocator. Artifact 17,205,296 bytes (Node bundle 14,869,215, which does not include the 245 MB of media packages it needed installed).
- Next: the WebSocket layer, black-box tests against the Node bundle first, then the Rust port; then the Claude and Codex gateways, then the cutover.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 6 (the WebSocket layer)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket (a `ws` client speaking to the process), `websocket.blackbox.test.ts`, written against the Node bundle first (the zod issue texts, the global sequence numbers, the order of snapshot and synchronized, and the subscription completing after a deletion were all read off its answers, not assumed): 42 passed and 2 skipped on Node, 44 of 44 on Rust. 178 Rust unit tests (the SHA-1 vectors and the RFC 6455 accept key, every framing rule `ws` closes a connection for with its status code, the heartbeat cutting off a client that never answers, the envelope and command schemas down to the order of the issues); clippy clean with `-D warnings`.
- Ported: the upgrade (`/ws`, the secret as `?token=`, a 401 `unauthorized`, the handshake checks `ws` makes, the first offered subprotocol chosen), the framing (`ws/frame.rs`: masking, fragmentation with control frames between the fragments, UTF-8 checked on text, a 100 MiB cap, the close codes `ws` uses), the envelopes and requests (`ws/{envelope,methods}.rs`, `orchestration/wire.rs`: `ClientEnvelopeSchema` and `ClientOrchestrationCommandSchema` as zod 4 reports them, including that a `z.unknown()` field is required and that a plain object and a discriminated union put `code` and `expected` in different orders), per-connection subscriptions with their own forwarding threads (`ws/subscriptions.rs`), and the heartbeat (a ping every 15 s, two unanswered cut the connection). The engine gained subscriptions into a channel the caller makes, so a subscription can be woken to end.
- Gap records closed in the Rust service: `orchestration.listThreads` and the shell snapshot list what is on disk after a restart (`rca-ws-list-threads-empty-after-restart`; the Node service answers an empty list while `getThread` still finds the thread), and a second subscription under a held id ends the first (`rca-ws-duplicate-subscription-id-leaks`; Node delivers every event twice and can no longer end the first). Both stay open for Node until cutover.
- Differences from the Node service, said plainly: a request that waits on the network (`server.probe`, `models.list`) is answered on its own thread, while the others are answered in the order they arrive (Node's are all concurrent); `server.probe` and `models.list` serve opencode only (slice 7 records why), so the probe lists the CLIs that are available here and `models.list` for another CLI is `models_list_failed` ("not served by this build"); an upgrade on any path but `/ws` is an ordinary 404 where Node left the socket open without an answer; a refused upgrade closes the connection; a store failure on dispatch is an error response (`dispatch_failed`). The `createdAt` of a `thread.create` is ignored in both services (the event carries the server's clock), which the test pins from Node's behaviour.
- Benchmark of the slice (same harness, n=5, `rca-pre-node-ws.json` and `rca-rust-slice6.json`, medians): cold start 32.1 ms (Node 482.9), idle RSS 5.8 MB (134.0). A `listThreads` request over a held socket 0.072 ms (0.130). A hundred sockets each opened, greeted and subscribed to the shell: 1.007 ms per connection (0.991, a tie) and 11.4 MB resident with all hundred held (143.4 MB). Artifact 17,346,032 bytes.
- Next: the Claude and Codex gateways (slice 7: decided below), then the cutover.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, slice 7 (the Claude and Codex gateways: not ported, with the evidence)

- Decision, said plainly because it narrows what "ported" means: the Rust service drives opencode only. `claudeGateway.ts` (1,494 lines, the Anthropic Agent SDK, which bundles a 263 MB `claude` executable and speaks its stream-json control protocol) and `codexGateway.ts` (1,220 lines, JSON-RPC to a `codex app-server` subprocess) are not ported, and the Rust service reports both CLIs as unavailable (`/api/agent-clis`, the WebSocket probe, `cli` in a chat body), where the earlier slices reported them available when their executable was on `PATH`.
- Why this is the deployed behaviour, not a regression: the production image is built by `wf` with one build argument (`GIT_COMMIT`, `clis/wf/src/build_image.rs`), so the Dockerfile's `AGENT_CLIS` stays at its default `opencode`; the build installs the claude SDK only when the list names it, installs `@openai/codex` only when it names codex, and prunes the packages of every backend not listed (`pruneAgentClis`); the Node startup gate refuses to start when `AGENT_CLI` names a backend the image pruned, and `availableAgentClis()` in a pruned image is `["opencode"]`. So the Node service the Rust one replaces on the VM serves opencode alone as well. Nothing in the repository's workflows or the compose file passes `AGENT_CLIS`.
- What stays true: the TypeScript gateways remain in the tree and in the Node bundle for a local run with `AGENT_CLIS=opencode,claude,codex`; the Rust service ignores `AGENT_CLI=claude` or `codex` as the startup gate will (slice 8 refuses to start with a message). The WebSocket and chat paths ask a backend for exactly the operations the `Backend` trait lists (start session, send turn, interrupt, answer a permission or a question, stop, open the event stream), so a Rust gateway for either CLI is a matter of implementing that trait and moving the CLI from "unavailable" to "available"; it is sized at roughly the line counts above and has no test double yet.
- Not verified here: the live VM's `/api/agent-clis` (the one probe through `wf vm exec` ran inside agent-shell, which cannot reach the app container's port); the cutover runbook reads it from the deployed service.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, `/runs` (one-shot runs on the shared planner)

- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket, `runs.blackbox.test.ts`, written against the Node bundle first: 13 of 13 on Node and 13 of 13 on Rust, none skipped (a Rust-only test I had written on the assumption that Node drops the text a failing attempt had produced was run against Node before it was kept, and Node passes it, so it is a plain test and no gap record exists for it). 183 Rust unit tests; clippy clean.
- Ported: `POST /runs` as `runOnce` runs it (`runs.rs`, `routes/runs.rs`): the ladder of the shape (or the pinned models), ranked by the run log's stats unless `OPENCODE_LADDER_IGNORE_STATS=1`, minus the pools the ledger says are cold; each attempt is a turn against opencode (`one_shot::run_capturing`, which now also returns the tool calls and keeps what a failing turn had produced); the tool calls are folded the way `collectTurn` folds them (snapshots of one call merged, names in lower case, `fileChange` as one `patch` per path, a `filePath` in every input that names a file); the diff of what the run wrote is `git diff --stat` scoped to the touched files; the plugin chain itself (the ladder, the cooldowns, the output checks, the run log row) is `rca-core`, the crate `wf dispatch` already uses, so both implementations now judge a run by the same code. The run log and the pool ledger are the files `wf` reads (`OPENCODE_DISPATCH_RUN_LOG`, `OPENCODE_POOL_LEDGER`, else `<repo root>/.agents/tmp/`).
- Differences from the Node service, said plainly: a model that belongs to the claude or codex CLI fails the attempt with the same text Node's provider service gives for a CLI the image does not carry (`Agent CLI "claude" is not available in this image ...`), where the Node bundle on a developer machine with the SDK installed would run it.
- Benchmark of the slice (same harness, n=5, `rca-pre-node-runs.json`, `rca-rust-slice9.json`, medians): a one-shot run against the fake opencode (its 100 ms delay is in both) p50 108.8 ms at 6.8 MB resident (121.6 ms at 168.9 MB); cold start 31.8 ms (446.5), idle RSS 5.8 MB (134.0); artifact 17,525,840 bytes.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, production parity (startup gate, log shipping, vault) and the dev deploy break

- Why: the Node service does four things at boot that the Rust one did not, and a cutover that drops them is a regression the black-box suite cannot see. (1) It fills the environment from the vault for every registered variable that is unset, (2) it ships log records to BetterStack over OTLP with the registered secrets scrubbed, (3) its startup gate refuses a configuration that names an agent CLI the image cannot serve, (4) it writes a heartbeat so a silent process is visible at the sink. All four are in the Rust service now: `secrets.rs` (the registry's variables from the same `registry.json` the vault tooling reads, the vault asked only for variables that are unset, a vault that is down warns once on stderr and the existing environment stands), `telemetry.rs` (the leetcode-backend exporter, `curl` to the OTLP endpoint, service name `remote-chat-agent`, one span per request around `app::handle`), `startup.rs` (`AGENT_CLI` unset or `opencode` serves; a typo names what is supported and what is available; `claude` or `codex` is refused with the reason, since slice 7 did not port those gateways) and `log::start_heartbeat()`. `main.rs` boots in the order Node does: secrets, logger, gate, heartbeat, listen.
- Tier run: black-box integration through a real socket against the Rust binary, the whole suite: 15 files, 304 tests, none skipped (the new `startup.blackbox.test.ts` pins that a typo is refused on both implementations with the same message, and that `claude` and `codex` are refused on Rust only). Integration: `rs/tests/telemetry.rs` drives the real binary against a stand-in `curl` and asserts that a request reaches the sink as a span and that a registered secret's value never appears in a shipped record. 195 Rust unit tests; clippy clean with `-D warnings`.
- Found by reading the Deploy workflow, not by the suite: since the Rust crate joined the `backend/` cargo workspace, every Deploy run on dev from that commit failed in the leetcode-backend leg (`failed to load manifest for workspace member .../remote-chat-agent/rs`, exit 101 at the `cargo build -p leetcode-backend-rs` step; runs 291 to 297, run 290 and earlier were green). Cargo reads every workspace member and its path dependencies even for `-p`, and that Dockerfile copied only its own crate. It now also copies `backend/remote-chat-agent/rs` and `clis/rca-core` (neither is compiled there). Checked by copying exactly the paths its `COPY` lines name into an empty directory and running `cargo metadata --locked --offline`: the workspace loads. The Rust service's own image needs the same member set and copies it.
- Not benchmarked here, on purpose: parity adds one heartbeat thread and one exporter thread that waits until a record is queued. The idle figures recorded for the `/runs` slice were taken before it; the post-state run rebuilds with parity and is the measurement that counts, so no separate run is spent on it.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent Rust service, post-state benchmark of the finished binary (local, n=5)

- Method: the harness that recorded the pre-state (`tests/bench/service.benchmark.test.ts`, n=5, medians, every scenario in one process per run), against the release binary with parity in place: `BENCH_LABEL=post BENCH_OUT=docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-post-rust.json pnpm run bench:service:rust`. Pre-state: `rca-pre-node.json` (the Node bundle, label `pre`), with `rca-pre-node-ws.json` and `rca-pre-node-runs.json` for the two scenarios added later. This is the binary on a developer machine; the same numbers measured inside the deployed container are the next entry.
- Result, Rust against Node (p50 ms, resident set after the scenario in MB): cold start 32.4 (503.4); idle 6.5 MB (133.6); after 300 health checks 6.6 (139.5); `/health` 0.31 (0.95); chat turn 105.8 / 8.6 (121.6 / 178.5; the fake opencode's 100 ms delay is in both); one-shot run 106.8 / 9.0 (121.6 / 168.9); convert PDF 12.8 / 9.0 (12.4 / 228.3); convert PDF from Chromium 12.8 / 9.1 (16.0 / 232.7); convert DOCX 1.1 / 9.3 (61.7 / 293.8); diff register and page 8.7 / 14.7 (39.5 / 326.8); markdown preview 0.95 (1.18); TypeScript preview 0.62 (1.21); Tamil check 156.8 / 38.3 (479.9 / 491.2); voice transcribe through the sidecar 9.7 (6.6); a WebSocket request 0.066 (0.130); a hundred WebSocket connections 0.97 per connection (0.99). Peak resident set of the whole run 43.4 MB median, 54.8 MB worst (Node 491.6, 497.1). Artifact 17,671,312 bytes (Node bundle 14,869,215, plus the media packages it needed installed).
- Said plainly: two scenarios are slower or level at the median because the Rust service spawns the same tools Node ran in process (`pdftotext` for a PDF, 12.8 against 12.4 ms; `curl` for the transcription, 9.7 against 6.6 ms). Idle memory rose from 5.8 to 6.5 MB with parity (the heartbeat and exporter threads, and the vault registry parsed at boot); the hundred-connection figure here is the whole process after every earlier scenario, not the isolated 11.4 MB recorded for slice 6.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent cutover: the VM runs the Rust service

- What shipped: `backend/remote-chat-agent/Dockerfile` now builds `rca-service` (a rust:1-bookworm builder stage on the backend workspace) and runs `/usr/local/bin/rca-service` as the container's CMD behind the existing entrypoint; `wf deploy plan` also ships the service when `backend/Cargo.{toml,lock}` or the embedded Tamil face change (plan test updated first, 16 of 16). Deployed with `wf deploy run remote-chat-agent --ref claude/rca-core` (build natively on the VM, push, redeploy through vm-ops-api), twice: `sha-a0fa1926`, then `sha-0dd11c76`. The Node bundle, its pruned agent SDKs, `node_modules` and `AGENT_CLIS` are gone from the image; Node and pnpm stay in it as tools (worktree installs, `agent-deps replay`), and never as the service.
- Tier run, in the CLAUDE.md order: (1) a runbook-style check against the deployed service through its real entry point, from agent-shell on the VM over the tailnet name `remote-chat-agent-prod:8787`: `/health` reports the deployed commit `0dd11c76...`; `/api/agent-clis` lists opencode as the only available CLI; `POST /runs` is 401 without the secret and 400 with it (empty body); `/ws` is 401 without a token; an unknown path is 404; one real turn through `POST /api/chat` against the live opencode server streamed `http-ok` (session, model_used `opencode-go/deepseek-v4-flash`, chunks, done); a WebSocket client created a thread with `thread.turn.start`, received the receipt (`thread.created`, `thread.message-sent`, `thread.turn-start-requested`), the assistant's reply and the delete receipt. (2) Black-box suite on the binary: 15 files, 304 tests, none skipped. (3) Unit and integration: 195 Rust tests, the telemetry integration test, clippy clean. The sink is checked too: BetterStack returns the service's own lines for what the probes caused (`server.listening`, `health.heartbeat` with its resident set, `ingestion.started` and `ingestion.ended` per thread, and `ws.upgrade.unauthorized` for the two probes that sent no token).
- leetcode-backend: redeployed from the same branch by the pipeline path (`wf deploy run leetcode-backend --ref claude/rca-serde-errors`, image `sha-1a311f7e`, exit 0); `/health` reports `1a311f7e2a104411880f3f1ee74e636b5b74aeaa`, the pushed head. From agent-shell: `POST /api/leetcode/format` without the session secret is 401 `unauthorized`, a body of 1.1 MB is 413, and BetterStack returns `server.listening` and `health.heartbeat` lines from the new container (`uptimeSec=0`). The authenticated routes were run afterwards (entry "authenticated probes on the VM", below).
- No resident Node process: the process table of the container five seconds after start is one process, PID 1, `/usr/local/bin/rca-service`, 5,180 kB resident (the Node image's PID 1 was `node dist/bundle/index.mjs`, 134,528 kB). The entrypoint `exec`s into the service, so no shell or Node stays behind.
- Container numbers on the VM (arm64, one image against the other, `docker run` with no volumes and no vault credentials): image 837,936,988 bytes against 904,610,891 (28 layers against 30), start to the first answered `/health` 393, 307 and 296 ms against 1,496, 1,183 and 1,155 ms (the `docker run` itself is about 180 ms of both), idle resident set 5.1 MB against 131.4 MB. The first Rust image was 1,114,084,028 bytes, 209 MB larger than Node's, because Debian's `ffmpeg` pulls 200 packages (libllvm 109 MB, icu, flite, mesa) for a tool that converts an upload to WAV; it is now the checksum-pinned static build the Node image's `ffmpeg-static` package fetched (same release, same provenance), which brought the Debian package count from 320 to 172 (the Node image has 117) and the image below Node's. Every media tool runs in the image: static ffmpeg 6.0 converts a tone to 16 kHz mono WAV, `pdftotext` 22.12, `tesseract` 5.3.0, `sqlite3`, and whisper.cpp with both models.
- Found while verifying, none a service fault: a WebSocket client from agent-shell failed with a bare socket error because that environment carries no `CHAT_AGENT_API_SECRET`, so the upgrade was a 401 (the script now reads the secret from the vault through `wf`); a local first connection "failing" after a restart was the client racing a three second boot, which is the vault lookup for every registered variable that is unset (the same lookup the Node service does); Node's built-in `WebSocket` client (undici) connects to the Rust service 30 of 30 times, so the handshake is not only `ws`-compatible.
- The diff viewer: `POST /api/diff` now requires `X-Bot-Secret` in the Rust service, and no caller of it exists in this repository (the only mentions are the service, its tests and its docs), so callers outside this repository, if there are any, must send the secret. The gap record stays open for that reason and for the rule that a gap closes through `/cf-compound`.

## 2026-10-08: remote-chat-agent: the Node service and the dependencies only it used are removed; `rca-edge` is retired

- What went (commit `0a62a74`, 168 files, 170 insertions, 29,792 deletions): the TypeScript server, routes, orchestration engine, WebSocket layer, media code (attachment, PDF, DOCX, OCR, speech to text, Tamil raster), the old TypeScript dispatch core that `wf dispatch` replaced in Rust, and the tests of all of it. `src/` goes from 116 files and 22,110 lines to 46 files and 8,089; 59 test files and three support files (a voice-planner bench, a stub binary, a Tamil live harness) go. The kept set is not a guess: it is what the TypeScript compiler reaches from `src/cli/main.ts` (45 files, including the type-only imports a bundler's import graph does not see), the `rca` CLI that `wf work` and `wf visual` still call, with the claude and codex gateways it drives, plus the two data files (`model-ladders.json`, `model-pricing.json`) that `wf`, `rca-core` and the reporting service read. Nothing imported the package's `./run` and `./stats` exports, so they are gone.
- What did not go, and why: the claude and codex gateways stay in the CLI because they are the only way `wf work` reaches an Anthropic model without an opencode credential (the standing credential policy); they are not served by the deployed service, which refuses `AGENT_CLI=claude|codex` by name.
- Dependencies: direct production dependencies of `@app/remote-chat-agent` go from 22 to 6 (`@opencode-ai/sdk`, `effect`, `zod`, the contracts, `@lib/secrets`, `@lib/logging`) plus the optional claude SDK; dev dependencies from 11 to 6 (`ws` and `jszip` stay for the tests). Removed: busboy, diff2html, express, ffmpeg-static, mammoth, pdf-parse, pdfjs-dist, playwright, sharp, shiki, tesseract.js, whisper-node, supertest, `@lib/rate-limit`, `@lib/vm-request-auth`, `@lib/voice-edit-dsl` and `@tooling/service-bundle` as dependencies of this package, and the build allowances for `ffmpeg-static` and `tesseract.js`. `pnpm-lock.yaml` loses 1,192 lines (16 added); the package's production tree lists 1,785 packages against 1,955 (the rest is peer-installed tooling and the claude SDK, which the image no longer carries). `sharp` and `playwright` stay in the workspace because `leetcode-auth-cookie-capture` and `browser-client` use them. Two libraries now have no consumer in the workspace (`@lib/vm-request-auth`, `@lib/socks5-forward`); they are left for their owner to remove.
- Tier run: black-box on the Rust binary by default (no environment variable, the harness builds the binary in global setup and every test runs, none skipped): 15 files, 304 tests. The `rca` package: typecheck and lint clean; 35 test files, 479 tests, 426 pass, 41 skipped and 5 fail, the same five (`agentCliGatewayContract`, the opencode-mock permission cases) that fail on the tree before this commit and that `docs/plans/greenfield/progress.md` already records, so they are not caused by it. e2e: `health` and `convert` pass through the new `process` target, which spawns the Rust binary (the health test was already failing because it expected `{ status: 'ok' }` alone and the service has answered with the protocol version and commit for a long time; it asserts what the body carries now); `chat` needs a real opencode and was not run here. wf black-box: `deploy` 16, `verify-dry-run` and `verify-tier3` 21, `report` 16, all pass. The span-coverage lint rule's test passes with the retired entrypoint.
- Behaviour that changed on purpose: `wf verify` tier 3 now runs the e2e suite against the deployed service (`REMOTE_CHAT_AGENT_BASE_URL`, default `http://remote-chat-agent-prod:8787`) where it used to spawn a local Node copy of the service against the real opencode, so the gate tests what was deployed; the e2e `process` target spawns the Rust binary; `wf report` runs the package's integration tests with the default vitest config (the separate integration config is gone).
- Through the pipeline as well as by hand: the `dev` Deploy workflow rebuilt and redeployed the Rust service after the Node removal (run 300, the `remote-chat-agent` leg took 2 min 21 s with the Rust layers cached) and again after the `rca-edge` retirement (`remote-chat-agent:sha-7a7c3820`). The same live checks, rerun on that image, pass: `/health` reports `7a7c3820...`, the auth gates answer 401 and 400 as before, a real `POST /api/chat` turn streams through the live opencode, and a WebSocket thread is created, answered and deleted.
- Fidelity of the media routes against the Node output, route by route, on the VM (the goal's test for keeping a Node worker): the Node image (`sha-effbe7f8`) and the Rust image (`sha-7a7c3820`) were started side by side with no sidecar and no vault and fed the same files through the real routes. `POST /api/voice-transcribe` (whisper.cpp behind ffmpeg, the path with no sidecar): the transcripts of all seven recordings (five WAV, one WebM, one M4A, from `tests/fixtures/voice`) are identical between the two images, and match the manifest's phrases except where whisper itself hears "dockstring" for "docstring" in both. `POST /api/convert`: the DOCX text is identical; the plain PDF has the same 563 words, differing only in the reading order of one table's cells (the header row and the "Idle RSS" cells swap places); the Chromium PDF (two columns, ligatures, Tamil) has 114 words in Rust against 126 in Node, because `pdfjs` splits ligature words ("con fi rming") and mangles the Tamil, which `pdftotext` returns exactly. So no route fails a fidelity test in Rust's disfavour, and no Node worker is kept. The Tamil check raster was compared in the evaluation entry above: same 920x448 picture, 0.69% of pixels differing by more than 32/255, mean difference 0.80/255; the two pictures were sent to the owner (again with this run).
- The `gh`-backed source-control routes, which the gap record `rca-source-control-gh-unverified` said no session could exercise, answered from the deployed Rust service: list of open pull requests and get of #329 both HTTP 200 with real data from `gh` (and `isDraft: true` for #329), `GET /api/vcs/status` 200 for the shared checkout. `createPullRequest` was not exercised (it opens a pull request), so the record stays open.
- The same suite on both implementations, run once more side by side: the black-box suite as committed at `24ebfbf` (the last commit with the Node service), run against the Node bundle built from that commit, is 15 files and 304 tests with 287 passed and 17 skipped (the Rust-only tests, which pin the gap records and Rust-only behaviours); against the Rust binary, the same 15 files are 304 passed and none skipped. So the 287 shared tests pass on both and the 17 pass on Rust.
- The viewer pages, same inputs through the Node image and the Rust image on the VM (screenshots sent to the owner): the file preview pages for a TypeScript file and a markdown file are byte-identical between the two. The diff pages are not: for a 30-line TypeScript diff the Node page's visible text is 486 characters and the Rust page's 1,210, and for a Rust-source diff 814 and 2,275, because the Node image's page keeps only the first token of every highlighted line (`AGENT_PUBLIC_URL` without `: 'https://bench.example',`, `use` without `crate::telemetry;`), checked in the HTML itself (`serde_json` occurs 0 times in Node's page and twice in Rust's). The cause is in the Node source (`routes/diff.ts` at `24ebfbf`, line 139): the line extractor is the regular expression `/<span class="line"[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/span>/g`, which is non-greedy and stops at the first `</span>`, and shiki wraps every token in its own nested `<span>` inside the `line` span, so only the first token of each line survives. The Rust highlighter emits whole lines and has no such extraction. The Rust page keeps whole lines, with the colour classes the tree-sitter highlighter produces (for example `#ff7b72` for `use` where shiki used `#F97583`). So the diff viewer, a route the owner named for a fidelity test, is better in Rust than the Node output it replaces, not worse.
- The original oracle, unchanged, against the Rust binary: the seven black-box files as committed at `5aee168` (the entry that recorded "7 files, 151 passed and 6 skipped" on Node, 157 tests) with that commit's own harness and fake opencode. 133 of the 157 pass. The 24 that do not fall into three groups, each a change made on purpose and recorded in its slice: (1) 18 diff-viewer tests register a diff without `X-Bot-Secret`, which the owner's decision forbids, so the Rust service answers 401 where the original tests expected 200, 400 or 503 (the committed file sends the secret); (2) 2 chat tests expect `Available: opencode, claude.` from a Node run on a machine with the claude SDK installed, where the deployed image and the Rust service offer opencode alone (the committed file reads the list from `/api/agent-clis`); (3) 4 chat tests depend on that commit's fake opencode starting its script at the prompt, which only a service that subscribes after prompting receives, whereas the gap fix needs the stream opened before the prompt (the committed fake starts the script once a subscription exists; with it alone swapped in, those 4 pass and 137 of 157 pass). The committed versions of those files, which carry the three changes, pass on Node (287 with 17 skipped) and on Rust (304, none skipped) as recorded above.
- Departures from the goal's text, recorded here because they were not when they were made: (1) step 2 said to grow `clis/rca-edge` into the HTTP skeleton, and the service is a new crate (`backend/remote-chat-agent/rs`) instead, `rca-edge` being retired below; (2) the oracle was described as 151 passed and 6 skipped and is 304 tests in 15 files, because the black-box slices the goal asked for (voice, transcribe, plan, tamil, WebSocket) and the later ones (`/runs`, startup, viewers) were added, each first against the Node bundle; (3) the goal's four `rca-*` gap records are twelve now, and the eight that describe Node defects the port fixes are each pinned by a Rust-run black-box test, 14 in all, passing (`vitest -t "rca-"`); the other four are unrelated to the port. Step 0's scratch-crate evaluation, with the Tamil pictures sent to the owner, is the entry "remote-chat-agent media evaluation" above and the strategy section of `docs/plans/rust-services/remote-chat-agent.md`.
- `rca-edge` is retired (commit `bac2cec`): the Rust front door written on 2026-10-06 to tunnel to the Node service, one route at a time. The whole service is Rust, so it fronted nothing; it was never part of any image's command, only compiled into the workspace and copied by three Dockerfiles (`remote-chat-agent`, `agent-shell`, `wf-cli`) and listed in the deploy plan's paths. It is 246 lines of code and 206 of socket tests, a 355 kB binary, removed with its workspace member and lock entry; `wf` builds with `--locked` from exactly the files the Dockerfiles now copy (checked in a clean tree), and the plan test pins the shorter path list. Its two jobs, the CORS policy and the 120 per minute limiter, are in `rca-service` and pinned by `http.blackbox.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-09: leetcode-backend on standard crates (standard-crates plan, phase 2, the pilot)

The Rust backends were written std-only: a hand-written HTTP/1.1 server, outbound HTTP by spawning `curl`, a thread per connection, a fixed-window limiter, CORS, an OTLP exporter and a JSON logger, all duplicated in `remote-chat-agent`. Owner decision (2026-10-09): standard crates, no hand-rolled infrastructure, clean architecture kept. `leetcode-backend` goes first because it is the smallest (2,011 lines).

Cost spike before committing to the stack (scratch crate, release, `opt-level = "s"`, lto): an axum + tokio (2 workers) + reqwest (rustls) + tower-http + tracing + OTLP (http-json) service with the routes of a typical backend is 4.12 MB, 9.8 ms to the first `/health`, 6.6 MB idle RSS, 238 crates; the std-only `leetcode-backend` was 0.63 MB, 5.7 ms, 2.5 MB.

Measured on the real service, n=5 medians, one fresh process per run, the same scripted session (`backend/leetcode-backend/tests/bench/service.benchmark.test.ts`, `bench:service`), pre = `origin/dev` at `9a08c6a`, post = this branch. Records: `docs/audits/benchmarks/leetcode-backend-{pre,post}-std.json`.

| Measure | pre (std-only, curl) | post (axum, reqwest, tracing) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Binary | 0.63 MB | 4.10 MB |
| Cold start to `/health` | 5.12 ms | 12.10 ms |
| Idle RSS | 2.59 MB | 6.42 MB |
| RSS after 60 health calls | 2.63 MB | 6.49 MB |
| Peak RSS (whole session) | 3.01 MB | 6.85 MB |
| `GET /health` p50 | 0.46 ms | 0.46 ms |
| `GET /api/leetcode/user-status` p50 (one relay to a local mock LeetCode) | 6.66 ms | 1.08 ms |
| `POST /api/leetcode/run` p50 (Python, one case) | 33.89 ms | 33.27 ms |
| `POST /api/leetcode/format` p50 (Python) | 37.09 ms | 35.42 ms |

Reading: the standard stack costs 3.5 MB of binary, 7 ms of start and 3.8 MB of resident memory, which the owner accepted as the price of not maintaining the infrastructure. The relay is six times faster because a `curl` process is no longer spawned per request; local code execution is unchanged (the child process dominates).

Tests, by tier. Integration: the TypeScript black-box suite (`tests/blackbox`, 79 tests), expectations changed first for the deliberate differences below (19 failed against the old binary, as intended), then 79 of 79 on the new binary, no skips. Flow: `leetcode-backend-core` 12 (the real use cases, every port a fake), `leetcode-backend-rs` 15 (the real axum router driven with `tower::oneshot`, fakes behind the ports), `service-kit` 7 (real subscriber and exporters against a local ingest, and the rate limit layer). Unit: 5 in core and the service crate, 5 in `service-kit` (redaction and the console writer). `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean. Not run yet: e2e on the VM (the pipeline deploys after merge), the BetterStack sink query, the image size.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library behaviour wins for framework-generated responses; bodies our handlers choose keep their shape): a body that does not fit its request type is 422 with the parser's plain-text message (was 400 with zod-style issues as JSON); a body that is not JSON content is 415 (was read as `{}` and failed validation); the bearer check runs before the body is read, so malformed JSON without the token is 401 (was 400); CORS preflight answers 200 (was 204); the rate limit is a token bucket of 120 per minute per peer address, refilled evenly, and its 429 body is the library's text (was a fixed window with `{"error":"rate_limited"}`); routes are exact, so `/HEALTH/` is 404 (was routed the way Express did); an unknown path outside `/api/leetcode` is an empty 404 (was `{"error":"not_found"}`); console log lines are the tracing JSON (`timestamp`, `level`, `message`, fields, `target`) instead of `time`, `level`, `event`; the OTLP body and attributes are unchanged in meaning. Two emulations of the TypeScript original were dropped: JavaScript truthiness for `isSignedIn` (now a boolean or false) and the error text "Cannot read properties of null" for a null GraphQL answer.

Architecture: `backend/leetcode-backend/core` holds the application layer (use cases, ports as traits, request types, the in-memory fakes behind a `testing` feature); `backend/leetcode-backend/rs` holds `infrastructure` (reqwest to LeetCode and the capture tool, a tempfile-and-rename JSON store, `tokio::process` for the code runner) and `api` (axum routes, bearer and admin checks, error mapping) and `main.rs` wires them; there is no `domain` layer because the service has no rules beyond the use cases. Where Rust does not fit the plan: request types derive `Deserialize` in the application layer so the api can read them straight from a body (a `NonEmpty` newtype replaces the validator crate, which the plan had listed); the capture port is a synchronous fire-and-forget `request_login` because the adapter spawns the request, which keeps tokio out of core; the only hand-written code left in the service is none, and in `service-kit` it is the redacting OTLP log layer (the standard appender cannot redact).

## 2026-10-09: remote-chat-agent on standard crates (standard-crates plan, phase 3, slices A to C)

`remote-chat-agent` was the larger copy of the same std-only stack as `leetcode-backend`: its own HTTP/1.1 server, WebSocket framing and SHA-1, multipart parser, `curl` for every outbound call, a fixed-window limiter, CORS, an OTLP exporter, a JSON logger and a regex markdown converter. Delivered as three stacked draft PRs on top of the leetcode pilot: #334 (axum, tokio, reqwest, tower-http, tower_governor, `tokio::process`, `service-kit`), #335 (`pulldown-cmark`) and #336 (a `core` crate with the thread view's domain and application layers, and flow tests). Open for later slices: zod-style error JSON to serde errors, and the `log.rs` facade call sites to `tracing` macros.

Measured on the real service, n=5 medians, one fresh process per run, the same scripted session (`backend/remote-chat-agent/tests/bench/service.benchmark.test.ts`, `bench:service`). pre = the std-only binary built from this branch's parent before the work started (17,671,312 bytes), post = the release build of #336 plus the `TCP_NODELAY` fix. Records: `docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-pre-std.json` and `rca-post-std.json`. Both runs read the same code fixture (`tests/blackbox/vcs.blackbox.test.ts`, because the Node `vcs.ts` the harness used to read was removed with the Node service), so they are comparable with each other but not with `rca-post-rust.json`.

| Measure | pre (std-only, curl) | post (axum, reqwest, tracing) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Binary | 17.67 MB | 21.61 MB |
| Cold start to `/health` | 32.54 ms | 33.76 ms |
| Idle RSS | 6.53 MB | 10.26 MB |
| RSS after 300 health calls | 6.61 MB | 10.35 MB |
| `GET /health` p50 | 0.40 ms | 0.39 ms |
| chat turn p50 | 105.52 ms | 104.97 ms |
| run once p50 | 106.18 ms | 107.45 ms |
| convert pdf p50 | 12.27 ms | 11.33 ms |
| convert docx p50 | 1.12 ms | 1.14 ms |
| diff register and page p50 | 7.90 ms | 8.00 ms |
| file preview (markdown) p50 | 0.83 ms | 0.68 ms |
| tamil check p50 | 151.73 ms | 149.41 ms |
| voice transcribe p50 (one call to a local fake STT) | 8.57 ms | 2.00 ms |
| ws request p50 | 0.06 ms | 0.09 ms |
| ws, 100 connections p50 | 0.90 ms | 0.81 ms |
| Peak RSS over the whole session | 54.69 MB | 54.08 MB |

Reading: the standard stack costs 3.9 MB of binary and 3.7 MB of idle resident memory, which matches the dependency-cost spike of the pilot (4.1 MB, 4 MB) and which the owner accepted as the price of not maintaining the infrastructure. Start-up and every route are unchanged within noise, and the whole session peaks no higher. The one clear gain is voice transcribe (about four times faster) because the call to the STT sidecar is a pooled reqwest request and no longer a spawned `curl`.

A regression the benchmark caught and the fix: the first post run showed `ws, 100 connections` at 44.0 ms against 0.9 ms, the 40 ms of a delayed ACK meeting Nagle's algorithm on the small handshake and hello writes. `axum::serve` does not set `TCP_NODELAY` and the old server did. Both services now set it on every accepted connection (`ListenerExt::tap_io`); the ws figure is 0.81 ms after. The leetcode-backend numbers recorded for the pilot were taken before the fix and move by noise only (run 33.27 ms to 28.75 ms, format 35.42 ms to 33.25 ms).

Tests, by tier. Integration: the TypeScript black-box suite (`tests/blackbox`, 304 tests plus one new case), expectations changed first for the deliberate differences below, then 305 of 305 on the new release binary, no skips; the 413 case repeated five times to check it is not racy. Flow: 7 in `core/tests/flow_orchestration.rs` (the real engine, reactor and ingestion loop over a fake agent, an in-memory log and a fake working tree) and 8 in `rs/tests/flow_http.rs` (the real router and layers driven with `tower::oneshot` over fake agents, a chat turn through to its SSE frames included). Contract: one `store_contract` runs against the in-memory store and the file store. Unit: 130 in the service crate and 37 in the core. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` is clean. Not run in this entry: e2e on the VM (next).

Deliberate behaviour changes (library behaviour wins for framework-generated responses; bodies our handlers choose keep their shape): a preflight is answered 200 by tower-http (was 204), and only an `OPTIONS` with `Access-Control-Request-Method` is a preflight, so a bare `OPTIONS` is routed (405) and counted by the limiter; the allowed methods, headers and max age ride the preflight and a simple response carries the origin and `Vary` (the allowed headers are written lower-case); 429 is a token bucket from `tower_governor` with the library's body (was `{"error":"rate_limited"}`); `/ws` is a route, so without an upgrade it is 400 (401 without the token) where it used to fall through to a 404, and the handshake refusals are axum's; an oversized body is refused by axum's body limit and no longer drained by the server; the markdown preview is CommonMark (paragraphs inside blockquotes and list items, newlines between elements), raw HTML in a document is shown as text and a `javascript:`, `data:` or `vbscript:` link target is replaced by `#`; log lines follow the `tracing` JSON shape and the core's events use snake_case fields.

Architecture: `backend/remote-chat-agent/core` has `domain` (types, projector, decider, ingest, translate, compat, accumulator, agent events; pure) and `application` (engine, reactor, ingestion and the ports `Backend`, `WorkingTree`, `Store`); `backend/remote-chat-agent/rs` has `infrastructure` (opencode client and backend, file store, git working tree, proc, outbound client, vault, documents) and `api` (router, ws, wire checks, shared state, CORS policy); `main.rs` is the composition root. Deviations to report: the voice DSL and planner, attachments, tamil, runs, edit stats and the opencode proxy routes still call concrete clients and sit outside the four layers; ids and timestamps are read ambiently inside `translate` and the engine; the engine and reactor use tokio channels and `spawn` directly; `MemoryStore` sits beside its port; `/runs` still calls `rca-core`'s synchronous `run_once` on a blocking thread (phase 4 removes that).

## 2026-10-09: wf outbound HTTP and OTLP on standard crates (standard-crates plan, phase 4, slices A to D)

`wf` reached every HTTP endpoint by spawning `curl` (nineteen `Command::new("curl")` sites: the local Metro probes, the signed vm-ops-api and tailnet calls, the BetterStack ClickHouse reads, the Gemini, Tailscale and ORDS APIs, the opencode preflight, the cooldown mirror, the live dispatch tee, the telemetry posts) and exported OTLP by writing a curl config file to a child's stdin.
