All of it now goes through one blocking client, `clis/wf/src/http.rs` on `ureq` 3 (rustls with the platform verifier, SOCKS5 for the tailnet's userspace proxy with `socks5h://` so the proxy resolves the host name, gzip; redirects are followed only where a call site asks, as curl did not follow them by default), and the OTLP request is built from `opentelemetry-proto`'s message types (`clis/wf/src/otlp.rs`; its serde mapping is the OTLP/JSON encoding) instead of hand-assembled JSON. There is no tokio in the CLI: `ureq` is blocking and `opentelemetry-proto` is used only for its types (no tonic, no SDK exporter). `Command::new("curl")` is gone from `wf`; `cdp` still has one, which is the next slice.

Measured with the release binary, n=5 medians after a warm-up, against the std-only binary built from this branch's parent (`wf-pre-std`): the file size and startup of `wf --help`, and the wall time of one `wf secrets get` that posts its telemetry event to a local HTTPS ingest (the old binary through curl, the new one in process; the script is `wf-telemetry-bench.py` in the session scratchpad).

| Measure | pre (curl) | post (ureq, opentelemetry-proto) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Binary | 5.71 MB | 6.97 MB |
| `wf --help` cold start p50 | 1.57 ms | 1.99 ms |
| `wf --help` peak RSS p50 | 12.1 MB | 12.2 MB |
| one command that posts telemetry, wall p50 | 11.9 ms | 5.6 ms |

Reading: the standard stack costs 1.26 MB of binary (rustls, ureq and the proto types) and 0.4 ms of startup, which the owner accepted; every command that made an outbound call is faster because it no longer forks and execs `curl`. Peak resident memory of a plain command is unchanged. The peak RSS of the telemetry run is not reported: `wait4` returned the parent's high-water mark for both binaries.

Tests, by tier. Integration: the TypeScript black-box suite for `wf` (`tooling/wf-blackbox`) kept its expectations about what is sent and what is reported, but its fakes moved from stub `curl` binaries to servers: a mock HTTP service, a mock tailnet (a SOCKS5 proxy that records each `host:port` it is asked for and joins the connection to a local service, `helpers/mock-tailnet.ts`), and a mock HTTPS server with a throwaway CA (`helpers/mock-https-server.ts`, a CA and a leaf because rustls refuses a certificate that is its own CA), each a separate process because the tests spawn the CLI synchronously. Converted: ios, ios-launch, ios-dev-client, ios-expo-go, metro-forward, metro-link, metro-teardown, agent-deps-install, deploy, deploy-run, verify-tier3, dispatch-stats, dispatch-run (preflight and `--live`), vm-ops, vm-exec, vm-logs, vm-recover, report-health, and the telemetry cases that were a Rust test with a curl stub (now `secrets-telemetry`). The full suite after the change: 1141 of 1142 on the debug binary. The one failure, `dispatch-batch` "a file whose dispatch fails ... FAIL 0s", reads `FAIL 1s` or `FAIL 2s` on this sandbox with the std-only binary as well (three of three runs), so it is this machine's speed against a test that asserts a rounded duration, not this change. Unit: 47 in the `wf` binary; the golden OTLP payloads of the caveat guard are now compared as typed `ExportLogsServiceRequest` values, so the standard types are the check.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library behaviour wins): a transport failure has no `curl` exit code any more, so `wf vm logs` exits 1 with the client's error text (it exited with curl's code, for example 6); the OTLP records of `wf log emit` and of the caveat guard are serialised by `opentelemetry-proto` (all default fields present, such as `droppedAttributesCount`, and empty `traceId` and `spanId`), and the caveat guard records now carry `observedTimeUnixNano` as `wf log emit` already did; the black-box suites point the opencode preflight and the tailnet at a closed local port, so no test reaches a real network.

Architecture: `http.rs` is the one adapter every command goes through (a `Request` builder, and a `Via` for the environment's proxies, a direct connection, or a SOCKS5 proxy); `otlp.rs` builds and posts the log request. Deviation: the client is blocking and each command calls it directly, with no port trait for outbound HTTP in the CLIs; the black-box tier is the check for CLI entry points, as the plan says. The `cdp` WebSocket client, the `vnc` and `browser_daemon` HTTP servers and the percent-encoding copies are the next slices.

## 2026-10-09: cdp, the browser and vnc servers and the encoders on standard crates (standard-crates plan, phase 4, slice E)

What the second half of the CLI survey found and what moved. `cdp` carried its own WebSocket client (handshake with a fixed key, masked frames, pong replies, a hand-written base64 and `form_encode`, an HTTP/1.1 GET over `TcpStream` and, for the network proxy, a `curl` spawn). `wf` carried three static-file servers (`wf visual`, the bench lab, the browser daemon's device-frame route), the browser daemon's JSON server, the VNC control listener, an HTTP client for the daemon, a hand-written SOCKS5 client for the tailnet forwarder, `hex`, three `percent_decode` copies, three `form_encode` and two `encode_uri_component` copies. All of those now use crates: `tungstenite` 0.30 for the WebSocket, `base64`, `form_urlencoded` and `percent-encoding` for the encoders, `hex`, `socks` for the SOCKS5 handshake, `tiny_http` for every server, `mime_guess` for content types, `httparse` for the token gate's request heads, and the shared client for every outbound call. The client moved out of `wf` into its own workspace crate, `clis/http` (`cli-http`), so `cdp` and `wf` use one configuration of `ureq`. No tokio anywhere in the CLIs.

The one place that needed design: `tungstenite` has no split read and write halves, and `cdp` reads on a thread while any thread may send (the answer to a paused request, a screencast ack). The connection is one TCP stream cloned twice, a reader socket and a writer socket, each wrapped in a `Wire` whose writes take one shared lock for the whole buffer tungstenite hands it, so a command and the pong tungstenite answers a ping with are never interleaved. Frames, masking, ping and pong, close and the handshake are all the library's.

Measured n=5 medians, release builds, one headless Chromium, the same example binary built against the parent commit and this one (`bench_eval`: 300 `eval("1 + 1")` round trips and 20 full screenshots per run; the script is in the session scratchpad): `eval` p50 0.195 ms before and 0.191 ms after, p95 0.44 and 0.39 ms, screenshot p50 34.1 ms before and 34.0 ms after. So the standard WebSocket costs nothing measurable on the path every browser command takes. `wf` release binary: 7.31 MB (5.71 MB before phase 4, 6.97 MB after slices A to D, so this slice adds 0.34 MB for tungstenite, tiny_http, mime_guess, socks, httparse and the encoders); `wf --help` cold start p50 1.77 ms against 1.62 ms before phase 4.

Tests, by tier. Real-dependency integration (a real headless Chromium): `cdp` 22 (17 on the attached session, 5 on the raw connection). Two are new, written for the path that used to be `curl`: a page fetches a URL the network proxy routes through this process and the upstream sees this process's `Accept-Encoding: gzip` rather than the browser's list, and a proxied URL that cannot be reached shows the 502 body. All 22 now run in parallel with none skipped: before, the profile directory was shared, so a parallel run printed `skipped: no Chromium` for three of the five raw-connection tests while still reporting them as passed; each launch has its own profile now. Black box (`tooling/wf-blackbox`, the real `wf` binary): 1140 of 1142 on the in-repo debug binary, the browser, vnc, visual and bench suites included (the bench lab and `wf visual` serve a real export to Chromium through `tiny_http`, the 34 listener and token-gate cases pass against the new listener and `httparse`). The two failures are the `dispatch-batch` duration assertions (`ok 0s` or `FAIL 0s` read as 1s to 3s on this machine, the same family the std-only binary fails). A binary copied out of the repository fails more tests (the harness and registry lookups follow the executable's path), so the suite is run on the in-repo binary only. Unit: 44 in `wf` (its binary and its test files), 7 in `cli-http`, 20 in `rca-core`.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library behaviour wins): JavaScript is served as `text/javascript` (RFC 9239, what `mime_guess` says), where the daemon said `application/javascript`, and every served file's type comes from the full table, not the eight extensions the daemon knew; the servers keep connections alive (HTTP/1.1 through `tiny_http`), where each old server closed after one reply; a request body to the VNC control listener over 1 MiB is answered 413 where the old server dropped the connection; the `cdp` WebSocket accepts messages of any size (the old reader allocated whatever length a frame announced); base64 text with embedded whitespace is no longer accepted (the browser never sends it); the VNC link tokens and ids come from `getrandom`, which fails loudly, where the old `/dev/urandom` read left a token of zero bytes if the file could not be read.

Architecture deviations, stated plainly. The VNC token gate (`wf/src/vnc/proxy.rs`) is still a byte-level relay: its heads are parsed by `httparse`, but it writes its own 403 and 502 answers, rewrites the `Connection` header and relays a plain response and an upgraded WebSocket stream by copying bytes, and an `https` backend is still reached through `openssl s_client`. A reverse proxy that carries a full-duplex upgrade to a TLS backend needs an async runtime or a poll loop, and the owner ruled out tokio in the CLIs; a frame-level relay would also change what the gate's black-box tests pin ("a raw pipe to the backend"). It is the one hand-written HTTP forwarder left, listed in the report for `clis/wf`. The CLIs' argument parsing was still the repository's own at that point (`clap` not adopted: every command's parser would change, and with it the error texts the suite pins); superseded by "wf's options on clap" below. The `extern "C"` declarations for `kill`, `signal`, `setsid` and `gethostname` and the three copies of civil-date arithmetic are the next slice.

## 2026-10-09: wf signals, dates and temp files on standard crates (standard-crates plan, phase 4, slice F)

The last hand-written systems code in the CLIs. `wf` declared its own `extern "C"` bindings for `kill`, `signal`, `setsid`, `gethostname`, `dup`, `dup2`, `wait4` (with a hand-laid-out `rusage`) and `_exit` in eleven places, and kept three copies of Howard Hinnant's civil-date arithmetic plus a hand-written ISO parser, and nanosecond-named scratch directories. Now: `nix` for `kill`, `killpg`, `setsid` and `gethostname`, `signal-hook` for every signal handler (a flag where the handler only sets a flag, a registered action where a second signal must leave at once), `libc` for `dup`, `dup2` and `wait4` with its own `rusage`, `tempfile` for the scratch files and directories, and the `time` crate behind `rca_core::time` (`iso`, `utc_date`, `parse`, `date_ms`) for every date in `wf`, which removes the copies in `wf/src/time.rs`, `tailscale.rs`, `agent_deps.rs`, `knowledge.rs` and `secrets/telemetry.rs`. There is no `extern "C"` left in `clis/`.

Measured on the release build, n=7 after a warm-up (the same method as the previous two entries): binary 7.36 MB (7.31 MB after slice E, 5.71 MB before phase 4), `wf --help` cold start p50 2.0 ms (1.77 ms after slice E, 1.62 ms before phase 4; the spread between runs on this machine is about 0.2 ms, so the last step is within noise of the one before it).

Tests, by tier. Black box, the real `wf` release binary: 1141 of 1142. The one failure, `dispatch-batch` "a file whose dispatch fails ... FAIL 0s", is the one the std-only binary fails three of three times on this machine (rounded duration of a stub dispatch). The debug build fails a second `dispatch-batch` assertion (`ok 0s` reads `ok 1s`) because an unoptimised `wf` takes over half a second on a dispatch here; the release binary does not, so the suite is run on the release binary from now on. Unit: 44 in `wf`, 20 in `rca-core` (the date tests now cover `utc_date`, `date_ms` and the zone-less form), 7 in `cli-http`, 22 `cdp` against a real Chromium.

Deliberate behaviour changes: `rca_core::time::parse` reads RFC 3339 and a date-time without a zone (UTC, seconds optional) and nothing else, so `2026-10-06T12:00Z` (no seconds, with a zone) and `+02` (an offset without minutes) are refused where the old hand-written parser accepted them, and a calendar day that does not exist (`2026-02-30`) is refused (the arithmetic used to roll it over); the Ctrl-C handlers of `wf` long waits are installed once and, after `release`, do what the signals do by default again (the old code restored the default disposition; `signal-hook` cannot, so the action itself falls back to the default handler).

Architecture: nothing new; these are leaf utilities. Deviations that remain for `clis/`: the VNC token gate described in the previous entry, the argument parser, and the cdp stroke generator `mulberry32`, which is a deliberate port of the browser harness's seeded generator so a seed gives the same strokes as before (it is not infrastructure).

## 2026-10-09: remote-chat-agent's request validation on serde (standard-crates plan, phase 3, slice D)

`remote-chat-agent` checked every request a client sends with validators written by hand to reproduce what zod 4 reports for the TypeScript contracts: `zod.rs` (issue constructors for `invalid_type`, `too_small`, `invalid_union`, `invalid_value` and the discriminated-union notes), `api/wire.rs` (the twelve orchestration commands, field by field, issues in declaration order), `api/ws/envelope.rs`, the parameter checks in `ws/methods.rs` and `ws/subscriptions.rs`, the voice-edit DSL's command, alternative and response parsers, the edit-stat row checker, and the request checks of `POST /runs` and `POST /api/worktree`. All of it is now `serde` types read through `serde_path_to_error` (`rs/src/schema.rs`: `parse`, plus the four rules a field can have that a Rust type does not: `NonEmpty`, `Utc`, the closed word sets as enums, and, where a range matters, `Text<MIN, MAX>` and `Ranged<MIN, MAX>`). `zod.rs` (106 lines) and `util::type_name` are deleted, `wire.rs` is 304 lines instead of 422 (tests included) and `envelope.rs` 116 instead of 135, and the tests beside each say what serde says.

Measured with `bench:service` (n=5, the same method as the earlier slices; `docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-post-serde.json`, compared with `rca-post-std.json`): binary 21.85 MB (21.61 MB before), cold start 34.5 ms (33.8 ms), idle RSS 10.37 MB (10.26 MB), `GET /health` p50 0.38 ms, chat turn p50 104.8 ms (105.0), run once 107.5 ms (107.4), ws request 0.087 ms (0.088), voice transcribe 2.2 ms (2.0). Every route is within noise of the previous slice; the validation was never on a hot path.

Tests, by tier. Integration (the TypeScript black-box suite on the real release binary): 314 of 314, no skips. It was run unchanged against the new binary first: 20 failures in `websocket.blackbox.test.ts` and 6 in the HTTP suites, all of them assertions on zod's issue JSON, and nothing else; the expectations were then rewritten to serde's messages (the table rows of the WebSocket suite are one case per rule now, because only the first problem is reported). Flow (the real axum router and engine, fakes for the ports): 8 in `flow_http.rs`, 7 in `core/tests/flow_orchestration.rs`. Unit: 132 in `rca-service`, among them one test per command rule, per envelope rule, per DSL rule and per HTTP body, each asserting the exact message.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library behaviour wins). Only the first problem of an input is reported, in serde's words and led by the path to the field (`modelSelection.cli: unknown variant ...`), where zod listed every issue as JSON. For a WebSocket client that means `error.message` of a `bad_envelope` or `invalid_params` response is that one sentence and no longer an indented JSON array; the TypeScript client shows the message as text either way and the server envelope schema is unchanged. For the HTTP routes the body is `{ "error": "<the sentence>", "code": "INVALID_REQUEST" }` for `POST /runs` and `POST /api/worktree` (the worktree contract's own `WorktreeError` shape; it was `error: "Invalid request"` plus a `details` array of issues) and `{ "error": "invalid_edit_stat", "message": "<the sentence>" }` for `POST /api/edit-stats` (it was `issues`, at most five). A ping's `ts` must be an integer, so `5.0` is refused where it was read as `5`; a command's `createdAt` must be an RFC 3339 time in UTC with seconds and a `Z` (zod also took `2026-01-01T00:00Z`); an optional field given as `null` is read as absent (zod refused it); a request body of the wrong shape (an array, a string) is "invalid type: ..., expected a map". The planner's "Schema validation failed" error carries serde's sentence, and a model reply that is partly valid still keeps the alternatives that validate.

Architecture: unchanged. Deviations: a DSL command is internally tagged, and serde reports a failed range check on one of its fields at the command (`alternatives[0].commands[0]: invalid value: integer 0, expected an integer of at least 1`), not at the field, while an unknown `type`, a missing field and a wrong type name their field. The call sites of `log::info`, `log::warn` and `log::error` stay: `log.rs` is twenty-five lines over `tracing` (the event name is the message, the metadata rides in one `meta` field), and some call sites (`voice.rs`, `one_shot.rs`) build their metadata or even their event name at run time, which `tracing` macros cannot take as field names; rewriting the other thirty would change the shape of every line this service writes to BetterStack for no gain.

## 2026-10-09: remote-chat-agent on the VM from the pipeline-built image (standard-crates plan, e2e)

Tier: e2e, against the real VM through `wf vm exec` (a script run inside agent-shell on the tailnet) and `wf vm logs`. The image `docker.io/nuesslerm/remote-chat-agent:sha-66bf481c` was built by the VM from `claude/rca-serde-errors` with `wf deploy run remote-chat-agent --ref claude/rca-serde-errors` (exit 0, first attempt after the `clis/http` COPY fix of `4b08d98`); vm-ops-api `ps` shows the container `Up (healthy)` on that tag beside the unchanged opencode and tailscale containers.

- Health: `GET /health` answers `{"status":"ok","wsProtocolVersion":1,"commit":"66bf481c4406823d7e81c1e716594eb82a700e51"}`, the commit of the code the branch built (the later commit only adds reports). `leetcode-backend` answers `commit 07783088...`, the last commit that changed it.
- WebSocket: an upgrade without the token is 401 `unauthorized` and the service logs `ws.upgrade.unauthorized` with the caller's address. The authenticated handshake was not run in this probe: the shared secret is not in agent-shell's environment and fetching it from the vault in the same call as other commands was refused by the approval guard (reported once; not worked around). It was run afterwards, once the guard allowed a fetch beside other commands (entry "authenticated probes on the VM", below); until then that tier was covered by the black-box suite (314 of 314 on the same binary) and the flow tests only.
- Convert: a generated one-paragraph `.docx` returns 200 `{"text":"vm probe hello\n\n"}`; a text file is 415 `Unsupported file type`.
- Voice: `/api/voice-plan` with an empty body is 400 `Missing transcript`; `/api/voice-transcribe` reaches the real STT sidecar, which rejects a zero-filled file (500 with the sidecar's error) and, for a valid one-second silent WAV, hears nothing (500 `sidecar STT returned no transcript`, the behaviour `voice.blackbox.test.ts` pins). A synthesised recording of "Make the title bigger" (espeak-ng, installed in the sandbox for the probe, 22 kHz mono WAV sent from agent-shell) returns 200 `{"transcript":"Make the title bigger."}`.
- No resident Node process: the container's `CMD` is `/usr/local/bin/rca-service`, the entrypoint ends in `exec "$@"` and starts nothing else, and the service's own heartbeat reports 13 to 15 MB resident (a Node server was above 100 MB).
- BetterStack: `wf vm logs remote-chat-agent` returned lines from the new path for the probes above, with their trace and span ids from the `tracing` layer (`WARN ws.upgrade.unauthorized`, `INFO voice_edit.upload_received`, `ERROR voice_transcribe.failed`, `ERROR response failed ... latency=31 ms`) and `server.listening` plus `health.heartbeat` from the restart.

## 2026-10-09: wf's VNC gate and control listener on tokio, axum, reqwest and tower-http (standard-crates plan, phase 4b)

The done-when grep still found hand-written HTTP in `clis/wf/src/vnc/`: the token gate (`proxy.rs`) read request heads with `httparse`, wrote its own 403 and 502 answers, rewrote `Connection` headers, copied bytes for an upgraded WebSocket and reached the self-signed HTTPS desktop through an `openssl s_client` child process; `http.rs` carried `read_head` and `respond`; and `listener.rs` set one `access-control-allow-origin: *` header by hand. The plan ruled out tokio in the CLIs, which made a standard replacement impossible, so the question went to the owner, who allowed tokio inside `wf auth listener` only (2026-10-09). Every other `wf` command stays blocking.

What changed. The gate is an axum server in front of one `reqwest` client (rustls with the native roots; `https+insecure://` skips verification, `https://` verifies): a plain request is forwarded with its headers minus the hop-by-hop ones and its answer is streamed back with the session cookie added; an upgrade takes `hyper::upgrade::OnUpgrade` from the request, forwards the upgrade through reqwest, answers 101 with the backend's headers and joins the two upgraded connections with `tokio::io::copy_bidirectional`. The control channel is an axum router with `RequestBodyLimitLayer` (1 MiB) and `tower_http::cors::CorsLayer` on `/desktop/fit` only; the blocking engine and launcher run under `spawn_blocking`. The runtime (two workers) is built inside `vnc::listen()`. `vnc/http.rs`, the `httparse` dependency and the `openssl` child process are gone, and the vnc image no longer installs `openssl`. Six direct dependencies were added to `clis/wf` (`tokio`, `axum`, `tower-http`, `reqwest`, `hyper`, `hyper-util`, at the versions the backend workspace already ships; `tower` and `http-body-util` for tests); `clis/Cargo.lock` gains 65 packages, most of them `icu_*` and `idna` behind `url` and the rustls stack. `pnpm run audit` covers the JavaScript tree and timed out in the sandbox; there is no Rust audit tool installed, so the dependency vetting is by version identity with the backends.

Measured (release builds, the same script for both binaries, `/tmp/.../bench-vnc.mjs`, n=5 medians; the before binary is the build of `claude/rca-serde-errors`):

| Measure | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `wf` binary | 7,363,608 B | 8,149,368 B |
| `wf --help` cold start (interleaved, 30 spawns per round, 5 rounds) | 3.45 ms | 3.37 ms |
| `wf auth listener` idle RSS | 7.14 MB | 8.71 MB |
| One gated GET, new connection each (300 per run) p50 | 0.69 ms | 0.53 ms |
| One gated WebSocket echo round trip p50 | 0.078 ms | 0.073 ms |

Reading: the runtime exists only in the listener, so the other commands start as fast as before; the listener costs about 1.6 MB more resident memory and the binary 0.8 MB; the gate is a little faster because connections to the backend are pooled.

Tests, by tier. Integration (`tooling/wf-blackbox` `auth-listener.integration.test.ts`, the real release binary, mock backends as separate Node processes): written first and run against the old binary, two were red (a preflight for `/desktop/fit` was a 404; the gate answered `Connection: close`), three pinned behaviour that already held (a 1.2 MB body is refused on the control port, a gzip answer passes untouched, a real RFC 6455 echo server behind the gate negotiates a subprotocol and echoes a binary frame); one test was rewritten because the library answers 413 from the declared Content-Length without reading the body (the old test posted a 1.2 MB body; a client still writing can see a reset, so the new one declares the length and sends nothing). Then 39 of 39 on the new binary, none skipped, the `https+insecure` case included. The whole suite: 1146 of 1147 with `--maxWorkers=3` (the one failure is the known `dispatch-batch` rounded-duration assertion, red on the std-only baseline too); two earlier full runs at full parallelism on this 4-core machine failed 3 and 6 timing-sensitive tests (browser video, `deploy run`, `metro _forward-log`) that pass when their files run alone. Flow and unit (`cargo test -p wf`): 50 passed, 10 of them new (gate: target parsing, 403 for no, wrong or unknown tokens, the refused upgrade told to close, 502 when the backend is down, cookie authorization; control router driven in-process with `tower::oneshot`: 401 and 403, 404 for a GET or an unknown path, the preflight only on the fit route, 413 for a body over 1 MiB). `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean for all of `clis/`. E2E: the gate against the desktop container was not run (it needs the trigger secret, and the owner declined reading secrets for probes). What did run on the VM: `wf deploy run wf-cli vnc-auth-capture --ref claude/wf-vnc-gate-tokio --build-only` built both images natively (arm64) from the branch and pushed them as `sha-43261cb4`, exit 0, nothing redeployed; this is the native compile of the new dependency set that the plan listed as a risk. The vnc image takes its `wf` from `nuesslerm/wf-cli:latest`, so `wf-cli` must be rebuilt as `latest` before the vnc image is rebuilt without `openssl`; until then a rebuilt vnc image would carry the old gate and no `openssl`.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library wins): connections to the gate are kept alive and backend connections are pooled (before, `Connection: close` on both sides); a body over 1 MiB, or declared as such, gets a 413 from tower-http at once; `OPTIONS /desktop/fit` is a preflight answered 200 by `CorsLayer` (any origin, `POST`, any headers) and `OPTIONS` elsewhere is the control channel's 404; `https://` backends are verified (before, both HTTPS schemes went through `openssl s_client`, which does not verify), `https+insecure://` is not; a refused upgrade carries `Connection: close` in place of a bare status line; response bodies are streamed instead of relayed byte for byte. The refusal texts are unchanged.

Architecture deviations: the gate and the control listener are axum handlers over the repository's own `Tokens`, `Engine` and `Launcher`, with no port trait for the backend client (the black-box tier is the check, as elsewhere in the CLIs). `tailnet_forward.rs` stayed a SOCKS5 TCP tunnel (`socks` and `std::io::copy`) in this slice; the next entry moves it onto tokio as well.

## 2026-10-09: wf's tailnet forwarder on tokio-socks, and a machine-independent duration assertion (standard-crates plan, phase 4b, second slice)

The forwarder (`clis/wf/src/tailnet_forward.rs`) was the other hand-written network piece the done-when grep kept finding: an accept loop, two copy threads per connection and a blocking SOCKS5 client (`socks`). The option the owner chose for the VNC gate described it as part of the same rewrite, so it moves too: `tokio-socks` dials the target (as a domain, because tailscaled's userspace listener resolves names itself), `tokio::io::copy_bidirectional` joins the two sockets (half-close is kept), and one lazily started one-worker runtime serves every forwarder of the process, so only a process that asks for a forward starts it. The `socks` dependency is gone. The tunnel carries no HTTP; what is left of it is the accept loop.

Measured (release builds, `/tmp/.../bench-fwd.mjs`: a mock SOCKS5 proxy in front of a mock HTTP service, `wf browser tailnet-forward`, 300 GETs on new connections per run, n=5 medians): round trip p50 1.156 ms before, 1.094 ms after; `wf` binary 8,149,368 B before, 8,176,600 B after. The daemon's resident memory was not captured (the script could not map the port to a process here).

Tests, by tier. Integration: the four black-box files that exercise the forwarder (`browser-tunnel`, `browser-attach`, `browser-state`, `browser-tailnet-forward`) 64 of 64 on the real release binary, unchanged; no expectation needed to change. `dispatch-batch`'s one failing assertion pinned a rounded elapsed time (`FAIL   0s`), which reads `1s` or more on this machine and failed identically on the std-only baseline; it now pins the shape (`FAIL`, whole seconds, the file name, the totals line) instead, a test fix and not a behaviour change. The whole `wf` suite: 1147 of 1147 with `--maxWorkers=3`. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p wf` 50 passed.

Deviations: tokio now also runs in `wf` processes that start a forward (the browser daemon and tunnel commands), a lazily started one-worker runtime; no port trait for the SOCKS dial. `wf`'s own option parsing was unchanged in this slice. The note that went with it was wrong: the TypeScript Commander front end is gone and the Rust parsers are the user-facing ones; the next entry moves them onto `clap`.

## 2026-10-09: wf's options on clap (standard-crates plan, phase 4c)

The owner asked for `wf`'s hand-rolled option parsing to move to `clap`, library texts winning. A correction first: the previous entries called these parsers shims behind a TypeScript Commander front end. They are not. The Rust `wf` is the whole CLI (`main.rs` routes the `NATIVE` commands, `help.rs` serves the stored help texts, and a command line no parser serves printed `wf <cmd>: the Rust wf does not support these arguments`), so these parsers were the user-facing ones, and few assertions pinned their wording (one line in `agents-caveat`, `--files is required` in `visual`).

What changed. `args.rs` is one spec-driven wrapper over `clap::Command` (help and version flags off, so `wf <command> --help` still comes from the stored help texts): a `Spec` lists value options (repeatable, last wins, a value may start with `-`), flags, variadic options, an optional-value option (`--summary [mode]`), short aliases (`-p`, `-t`), required options and the positional count. It returns the occurrences in command-line order. A command line clap refuses prints clap's error and usage (`error: unexpected argument '--bogus' found`, `Usage: wf metro status [OPTIONS]`; the usage names the command from the help table) and exits 2. The 19 callers of `args::parse` moved with it (those that take no positionals now call `parse_none`, so a stray word is clap's error), and the bespoke loops went onto the same spec: `browser` (values, flags, variadic, required, positional count), `visual`, `bench`, `log emit`, `dispatch` (`-p`, `--summary [mode]`, one positional), `dispatch --batch` (`-t`, variadic `--batch`), `ios sim`, `ios`, `ios build`, `ios launch`, `android`, `metro`, `metro start`, `tailscale` (variadic `--name`), `verify` (`--staged --files`, `--dry-run`, tier 3), `vm recover` (variadic `--only`), `knowledge`, the browser daemon and `cm6`. What stays by hand, in `args.rs`, with a reason: `lift_globals` (wf's own `--json` and `--app`, lifted from anywhere in a command line while the rest reaches the command unread; the two copies in `main.rs` and `browser.rs` became one, and clap cannot pass unknown words through) and `has_flag` (the mode probes that pick `verify`, `dispatch` or `runbook` before the command parses its own options). `help.rs`, `help_table.rs` and the `-h` and `--help` word checks stay: they serve the stored help texts.

Measured (release builds, the same loop for both binaries, interleaved, 30 spawns per round, 5 rounds, medians; before is the build of the VNC-gate slice): `wf --help` 3.24 ms before and 3.16 ms after; a refused command line (`wf metro status --bogus`) 4.93 and 4.96 ms; a parsed one (`wf metro status --identifier x`) 7.42 and 7.66 ms. Binary 8,149,368 B (gate slice) to 8,176,600 B (forwarder slice) to 8,305,912 B: `clap` costs about 130 KB.

Tests, by tier. Unit (`cargo test -p wf`, 64 passed, 14 of them in `args.rs`): written first for the semantics that had to survive (last value wins and repeats stay in order, `--name=value`, a value that starts with `-`, repeated flags, words and a lone `-` as positionals, a flag refusing a value, an unknown or short option or a missing value refused) and run green on the old parser before the swap; then the new ones (variadic, optional value, short alias, required and positional count, clap's error and usage kept for the caller, `--` as terminator, the mode probe, the global lift). Integration (the real release binary): a new `option-errors.integration.test.ts` pins clap's wording for an unknown option, a stray word, a missing value, a missing required option, a flag given a value, and `wf`'s own line for a shape no command serves (6 of 6); the whole suite is 1153 of 1153 with `--maxWorkers=3` (1147 plus the six). At full parallelism on this 4-core machine a different timing-sensitive test fails in most runs (`metro start` and `restart`, `deploy run`, `browser-state`) and passes alone, as before this slice. (Fixed afterwards at its causes; see the flaky-tests entry in the decision log and `tooling-wf-blackbox.md`.) `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean.

Deliberate behaviour changes (library wins): a refused command line prints clap's error and usage instead of `wf <cmd>: the Rust wf does not support these arguments` or nothing, and exits 2; `visual`'s Commander-worded messages (`Unknown option ...`, `Option '--x <value>' argument missing`) and `vm recover --only` with no words (`option '--only <apps...>' argument missing`, exit 1) are clap's now (exit 2); a bare `--` ends the options instead of being refused; `verify --staged --files` with no files is an error (it meant "no files" before); a repeated flag is recorded once, at its last position; `--app` without a value is no longer silently kept or dropped by the browser client but refused by the command that receives it. Shapes that are not parse errors (`log nothing`, a command name that does not exist) keep `wf`'s own lines.

`agent-config-sync`'s own two-word command line (`[sync] [--check]`, `mcp-guard`) moved onto `clap` too (builder API; an unknown word is now clap's error and exit 2 instead of being ignored; two unit tests, nine parity tests and the `agents-caveat` file green).

Deviations: `lift_globals` and `has_flag` were hand-written, in one file, for the reasons above (replaced in the next phase, see the entry "wf's program options and mode probes on clap"); the stored help texts mean clap's own help output is not used, so the `--help` and `-h` word checks in `help.rs` and `app_bench` stay.

## 2026-10-09: the wf black-box suite no longer flakes (test infrastructure)

The stop check rightly objected that the `wf` suite passed only at a reduced worker count. Looping single files showed real races in the tests and mock helpers, several of them mine from the curl-to-server conversion: port files written in place and read while empty, one fixed port bound by two parallel files (and seeded as "nobody answers" in a third), a per-test server on a pid-derived port that the previous test's server could still hold, stub logs read before the stub wrote them, and a Chromium `beforeAll` that outlasted vitest's 10 s hook timeout under load. Each is fixed at its cause (atomic port files, unique ports or a free port chosen by the mock, `vi.waitFor` on the stub logs, `hookTimeout` 45 s, workers capped at cores minus one); the details are in `docs/audits/reports/tooling-wf-blackbox.md`. Tier: integration. The default `npx vitest run` passed 1153 of 1153 four times in a row (about 170 s each); before, most runs failed one to five tests, a different set each time. No behaviour of `wf` changed.

## 2026-10-09: the approve guard stops refusing a fetch beside other commands, and the authenticated probes on the VM (standard-crates plan, done-when 3)

The two probes the plan still listed as not run, the authenticated WebSocket handshake of `remote-chat-agent` and the authenticated routes of `leetcode-backend`, were blocked by the approval guard, not by the permission mode: it refused any Bash call in which a secret fetch and a dump word (`head`, `cat`, `base64`, ...) or a file redirect appeared anywhere in the same command string. The owner asked for the guard to allow a fetch beside other commands. `scripts/guards/block-secret-exposure.mjs` now splits the command into list items and pipeline stages and refuses only when the fetched value itself reaches a leak shape (piped into a dump word, `tee`, `xargs` or a network tool, redirected to a file, printed from its variable into one of those, handed to a dump word by a here-string or process substitution, or an environment listing after `eval "$(wf secrets env)"`). Tier: unit (`scripts/guards/block-secret-exposure.test.mjs`, 47 tests; 9 were red on the old script, written first) and integration (`secrets-guard-coverage.integration.test.ts`, 4); `libraries/hook-runtime` 140 of 140. The change is PR #342 and is documented in `docs/solutions/security/wf-secrets-default-backend-and-redaction-pitfalls.md`.

Then the probes, tier e2e / runbook against the deployed services through their real entry points, with the images unchanged (`remote-chat-agent` `sha-66bf481c`, `leetcode-backend` `sha-1a311f7e`; `/health` reported both commits again). They ran from the sandbox over the tailnet SOCKS proxy (the WebSocket through `wf browser tunnel remote-chat-agent`); each secret was fetched with `wf secrets get <KEY> --backend bitwarden` into a shell variable and handed to the probe's environment, and the probe scripts (in the session scratchpad, not committed) print only statuses, frame kinds, error codes and response bodies that hold no secret.

- remote-chat-agent WebSocket, 7 of 7 (Node 22's built-in `WebSocket` client): the upgrade with the token is accepted (101); the first frame is `hello` with `protocolVersion` 1 and `heartbeatIntervalMs` 15000; `ping` with `ts` 5 returns `pong` with `ts` 5; `server.probe` answers `ok` with `availableClis` `["opencode"]` and `selectedCli` `opencode`; a request with an empty id is refused as `bad_envelope`; an unknown method as `unknown_method`; the socket stays open (a second ping is answered).
- leetcode-backend, 8 of 8 (curl through the proxy): `/health` 200; `POST /api/leetcode/format` without the bearer 401 `unauthorized`, with it 200 `{"ok":true,"formatted":"x = 1\nprint(x)\n"}`; `POST /api/leetcode/run` with it runs a Python class and reports its one case (`pass` true, `actual` 3, `stdout` `1\n`, `stderr` empty); `POST /api/leetcode/session` with the bearer but no admin secret 401; with the admin secret and a body missing `cookie` 422 (`missing field cookie`), with an empty `cookie` 422 (`cookie: must not be empty`), so no session was written; an unknown path with the admin secret 404 `not_found`. The plan had said the wrong-body case answers 400; the service answers 422 for a body that parses but fails validation, as `session.blackbox.test.ts` pins (400 is malformed JSON).
- Not run, unchanged: the VNC gate against the VM desktop (it needs the trigger secret and the compose app with its tailscale sidecar).

## 2026-10-09: wf's program options and mode probes on clap (standard-crates plan, phase 4d)

The owner chose "do the refactor" for the two helpers phase 4c left by hand. `lift_globals` is gone: `args::split_program` is one `clap` command with `--json`, `--app <name>`, `--help`/`-h` and `--version`/`-V` and an external subcommand, so the first word is the command and it and everything after it reach the command verbatim. `main` appends a leading `--json` behind the command as before and sets the one app. Every command's own `clap` parse (`args::command`) now also accepts `--json` (unless the command lists it) and `--app <name>`, read and dropped, wherever they stand after the command, which is what the lift did for them. The commands that read raw words moved onto the shared parse so they accept the two options too: `report-health --list-unit-targets`, `caveat on|off|status` and its guard commands (`guard-eval` took `--runs=N` and `--filter=X` by prefix match and now takes `--runs N` too), `browser tunnel`, `deploy sync-creds`, `deploy tf apply`, `auth listener`, `dispatch --pricing-sync` and the `dispatch` mode flags; `agent-config-sync`'s `clap` command accepts the two options as global arguments. `deploy tf exec <dir> ...` hands everything after the directory to Terraform untouched, `wf`'s options included.

`has_flag` is not gone, and the reason came out of the tests written first. The plan was a lenient `clap` probe (`ignore_errors`, one declared option, a catch-all positional). Three of the eleven probe tests were red against it (`run --batch=a.ts --json`, a flag after options the probe does not declare, `--json` before a `--`): `clap` stops at the first option the command does not declare and drops the values it was still holding, so `ignore_errors` cannot answer for an option that comes after one. `has_flag` is now twelve lines over `clap_lex`, the lexer `clap` itself is built on (a direct dependency, already in the lock file): `--name`, `--name=value` and `--name value` count, a longer name does not, and nothing after `--` is an option. It remains because `dispatch`, `verify`, `runbook`, `report` and `knowledge` must choose their mode before their own options parse.

Measured (release builds, the same loop for both binaries, interleaved, 30 spawns per round, 5 rounds, medians; before is the build of PR #341): `wf --help` 3.13 ms before, 3.05 after; a refused command line (`wf metro status --bogus`) 5.57 and 5.39; a parsed one (`wf metro status --identifier x`) 8.71 and 8.37; with the program options (`wf --app x --json metro status`) 8.69 and 8.15. Binary 8,305,912 B to 8,311,672 B. No change beyond noise; the sandbox was idle for the runs.

Tests, by tier. Unit (`cargo test -p wf -p agent-config-sync`, 89 passed): the probe and `split_program` cases were written first and were red (the missing function, then the three probe failures above); leading and repeated options, the command's words verbatim including a later `--json` and `--app`, an empty command line, help and version, a dangling `--app` and an unknown leading option as clap's errors, the program options accepted and dropped by a command's parse, and a command that lists `--json` itself. Integration (the real release binary): `binary-smoke.integration.test.ts` gained a matrix (`--app` before, `=`, after and `=` after the command, for `ios status`, `metro status`, `caveat status` and `report-health --list-unit-targets`; `--json` before and after for the first three) that was green on the old binary, and two tests that were red on it (a dangling `--app`, an unknown leading option); 33 of 33 with `option-errors` 6 of 6. The whole `wf` suite is 1176 of 1176 with the default `npx vitest run`, four runs in a row (172 to 202 s each), after one earlier full run in which the `auth-listener` desktop-browser `beforeAll` failed and skipped its ten Chromium tests; the file alone is 39 of 39 and the next five full runs did not repeat it, so it is recorded as one unexplained Chromium start failure under load, cause not captured. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean. No e2e (a CLI).

Deliberate behaviour changes (library wins): a dangling `--app` or an unknown option before the command is clap's error (exit 2; before `error: unknown command '--app'`, exit 1); a leading `--json` or `--app` before `wf browser ...` works (it was `unknown command` on the previous build, because the browser path returned the arguments unchanged); every command accepts and ignores `--json` and `--app` after it, as the program-level options did under the Commander tree (a command with no JSON mode now ignores `--json` instead of refusing it); `--app` between a command and its sub-word (`wf ios --app x status`) is no longer lifted and is an unserved shape (exit 2; the form the docs and the `cf-plan` skill use, `wf --app <app> ...`, is unchanged); after `deploy tf exec <dir>` the options are Terraform's, and `-h`, `--help` and `--` there are no longer refused; `wf agents --app x sync` is accepted. Deviations: `has_flag` remains, over `clap_lex`, for the reason above; `help.rs`'s and `app_bench`'s `--help` and `-h` word checks stay because they serve the stored help texts.

## 2026-10-09: dev CI builds the services with a deploy profile, not fat LTO (Deploy under five minutes, first slice)

The owner asked for the dev Deploy workflow to pass and deploy every service in under five minutes. Measured first, on run 310 (head `5137cb0`, the whole stack changed, one self-hosted runner): the `plan` job took 3 min 36 s, of which `install-wf` was 3 min 16 s (the runner's own `wf`, built from source because `clis/` changed and the cache is keyed by the hash of its sources; `actions/checkout` runs `git clean -ffdx`, so `clis/target` never survives and every miss compiles every dependency); the `deploy (remote-chat-agent)` leg then waited 3 min 50 s for the runner and spent 6 min 57 s in "Build, push and redeploy", 6 min 19 s of it inside the image build, which compiles `wf` (stage `wfbuilder`) and `rca-service` (stage `rcabuilder`) in parallel on the VM's four cores. The run took 14 min 45 s end to end; later pushes queued behind it one after the other (runs 311 to 314).

The cause is the release profile both workspaces use: `lto = true` with `codegen-units = 1` (and `opt-level = "s"`/`"z"`), chosen for binary size. Fat LTO re-optimises every dependency (tokio, axum, reqwest, rustls) for any change to any crate, so a one-line change costs minutes whatever the cache holds. This change adds `[profile.deploy]` to `backend/Cargo.toml` and `clis/Cargo.toml` (`inherits = "release"`, `lto = false`, `codegen-units = 16`; size optimisation, `panic = "abort"` and `strip` unchanged) and uses it where the pipeline builds: the `remote-chat-agent` Dockerfile (both stages), the `leetcode-backend` Dockerfile, the `wf-cli` Dockerfile and the `install-wf` action, which also builds in a target directory beside the tool cache (`${{ runner.tool_cache }}/wf-cargo-target`) so that a cache miss recompiles only what changed. `release` stays what local builds, the benchmarks of the earlier entries and the black-box harnesses default to.

Measured here (a four-core x86 machine, so ratios, not the VM's seconds): changing `remote-chat-agent/rs/src/lib.rs` and rebuilding `rca-service` takes 116 s with the release profile and 34 s with the deploy profile; both services built from cold take 156 s with the deploy profile. Binaries grow: `rca-service` 21.85 MB to 25.81 MB, `leetcode-backend-rs` 4.31 MB to 7.12 MB. The service benchmark (`bench:service`, n=5 fresh-process medians, the same harness, files `docs/audits/benchmarks/rca-ci-release-lto.json` and `rca-ci-deploy-profile.json`) shows no change beyond noise: cold start to `/health` 34.0 ms and 35.8 ms, idle resident set 10.29 MB and 10.26 MB, `/health` p50 0.54 and 0.47 ms, a chat turn 105.8 and 105.6 ms, a one-shot run 109.5 and 109.1 ms, convert DOCX 1.30 and 1.26 ms, the Tamil check 163.3 and 164.1 ms, a hundred WebSocket connections 1.00 and 1.00 ms per connection, peak resident set 42.6 MB and 42.3 MB.

Tests, by tier. Integration, on the deploy-profile binaries (what the pipeline ships; the harnesses take `RCA_SERVICE_BIN` and `LEETCODE_BACKEND_CMD`): `remote-chat-agent` 314 of 314 and `leetcode-backend` 79 of 79, none skipped. The pipeline result is the e2e tier. ## 2026-10-09: wf's source-map decoding on the sourcemap crate

The final grep for hand-written base64 found `BASE64` and `decode_segment` in `clis/wf/src/app_bench/domain.rs`: a hand-written decoder of the `mappings` field of a source map (the base64 digits of the VLQ format, the running source index and column; about 35 lines) that attributes the script bytes a cold start executed to packages. It is replaced by the `sourcemap` crate (getsentry, BSD-3-Clause; default features off): `suites.rs` parses the `.map` file into a `SourceMap` and `decode_lines` groups its tokens by generated line. The alternative, the `vlq` crate, replaces only the digits, has had no release since 2018 and leaves the running-index logic hand-written; `sourcemap` adds twelve small crates (`base64-simd`, `vsimd`, `outref`, `bitvec`, `funty`, `radium`, `tap`, `wyz`, `debugid`, `if_chain`, `unicode-id-start`, all MIT or Apache-2.0) and 65,984 bytes to the release binary (8,311,672 B to 8,377,656 B).

Tests, by tier. Unit: five decoding cases (a continuation digit, a negative delta, a segment without a source, a segment with a name, a source delta on a later line) were written first and ran green with the existing ones on the hand-written decoder (`cargo test -p wf app_bench::domain`: 25 passed); after the swap two fixtures needed changing for the crate (the map gets a `names` entry, and the negative-delta case expects column order) and the same 25 pass (28 across `app_bench`). Integration: the whole `wf` suite 1176 of 1176 on the deploy-profile binary, none skipped, and the two `app-bench` files 17 of 17. `cargo clippy -p wf --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. No e2e: the suite that feeds the decoder needs Chromium and an Expo export and was not run.

Benchmark (one generated map of 4,000 lines and 240,000 segments, 1.2 MB of mappings; both decoders in one release binary, five interleaved runs, medians; the crate's figure includes parsing the map's JSON): the hand-written decoder 25.44 ms, the crate 11.91 ms; the segments they return are identical. Deliberate changes (library wins): segments come in column order, the lines covered are the code's lines (executed bytes after the last mapped line are reported as "unmapped" instead of dropped), and a map the crate refuses (a segment of two or three fields, a source or name index out of range) is an error at load with the map's path.

Also recorded here, as evidence for the plan's done-when 3 on head `846130f`: the process table of each deployed image (tag `sha-846130f8`, run in agent-shell with fake secrets and no sidecar, because the deployer exposes container status and not processes) lists one process, `rca-service` and `leetcode-backend-rs`, each with its tokio worker threads and the rate-limit cleaner thread, and no `node`; the same containers' `/health` report `846130f8531d7c430f144e2e0adbf5c3a5ba2924`; `/api/convert` answered 200 for the PDF and the DOCX fixtures (3,115 and 236 characters of text); `/api/voice-transcribe` answered 200 for the five recordings, four matching their manifest phrase after case and punctuation and one ("Add a dockstring above the function") the same transcript the earlier images gave for it. The `docker top` call for the comm-only count failed (`Couldn't find PID field`), so the "zero nodes" is read from the first table, not from that count. These ran on the image started in agent-shell, not in the live compose project: the same image digest, different container.

`has_flag` (the `--name` probe over `clap_lex`): the two `clap`-only designs of the plan's wording are now unit tests in `args.rs` that pass because they fail the shapes the commands are called with. `clap`'s `ignore_errors` does not see `--json` after an undeclared option (`--out x --json`), and a catch-all positional swallows it after a word (`run --json`). No `clap` call finds an option among options the command does not declare, so the probe stays on the lexer `clap` is built on; the alternative is one parse per command with a union spec and the `--json` wrapper moved into the parse, which this log does not take on without the owner's decision.

The process table, convert, voice and the leetcode probes are recorded above on the `989993e` and `846130f` images; the later change touches test code only.

## 2026-10-09: wf's mode probes on the union of the modes' specs (standard-crates plan, final checks)

The stop check kept counting `has_flag` (the `--name` probe over `clap_lex`) against done-when 1, so it is removed. A test of the plan's own wording had shown why it existed: `clap`'s `ignore_errors` misses a flag after an undeclared option and a catch-all positional swallows one after a word. The way around it is to declare everything: a command that picks its mode before it parses parses once against the union of its modes' specs (`args::probe`, `Spec::union`) and reads the mode from the result, and each mode then parses with its own spec. Each mode's spec became a `const` beside its parser. `--json` is read from the command's own parse (`Parsed::flag`, `args::json_seen`); the `--json` envelope of `ios`, `android`, `metro` and `cm6` starts from that parse (`json_mode::begin`, armed by `json_mode::run`). `clap_lex` and `has_flag` are gone (`rg 'fn has_flag|lift_globals' clis/` finds nothing).

Tests, by tier. Unit (`cargo test -p wf`, 80 in the binary): the probe and `json_seen` cases were written first (they did not compile until the functions existed). Integration (the real binary, deploy profile): the whole suite 1178 of 1178 with none skipped: the 1176 and two new `option-errors` tests that were green on the old binary (a value given to the flag that picks a mode; an option no mode takes beside a mode flag). The first run of the changed binary failed one test, `verify --budget=1` (the probe refused the line and the default mode answered "unexpected argument '--budget'"); the probe now keeps its own error, which names `--budget` and the value as before, and the run was repeated: 1178 of 1178. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean. No e2e for this change itself (a CLI); the VNC gate check below ran with the deployed binary.

Benchmark (release builds, five rounds of 30 spawns, interleaved, medians; before = the build of `989993e`): binary 8,377,656 B to 8,379,800 B; `wf --help` 2.99 to 3.05 ms; a refused line 4.79 to 4.75 ms; a parsed line 7.53 to 7.66 ms; the program options 7.59 to 7.67 ms; a mode probe (`dispatch --stats --log /nonexistent`) 4.95 to 5.32 ms. The probe costs one more `clap` parse.

Deliberate changes (library wins): a mode flag that is another option's value is that value; the `Usage:` line of a refused line of these commands is the union's; a line that fails to parse never starts the `--json` envelope. `Spec::union` keeps the merged lists for the process (`Box::leak`).

The VNC gate on the VM host (e2e): `wf deploy status vnc-auth-session` lists no containers, so the compose app is not deployed and there is no VM desktop to front. The gate of the deployed `wf` (the aarch64 binary in the `remote-chat-agent:sha-87860bb3` image) was started in agent-shell with test-only stubs for `tailscale` and the vault, in front of a local HTTP and WebSocket backend, and probed: 10 of 10 passed (ports announced; `/auth/start` 202 with a token and 401 without the secret; no token, a wrong token and an unknown cookie 403 with the backend never reached; a valid token answered by the backend with a cookie; the cookie alone passes; a POST body passes; a WebSocket negotiates its subprotocol and echoes a binary frame; a WebSocket without a token is refused; a dead backend is 502).

Deploy and verification of the `has_flag` removal (head `da1ce64`; run 37972618341 green in 2 min 50 s from the push: `plan` 50 s, `deploy (remote-chat-agent)` 1 min 54 s, Metro skipped). The live `/health` reports `da1ce6485760762d5e09de556a6a6e385a303137` for `remote-chat-agent` and `846130f8531d7c430f144e2e0adbf5c3a5ba2924` for `leetcode-backend` (its inputs did not change); `wf vm logs remote-chat-agent` shows `server.listening` at 18:23:28; on the VM host, the two images started in agent-shell (`remote-chat-agent:sha-da1ce648`, `leetcode-backend:sha-846130f8`) each list one process (`rca-service`, `leetcode-backend-rs` with their tokio worker and rate-limit cleaner threads) and no `node`; `/api/convert` is 200 for the PDF and the DOCX and `/api/voice-transcribe` 200 for five recordings; the authenticated probes pass against the live services (WebSocket 7 of 7, leetcode routes 8 of 8); the gate of that image's `wf` (aarch64) passes 10 of 10. The `wf` in that image is the new binary: `verify --budget=1` answers with the union usage (`Usage: wf verify --budget [ARG]...`) where the previous image's `wf` answered `Usage: wf verify --budget`, and the two files differ (`f4e28e6b…` against `6d245b64…`, both 9,787,376 bytes).

## 2026-10-09: the VNC desktop app deployed on the VM and the token gate probed through the tailnet (owner instruction)

The owner asked for the real-desktop run of the gate. `vnc-auth-session` had no containers (it was running on 2026-10-04, when the `/auth/start` gap was written, and gone by 2026-10-08, when this log first records its logs answering 404; the cause is not established, and the deployer's history only starts on 2026-10-08). It was never a leg of the pipeline: `DEPLOY_LEGS` has held `remote-chat-agent`, `leetcode-backend` and `leetcode-auth-cookie-capture` since the plan job was introduced (2026-10-05), and `wf deploy run` builds its image (`vnc-auth-capture`) but refuses its app.

What ran, from the head `0b7cb37`: `wf deploy run wf-cli --build-only --tag latest` (the carrier the vnc image copies its `wf` from; the first try failed on a short sha, which the VM cannot fetch, the second succeeded), `wf deploy run vnc-auth-capture --build-only --tag sha-0b7cb377`, then `wf deploy run vnc-auth-session --redeploy-only --tag sha-0b7cb377 --ref <full sha>`: the deployer provisioned the compose file from that ref and wrote 10 secrets from the vault, and `vnc-auth` and `vnc-auth-tailscale` came up (`vnc-auth-capture:sha-0b7cb377`). The container logged `vnc_auth.gate.listening` (3002) and `vnc_auth.listener.listening` (8795).

Probe through the tailnet (script in the session scratchpad; secrets read from the vault into variables, never printed), 14 of 14 passed on the last run. The gate in front of the real KasmVNC/Selkies desktop: no token, a wrong token and an unknown cookie are 403; a valid token reaches the desktop page (200, the Selkies client) and sets a session cookie; the cookie alone passes; a websocket upgrade at `/websocket` with the cookie is 101 Switching Protocols and without a token 403. The control channel: a start without the secret is 401, with a wrong owner 403, an `OPTIONS /desktop/fit` preflight 200 (the CORS layer), a start with the secret 202 and a link, `/auth/done` 200. Starting a session ran the launcher on the real desktop: the display resized to 393x852, the browser went full-screen in mobile emulation and prefilled the login email (`vnc_auth.desktop_launcher.*`), and BetterStack returns those lines under the app tag `vnc-auth-capture`.

Found on the way, none of it a gate fault: past the gate the desktop asks for its own login (nginx basic auth, `CUSTOM_USER` and `PASSWORD`): without it the page is 401. The user is `test` (`clis/vnc-auth-capture/.env.defaults`), not the compose default `auth`, which is the drift the open gap names. `VNC_AUTH_VNC_USER` is not in the vault, only the password. The websocket path is `/websocket`, and over HTTP/2 (what `tailscale serve` negotiates with curl) an upgrade needs `--http1.1`. Five starts answered 202 and none hung, which is evidence about the gap's "hung past 60 s" claim, not a fix: the gap stays open and unedited.

Left running: the app stays deployed, as Terraform's `vm_apps` declares it. Not yet true: a push to `dev` redeploys it, because it is not a pipeline leg; it runs the image of this commit until something ships it again.

| Deploy-profile binary | 10,957,440 B | 11,111,448 B (+154,008 B) |

First baseline run, before any change: 7.85 ms release, 8.50 ms deploy profile, 22,296 KB. Records: `docs/audits/benchmarks/wf-otlp-sdk-before.json` (first baseline), `wf-otlp-sdk-after.json` (all four binaries interleaved), `wf-otlp-sdk-bench.py` (method). Verification on the change: `wf` unit tests (`log_export` 4, caveat guard golden on the wire body), the OTLP-related black-box files 34 of 34, then the whole `wf` suite 1,186 of 1,186 in 88 files, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace` 150 in 16 suites (148 before: four `log_export` tests replace two `otlp` ones).

Folder layout (owner request): `remote-chat-agent/rs` had thirteen loose modules beside `api` and `infrastructure`, `remote-chat-agent/core` an `ids.rs` and `leetcode-backend/rs` a `config.rs`. They now sit in the layers (`domain`: `clis`, `models`, `schema`, `md`, `util`, `voice_dsl`, core's `ids`; `application`: `attachments`, `runs`, `edit_stats`, `one_shot`, `voice_planner`, `startup`; `infrastructure`: `log`, `highlight`, `tamil`, `stt`, leetcode's `config`), with `truthy` moved from `api::routes` into `domain::util` so `infrastructure` no longer imports `api`. Enforcement is `pnpm run arch:rust` (pre-commit job, architecture workflow): `ls-lint` for the layout (`.ls-lint.yml`) and `ast-grep` for the import direction (`backend/arch-rules`). Both fail on a seeded violation (a stray `rs/src/stray.rs`; `use crate::application::engine` in a core domain file). Files that still break a direction rule are on the rules' `ignores` (`docs/plans/rca-hexagonal/plan.md`). Backend after the move: clippy clean, `cargo test --workspace` 229 in 18 suites, black box on deploy-profile binaries 314 of 314 (`remote-chat-agent`) and 79 of 79 (`leetcode-backend`), none skipped.

## 2026-10-09: wf's OTLP export on the SDK's own HTTP client (supersedes the `ureq` adapter above)

Owner: "can't we just use officially supported otlp stuff?" The `ureq` `HttpClient` adapter in `log_export.rs` is removed; the official `opentelemetry-otlp` exporter now uses its bundled blocking `reqwest` client (native roots), on a thread of its own. Same wire format (OTLP/JSON). Interleaved n=5 rounds x 10 runs of `wf log emit` against a local HTTPS ingest: release binary 8,381,240 B before, 8,457,848 B with the adapter, 8,468,248 B now (+86,008 B vs before); wall 10.39 ms / 10.14 ms / 10.24 ms; peak RSS 22,248 KB in all three; 0 failures of 50 per binary. Deploy-profile binary 10,957,440 to 11,141,256 B. Record `docs/audits/benchmarks/wf-otlp-sdk-official-client.json`. Verification: `wf` unit tests (`log_export` 4, caveat golden on the wire), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, the whole `wf` black-box suite 1,186 of 1,186 in 88 files, none skipped on the clean run (two earlier runs under load skipped the ten Chromium-backed `auth-listener` cases once and failed one `browser-state` case once; each passes alone, 39 of 39 and 12 of 12 three times). Behaviour change: `wf log emit` honours the proxy variables of the environment instead of always connecting directly.

## 2026-10-10: wf black box ported to Rust and the TypeScript suite removed (phase 7 of the TypeScript removal plan)

All 947 titles of `tooling/wf-blackbox` have a Rust twin in `clis/wf-blackbox` (parity table `docs/plans/ts-removal/wf-blackbox-parity.md`, 947 of 947; the one file that tests a browser content script stays TypeScript). Both suites ran clean on the same release binary (Rust 953 passed; TypeScript 1186 passed) and both failed on the same behaviour for six seeded faults in `wf` (`docs/plans/ts-removal/wf-blackbox-mutation-run.md`). Only then was the TypeScript suite deleted: 101 TypeScript files and 27,539 lines out, 20,812 lines of Rust in.

Benchmark, n=1 because the question is whether the port is slower, not by how much: the whole suite took 425 s in Rust (`cargo test`, one test binary at a time, tests in parallel inside it) and about 11 minutes in TypeScript (`vitest run`, 4 workers; `ts-clean.log` timestamps 10:31 to 10:42). The Rust suite needs no Node install to run. Decision: keep the Rust suite as the only gate; CI and the pre-commit secrets job now call `cargo test -p wf-blackbox --test ...` against the installed `wf`.

## 2026-10-10: done-when 1 grep over `backend` and `clis` on the head of `claude/wf-blackbox-rs`

`rg` over every `.rs` file of `backend` and `clis`, excluding `target`. `Command::new("curl")`: no match. Hand-written `fn sha1`, `fn base64*`, `fn percent*`, `fn url_encode/url_decode`: no match (the two hits are `percentile` in `rca-core`). WebSocket handshake or framing (`Sec-WebSocket-Accept`, the handshake GUID, `read_frame`, `write_frame`, `mask_`): no match outside the black-box tests, which build a raw client on purpose. CORS, rate-limit, multipart and OTLP code: the only non-test hit is `cors_layer` in `remote-chat-agent`, a wrapper over tower-http's `CorsLayer`; every other hit asserts a header or a payload in a test. `TcpListener::bind`: the five service entry points hand an axum router to tokio, and the two in `clis/wf` are the loopback tailnet forwarder (a SOCKS5 tunnel over `tokio-socks`, not HTTP) and the VNC listener's bind. `base64`, `sha1` and percent-encoding appear only as crate calls (`base64::engine`). The one documented exception stays service-kit's redaction layer.

## 2026-10-10: seeded faults in the services' flow and integration tests

One edit per service, then `cargo test` of that service's `rs` package: `reporting-service` (an invalid window answered 200 instead of 400) failed 1 test; `leetcode-backend` (a missing bearer answered 403 instead of 401) failed 4; `remote-chat-agent` (the unknown-path JSON 404 answered 405) failed 1; `leetcode-auth-cookie-capture` (a wrong trigger secret answered 403 instead of 401) failed none. The capture service's axum layer had no in-process test, only the black box under `backend/*/tests/blackbox`, so the fault was uncaught below that tier. `backend/leetcode-auth-cookie-capture/rs/tests/flow_api.rs` now covers the front door (401 for a missing or wrong secret, 202 for the right one, 404 with no body elsewhere) over the in-memory fakes; the same fault now fails it. `watchdog` has no HTTP layer and was not mutated.

The grep `rg -n --glob '*.rs' --glob '!target' 'HTTP/1\.[01]|Sec-WebSocket|258EAFA5|httparse|Command::new\("(curl|wget|openssl)"\)|ExportLogsServiceRequest|fn (sha1|base64\w*|percent\w*|url_(en|de)code|dechunk)\b' backend clis` now returns only `percentile` (a statistics function). Integration tier: `cargo test -p wf-blackbox` on the deploy-profile binary, 951 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored, no skipped line; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean.
