## 2026-10-10: the `wf` surface, four verbs and four nouns (plan B of the owner's naming request)

`wf` now takes `check`, `ship`, `ask`, `plan` (verbs) and `app`, `vm`, `secret`, `repo` (nouns). `surface::resolve` turns each into the argv of the command that does the work; the 24 old top-level names still run unchanged and print one stderr line naming their replacement (silenced with `WF_ALIAS_NOTICE=0`, which the black-box harness sets). The global `--app` option is also spelled `--frontend`. Deliberate behaviour changes: the new spellings, the notice, `wf --help` listing eight commands. Cost: n=5 medians of 100 runs, `wf --version` 3.69 ms, `wf verify --help` (old name, through the resolver) 3.73 ms, `wf check --help` 3.59 ms; the binary is 11,153,064 bytes. Checks: `surface` unit tests 11 passed; `cli_surface` black box 9 passed; the full `wf-blackbox` run 958 passed with two load-sensitive tests (`cli_work_run` SIGTERM, `cli_browser_attach` scroll) that pass alone, three times, and on a rerun of their files; clippy `-D warnings` clean. One security finding fixed in the same change: the secret-exposure guard (`scripts/guards/block-secret-exposure.mjs`) and the approve deny list (`libraries/hook-runtime/src/deny.json`) matched only the plural noun, so the singular spelling of the value-revealing subcommands would have bypassed them; both now match an optional trailing `s`, with three new guard tests, and the coverage test checks both spellings. The aliases are removed in a later small PR once `rg "wf (verify|deploy|work|dispatch|secrets|auth|agents)\b"` finds nothing outside the alias table.

## 2026-10-10: the `wf` surface without aliases (round two of the naming request; supersedes the entry above)

Old top-level names are unknown commands (exit 1, `unknown command '<name>'`); `routes.rs` holds one command table and nothing translates. Verbs `check ship ask plan`, devices `android browser`, nouns `vm secret repo`. The global `--app` option is `--frontend`. Metro is a deployed service (`wf ship mla-tamagui [--clear-cache]`, first matrix leg of Deploy; no separate `metro` job or plan field), `wf ios` and the local per-slot Metro are removed, `wf ship eas` publishes the EAS Update, `wf android` is a thin layer over adb.

- Integration tier, head `698b0d1d4` binary (deploy profile): `cargo test -p wf-blackbox --no-fail-fast` 803 passed, 0 failed; `cargo clippy -p wf -p wf-blackbox --all-targets -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p wf` 88 + 1 + 3 passed; lefthook `verify` green on both commits.
- Runbook tier, deployed services, head `666924c3f`: Deploy runs 362 and 363 green (plan, `mla-tamagui`, `remote-chat-agent`; 363 was docs only). `wf ship --status`: leetcode-backend `sha-666924c3`, leetcode-capture `sha-666924c`, vnc-auth `sha-666924c`, reporting-service `sha-666924c`, remote-chat-agent `sha-d5265d81` (package unchanged by the later docs commits), all healthy; `mla-tamagui` alive at `666924c3f`. `wf ship mla-tamagui --clear-cache` took 1 m 58 s and ended with `mla-tamagui on dev: commit 666924c3f exp://metro-runner-main:20000`.
- `wf check --deployed 666924c3f` from the sandbox fails tier 3 only because Chromium there does not use the tailnet proxy (`ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED metro-runner-main`); `verify-deployed.yml` has not run on `dev` since it was renamed, so tier 3 is not covered by CI. It also wrote a fix unit under `docs/plans/fix-666924c3/`, deleted unmerged.
- Cold start, n=5 after 3 warm-ups, deploy-profile binary: `wf --help` median 3.64 ms (3.31 to 4.00), `wf ship eas --help` 3.59 ms, `wf vm net --help` 3.45 ms; baseline 3.6 ms, unchanged. Binary 10,958,056 bytes.
- Rollout order: `agent-shell` and `vm-ops-api` are hand-deployed and still run `sha-4e0614d`, whose `wf` only knows the old names. vm-ops-api's redeploy runs `wf secret sync-env` inside agent-shell, so deploy agent-shell first, then vm-ops-api; the reverse order breaks every redeploy.

## 2026-10-10: Android on the VM (redroid)

Browser baseline on the VM (Playwright headless shell, the shared Metro web page, 15 s virtual-time budget, 3 runs): 169 s on first load while Metro compiled, then 5.6 s and 4.8 s wall, 628 and 636 MB peak RSS. User-mode QEMU TCG of a CPU loop on the same host: 0.41 s native, 4.0 s emulated, about 10 times slower. `/dev/kvm` is absent. `binder_linux` from `linux-modules-extra-6.17.0-1020-oracle` loads with `insmod` (approved by the owner) and Android's init starts, but redroid cannot boot in the `agent-dind` daemon: Android's `libprocessgroup` writes to `cgroup.procs` fail with ENOTSUP because every cgroup there is under a threaded cgroup root (`/sys/fs/cgroup` reads `domain threaded`, `--cgroup-parent` does not help). No redroid boot time or memory was measured, so the 5 times rule is not decided by numbers. Removing `binder_linux` fails with "Device or resource busy" (the module stays loaded until the VM reboots; nothing uses it).

## 2026-10-11: checks left open by the surface round, and decisions

- Tier 3 on `d9d695abc` (`wf check --deployed d9d695abc --no-publish-eas`, run from the sandbox): tier 1 pass; app-flows `/`, `/design-system/storybook` and `/settings` pass; `/leetcode/two-sum` fails (`"twoSum" never rendered`) in three runs. The tier-3 browser could not resolve tailnet names from the sandbox, so `VisualBrowser` now takes `WF_SOCKS_PORT` (the `wf browser` rule: `--proxy-server=socks5://localhost:<port>`; unit tests in `visual.rs`). Cause of the remaining failure: the check expects the text `twoSum` in the page, but in the plain 1280-wide viewport the page body reads `Two Sum, Array & Hashing, Easy, Plan, Code, Python, ...` (screenshot sent) and `twoSum` appears only in the Code editor, which `wf browser open --tailnet --device iphone-15-pro` renders (`class Solution: def twoSum`, 10 s after navigation). The expectation dates from the native `wf verify` port (`413c084b3`); this round touched neither the leetcode UI (last change 2026-10-06) nor the check. Left as it is: changing what tier 3 expects is the owner's call; the likely fix is expecting `Two Sum`. `verify-deployed.yml` has not run on `dev`, so CI does not catch it.
- BetterStack: no row from the Metro server in the last 7 days (`wf vm logs --since 7d --grep metro|dev-bridge|mla-tamagui`: 0 rows each), before or after the restart; the services' rows are present (reporting-service, vm-ops-api, agent-shell, the four heartbeats). The Metro runner does not ship its log; the restart itself is shown by `wf ship mla-tamagui --clear-cache` (dev-bridge lines: `cache restored`, `installed`, `postinstall done`, then `mla-tamagui on dev: commit 666924c3f exp://metro-runner-main:20000`), `packager-status:running` on `/status` through the tailnet name, and the Expo manifest (`runtimeVersion exposdk:57.0.0`, launch asset on `metro-runner-main:20000`) that Expo Go fetches. A Metro log in BetterStack is a gap, not a regression.
- Deploy order, checked in agent-shell (`wf vm exec`): its `wf` is the 2026-10-08 build, `wf secret` is `unknown command 'secret'` and `wf secrets` works. vm-ops-api `sha-4e0614d` calls `wf secrets sync-env`; the repository's `redeploy.ts` now calls `wf secret sync-env`. So the old vm-ops-api with a new agent-shell, and the new vm-ops-api with the old agent-shell, each fail every redeploy until the other is deployed. Deploy agent-shell first, then vm-ops-api straight after, and expect redeploys to fail between the two.
- Names: `repo knowledge for` is `rank` and `repo knowledge which` is `locate` (verbs; `for` and `which` are gone). `ask --stats` and `--savings` stay: they read the report of the asks, as `check --health` reads the repository. 90 unit tests, the knowledge, help and surface black-box suites and clippy pass on the renamed commands.
- Android on the VM, why it is unmeasured: the Docker that `wf vm exec` reaches is the nested `agent-dind` daemon (its pid 1 is Debian, `/sys/fs/cgroup` reads `domain threaded`), not the Ubuntu host's. A privileged container there starts Android's init but every service dies on `cgroup.procs` writes. Recreating the VM does not change that. Measuring redroid needs it on the host's Docker, as its own compose project like `metro-runner`, with `binder_linux` loaded at boot.

Tier 3 after the expectation change: the check now expects `Two Sum` on `/leetcode/two-sum` (`twoSum` never appears in the plain viewport's text, 90 s of polling, 268 characters; the black-box fixture page changed with it, `cli_verify_tier3` 7 of 7). `wf check --deployed fab156b74 --no-publish-eas` from the sandbox: tier 1 skip (no changed apps), tier 3 pass, all four app-flows routes, report `verify/fab156b744f1b46096e9708c0f7d251f015269a1.json`, exit 0.

## 2026-10-11: redroid on the VM, measured (supersedes the "no numbers" paragraphs above)

Question: can the A1 VM run Android for UI checks at under 5 times the browser's time and memory. Answer: yes, as its own compose project on the host's Docker. Artifact: https://claude.ai/artifact/EVRtwtKL71EZyS1epsWFBR.

- Why it did not boot before: `wf vm exec` reaches the nested `agent-dind` daemon, whose cgroup root is `domain threaded`; Android's init gets ENOTSUP writing `cgroup.procs` and shuts down (exit 129). On the host's Docker (`reporting-service` slot, throwaway branch `spike/redroid-as-reporting-service`, redeployed with `wf ship --redeploy-only reporting-service --ref <sha> --tag latest`; the branch name as `--ref` served a cached compose file for about 5 minutes, so the commit SHA is the ref) `redroid/redroid:13.0.0_64only-latest` boots, with a `binder` container loading `binder_linux.ko` from `linux-modules-extra-6.17.0-1020-oracle`.
- Same page in both: the app's public web export. Browser (Playwright headless shell, 720x1280, 5 runs, redroid idle beside it, host load 1.16): 1.5 to 1.7 s, process RSS sum 593 to 604 MB, PSS sum 298 to 309 MB, PNG 100,525 B. Redroid (WebView shell by VIEW intent, software rendering, 3 runs, load 2 to 3.8 at start): 3.0 to 3.5 s until three equal screenshots larger than the blank page (115 KB against 43 KB), which itself costs about 3 s, so an upper bound; Android's used RAM 1,480 MB after load (905 MB PSS + 575 MB kernel), 1,240 MB idle; ps RSS sum 5.2 GB (double counts zygote's shared pages, not comparable); 171 to 191 % of 200 % CPU idle; screencap 0.68 to 0.81 s; boot 29 s on a fresh `/data` (volumes `redroid-data-c2`, `-c3`), 17 to 23 s on a used one.
- Ratios against the rule: time 1.9 to 2.2 (upper bound); memory 3.0 on process PSS, 4.9 counting Android's kernel share, 8.7 on summed RSS (not comparable). Under 5 on the comparable measures, the all-in one by 2 %. Verdict: go for a spike.
- Not measured: the shared Metro link and Expo Go. The new tailnet node cannot reach `metro-runner-main:20000` (`curl` connect fails, internet is 200), so the first two probes showed a blank page. The tailnet access rules need a line for the emulator node.
- State left on the VM: `reporting-service` is back on `sha-666924c` (healthy); two exited containers `reporting-service-spike-probe` and `-spike-binder`, volumes `redroid-data`, `-cold`, `-c2`, `-c3`, and the `redroid` and `ubuntu:24.04` images remain on the host's Docker; `binder_linux` is loaded (`rmmod` says busy until a reboot) and the verdict is go, so it stays.
- Repository: `infra/oracle-cloud/android-emulator` (redroid, binder loader, tailscale sidecar), registered in vm-ops-api's known apps, container names, compose path and trunk path map, in the secrets groups and `main.tf`; not in the recovery sweep. The running vm-ops-api (`sha-4e0614d`) does not know the app yet.

Correction to the rollout note above: the redeploy script that runs `wf secret sync-env` runs in a sibling container of the vm-ops-api image (`siblingImage`), whose `wf` is baked in at build from `nuesslerm/wf-cli:latest`; agent-shell's `wf` is not in that path, so there is no agent-shell-before-vm-ops-api order. The real hazard: a vm-ops-api image built while `wf-cli:latest` still holds the old `wf` would call `wf secret` with a `wf` that only has `secrets`. `wf-cli:latest` was rebuilt from the head on 2026-10-11 (pushed 03:15 UTC) so the next vm-ops-api build is consistent. That build currently fails: `redeploy-self` ends in `trunk_deploy.app.failed stage=build reason=build script failed` within 12 s, three times, while the same script (`buildTrunkImageScript`) run by hand through `wf vm exec` exits 0 and pushes `vm-ops-api:sha-1867d1e` and the watchdog image. Cause not found (the job output is not logged); vm-ops-api still runs `sha-4e0614d`.

Head `4b0011e22`, run 2026-10-11: `wf check --deployed 4b0011e22 --no-publish-eas` (sandbox, `WF_SOCKS_PORT=1055`) tier 3 pass, exit 0, report `verify/4b0011e22dd101fc4e029ce88d324c4071fc72c0.json`. Cold start n=5 after 3 warm-ups on the deploy-profile binary: `wf --help` median 2.80 ms (2.78 to 3.01), `wf knowledge rank --help` 3.19 ms (2.85 to 3.50); baseline 3.6 ms. Stash `archive-sweep` (the aliases-era rewrite of `docs/archive`) dropped: the archive keeps its dated spellings. The tailnet access rules are not reachable from `wf` (`wf vm net` has devices, up and auth-key only), so the emulator node's rule for `metro-runner-main:20000` is a Tailscale admin change.

Native app on redroid, run 2026-10-11 (head `7b8bee986`): EAS build 30dae6da failed in Gradle (`Skia prebuilt binaries not found`, pnpm skips the react-native-skia postinstall); `eas-build-post-install: install-skia` fixed it, build 30dae6da... (second build `30dae6da-96e6-4f62-a7d3-8f338f5f7144`) finished as a 160 MB APK with the new `android-apk` profile in `eas.json`. Installed on redroid by a driver container in the `android-emulator` project over localhost adb (adb over the tailnet reported the device offline during the install; the tailscale sidecar was being recreated by the watchdog because redroid starved its healthcheck, so redroid is now capped at 1.5 CPUs and the check allows 30 s and 6 retries). Install 3.6 s. Routes opened through `mla-tamagui://` deep links, text found with `uiautomator dump` (about 1 s per poll, so upper bounds): home 5.7 s first, storybook 3.6 s, settings 3.8 s, two-sum ("Two Sum" found) 5.0 s; five cold starts of home 4.5, 4.5, 4.5, 4.6, 4.5 s; screencap 63,587 bytes; app 262 MB PSS (422 MB RSS); load average 1.8 to 2.7, host MemAvailable about 5.8 GB. Against the browser baseline (1.5 to 1.7 s, 298 to 309 MB PSS): time about 2.8 times, app memory 0.9 times, whole-Android memory 4.9 times from the earlier measurement. Both under the 5 times rule. Decision: go for the spike. Not measured: the Expo Go flow on the shared Metro link (the tailnet ACL has no line for the emulator node). vm-ops-api was rolled to `sha-7b8bee9` after a one-line bootstrap Dockerfile (restored in `879d8e661`) got the old deployer past its stale watchdog build context.

Verification on head `d4f246cd8`, 2026-10-11 (task #77): CI on the head: `plan` success, `deploy (mla-tamagui)` success, GitGuardian neutral. `wf ship mla-tamagui --ref d4f246cd8 --clear-cache` 122 s, `wf ship --status mla-tamagui` reports commit `d4f246cd8`, alive, `exp://metro-runner-main:20000`; Metro `/status` answers `packager-status:running`, the Expo manifest answers 200 (2,596 bytes), the tap-through page `http://vm-ops-api-main:8080/expo-go?url=...` answers 200. `wf check --deployed d4f246cd8 --no-publish-eas` failed the first time (tier 3 `Page.navigate timed out` on `/` and storybook) because Metro was recompiling its 17 MB web bundle after the cache clear; fetching the bundle once (31 s) and re-running gave tier 3 pass, exit 0; the generated `docs/plans/fix-d4f246cd` was deleted. A cold Metro after `--clear-cache` therefore needs one warm-up request before the 30 s navigation budget of tier 3 holds. BetterStack: `wf vm logs --service mla-tamagui` returns no rows (Metro has never shipped logs; known gap, not fixed here). VM state: all services Up and healthy; vm-ops-api `sha-7b8bee9`; agent-shell still runs image `sha-4e0614d` with a `wf` built 2026-10-08, so it has not received the redesign yet. Deploy order, checked: vm-ops-api first (done, `sha-7b8bee9`), then agent-shell (pending, to be rolled together with the nested-dind change). Host load 0.55, 1.5 GB free and 4.1 GB available with redroid up. Cold start of the head `wf`, 5 rounds of 100 runs: `wf --help` 2.94, 3.19, 2.98, 2.70, 3.05 ms (median 2.98), `wf ship --help` 3.24, 3.28, 3.26, 2.94, 3.00 ms (median 3.24), against the 3.6 ms baseline.

Decisions closing the wf redesign goal, 2026-10-11: the `archive-sweep` stash was dropped (its content is on dev through the docs sweep); the two stashes still in the sandbox (`secrets-relocation-attempt`, `regenerated wf bundle`) belong to other branches and are kept. `knowledge for|which` stay renamed to `rank|locate` (`for` and `which` are prepositions, not verbs; `find` and `gem` were rejected because `find` collides with the shell's and `gem` says nothing). `ask --stats|--savings` stay: they are read-only reports on asks, the object they act on is the same, and moving them to `repo` would split one workflow across two nouns. Open and handed to the owner: the Tailscale access rule for the emulator node (Metro `:20000`, remote-chat-agent, leetcode-backend), so the Expo Go flow on the shared Metro link was not run on redroid and the live-backend runbooks cannot run yet; the redroid image measured was `13.0.0_64only-latest`, not 14.0.0 (the 14 image was not tried); `binder_linux` stays loaded because the verdict is go (no `rmmod`), with the `binder` container reloading it on boot; VM-side cleanup (exited `reporting-service-spike-*` containers, `redroid-data*` volumes, images) and deleting the branches `spike/redroid-as-reporting-service` and `spike/android-apk` wait for the owner's confirmation; the nested-dind cgroup experiment (diagnosis: the nested container sees a threaded root with only `cpuset cpu pids`, no controllers delegated; fix candidate: the `docker:dind` evacuation) was blocked by the permission classifier as a security weakening and is parked in `stash@{0}` of the sandbox, patch saved outside the repository.

Nested dind fix and Expo Go on the shared Metro link, 2026-10-11 (head `9bb1db125`): diagnosis in agent-shell's nested dockerd: a nested privileged container has `cgroup.type threaded`, controllers `cpuset cpu pids`, and `mkdir` then a `cgroup.procs` write fails (`Not supported`), which stops redroid's init; the agent-shell app container itself is a normal `domain` root with all controllers. Fix: the `docker:dind` cgroup v2 evacuation in the dind role of `infra/oracle-cloud/agent-shell/entrypoint.sh` (processes to `/sys/fs/cgroup/init`, controllers into `subtree_control`). Proven first in a throwaway container from the same image (`android-emulator-dindtest`: child cgroup `domain`, controllers `cpuset cpu io memory hugetlb pids rdma misc dmem`, `child_procs_ok`, redroid 13 booted inside in 65 s), then rolled out in the order vm-ops-api (`sha-c0bc8e5`), image `agent-shell:sha-df62946` built on the VM, `wf ship --redeploy-only agent-shell --tag sha-df62946`; the first redeploy recreated only the `app` container, `--recreate-all` was needed for `agent-shell-dind` and `-sessions` (both were still on `sha-d2b25a6`). After the rollout the same nested probe shows `child cgroup.type=domain` and `child_procs_ok`. Redroid 14 (`14.0.0_64only-latest`) inside agent-shell's docker, 1.5 CPUs, adb on the bridge address from `wf vm exec`: boot to `sys.boot_completed` 24 to 49 s (49 s included the image pull), APK install 12.9 s (nested fuse-overlayfs; 3.6 s on the host Docker), routes home 4.8 s, storybook 3.5 s, settings 4.3 s, two-sum 5.4 s, five cold starts of home 4.4, 4.5, 4.5, 4.6, 4.9 s, app 228 MB PSS (366 MB RSS), load average 2.6 to 4.0. Expo Go 57.0.9 on `exp://100.112.131.75:20000` (the Metro node's tailnet address): ping 1.4 to 4.5 ms from inside the nested container, so no access rule was needed: the tailnet policy (read through the OAuth client with `policy_file` scope, no change made) is `grants: * to * ip *` and `android-emulator-main` carries `tag:mobile-learning` like every service; MagicDNS names do not resolve inside nested containers (public DNS fallback), so use the address. First run showed `Something went wrong` with a name, then with the address the app loaded and threw `Skia web loader is web-only` (uncaught, Expo Go): `typeof window !== 'undefined'` is true on React Native, so the module-scope `LoadSkiaWeb` call hit the web-only stub in `lib/skiaWeb.ts`; fixed in `TracingCanvasSkia.tsx` and `charts-spike.tsx` with `Platform.OS === 'web'` (flow tests `test:flow:skia` 15 of 15; commit `9bb1db125`, Metro re-pointed by the Deploy leg). Rerun: Expo Go first-run developer-menu screens dismissed (9.2 s and 14.6 s after the link), the home screen text `Blind 75` found 19.9 s after the link opened, Metro warm; against the browser's warm 4.8 to 5.6 s that is about 3.5 to 4 times at worst (about 2.7 times without the 14.6 s of menu screens), under the 5 times rule. VM cleanup, run by a one-shot service in the android-emulator project with the host socket (owner-approved, removed afterwards): removed containers `android-emulator-dindtest`, `-dindprobe`, `reporting-service-spike-probe`, `-spike-binder`, volumes `android-emulator_dindtest-docker`, `-dindtest-sock`, `reporting-service_redroid-data`, `-c2`, `-c3`, `-cold`, and the `redroid:13.0.0_64only-latest` image; kept `android-emulator_redroid-data` (the project's own, in use). The host `android-emulator` project now starts `app` and the flow driver only with `COMPOSE_PROFILES=host`/`flow` (default: tailscale and binder only), so redroid runs on demand. Still open: the spike branches `spike/redroid-as-reporting-service` and `spike/android-apk` (deleting remote branches waits for the owner's word), the native device-lab runbooks, BetterStack Metro lines (Metro never ships logs).

## 2026-10-11 Native voice edit on redroid: three app and setup findings, and the fast loop (Task #81 part B)

- Finding 1 (app bug, Android, SDK 57): every voice transcription failed with "Server error (0): Unsupported FormDataPart implementation". The global `fetch` is expo/fetch, which rejects RN's `{ uri, name, type }` form part and RN's Blob alike. Fixed in `voiceEditTransport.ts`: on a phone a FormData body goes through `XMLHttpRequest` (still reads the file part natively); unit tests Given/When/Then (`voiceEditTransport.native.test.ts`). iOS unverified; the same fetch is in use there.
- Finding 2 (setup): Metro bakes the MagicDNS name `remote-chat-agent-prod`; Android in redroid resolved with its own DNS and failed ("Network request failed"). Fix: boot redroid with `androidboot.redroid_net_ndns=1 androidboot.redroid_net_dns1=100.100.100.100`.
- Finding 3 (audio): redroid has no microphone, so the recording is injected at file level: a root loop on the device replaces each new `Audio/recording-*.m4a` with the spoken clip (a new inode, so the recorder's own writes go nowhere). The app reads and uploads our clip (byteSize equal to the clip), so the path under test is the app's own: recorder, file read, upload, transcript. Declared deviation in the result.
- Result of one turn on valid-palindrome row 2: transcript "Set S to only itself the numeric characters in lowercase." (backend STT, 1 s), review sheet shown.
- Fast loop: one persistent redroid on the VM with Expo Go (`lab-up.sh`, port 5555 published on the agent-shell node), reached from the sandbox through the tailnet SOCKS proxy (`socks_forward.py`), `wf android` run locally. A JS fix now reaches the device in about 6 minutes (CI plus Metro redeploy) instead of about 20 (EAS build). The sandbox cannot run redroid itself: its kernel has no binder and no /dev/kvm.
- Quirks to handle in drivers: uiautomator fails with "could not get idle state" during animations (retry), and a LogBox toast at the bottom intercepts touches near the voice toolbar (dismiss it first).

## 2026-10-12 why a deploy took 25-80 minutes (VM contention, vault stall) and what the wf build costs

- Measured on the VM (2 OCPU, 12 GB, the whole Always-Free A1 pool; no swap): 11.1 GB used, 0.8 GB available, load 9-11, CPU pressure "some" avg300 83 %, memory "some" avg300 2 %, io "some" avg60 38 % (the build supervisor admits below 20 %). Memory is tight but not thrashing; CPU is the bottleneck.
- Run 455 (`ff550f7`): `plan` job 4.5 min (`install-wf` 3 m 44 s, cache miss), `deploy (remote-chat-agent)` 6.5 min wait + 6 min, `deploy (vnc-auth-session)` 36 min waiting for the single runner + 24 min (wf carrier cargo build 8 min, image build, a Bitwarden 503 stall of 13 min inside the poll loop, a 111 s layer extraction). Quiet periods deploy in 3 min (runs 451, 452); stacked pushes queue behind one runner for 40-80 min.
- Build benchmark (`cargo build --profile deploy -p wf`, 2 CPUs pinned, n=1, unloaded x86 sandbox, peak RSS summed over the process tree): current `opt-level z` 181 s clean / 29 s incremental / 936 MB; `opt-level 1` 195 s / 36 s / 954 MB; `opt-level 1` + lld 195 s / 33 s / 944 MB. Decision: keep the profile; the same incremental build took 3.5 min on the loaded VM, so contention, not codegen, is the cost.
- Changes: `vmops::resolve_credentials` is read once per process (a deploy polled the vault every 10 s; a failure is not cached); the redroid compose swaps `cpus: 1.5` (a 75 % hard quota on a 2-CPU machine) for `cpu_shares: 128`, which only yields under contention; the login browser drops the Selkies/XFCE/X/pulse/nginx desktop (deployed; its per-container memory could not be read from agent-shell, only `free`).
- Not done, needs a decision: cancel in-flight deploy legs when a newer push supersedes them (risks a half-applied redeploy), a per-container `docker stats` probe, and memory caps for Metro and the emulator.

## 2026-10-13: viewer viewport, `/stats` probe, secrets cache, memory caps

- Viewer: headless Chromium reports an 800x600 screen and letterboxed the page; `emulate_phone` at 393x852 (CDP device metrics) now sets the viewport explicitly, and the black-bar blackbox test asserts a 393x852 JPEG.
- Probe: `vm-ops-api` `GET /stats` (host meminfo/load + per-container working set and CPU%), surfaced as `wf vm ops stats`. First reading under load: 11.9 GB host, no swap, 973 MB available, load 17-18; `agent-shell-dind` 1.9 GB at 136-152% CPU, `gh-runner` 0.96 GB, `metro-runner` 0.94 GB idle, `android-emulator` 0.40 GB idle.
- Caps: Metro `mem_limit: 1536m` + `cpu_shares: 256`; emulator `mem_limit: 2560m` (it already had `cpu_shares: 128`). Not measured under a booted emulator; revisit with `wf vm ops stats` while it runs.
- Memory-for-CPU trade: rejected. Available RAM is about 1 GB and there is no swap, so there is nothing to spend on caches or tmpfs build dirs; the bottleneck is 2 OCPUs shared by builds.
- Secrets: lookups retry through an XChaCha20-Poly1305 encrypted local cache (`chacha20poly1305`, RustCrypto, MIT/Apache-2.0, no extra native deps) that never holds the admin source; sources are searched read-first and `wf secret get` loads only the requested key; `vm-ops` credentials are cached per process.

## 2026-10-13: slim Android device, lease, test queue, and what went wrong on the way

- Slim device: every package of the stock redroid image is core (kept), in a named feature (`--with` keeps it) or removed; a unit test fails on an unclassified package. Measured 792 to 517 MiB, idle CPU 13% to 0.1%, 85 to 67 processes. Decision: slim is the default for `wf android up`, `--stock` for anything touching system UI, `wf android features` shows what the device can do. Not slimmed: the launcher and Settings (the home activity must exist for `sys.boot_completed`), `sdksandbox`, `android.ext.services`.
- Bug I introduced: the slim rollback never rolled back (system_server rewrites its package state on shutdown), leaving the shared device half-slimmed and not finishing boot for hours, and I misread it as "the set does not boot". Fixed by removing the container and volume, then booting fresh, with a black-box test that the script never deletes state in a live container. Lesson: a boot proof must end in a recreated clean device, not an edit in place.
- Lease: one holder, FIFO line, TTL with heartbeat, a flock-guarded JSON state on the machine that owns the device; mutating `wf android` verbs refuse naming the holder where the state is local (the VM). Parallel use of one screen is rejected; a second overlay display does exist (display 3 launched Settings while Expo Go ran on display 0) but screencap and touch per display are untested, so lanes stay one.
- Test queue (`wf test`): the sandbox picks runbooks (named, `--all`, or `--changed` by package from the runbook front matter), the VM worker runs them in order (deploy before ad-hoc) with `wf run` and the local agent, under the lease for android, and writes a result with the recording. The worker waits until the dev Metro includes the last app change of the job's commit. The VM runs the wf of the commit: `wf test sync-wf` builds it on the VM in its own checkout.
- VM cost lesson: every push to `dev` that touches `clis/` schedules deploy builds on the single 2-OCPU runner, and a wf build on the same VM then competes with them (MemAvailable fell to 13 MB, load 9). Iterating by pushing every fix to `dev` made the VM slower for everyone. Decision: iterate on `claude/device-lab`, merge to `dev` at milestones. The shared `/workspace/repos/expo-hello-world-app-2` checkout is reset by deploy builds under any other build, so ad-hoc builds use their own checkout (`/workspace/repos/wf-sync`, `/workspace/repos/wf-test`).

## 2026-10-13: parallel lanes on one redroid: rejected

- Test (under the lease, real device): lab app on display 0, Settings on an overlay display (id 2). Rendering works for both at once; capture does not: `screencap -d 2` rc 1, `uiautomator dump --display 2` no nodes, `input -d 2` unverifiable. Cost: +385 MB device RAM (1.18 to 1.57 GB), lab app 205 MB PSS, Settings 75 MB. Decision: one lane; runs queue behind the lease. Not tried: Android multi-user copies of one app (same memory problem, and the VM has 0.3 to 1 GB free).

## 2026-10-13: Android baseline recorded (C1)

- Decision: cold start is launch to the app's first `ReactNativeJS: Running "main"` log line, read with `logcat -v epoch -e ... -m 1`, not `am start -W` (it reports a timeout on the 1.5-CPU device, so its 11.8 s was the timeout). Evidence: three runs on the shared slim device under the lease gave 3.98, 4.97 and 5.65 s; the first probe saw the process start at 1.2 s, the marker at 5.5 s and the app's first debug logs at 14 s, so the marker is the earliest point where our JS runs.
- Cost: n=3, one APK install, about 3 minutes of device time, no teardown, no second device. Tolerances follow the spread seen (cold start 40%, PSS 25%, RSS 20%, Java heap 35%, native heap 50%) so a re-run does not fail on noise; a regression larger than that still fails `wf bench compare --suite android`.
- Tool fix on the way: `wf android lease ... --json` from a sandbox printed `{"ok":true}` and sent the VM's envelope to stderr; the VM's last JSON line is now relayed as the result.

## 2026-10-13: gates re-run, and where I stopped

- Decision: run the web gates as the committed Lua scripts (`wf run --platform browser`) instead of hand-driving `wf browser`; the scripts are what the queue will run, so a pass here and a pass there are the same test. Cost: one export (about 2.5 min), then G3 2 min, G2 4 min, G1 2 min per problem. Evidence: all three passed, with `video-check` dots on every tap.
- Decision: G1 uses the planner from the checkout, because the deployed one still times out on every planner turn (22 spoken turns on the checkout planner, 10 to 22 s each). Not fixed: the cause is the VM's stale model setting, which a sandbox cannot read. Corrected on 2026-10-14: the deployed planner was not broken; see the 2026-10-14 entry.
- Decision: stop on Android G2 after two runs and one tool fix (stroke pacing). Both runs are recorded as failures with their job ids; the earlier pass is marked stale in its result file. More probing needs a way to read the badge and the first warning from the device, which is a script change, not a quick probe.

## 2026-10-14: one device, a taller screen, and corrections

- Decision: the default screen is 432x1200@192 instead of 540x960@240. It has the same pixel count (518,400), so encode and screenshot cost stay the same, but it is 360 x 1000 dp instead of 360 x 640, so more code shows above the keyboard. The scripts read `wm size` and scale every position measured on 540x960. Commit `180a104ac`.
- Decision: the queue's Metro wait counts only changes under `frontend/mla-tamagui` and `libraries`, and the EAS marker (`frontend/.claude/.eas-published-sha`) is untracked. Evidence: a commit that only rewrote the tracked marker made the worker wait 20 minutes for a Metro redeploy that never came, and two queue jobs errored. They were not timeouts. Commits `897ec61a4`, `3a4db397d`.
- Decision: UI-server strokes are paced: at most 30 points, at least 8, two steps per segment. Evidence: one-step strokes arrive in about 100 ms, and the Tamil pad scores them too short; on the device one step fails and four steps (13 s) score 97. Commit `fef958e3d`.
- Decision: a second device was tried and dropped. Evidence on the VM (2 OCPU, 12 GB, no swap): two devices under test left 46 to 923 MB MemAvailable; a deploy build alone left 281 to 503 MB; one device under test uses about 1.2 to 1.4 GB; the buildx builder holds about 0.9 to 1.5 GB; with one device idle and the builder running, 782 MB was available. One device, up only for the post-deploy test step, down after. Gap: `docs/gaps/vm-memory-saturation.md`.
- Decision: `wf android down` stops Android gracefully, `docker stop -t 30` and then removal. Evidence: a hard `docker rm -f` corrupted the `rdl-data` volume once. Commit `c09291b56`.
- Correction: the deployed voice planner works. From the VM, `/api/models/preferences` resolves `kimi-code-plan-global/kimi-for-coding-highspeed`, and `/api/voice-plan` answers 200 in 22 s cold and 7 s warm. Browser G1 passed against the deployed agent (22 turns). The 2026-10-13 entry said it timed out; that was the runbook script moving on before planning finished. It now waits for `ProblemEditorVoiceProcessing` to disappear (`7a08ea144`).
- Decision (owner): `VM_GATEWAY_AUTH_SEED` is rotated by the owner by hand; no agent step rotates it. The spike branches `spike/redroid-as-reporting-service` and `spike/android-apk` stay. Task-list items #69, #76 and #78 are separate work, not part of the device-lab plan.
- Decision: queue job results carry `durationS` and a per-verb timing table read from `WF_RUN_TIMING`; the Metro wait follows the deploy's own rule (`pnpm --filter "...[<metro commit>]" list` naming `mla-tamagui`), with the path rule and a 5 minute cap when pnpm is missing; `wf run --platform android` force-stops Expo Go before the script. Commit `2d660e5d3`.
- Decision: the device-tests workflow brings the device up before the queue and takes it down under `if: always()`. Finding: its `workflow_dispatch` input description held an unquoted colon, which is invalid YAML, so GitHub could not parse the workflow until now. Commit `e3e123570`.
- Benchmark (P8, agent proof): `wf bench agent-proof --arm <arm>` hands an agent the four-step two-sum task plus the whole `wf run --api` text, extracts the one Lua script it answers with and runs it recorded in the browser against a local fixture export (`EXPO_PUBLIC_LEETCODE_FIXTURES=1`, `127.0.0.1:8810/mla-tamagui`). n=1 per arm from the sandbox: scripted 4.1 s (run only); Sonnet via `claude -p` (no tools, no MCP, outside the repo) 12.0 s agent + 4.0 s run, $0.028, 6,114 tokens, passed; opencode on the ladder (MiniMax-M3, first rung) 85.4 s agent + 3.9 s run, $0.011, 22,249 tokens, passed. Every arm passed. Results: `docs/runbooks/.results/agent-proof.json`. A VM opencode arm is still to add.
- Finding: the task as first written (four steps) fails on the web export for every arm. The editor autofocuses and its keyboard covers Run Local, so the tap lands on the toolbar. The task now says to lower the keyboard as a user does (swipe the toolbar's action row, tap Done, wait for the rails to go). A swipe during the first render is lost, so it retries up to 3 times.
- Finding: Sonnet without the repo context (12 s, $0.03) costs far less than the 2026-10-12 `claude -p` run (127 s, $0.18). Opencode from the sandbox is slower than Sonnet here (85 s), and its prompt carries the repo's AGENTS.md (22K tokens).
- Decision: `WF_WAIT_BUDGET_MS` stays at 10,000. Rule: max(2000, p95 of successful waits x 2, rounded up to 500), capped at 10,000. Pooled: n=18, p50 53 ms, p95 5,187 ms. Browser alone: n=13, p95 274 ms, which would allow 2,000. Android alone (the G3 run's `WF_RUN_TIMING`): n=5, 3.2 to 5.2 s per uiautomator wait, which reaches the cap. One budget serves both platforms, so it cannot drop yet. Next step: a per-platform budget. New: `WF_WAIT_LOG=<file>` logs every budgeted wait with its outcome, because the Android timing log has no ok flag.
- Decision: the vnc-auth-capture image no longer carries `wf` and the wf-cli carrier is no longer part of its release. Finding: the container ran `wf` for exactly one command, `exec wf secret login listener` (the s6 service `clis/vnc-auth-capture/docker/s6/svc-vnc-auth/run`); no init hook, compose service, descriptor login command or vm-ops-api route runs `wf` inside it. The listener is now `vnc-auth-listener` (`clis/vnc-auth-capture/listener`), a crate with its own workspace and lockfile that compiles only the wf sources the listener runs through `#[path]` modules (`vnc/`, `secrets/`, `args.rs`, `env_file.rs`, `log_emit.rs`, `log_export.rs`, `root.rs`, `time.rs`, plus the cdp, http and rca-core crates), built in the Dockerfile's own `listener` stage and copied as the image's last layer. The `extra` paths of the vnc-auth-session leg are exactly those sources, so a change to the rest of wf, `clis/Cargo.toml`, `clis/Cargo.lock` or `infra/oracle-cloud/wf-cli` no longer plans the leg. A unit test in `deploy.rs` keeps the leg list, the Dockerfile's copies and the crate's modules equal. The carrier mechanism in `build_image.rs` (build wf-cli first, pass `WF_CLI_TAG`) had only this image as a user and is removed. vm-ops-api and leetcode-auth-cookie-capture still copy `nuesslerm/wf-cli:latest`, and `wf test sync-wf` still builds the wf-cli Dockerfile. Neither changes.
- Evidence (build cost, sandbox with 4 x86 cores, clean target, warm registry): `vnc-auth-listener` release build 67 s, 5.6 MB; the full `wf` deploy profile 122 s, 15.9 MB. Rebuilt after touching every `.rs` file, which is what the Docker cache-mount stage does: 9 s against 31 s. On the VM the carrier's cargo build took 8 min in run 455. In the same run the leg took 24 min plus 36 min waiting for the runner. Most of that cost was the leg running at all: a new wf binary sat before the Node, npm and desktop layers, so all of them were rebuilt, pushed and extracted again. Pushes that now rebuild vnc-auth-session: anything under `clis/vnc-auth-capture/`, and the listener's own sources listed above. Tests: listener-crate unit tests 46 of 46; `wf` unit tests 160 of 160 (4 new leg tests); black-box `cli_deploy`, `cli_deploy_run` and `cli_auth_listener`, 69 of 69 on `wf`; `cli_auth_listener` 28 of 28 against the slim binary (`VNC_AUTH_LISTENER_BIN`, real Chromium). Clippy is clean for `wf`, `wf-blackbox` and the listener crate. Not run: a `docker build` of the image (no Docker daemon in the sandbox), or anything on the VM.
- Decision: the queue warms the bundle Expo Go actually loads before a device job: it reads the dev Metro's Android manifest and fetches its launch asset (`frontend/mla-tamagui/index.bundle` with a `baseUrl` transform), recorded as `metroWarmS`. Evidence: warming the expo-router entry instead took 644 s and Expo Go still missed its 300 s first launch (job `1a13a0e6a32-7b288`); after the fix the next jobs warmed in 3 to 5 s and passed. Commit `6ce6587ce`.
- Decision: job results carry a VM sample every 20 s (`vm.loadMax`, `vm.memAvailableMinMb`). Evidence: today's slow and failed runs all coincided with deploy builds at load 13 to 20. Commit `c7d93bb85`.
- Finding and fix: two Tamil screens kept state when a link opened another lesson or subject on the same screen (letter 6 of 12 on the k series; the previous word's ink on the next pad, which cost two extra Check rounds per word). Both routes now key their screen by id, each with a flow test that fails without the key. Commits `db9b99e56`, `3aa1166af`.
- Decision: on Android, G3 taps each toolbar key once per problem and types repeats on the keyboard; brackets always go on the keyboard there, because the bracket keys insert a pair that Android typing does not type over. The gate (every line typed, Run Local passes, keyboard and rail, zero warnings) is unchanged. Commits `eeb13f8ec`, `3aa1166af`.
- Decision: no `dev` pushes with `clis/` changes while device jobs are queued: each one rebuilt images for 30 to 40 minutes and starved the dev Metro. The vnc image no longer carries the full `wf` (it builds only its listener), so most `clis/` pushes no longer rebuild it (`0f72e0e97`).
- Finding: the first slim vnc build (deploy run 515 at `3aa1166af`) still hit the leg's 40-minute timeout. The image no longer builds `wf`, but `vnc-auth-listener` compiled its 253 crates cold on the VM: 1,346 s in, it had only reached `hyper-rustls`, with three device jobs and Metro on the same 2 OCPUs (load 13 to 17). The same build takes 67 s on the sandbox's 4 x86 cores. Its cargo registry and target directories are BuildKit cache mounts, so the next build starts from what this one compiled; deploy run 516 at `df90f3a2d` is the check.
- Decision (owner): the device has a real phone's proportions. The default screen is 480x1040@213, a Galaxy S25 in dp (360 x 780, 19.5:9), instead of 432x1200@192 (360 x 1000 dp, 2.78:1, taller than any current phone). The Pixel 9 (412 x 915 dp) was the first proposal; the S25 keeps the 360 dp width every script position was measured at, and its 499,200 pixels are under the earlier 518,400. `wf android up` now converges: a running device at another size is stopped with `docker stop -t 30` and booted at the asked size. The Android goldens at the old size are removed and re-recorded by an `--update-goldens` run.
- Decision: `wf test sync-wf` became `wf ship worker`, since it deploys the worker's wf. Finding: a sync took 11 minutes (13:30 to 13:41 on 14 Oct) for a one-file change: a full image build that sent a 297 MB context, imported the registry cache and touched every `.rs` file, which recompiles the whole `wf` crate. Evidence on the sandbox (4 x86 cores, warm dependencies): `wf` deploy profile after touching every file 33 s, after touching one file of the `wf` crate 37 s, the same with incremental compilation 2.5 s for a one-line change. The new verb builds in a rust container over a kept volume (git checkout plus target directory, incremental): `git checkout` rewrites only changed files, so the touch-all workaround is not needed.
- Decision: `wf test cancel <jobId>` takes a queued job out of the queue. A hand edit of the job file on the VM was refused by the session's permission classifier, and a queue needs a cancel verb anyway.
- Evidence (`wf ship worker` on the VM, 14 Oct): the first build into the empty volume took about 45 minutes (cold registry and target, load 10 to 30 from deploy builds); the next, from d39f6178b to b19024c21 (a merge that touched about 15 `wf` source files), 313 s; the same commit again, 13 s on the VM and 18 s wall. The old `wf test sync-wf` took 11 minutes for a one-file change.
- Finding and fix: the first G3 run at the S25 size (job 1a13b0a69eb-8fea2) failed at Run Local because deploy run 524 (a `dev` push that changed `libraries/flow-test-harness`) restarted the dev Metro mid-run: Expo Go showed "Cannot connect to Expo CLI" over the editor. The worker now reads Metro's commit when the job starts and when it ends; a failure across a change (or with Metro down at the end) becomes `error` with that note, since it says nothing about the app. Pushes that reach `mla-tamagui`'s package graph wait for an idle queue.
- Finding and fix: the browser debugger never resolved a point on a real Expo web export. Metro's source maps carry `x_facebook_sources`, and `SourceMap::from_slice` refuses those as another format, so every page reported "0 files with maps"; the black-box tests passed only because their fixture maps are plain. Maps are now decoded with `sourcemap::decode_slice`, taking the Hermes variant's standard mappings. The debugger probe on a `--source-maps --no-minify` export then hit `ProblemEditorScreen.tsx:501` and `:511` with stacks at original lines. The same parser serves the Android (Hermes) debugger.
- Decision (owner): deploys may run while device tests run. A job whose failure or error comes with a dev Metro move, a Metro that is down at the end, or a Metro wait or manifest error is queued again once (`attempt` 2); the first attempt's results stay beside it (`<id>.attempt1`), and `result.json` carries `attempt` and `metroCommit`. Supersedes the 14 Oct rule that held pushes while jobs ran.
- Decision (owner): every harness run is a row in the Autonomous DB table `HEALTH_RPT.TEST_RUNS` (ORDS alias `test_runs`), for the dashboards, beside `health_reports` and `edit_events`: harness, runbook or suite, job, sha, Metro commit, verdict, duration, attempt and retry reason, steps, screenshots, recordings, goldens, warnings, debugger hits, tests, the timing table. Created 14 Oct with `wf test metrics setup` (REST-enabled SQL as ADMIN); its URL is the vault key `TEST_RUNS_ORDS_BASE_URL`. `wf run` and the queue worker post at the end of each run, the flow reporter through `wf test metrics record`; `wf test metrics list` reads rows back. Writes are best-effort: a failed post logs one line and never changes a verdict. First rows: the browser debugger probe (pass, 9.1 s, 2 hits) and a flow debug run.
- Finding (builds on the VM, 14 Oct): every push with a `clis/` or `backend/` change rebuilt the remote-chat-agent image for 15 to 40 minutes, and the device jobs waiting on Metro meanwhile timed out (metroWarmS 1,397 s; "answered 000 after 1,303 s"; MemAvailable 226 to 416 MB, load 12 to 30 on 2 cores). Three causes, each fixed: (1) `docker buildx prune --max-used-space 20GB` ran before every build while the cache held 21.4 GB, so the cargo registry and target cache mounts were emptied (the backend target mount showed 0 B, usage 0) and each build compiled from nothing: the cap is 60 GB (the disk has 59 GB free of 145). (2) `COPY` gives every source a fresh mtime, so cargo recompiled every workspace crate on every build even with the cache intact (and `wf-cli` touched every `.rs` on purpose, which forced the same): `infra/oracle-cloud/build/cargo-touch-changed.sh` ages unchanged files and freshens changed ones against a content manifest kept in the target cache mount, in all five cargo stages (wf-cli, vnc listener, rca's wf and service, leetcode-backend); unit-tested in `deploy.rs`. (3) The builder ran at the default CPU weight: it is now `--cpu-shares 128` (the worker's build container too, and the CI runner 256 in agent-shell's compose, applied on its next by-hand deploy), so Metro, the device and the services win contention and builds take the idle time. No cache leaves the VM. sccache was considered and not added: with correct mtimes cargo's own fingerprints give the same incremental result without a new tool.
- Finding: Metro's transform cache sat in `os.tmpdir()` inside the metro-runner container's own layer, gone on every container recreate; `metro.config.js` now stores it under the worktree's `node_modules/.cache/metro`, on the workspace volume (verified: a write through the configured store lands there).
- Finding: `test.yml` was `workflow_dispatch` only (last run 5 Oct) and `verify-deployed.yml` listened to a feature branch, so a push to dev ran Deploy alone despite the root instructions; both now run on every push to dev. `install-wf` used GitHub's cache service and a non-incremental build (3.5 to 5 min per job): it builds incrementally in the runner's own target directory instead.
- Finding (CI on the VM, 14 Oct, Test run 6 at `6d84d516e`): the test job's `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` hit its 10-minute step timeout. `actions/checkout` cleans the runner's workspace, so every run relinked node_modules from scratch: 7.5 minutes of node-gyp (`utf-8-validate`, `bufferutil`, `tree-sitter`) plus seven workspace `prepare` builds, each `tsc` taking about 60 s at load 25 to 36; the root `postinstall` would then have cold-built the release `wf` binary (`pnpm --filter @cli/workflow-cli run build`), a second full cargo build beside `install-wf`'s. The two G3 unit jobs (1a13b49638f-966f3, 1a13b496a5c-96716) and the G2 vowels job (1a13b497a4a-96761) running under that load timed out at 300 s with every device call slowed (a screenshot 9.5 s, `edit-text` 10 s, 65 finds 106 s). Fixes: every self-hosted checkout keeps the workspace (`clean: false`), so `pnpm install` links in seconds; `scripts/postinstall.mjs` skips the cargo build and the agent-config sync when `CI=true` (a workflow gets wf from `install-wf`). Owed: the gh-runner at cpu-shares 256 (compose) needs the by-hand agent-shell redeploy; until then a CI job competes with the device at equal priority.
- Finding and fix: every `wf test worker` run took about 1.9 s longer than its jobs (black-box test `given_two_devices_and_two_android_jobs…` at 3.8 s against a 3.6 s budget): the metrics writer called `bws secret list` for the absent password even when `TEST_RUNS_ORDS_BASE_URL` was set empty, which is how the harness disables it. An empty URL now disables the writer without a vault call. Note for the harness: `wf-blackbox` runs `clis/target/release/wf`, so a source change needs `cargo build --release -p wf` before its suites say anything about it.
- Decision: `wf run --debug` on Android reports where the attach stands. The probe job 1a13b4929bd-96646 passed its UI with 0 hits and nothing said why; the run now prints `debug status: {...}` (Metro polls, the inspector targets Metro listed, the attached page, the debugger's scripts and maps, the last error) and the report carries it as `debugStatus`. Without a runtime the hits are empty, never an error; the status carries the reason.
- Decision (owner, 14 Oct): every CI test run leaves standard evidence a recurring Claude job turns into report artifacts with images, recordings and metrics. The unit is a run bundle: `wf test publish <dir>` takes a `wf run` record dir, a flow report dir or a queue job's results, uploads every evidence file (html, json, jsonl, png, jpg, mp4, webm, log, txt; three levels deep; 60 MB per file, 250 MB per bundle) to the verification-artifacts bucket under `verification/test-runs/<runId>/`, writes `bundle.json` (schema 1: kind, harness, suite or runbook, sha, verdict, duration, report {html, json}, screenshots[], recordings[], debug, timing, files[{path, url, bytes, type}], notes[], metrics row) and lists the run first in `verification/test-runs/index.json` (newest 500), then posts the `test_runs` row with the bundle's report URL as `report_path`. Producers: `test.yml` runs the flow tests of the touched packages and publishes `.flow-report`; `verify-deployed.yml` publishes every `wf run` record dir of the tier-3 gate; the VM queue worker publishes each job (`WF_TEST_PUBLISH=1`, set by `wf test worker --ensure`); `wf run --publish` for hand runs. The bucket's read side is anonymous by URL and objects expire after 30 days, so the reporter reads `index.json` and each `bundle.json` without a credential. Rejected: GitHub Actions artifacts (off the VM, 90-day retention we do not control, no public URL for a page to embed) and ORDS as the file store (rows are for numbers; files are the bucket's job).
- Decision: a run told to stop still ends with its report. The queue's timeout killed `wf run` outright, so the four timed-out jobs of the S25 batch had no report at all, against the hard rule. The worker now puts a run in its own process group, sends SIGTERM and waits up to 60 s before SIGKILL; `wf run` catches SIGTERM and SIGINT into a flag that every sleep slice, wait loop and every 1,000 Lua instructions check, so the script fails with "stopped: SIGTERM received after N s", the recording stops, the hits are read and the report is written with verdict error and exit code 124. Android waits run as child `wf android wait` processes, which the group signal ends at once; a browser wait is one CDP call of up to its budget (240 s for `wait_slow`), so a stop during one lands after that call, within the worker's grace. Black-box: a script that signals its own run ends at the next sleep slice with the report; a runner that reports on SIGTERM has its report in the job's result.
- Evidence (CI on the VM after `clean: false` and the postinstall skip, Test run 8 at `0a339fa75`): `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` 3 min 44 s (run 6: over the 10-minute cap), typecheck and unit tests of the touched packages 6 min 51 s, `install-wf` 4 s (incremental on the runner's volume), the whole job 11 min 34 s plus 6 min 30 s queued behind the deploy legs; Deploy 534 green in 26 min (533 took 2 h 32 min: the first build after the cache change seeded every manifest). `wf ship worker` from `6d2ccfe73` to `0a339fa75` (nine wf source files) took 9 s. Finding: `verify-deployed.yml` has failed on every push since it moved to push (runs 2 to 4): `install-oci` runs `wf vm creds sync` before `install-wf` put `wf` on the PATH ("wf: command not found"); the steps are now in the right order.
- Decision (owner): the Lua runner is not a base command. `wf run` goes; each harness runs its own scripts with the bindings it has: `wf browser run --script`, `wf android run --script` (`droid` accepted), `wf flow run` (TypeScript flow tests, no Lua), over one shared core (record dir, stop, report, publish, metrics row). The report page is the same renderer for browser and Android, now checked by rendering it through the browser harness at the iPhone 15 Pro preset: the device frame got a `document` mode (`frame=document`, `WF_BROWSER_FRAME=document`) where the status bar and home indicator take their own rows instead of overlaying the page, since a report is not the app under test; the page itself stacks the facts under 480 px, wraps hashes and paths, opens debugger hits with their stacks, always shows the JS warnings, debugger hits and timing sections (timing lines now go to `<record dir>/timing.jsonl` by default on every platform, the browser driver included) and says "none" where a section is empty, so a pass page and a fail page have the same shape.
- Decision (owner): `wf run` is gone from the surface. Each harness runs its scripts itself: `wf browser run --script <lua>` and `wf android run --script <lua>` (the platform is fixed and `--platform` refused; `--api` prints the harness's reference), and `wf flow run [<test file>]` runs the vitest flow tests into `.flow-report` (TypeScript, so no `--script`; the tests' own `logAt`/`breakAt` are the points). One core (`run.rs`) serves the two device harnesses; the queue worker calls `wf <platform> run --script` for each job; `wf run` stays as the old spelling for one release and is no longer listed. The browser-harness screenshot gap (a capture that came back as the frame's chrome tiled 22 times, run `dbg-browser7`, not reproduced in two reruns) is filed in `docs/gaps/browser-harness-screenshot-tiled-chrome.md`.
- Finding (report artifacts, 15 Oct): a claude.ai artifact page does not load `<img>`/`<video>` from the OCI bucket (the first Routine page showed neither), so report pages keep their media in the artifact's asset store (`capabilities: {assets: {}}`, `asset: true` uploads, `/_blob/<id>` urls) and keep the bucket url as a link; the Routine prompt says so, and renders the page through the browser harness at the phone preset before publishing.
- Decision (owner): the browser harness's on-page markers are numbered with the input log's action id and animate (tap ripple, drag finger with arrowhead, stroke draw-on), older ones dimmed; `video-check` keeps its contract (tap blue within 20 px, 300 ms) and still passes.
- Finding and decision (owner asked why a remote-chat-agent deploy takes 19 min, 15 Oct): 17 of the 19 min (12:05 to 12:22 in Deploy 38730467441) are the `wf` binary's cargo build inside the image's `wfbuilder` stage, not the service: `wf` is one large crate, so any change under `clis/wf` recompiles all of it at `opt-level = "z"`, and the `deploy` profile had incremental compilation off, on a 4-OCPU VM also running the device, Metro and verify. The same build in `wf ship worker` with incremental on took 367 s. Both image builds of `wf` (remote-chat-agent and the wf-cli carrier) now set `CARGO_PROFILE_DEPLOY_INCREMENTAL=true`; their target dir is already a BuildKit cache mount. The next deploy with a `clis/wf` change is the benchmark; if it stays above 5 min, the image copies the runner's prebuilt binary instead.
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): one screenshot model in all three harnesses, as in Flutter's golden tests. A test takes screenshots by name (`device:screenshot(name)` in Lua, `screenshot(name)` in the flow harness) and the run always keeps them; `--goldens` compares every screenshot taken with its golden (a mismatch or a missing golden fails), `--update-goldens` writes them; without a flag nothing is compared. The Lua runners' goldens directory option is now `--goldens-dir`; `device:golden` stays as the old name of `device:screenshot` for one release. Also decided: optional network stubs from Lua (browser through CDP, Android on the network path), device runs as a workflow instead of the VM test queue, and one incremental build of every CLI on the VM before Deploy (plan in `/root/.claude/plans/declarative-knitting-hummingbird.md`, sections 2 to 3a).
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): no device test queue. Test runs follow code changes, so the VM's one Actions runner sequences device runs with Test and Deploy: the Device tests workflow (after Deploy on dev, or dispatched with `sha`, `runbooks`, `flags`) runs `wf test device`, which picks runbooks like `wf test plan`, runs each unit through its harness in turn under the runbook's timeout, and publishes each run as a bundle. Gone: `wf test enqueue|worker|list|status|result|logs|wait|cancel|fetch`, the VM worker, `wf ship worker`, the job and lease files. The goal criteria that read the queue (2026-10-13 b2 and b4, 2026-10-14 p1 and p2) now read the same facts from `wf test device` and its tests; no threshold changed.
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): every CLI exists once on the VM, built incrementally before Deploy. Step 1: `clis.yml` (Deploy's first job, so every push to dev) runs `install-wf`, which builds a static musl `wf` (rustls and vendored Lua link statically, so one binary runs on noble, bookworm, jammy and scratch alike) into `/opt/clis/<clis tree hash>/` on the `clis` volume and flips `/opt/clis/current`; it keeps current plus three builds for a one-flip rollback. The key is the clis/ tree, not the commit, so a job whose tree is installed builds nothing (every Deploy, Test and device job used to spend a 15 s cargo no-op). Measured here: the musl build is 2m08s cold, 17 s for a wf-only change, 0 s for an installed tree; 72 flow goldens compare in 2.9 s with the musl binary against 3.0 s with glibc, so the musl allocator costs nothing visible. Step 2 (next): the containers mount `clis` read-only and link `/usr/local/bin/wf` to `current/wf`, the `wfbuilder` stages and the `wf-cli` carrier image go, and cloud-init seeds the volume.
- Decision (15 Oct): Android network stubs go through the network path, DNAT, not a Hermes fetch shim. Spike on the VM (job b9505c05): as root in redroid, `iptables -t nat` works; with a rule sending 10.123.45.67:8789 to 172.17.0.1:18093, a request from the root shell and one from an app uid (10050) both reached a server in agent-shell's network namespace, which the runner shares, so a server inside `wf android run` is reachable at the device's gateway. The dev Metro bakes the backends in as plain `http://<tailnet IP>:8789` (leetcode) and `:8787` (agent), so the server sees each request as sent and forwards unmatched ones to the host the `Host` header names. One rule per backend port (`WF_ANDROID_STUB_PORTS`), tagged `wf-stub`, cleared at the end of the run and before a new set. The shim fallback is not needed. Found on the way: the device had been left up between runs, holding 403 MB while the VM had 878 MB available during an image build; `wf android down` freed it.
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): only focused tests run. A runbook with `focus: true` is selected when a push reaches it (`--changed`), and `--all` or a name also reach focused runbooks only; naming an unfocused one is an error that says to focus it. `manual:` is gone (the stop probe is simply unfocused). Focused now: G1 voice edit, G2 Tamil writing, G3 CodeMirror typing and the stubbed Run Local, all on Android. Why: the first device-tests job ran every Android runbook a `clis/wf` change reached, 9 units in 36 min; each focused unit is to finish in 60 s. Flow tests keep running per affected package in Test (my call, flagged to the owner: selecting them by focus would drop most of that coverage).
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): scripted Lua tests are e2e tests; a runbook is plain text an agent drives. The scripts moved from `docs/runbooks/scripts/` to `e2e/<name>.lua` (stubs to `e2e/stubs/`), each with its metadata in a `--[[--- ... ---]]` header (platform, against, packages, timeout, units, focus); the `docs/runbooks/device-lab-*.md` files keep their prose and point at the test (`e2e:`). `wf test device` became `wf test run` (the old verb exits 2 and names it), `wf test plan` lists `tests`, the modules are `e2e_tests.rs` / `e2e_run.rs`, the black-box suite `cli_test_run`, and the workflow `e2e-tests.yml`. Goal criteria 10-13 b2, b3, b4 and 10-14 p1, p2.harness-hygiene, p4 now name these, with the same checks (cargo filters match 14 unit tests and 7 black-box tests; b3 still needs four or more tests with script, platform and target). Flow tests stay in Test per affected package (not selected by focus).
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): the run bundles are the record. Goal criteria p1, p2, p3 and p5 (Android G1, G2, G3) no longer read hand-written `docs/runbooks/.results/*.json`; they read `wf test plan --all --latest --json`, which adds each unit's newest published run from the bundle index (each index entry now carries the run's args, screen, warning count, wall time, verb count and read-back). Same gates (pass, 480x1040@213, zero warnings, recordings, a commit at or after the last app change, timing, read-back, on a test report page), every unit instead of one record per gate, and p3 is stricter: under 60 s per unit (was under 300 s per gate). The browser half of p5 still reads the browser records.
- Decision (owner, 15 Oct): one CI pipeline per push to dev, each stage its own job so each is a check on the PR: clis -> test -> plan -> deploy legs -> verify -> e2e-tests. Test (typecheck, unit and flow tests of what changed) is no longer its own push-triggered workflow: `test.yml` is a reusable stage the pipeline (`deploy.yml`, now named CI) calls after the CLI build, so it uses that build and a red test deploys nothing. Before, Test and Deploy ran side by side on the one runner and each built wf.
- Decision (16 Oct): toward 60 s per e2e unit, the per-call cost comes down before more splitting. Android `find` (objInfo, about 16 node lookups each waiting for 500 ms without accessibility events) drops UiAutomator's idle wait; scripts already wait for what they need with `wait`, and the next device run shows whether any find relied on the idle wait. `press` moves to the UI server's pressKeyCode. Units split where one unit did a whole lesson or solution: editor-typing parts of four lines seeded through the dev-only `?seed=` param (the last part runs Run Local and takes the golden), tamil vowels two letters a unit reached with Next, words one per unit. Every assertion stays: each part checks all lines up to its last, the toolbar and keyboard, and zero warnings.
- Decision (16 Oct, A2 build caches, benchmarked): every image keeps its cargo target in its own cache mount (`id=<image>-target`) and builds incrementally; npm, pnpm and bun installs mount their stores. One run per case on the VM through `wf ship --build-only`, a one-line change in each service crate, before (shared target cache, no incremental) and after: remote-chat-agent 250 -> 47 s, leetcode-backend 25 -> 37 s, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture 47 -> 37 s, reporting-service 47 -> 37 s, vnc-auth-capture 69 -> 35 s (sum 438 -> 193 s). leetcode-backend is 12 s slower on one run each, inside what fetch and push vary by, and is kept: the shared cache is what cost most. The full job logs (`wf vm exec --log`, new) showed the backend images overwriting each other's compiled crates in the one `backend/target` cache: an unchanged leetcode-backend recompiled 22 crates (11 min, `[cargo-touch] changed=0`), reporting-service 30 (11 min), and a one-line change in remote-chat-agent 154 (21 min) after other images had built. With one cache per image, a change recompiles only its own crate. First build into a new cache: 13 to 27 min per image, once. The guard `build_cache_tests` now also fails a cargo target cache without an image id.
- Decision (owner, 16 Oct): every run records where its time goes as nested spans in timing.jsonl, the same model for every harness: phases from `wf run`, `device:step` parts, each device call with its script line and outcome, Lua sleep, and the parts inside a call (the `wf android` child, adb, UI-server RPC) nested through `WF_SPAN_PARENT`, so no second counts twice. The summary rides into report.json, bundle.json, the index entry and the test_runs row (no schema change), and replaces the one-off device probe. Per-call fixes are measured before and after from these spans.
- Decision (owner, 16 Oct): `wf test plan --latest` is gone; `wf test results --query "<terms>"` reads what publish wrote (the test_runs table and the bundle index, joined on runId) and runs nothing. The goal criteria that read the newest run per unit use `newest units`, which keeps only the focused tests' current units and lists a unit with no run as missing, so the criteria stay as strong as before (a unit without a run fails them).
- Decision (owner, 16 Oct): the Android app is started cold once per e2e job, not per unit. The first runs with spans showed every unit spending 57 to 76 s reloading the dev bundle after the per-unit force-stop, more than the 60 s budget by itself. `wf android run` now reuses the running Expo Go (`--cold-start` restores the force-stop); `wf test run` starts it cold before the first unit, after a unit that did not pass and after a Metro move, as its own recorded run, so a broken app is never inherited. Every test opens its first screen with one deep link on every platform; the old web reasons for walking from home (prerender hydration, route restore) no longer hold on today's export (6/6 deep-link checks, cold and reused). The browser gets no warm-up: a page loads in 1 to 3 s and its long units are backend waits. Cold and reused starts are both recorded (`appStart`, `startupS`), and the cold start is split into process, manifest and bundle, and app start, so the next cut is chosen from numbers.
- Decision (owner, 16 Oct): `wf run` is removed; it only delegated to `wf android run` and `wf browser run` and said nothing about what it ran.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): CI jobs each do one thing, named for it: `clis` -> `flow-tests` -> `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` (one job on purpose: on the one runner, three jobs would pay checkout and install three times, about 2 min a push) -> `deploy-plan` -> `deploy (<service>)` (done when the service serves the pushed commit, `wf ship --wait-serving`) -> `service-e2e-tests` (`test:e2e:deployed` of every changed package; only remote-chat-agent has one today) -> `browser-e2e-tests` -> `android-e2e-tests` (one device, booted cold at the start of the job, warm across its tests, taken down at the end). `verify deployed` is gone: its unit re-run duplicated the checks job, its health wait moved into each deploy leg, its route smoke test became the focused browser e2e test `e2e/app-routes.lua`, its backend suite became `service-e2e-tests`, and its report is replaced by the run bundles every test already publishes. The service and browser e2e jobs stay separate although both test deployed services: the browser one shares `e2e-tests.yml` with the Android one (selection, recordings, bundles, dispatch), and a red check then names the layer; merging them would save about 1 min.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): one test plan per push, used by every job. `wf test plan` lays out every test the change needs as JSON, by CI stage (flow-tests, typecheck-lint-unit-tests, service-e2e-tests, browser-e2e-tests, android-e2e-tests), each entry with its kind and why it was picked ("focused", "changed", "depends on <pkg> (pnpm)", "packages: <dir> changed"). Package tests follow pnpm's dependency graph and e2e tests their headers, which stays internal. Deploy's `test-plan` job writes it once (`--github-output`: the plan and each stage's count); each stage job is skipped at count 0 and otherwise runs `wf test run --stage <stage> --plan <file>`: one `pnpm --filter ... run <script>` per kind, every kind even after a red one, or the e2e tests through their harness. Before this the service and browser jobs picked tests two different ways (pnpm graph vs headers), which read as two rules for one question. Black-box tests: `cli_test_plan` (4).
- Decision (17 Oct): G1, G2 and G3 run in the browser e2e job too. Their scripts already run on both harnesses (one deep link to the first screen, checked on the web export 6/6), so their headers say `platform: android, browser` and the plan lists each in both e2e stages. Goal p6 (browser green) read hand-written `docs/runbooks/.results/*.json`; it now reads the published runs like p1, p2, p3 and p5 (`wf test results --query "... platform=browser newest units"`), requiring every current unit to pass at or after the last app change. G1's deployed planner is shown by its base: the dev Metro, whose app devBridge builds with EXPO_PUBLIC_AGENT_BASE_URL at remote-chat-agent-prod. The cost is the browser runs on each app push (browser voice-edit took 195 s on 16 Oct, mostly backend waits).
- Decision (17 Oct): goal p0 (one healthy device) and p4 (device up only while testing) no longer conflict. The Android e2e job checks the device's health (`wf android features`: booted, activity service, restart proven, at least 40 packages disabled) right after `wf android up` and before the app's cold start, so no test runs on an unhealthy device. p0 reads the live device when it is up; when it is down (no rdl container) it requires that health step in the workflow and a passing cold start (warm-app) published in the last two days.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): one `plan` job decides both which tests run (`wf test plan`) and what ships (`wf ship --changed`); test-plan and deploy-plan were two jobs paying checkout and setup twice. The deploy legs now need the plan and the two test jobs directly, so a red test still deploys nothing. The plan's base is `last-green`, the newest commit on dev whose CI passed (gh run list), not the push's `before`: run 585 (G1-G3 in the browser) was cancelled by GitHub while queued, and run 586 then planned only its own diff and skipped them. A red run's tests also run again on the next push.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): every push compiles its Rust once per purpose, never twice at the same time. vm-ops-api's compose file set `TRUNK_POLL_MS=0` to leave deploys to CI, but the server read it as `Number(TRUNK_POLL_MS || 60000)`, so 0 meant every minute: the trunk deployer kept building the same images as CI's deploy legs, alongside CI's unit tests, on a VM whose runner sees 2 CPUs at a load of 9 (run 589: unit tests 25 min, deploy legs 16 to 30 min each). `trunkPollMs` now reads 0 as off (unit-tested); `wf ship vm-ops-api` still asks it for a build on demand.
- Correction (17 Oct): the trunk-poll entry above was wrong. `TRUNK_POLL_MS` is the string "0", which `||` keeps, so vm-ops-api's dev poll was already off (its log says `trunk_deploy.disabled`); `trunkPollMs` only makes that explicit. What starved CI was a second device: the resident `android-emulator` compose app (redroid 540x960, restart unless-stopped) ran around the clock beside the on-demand `rdl`, at 127 % CPU on a VM whose runner sees 2 CPUs (load 19, 530 MB free).
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): `android-emulator` is retired: its compose dir, vm-ops-api app entry, main.tf app-list entry (instance metadata is ignore_changes, so no recreate) and secret group are removed, and its containers stopped through vm-ops-api's retire route. `rdl` is the one device, up only during the Android e2e job.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): unit tests give way to as few, as meaningful flow tests as possible, for the Rust services and the CLIs too: each test enters through the program's main entry point (the router `main` builds, or wf's top-level dispatch) with every external dependency doubled and injected, like the frontend flow tests; the code is refactored for that injection. One flow binary per crate, in the default test profile, under a `test:flow` script, so the flow-tests stage runs them all. reporting-service: 22 Rust and 39 TS tests became 2 flows; watchdog: 17 Rust and 19 TS became 2 (8 test binaries to 1, incremental compile 9.5 s to 3.3 s).
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): CI runs the cheap, decisive checks first: `plan` -> `flow-tests` -> `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` -> `clis` (the wf build) -> deploys -> e2e. The plan and the two test jobs run the wf the previous push published (`install-wf` with `current: "true"`), so a change to the planner itself takes effect from the next push.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): the VM is recreated only by `wf vm terraform apply --recreate`, never a raw `terraform -replace`. The raw replace recreated the VM at 19:16 but left the old nodes' tailnet records holding the bare hostnames, so every service came back as `<name>-1` and vm-gateway, CI and wf could not reach them. `--recreate` checks the state holds the VM, destroys it, deletes every tailnet record its nodes left (`retire_vm_records`), then creates it again (retrying while OCI still counts the old boot volume); `wf vm terraform exec` refuses a `-replace` of `oci_core_instance.main` and names `--recreate`.
- Fix (owner, 17 Oct): wf's free-tier guard still used the old A1 pool of 4 OCPU / 24 GB; it now uses 2 / 12, the pool since 2026-06-15 (`infra/oracle-cloud/always-free-quota.tf`). The VM never left the free tier: it was sized 2 / 12 and OCI's compartment quota caps A1 at 2 / 12 regardless of wf. The guard now counts what a replaced or resized instance holds as freed (else every in-place change of a pool-filling VM would be blocked), and `--recreate` runs the same check on the configured VM before it destroys anything; before, its create was a plain targeted `terraform apply` with no wf check.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): `--recreate` is destroy, tailnet cleanup, then the same guarded `apply` as every other change (free-tier check, state snapshot, convergence check, rollback), instead of its own targeted `terraform apply`. Every guarded apply now passes the VM inputs (bootstrap token, ADB password, deploy key) and retries a QuotaExceeded create, so the two paths cannot drift.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): a recreate brings the VM up with no hand step. cloud-init no longer keeps its own app list and secret sets (they had drifted: no vnc-auth-session or reporting-service, no GitHub token for the runner, no vault URL); it reads infra/oracle-cloud/vm-apps.json and wf's clis/wf/src/secrets/groups.json, the same files Terraform and `wf secret sync-env` read, and appends .env.local like a redeploy. Images: Docker Hub stays the source (a fresh VM has no images, and sha tags are the rollback); every dev-head build now moves `latest`, which cloud-init pulls. Terraform: 19 resources that existed outside the state were imported, the budget alert email moved to the vault, and the E2.1 rescue host (OCI refuses it here) sits behind var.rescue_host, so a full guarded apply converges.
- Decision (owner, 17 Oct): every deploy goes through CI, never by hand (`wf ship` from a session included). Commit tags steer a run: `[ci: <jobs>]` runs only the named test jobs (flow, unit, service, browser, android, any combination); `[deploy: <services>]` deploys those even when their code did not change (a rotated secret). plan, the wf build and changed services' deploys always run. A run with `[ci: ...]` is still a green base for the next plan; tag only when the skipped jobs are known not to be affected.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): flow tests test the whole service through its main entry point, with only external dependencies doubled (the 17 Oct shape stands); unit tests cover the business-logic layer. A rule restricting flow tests to the application layer (f8e92737f) misread the ask and was removed the same night, before any test changed. Every service follows the domain/application/infrastructure/api folder layout; vm-ops-api (TypeScript) moves to it and joins the layout and import-direction checks.
- Fix (18 Oct): the pnpm store cache had been a 0-byte object since 13 Sep (concurrent background saves shared one /tmp archive; curl then PUT an empty body; uploads were also cut at 300 s), so every fresh Metro installed cold, and once was killed (exit 137). Saves now use their own archive, gzip-test before upload and before extract, stream with curl -T, and get 30 min.
- Decision (18 Oct): CI deploys vm-ops-api first, not last. The other legs deploy through it, so a change to how it redeploys (17 Oct's `up -d` that recreates a sidecar whose config changed) reaches them in the same run instead of needing a second push; its self-build waits until it serves the commit, so the next leg calls the new one. Considered: keep it last and push a `[deploy: …]` follow-up each time; rejected as a two-push dance for every deploy-path change.
- Fix (18 Oct): Android stubs reset any connection to a stubbed port that predates the DNAT rule (a filter-table REJECT for connections without the DNAT state). With the app reused across units (16 Oct), Expo Go's keep-alive connection to the leetcode backend outlived the rule and Run Local reached the live backend. Considered: a cold start for stubbed tests (pays the 14-18 s start again and hides the case from reused runs); rejected.
- Decision (18 Oct): Android goldens are accepted from CI runs, not recorded on a device by hand: `wf test goldens <runId>` copies a published run's named screenshots (every `device:screenshot(name)` is already in the bundle with its sidecar) into docs/runbooks/goldens/<harness>/ for review and commit. Considered: a CI dispatch with `--update-goldens` (writes only on the runner's checkout, which nothing commits) and a hand-held device run (no hand device access, by the owner's rule).
- Fix (18 Oct): the Metro deploy leg runs last.
