At 99aa0efce Metro bundled for Android and was then OOM-killed ("Killed" in its log) while vm-ops-api's self-build ran in the next leg, so every Android and browser unit met a dead Metro; the Android job waited 20 min on it before saying the app "changed since Metro's commit". After every image build Metro bundles alone, and `wf test run` now names a dead Metro after 5 min (`WF_METRO_DOWN_S`) with the way back (`[deploy: mla-tamagui]`). Considered: lowering the builds' OOM priority (agent-shell's compose is not CI-deployed, so it would not persist through CI).
- Correction and fix (18 Oct): the 99aa0efce Metro death was not the build beside it. At b44e9c1bc Metro was the only leg and died the same way: its container's 1536m cap (set 13 Oct for an Android-only Metro) cannot hold the web graph on top of the Android one, and the browser job now loads the web bundle (it never reached Metro while the device-frame page was missing). The cap is 3584m with Metro's V8 heap at 3072 MB below it, and the Metro deploy builds the web bundle too and fails if Metro does not survive both. Metro-last stays: it is still the leg with the memory peak.
- Fix (18 Oct): stubbed-run-local's "12 / 12" was the outcome editor-typing left on the reused app's Two Sum screen, read before the stubbed run answered (zero packets on every stub rule; the before-run screenshot shows it). The script now waits for the stubbed 3 / 3. Two wrong turns on the way: the keep-alive reset rule (kept, harmless) and an IPv6 refuse rule (reverted: the VM kernel has no ip6tables filter table, and its failure left the IPv4 rules on the device, which broke every later unit's backend calls). A stub setup that fails now clears what it added.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): time limits everywhere, sized to measured runs. CI took 2 h a push because failing e2e units each burned their whole budget (up to 300-3600 s, plus a 60 s kill grace) one after another: browser 76 min, Android 35 min, the rest about 5 min together. Now: unit timeouts near 2-3x a green run (editor-typing, tamil-writing, voice-edit and app-routes 180 s, as browser typing keeps its human 150-270 ms per key, stubbed-run-local 60 s, default 180 s; green Android units take 16-48 s), a 15 s grace, three failing units in a row skip the rest of the job (`WF_TEST_MAX_FAILS`), and job caps of 30 min (e2e), 15 (flow), 25 (typecheck, lint, unit), 30 per deploy leg, 10 (service e2e).
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): focus alone picks the e2e tests CI runs. Every focused test runs on every push, whatever changed, and only focused ones run; the `packages:` headers are gone, so a change to wf (the harness) no longer runs every e2e test by itself. The only commit tag left is `[ci: <stages>]`, which narrows the test jobs; `[deploy: <services>]` is gone (a dead Metro is always a changed leg, so the plan restarts it), and the plan and deploy plan always run. Also: a tap by test id now finds and presses its view in one call on the device (uiautomator2's click(selector)), so a layout that moves between a lookup and a tap cannot make it miss; and a job checks once that the dev Metro serves it, then only that Metro's commit is unchanged (one status call a unit instead of two git fetches, about 5 s a unit).
- Incident (18 Oct, CI run at 0150f765c): the Android job never finished and the runner went offline. Timeline from the container logs: the VM had 10572 of 11927 MB in use at 13:44 (agent-shell heartbeat), before the device started at 13:45; the device pushed it over, `docker info` in agent-shell-dind stopped answering within 10 s, and at 13:51:26 the watchdog recreated agent-dind with `docker compose up -d --force-recreate agent-dind`. Without `--no-deps` compose also recreated its dependency, the tailscale sidecar, and every container sharing that sidecar's network (the CI runner, `app`, `sessions`) was left in a dead namespace: the runner logged "Resource temporarily unavailable (broker.actions.githubusercontent.com)" at 13:56 and never reconnected; GitHub failed the job at 14:00:43 with "Take the device down" unrun. Decision: the watchdog recreates with `--no-deps`, and recreates any running container whose network container is gone (reason `orphaned_network`), so a stranded runner comes back within one sweep; a backend/watchdog change now plans the vm-ops-api leg (it ships in that compose; it did not before). The Android job checks the VM has 2000 MB available before the device starts and fails at once with the stats otherwise, and the browser job stops its daemon at the end so Chromium does not hold memory through the Android job. Open: about 6 GB of anonymous memory sits in containers docker cannot sample (they read 0 MB in /stats because their network is gone); a recreate of agent-shell is needed to bring the runner back and to free it, and auto mode refused that by-hand redeploy, so it waits for the owner.
- Browser warnings (18 Oct, CI at 0150f765c): editor-typing part 1 failed on `props.pointerEvents is deprecated`, raised by gluestack's overlay host (`OverlayView` in `@gluestack-ui/core/overlay/aria/Portal`, behind the Reset dialog), not by our AlertDialog; part 3 failed on `"shadow*" style props are deprecated`, raised by sonner-native's toast elevation style when Run Local shows "All tests passed". Both patched (pnpm patches: pointerEvents into style; the toast gets a `boxShadow`, on every platform). Part 2's "recording was not written" carried no frame count, so it came from a daemon started by an earlier job's wf: the browser job now stops any daemon before its runs and again when it ends. Verified with a local web export: part 1 recorded and published (run-59c8565e5832c636, recording read back, 93 KB) with no pointerEvents warning; the export itself raised React #419 (a Suspense boundary that fails to prerender), which the dev Metro does not, so parts 1 and 3 are confirmed on the next CI run, not locally.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): an e2e test should run only when the feature code it drives changed. The Nx plan's N4 is decided that way: the app's in-tree features (leetcode, tamil-writing) become packages (phase F), each e2e script names its features, and an Nx target per test caches a pass keyed by those packages, the app shell, the lockfile and patches, its backends and the device; wf stays out of the inputs. F and N4 come right after the N0 spike, because the e2e jobs are about 20 of a CI run's 35 minutes.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): Nx comes back (it was removed in greenfield P5 for plain pnpm) as the cache under every CI test stage, with no fallback: `wf test run --stage` hands each stage to `nx run-many` (nx 23.2.1, MIT, no Nx Cloud, no daemon) and fails at once when the workspace has no nx. Projects: the pnpm packages, the root, the in-app features leetcode and tamil-writing as nested projects (`feature-*`, the shell not a dependency), every crate outside a pnpm package (`crate-*`, with its path dependencies across workspaces) and one project per e2e test (`tooling/nx/e2e-tests.js`). An e2e test is keyed by its test case (script, its platform's goldens, the Android warm-up), the features it names (`features:`) with their libraries, the app shell's own files and libraries, its backends (`backends:`, with their crates), the lockfile and patches; wf is not an input. Only passes are cached (Nx 23 caches failures only with NX_CACHE_FAILURES), run flags always run fresh, and `--probe` tells the Android job whether to boot the device at all. Contract: `clis/wf/tests/flow/nx_cache.rs` runs the real Nx on a copy of the repo with the work doubled, 30 cases (19 e2e edits on both harnesses, 5 package and 11 crate edits, a failed run, run flags, and every edit put back) in about 2.5 min; the plan schedules it whenever a file that shapes the graph changes. Found while building it: pnpm-inferred targets lose plugin inputs to targetDefaults, so crates inside a pnpm package stay that package's files; a workspace's lockfile is an input of its own crates, not a dependency, or clis/Cargo.lock would rerun the backend services; Nx puts node_modules/.bin (with its own wf) first on PATH, so a test's command runs the stage's wf by path.
- E2e unit time (18 Oct, 59 passing Android units of the last day, 1,780 s): `appears` took 637 s (36%), and 201 of its 325 calls were misses that waited out their timeout. Three always miss on Android and are now skipped or made instant: the web-only EditorHelperRails autofocus check in editor-typing (3 s a unit, 10 of 10 missed) and voice-edit (3 s a unit, 24 of 24), and Tamil's developer-menu check before every stroke, now a `find` instead of a 0.5 s wait (102 of 102 missed, 1.1 s each). About 75 s off a full Android run; to be confirmed on the next CI run. Left as is: the microphone dialog check (3.5 s a voice unit; the dialog does appear once per device boot).
- Correction to the entry above (18 Oct, CI at 77078012d): the Android rails check was not only a miss, it was the time a reused app needed to apply the unit's link. A link to the problem screen the app already shows (a later part or chunk, another seed) remounts it a moment later, so the first wait passed on the old screen: editor-typing part 3 raised the keyboard on it, voice-edit chunks 2, 4, 6 and 8 failed (each odd chunk ran after a cold warm-up and passed), and stubbed-run-local ran Run Local on the old screen (12 of 12 from the live backend, then the remount cleared it). Fixed by leaving the screen first: when the problem screen is on screen, the script opens home and waits for it to go, then opens its link (about 1 s, only when needed). Not the 3 s wait back: it hid the race without closing it.
- Browser recordings empty in CI (18 Oct, every unit since the runner's Playwright moved to Chromium 151; the sandbox runs 141): Chromium 151 never answers the first `Page.captureScreenshot` of a fresh page, and the recorder waited out the 60 s call timeout, so a unit under its 60 s limit had no frame and `close` held the run until it was killed. Reproduced in the sandbox with Playwright's Chromium 151: a 74 s run kept 10.7 s of video. A capture is now given up after 5 s (`Cdp::call_within`), and the next one lands: the same run keeps 66 s. Contract: the cdp flow test with a browser double that never answers the first capture.
- Browser voice-edit never passed against the dev Metro (18 Oct): the Metro serves the app over plain http on a tailnet name, so the page is not a secure context and has no `navigator.mediaDevices`; the recorder's permission check fails and the app sits on a 00:00 recording bar whose Send does nothing (the export passed because 127.0.0.1 is secure). Chromium now counts the shared Metro's origin as secure (`--unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure`, from `WF_SHARED_METRO_URL`), as Expo Go on a phone has no such limit; checked in the sandbox over the tailnet: the turn records, transcribes and applies. The app's silent stall when the microphone is refused is a separate app fix (a recording bar with no recorder).
- Browser editor-typing's mid-line caret (18 Oct, two-sum part 2): `press("ctrl-end")` reached the page as a key named "Control+End" with no Ctrl held, so CodeMirror saw no shortcut and the caret stayed where the tap left it; a short last line hid it. The key now goes out as End with the Ctrl modifier (cdp flow test), and a seeded web editor, already focused from opening, is no longer lowered and tapped again before typing. Probe: the typed marker lands at offset 90 of 90 instead of 16.
- CI never planned a crate's flow test (found 18 Oct): `wf test plan` listed crates with `cargo metadata`, the plan job has no Rust toolchain, and a failed call was skipped, so wf's own flow suite (the Nx cache contract among it), cdp's and rca-core's never ran in CI. The plan now reads the Cargo.toml files itself (the `toml` crate already in the clis workspace), and an unreadable manifest fails the plan instead of dropping crates. The pipeline flow test plans with a `cargo` that refuses to run. CI's flow job grows by the crates a push reaches.
- Browser voice-edit's last failure (18 Oct, CI at 5c16073de: every turn now records, transcribes and applies): react-native-web's "props.pointerEvents is deprecated" warning on the first turn. A page warning now carries the functions that raised it (`(at warnOnce < createDOMProps < View < ...)`), which showed a View rendered by a forwardRef but not its owner; the review sheet is the first thing a turn mounts, and @gorhom/bottom-sheet passes pointerEvents as a View prop in its backdrop, hosting container and background. A fourth patch moves it into style, as the sonner-native, wheel-picker and gluestack patches did; verified by the next CI run (the dev Metro ships the patched package). Also: voice-edit's Done swipe repeats until Done is on screen (one swipe left it at x 436 of 393 in three sandbox runs).
- Leaner e2e keys (owner, 18 Oct: "backend services are already catered for by the service e2e tests"; "make this leaner"). An e2e test's hash is now its script and data, its goldens (and the warm-up on Android), the app shell's own files (mla-tamagui without its features), and `^production` of the features it names, which reaches exactly the libraries and npm packages those features import (Nx hashes each npm package at its locked version with its patch hash). Gone: the `backends:` header (voice-edit reran for remote-chat-agent and for every rca-core edit), the blanket list of the app's `@lib`/`@app` dependencies (a library only the shell imports now reruns only the whole-app tests), and the lockfile, `patches/` and workspace globals (`production` no longer carries `sharedGlobals`; the lockfile and `patches/` left `sharedGlobals`, since every task already hashes the npm packages it reaches). Contract suite: 23 edit cases plus a re-patched sonner-native (reruns only the tests that load it, not Tamil's); its workspace copy now carries pnpm's relative package links, without which Nx could not resolve an in-app feature's `@lib` imports.
- Crate flow tests beside the package flows (18 Oct): once the plan saw the crates, CI's flow job ran the package flows (4 min when nx.json changed) and then the crates one at a time (wf's 94 flow tests, about 6 min with the build) and hit its 10-minute step twice, the Nx contract suite passing inside it. The flow call now runs `test:flow,cargo-flow` together at parallel 4: cargo serialises crates that share clis/target, and the packages build in backend/target, so the stage takes about the longer of the two instead of their sum. The step limit stays.
- Lean deploy legs (owner, 18 Oct: "make the deploy legs lean too, vnc-auth-session ships every push"; chose both: a leg keyed on what its image is built from, and lockfiles counted per service). vnc-auth-session shipped every push because its listener pulled in `clis/rca-core` (for one date formatter, now `wf::time::iso`) and its leg listed `backend/Cargo.lock`, and every Rust leg shipped on any lockfile edit. `wf ship --changed` now plans a leg when its pnpm package (or one it depends on) changed, ignoring tests and Markdown (pnpm's `--changed-files-ignore-pattern`), when one of its own extra paths changed, or when a lockfile entry its own dependency closure reaches moved (`clis/wf/src/lock_closure.rs`: pnpm-lock.yaml from the app's importer through its snapshots, patch hashes included; backend/Cargo.lock from the service's own crates). Each leg says why, and `--since <sha>` replaces every running commit for a dry run. The watchdog, which ships in vm-ops-api's compose project, now counts too: a service-kit or backend lockfile change it builds from used to leave it stale. Nx `affected` was tried first and rejected for this: any nx.json edit and any `{workspaceRoot}` input marks every project, and it has no notion of a Cargo.lock closure. Measured: since 77078012d the plan went from every leg to vnc-auth-session (the cdp capture fix it compiles) and mla-tamagui (the bottom-sheet patch re-resolved 40 packages it bundles). Contract: `clis/wf/tests/flow/deploy_legs.rs` commits each edit type on a copy of the repo and checks the legs (flow, real git and pnpm).
- Tamil word unit over a minute (18 Oct, CI at 77078012d: word-uyir-aa 68.4 s): its first attempt traced the previous unit's word. The reused app still showed word-uyir-a's pad, the link to word-uyir-aa remounted the screen a moment later, and the trace followed the old template (the ink reads அம்மா over the ஆடு guide; the judge answered NOT YET in 30 s, the second attempt passed). The same stale-screen race the editor, voice and stubbed scripts already handle: `open_route` now leaves for Settings when the screen it opens is already up, then opens the link.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): no binary-keyed deploys. The idea was to build a Rust leg, compare its compiled binary with the running one and skip the restart when they match. A cache-hot leg takes about 60 s in all (vnc-auth-session: 55 to 68 s over the last runs), the build is what costs and it must run to know, and a skipped restart leaves the running image on its old commit, so every later push would plan the leg again. The lean planner (legs keyed on what each image is built from) stays the only filter.
- Tamil word template in the marker's font (18 Oct, browser CI at cc5f5078b: word-uyir-i NOT YET three times, 65 to 70). The ink followed the template exactly; the template was wrong. A word's template is printed as text, and with no font named the browser used its own Tamil font, whose ை is one curl, while remote-chat-agent draws the reference in the bundled Noto Sans Tamil, where ை is two arches. Android passed because its system Tamil font is Noto. The template now uses a `$tamil` font token (Noto Sans Tamil, loaded with expo-font from the bundled file) and is drawn only once the font is ready, so a trace never follows another shape. The flow harness stubs expo-font (jsdom lays out no glyphs); a flow test asserts the word template carries the font, and a local Metro run showed இலை drawn with the two-arch ை.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): every feature lives in `frontend/mla-tamagui/features/`. The four feature packages and `@lib/leetcode-contracts` (now `features/leetcode-contracts`, beside the in-app `leetcode` it describes) moved there and stay separate workspace packages, so Nx and pnpm keep hashing them on their own: each file belongs to the innermost package or project. The app's typecheck, lint and unit tests skip the package directories (`features/packages.mjs`, and `tsconfig.json` by name); each package checks itself as before. Their shared configs went to `frontend/feature-kit` (an Nx project of its own): left inside the app they made every package depend on the app project, and a Tamil edit reran voice-edit (the Nx contract caught it). The re-link also let the settings package resolve the app's own expo-router, whose typed routes rejected two `string` hrefs; they are `Href` now. Measured: the Nx and deploy contracts, and typecheck, lint, unit and flow of the five packages and the app, all green. Next (owner): the frontend-only libraries move to `frontend/`, each still its own package.
- Decision (owner, 18 Oct): the TypeScript frontend libraries live in `frontend/libraries/`, each still its own package so Nx keeps hashing them apart: app-config, app-di, flow-test-harness, gesture-geometry, logging, registry-service, rn-agentation, tamagui-design-system, voice-edit-dsl (every one used only by the app, its feature packages or each other). The libraries the backend, the CLIs or the agent hooks use stay in `libraries/` (browser-client, eslint-config-node-ts, eslint-plugin-mobile-learn, hook-runtime, secrets, vm-request-auth). The lockfile change is a pure path rewrite (importer keys and `link:` paths, 34 lines): letting pnpm re-resolve the moved importers changed 30 peer variants and gave the app two react-native type copies its typecheck rejected. Measured: Nx typecheck, lint, unit and flow for all 35 projects (82 tasks), wf's 95 flow tests and the Nx and deploy contracts, all green.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct): one Rust logging crate, `clis/telemetry`, with as much as possible inferred. It replaces service-kit's subscriber and redactor, `wf`'s OTLP exporter (`log_export.rs`), `wf log emit`'s own scrubber and Node `util.inspect` echo, and the secrets CLI's JSON POST to the app's legacy BetterStack source. A caller names its service once; the crate's version (compiled in), the commit (`GIT_COMMIT`), host and OS ride as resource attributes, and every record carries `code.filepath` and `code.lineno` (services also `code.namespace`) from the call site, through `tracing` metadata or `#[track_caller]`. `wf log emit` now echoes a JSON line, the same shape as the listener's console lines, and logs to the console even with no destination (it said "console only" and printed nothing). The secrets CLI's events now go to the backend and CLI source (`BETTERSTACK_OTLP_*`), as the registry says that source is for. Not done: TypeScript bindings to the crate (Expo Go cannot load native code; a WASM logger is slower than the JS one on the web).
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct): the caveat guard is removed ("I don't think that is useful anymore"): `wf caveat`, the Stop and `session.deleted` hooks behind `.agents/tmp/caveat-guard.on`, the `clis/caveat-guard` plugin with the repo's plugin marketplace and the `agent-plugins` workflow that only validated it, and its runbook. Its JS string helpers live on as `clis/wf/src/js.rs` for the knowledge and worker code.
- CI red on dev at e13675ddd (19 Oct): `@lib/flow-test-harness` typecheck could not find `react-native-gesture-handler` or `react-native-safe-area-context`; its tests imported both without declaring them, so they resolved only through hoisting, which the CI install did not give the moved package. Both are now its devDependencies at the app's versions (7a2570ac4).
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct): the TypeScript logger keeps only what the app uses, and nothing about a call site is passed by hand. `Logger.*` already carries the file, line, function, enclosing chain and `testID` from the Babel plugin, so `createLogger('Module')`, the one API that made a caller name its own module, is gone (no production code called it). Also gone, each with no consumer: the Node layer (`@lib/logging/node`: OTel providers, BetterStack transport, heartbeat, HTTP spans; the Rust services that once used it log through `clis/telemetry`), the remote forwarder and its `queueForRemote` stage, the rate limiter, the sprintf and filter plugins, and 14 dependencies (twelve `@opentelemetry/*`, two `@loglayer/plugin-*`). `Logger.event` is gone too: the app never wired its event sink, so every one of its nine calls was dropped. The five that fire rarely (websocket hello, an alternative blocked or dismissed, the keyboard toolbar's height, a web view blur) are now `Logger.info` or `Logger.debug` lines; the four on hot paths (editor content and selection per keystroke, toolbar scroll, web view inject) are removed rather than turned on. The root rule in CLAUDE.md now says `Logger.*` instead of `createLogger('Module')`.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct: "throw an error if it doesn't work and not do a dumbass fallback"): `logger emit` fails loudly. It prints the scrubbed line (console transport) and sends it (BetterStack) at once; when the line cannot be sent, for want of a destination or because the ingest refused it, it exits 1 with the reason. The code lives once in `clis/telemetry` (`cli` module and a static `logger` bin); `wf log emit` is a thin wrapper. vm-ops-api and agent-shell's heartbeat called a bun-compiled `logger` from `nuesslerm/logger-cli:latest` whose TypeScript source had been deleted, so the image could not be rebuilt; both now call the VM's `wf log emit`. `infra/oracle-cloud/logger-cli` builds the Rust `logger` for cloud-init's host copy. `cloud-init.sh.tftpl` is untouched, because editing it changes the instance's user_data.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct: "just add some logs into each service and add a couple of regular e2e tests to the existing tests"): no logging-proof verb. A `wf vm logs prove` verb was built, then removed again. Every service now writes one `http.request` line per request inside the request's span (`service_kit::trace::log_request`), and reporting-service's own request line is gone. Each service has one regular deployed e2e test, `rs/tests/e2e_deployed.rs`, run by `test:e2e:deployed` in CI's service-e2e stage: request the deployed service in a fresh W3C trace, poll BetterStack's backend logs table for that trace's line, and check the inferred attributes. Proven locally on 19 Oct: leetcode-backend run in this session with the real BetterStack credentials; its line was found in 16 s with every attribute. The browser's existing editor-typing test now opens the debug sheet and checks the editor's `editor.focus` line reached it; the app forwards nothing off the device.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct: "anonymised usage logs to see what commands get the bulk of usage and also log errors", chose an OCI table over BetterStack): every wf command posts one row to `HEALTH_RPT.WF_USAGE`: command path, exit code, duration, the last `wf:` error line (scrubbed, home as `~`), wf version, OS; never the host, the arguments or a key name. The table was created on 19 Oct with REST-enabled SQL as ADMIN from `infra/oracle-cloud/sql/wf_usage.sql`. `WF_USAGE_ORDS_BASE_URL` is in the registry, the vault, agent-shell's group and its compose. A detached wf posts the row; unset configuration and a failed post are both printed. ORDS inserts NULL for an absent column, overriding TS's default, so the row carries `ts`. The first real rows were `vm logs` (exit 1, its error scrubbed) and `build-sha` (exit 0). The secrets CLI's BetterStack events, which named keys, are removed.
- Decision (19 Oct, CI run 39226632372 at `c89e35355`): the flow step limit goes from 10 to 20 minutes and the flow job's from 15 to 30, reversing "the step limit stays" of 18 Oct. Evidence: the VM has 2 OCPUs (`nproc` 2 in agent-shell; `vm_ocpus` 2 is the whole Always-Free pool), so vitest runs the app's flow files on one worker. A push that changes `clis/telemetry` (every service and wf build it) and the app's logging library runs everything cold. mla-tamagui's 22 flow files took 591 s on CI against 90 s on a 4-core sandbox. wf built in 2 min 37 s, and its two `nx_cache` contract tests were still running at the limit (157 s on the sandbox). Five service crates rebuilt beside them. The step timed out at 10 min on `98eefaf39` and again on `c89e35355`; the last green run before them (`7a2570ac4`, wf already built) spent about 9 min in the step. A pass is cached by Nx, so only cold pushes are slow. Rejected: re-running to finish on the cache; it is green once, and red again on the next cold push.
- Fix (19 Oct): `wf <command> --help` and other help-served commands end before the env files are read, so `~/.config/wf/.env`'s usage keys were missed and the command printed "usage row not recorded". `usage::record` now reads the env files when a key is missing; the usage flow test has the case.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct: "can we just delete all the old code please"): code the Rust `wf` replaced is deleted, not kept beside it. Evidence for why it mattered: `service-e2e-tests` at `5fa8024a7` timed out because `@cli/workflow-cli`'s `bin/wf` shim shadowed the CI-built wf inside every `pnpm run` (`node_modules/.bin` comes first on PATH); with cargo on PATH the shim compiled its own release wf (about 6.5 min on 2 OCPU), then `wf secret get CH_BACKEND_HOST` took 408 s. Three callers still used the retired `secrets` noun: `@lib/secrets` (`wf secrets env`, rca's TS e2e helper), `backend/service-kit/src/secrets.rs` (the vault fallback of every deployed Rust service, so it had answered "unknown command" in production; now `secret env --keys`, with `tests/flow_secrets.rs`) and the EAS publish script's token fallback (removed: `wf ship eas` hands the token in). Deleted: `clis/workflow-cli`, `libraries/secrets`, `libraries/vm-request-auth` (nothing imported it), `clis/secrets/package.json` and its setup script (the registry stays), the unused jsonquery patch and postman-collection override, two agent-shell smoke scripts and its bun install, six ESLint rules no config enabled (invalid-regex, required-regex, no-tap-at-point, no-by-text-interaction, no-device-sleep, require-span-coverage), the playwright and workflow-cli tiers of `wf report run`, the `workflow-cli`, `ds-proposal` and `metro-concurrent-debugging` skills and the `enrich-leetcode` command. 140 docs about removed code moved to `docs/archive/` (115 solutions, 23 runbooks, 1 gap, 1 tool doc), references rewritten. A first pass also archived 38 open gaps, `docs/known-gaps.md` and 16 docs that live code cites; all were restored the same day: a gap is debt that can survive a rewrite, and whether it did is a question for the code, not for the archive. Each restored doc was then checked against the repository by an independent reviewer and a skeptic; several gaps turned out fixed by the Rust ports (the rca diff route now requires `X-Bot-Secret` and compares whole path components, `backend/remote-chat-agent/rs/src/api/routes/diff.rs:378,428`). Their dead paths were re-pointed to the Rust code file by file (`wf offload ask --batch`, one cheap-model dispatch per file), each checked by the guard below.
- Decision (19 Oct): `wf check --dead-refs`, run by `wf check --staged` in the pre-commit gate, fails when a live file names a repo path, a relative markdown link, a workspace package (`@cli|lib|app|feature|tooling/…`), a `wf` command (in code, in fenced blocks and in backticks) or a `pnpm run` script that does not exist. History (`docs/archive`, `docs/audits`, `docs/plans`, `docs/goals`), generated agent config, tests and fixtures are not read. It is the reverse of knip: knip finds code nothing uses; this finds text that names code nobody kept. 0.6 s on the tree. The first full run found 1363 references to code that was gone, including the README's `wf metro status` and three steps of `architecture.yml` calling scripts mla-tamagui does not have.
- Fix (19 Oct): `backend/service-kit` had no package.json, so no CI stage ran its flow tests (rate limit, telemetry, and now the secrets fallback). It is `@lib/service-kit` with a `test:flow` script.
- Fix (19 Oct): the wf flow harness passed the developer's real HOME to every `wf` it ran, so `~/.config/wf/.env` (the usage keys, `HEALTH_REPORTS_ORDS_PASSWORD`) leaked into the health-report and secrets-list tests and failed them in a sandbox that has the file. Each run now gets an empty temp HOME (cargo and rustup homes kept); a test that wants a user env file still passes its own HOME.
- Plan (owner, 19 Oct): sandbox-first verification (flow, browser, lint and typecheck run in the sandbox and replay on CI from one shared Nx cache, still running there whatever the sandbox missed; a drift report and contract test keep the two hashing alike; the pre-commit gate runs through Nx), an EAS Update on CI before the Android tests and Android against it, and BetterStack crash reports and logs instead of breakpoints on the device. Spec: `docs/plans/sandbox-first-ci/spec.md`; nothing switches before its benchmark.
- Fix (19 Oct, incident): a cheap-model dispatch of the re-pointing batch (`wf offload ask --batch`, the `mla-tamagui-sanity` runbook) ran `git stash; wf check --dead-refs …; git stash pop` in the shared checkout to compare against HEAD. The pop restored the worktree but not the index, so every staged rename of the sweep came back as an addition plus an unstaged deletion, and for a second every other in-flight dispatch saw HEAD. Nothing was lost (`wf check --dead-refs` still 0, the stash list unchanged), but opencode had no limit on such commands: its permission map was empty. `.agents/config/permissions.json` now denies opencode `git stash*`, `git reset*`, `git checkout*`, `git restore*`, `git switch*`, `git clean*`, `git rebase*` and `git push*`; the sync contract accepts a per-tool pattern map (flow test in `clis/agent-config-sync/tests/flow.rs`). Probe, n=1: asked to run `git stash list`, MiniMax-M3 got opencode's "a rule prevents this tool call" and the command did not run (52 s, $0.006).
- Fix (19 Oct, CI run 39307044623 at `dcab33aae`): the flow job's log went silent at 19:04:34 and the whole VM stopped answering on the tailnet (vm-ops-api `/health` timed out three times at 20 s) until about 19:31; the step then timed out at 20 minutes. The push touched comments in nearly every package, so Nx ran the flow stage cold, four tasks at a time (`wf test run` hard-coded `--parallel=4`): the wf crate's cargo build, the backend crates', and the app's and coding-agent's vitest suites at once, on 2 OCPUs and 12 GB shared with every service. `wf vm recover` found every app healthy once it came back, no reboot. `wf test run` package stages now take `--parallel <n>` (default 4, as before), and CI passes `--parallel 2` to the flow and typecheck stages. The test jobs run the wf the previous push published, so the first push after this still runs four wide; the tasks that passed before the stall replay from the Nx cache.
- Decision (owner, 19 Oct, CI run 39314824975 at `dbea00703`: "Move to deploy vault"): the four `CH_BACKEND_*` ClickHouse query keys move from the admin Bitwarden project to the deploy project. `service-e2e-tests` failed on every service with "CH_BACKEND_HOST is unset and `wf secret get CH_BACKEND_HOST` gave nothing": the registry marked them `vault: "admin"`, which resolves through the admin token only, and the VM runner holds only the deploy read token. They are read-only query credentials for the backend source, so the deploy grant is the right home; they stay out of every `--for` deploy group, so no host's `.env` gets them. The move went through a new `wf secret move <KEY> <PROJECT_ID> --apply` (one `bws secret edit --project-id` by id, dry run by default, refuses a missing or twice-named key, flow-tested), so the ids, values and notes are unchanged and nothing was deleted; the read token alone now resolves all four (exit 0, lengths 41/33/64/31, values never printed). `CH_MOBILE_*` stay admin.
- Fix (19 Oct): `wf browser debug-pause` could stop in the device frame's own code. The frame (`tooling/browser-harness/ui/src/App.tsx`) measures the page every 300 ms on the same renderer, so on a loaded sandbox (full `cargo test -p wf`, load 9) the pause landed in its `measure` and `debug-eval ticks` threw "ticks is not defined". The debugger now ignore-lists `/_mcp/` (`Debugger.setBlackboxPatterns`; Hermes, which lacks the call, is unaffected), so a pause or a step skips the harness's scripts. The flow test's loop page ticks once a second instead of every 20 ms, slower than the frame's timer: without the fix it failed 3 of 3, with it it passed 3 of 3 (about 6 s each).
- Decision (19 Oct, goal P5 "timed rerun of all three on Android with the VM sampler"): the sampler lives in the harness, not beside it. With `WF_VM_SAMPLE_SECS=<n>` a `wf android run` (or `wf browser run`) asks vm-ops-api's `/stats` every n seconds, once more at the end, and its report carries `vm` (`samples`: load 1 min, MemAvailable, the device container's memory and CPU; and their extremes), shown as a VM section on the report page. A failed sample is recorded, never fatal. The Android e2e job sets it to 20 s. The older sampler was a script beside the run whose rows never reached a report; the CI runs of 19 Oct carried none, so P5 stays open until a fresh Android run has them (the Nx cache replays unchanged tests, so the rerun is an `e2e tests` dispatch with a run flag, which always runs fresh).
- Change (19 Oct, plan item E, first step): the Android warm-up (`e2e/warm-app.lua`) waits for the app's first frame with `device:appears("id:DebugFab", 3)` instead of a find followed by a fixed `sleep(1)`, answering a Continue or Reload dialog between probes. Baseline, 34 passing warm-ups in the last 14 days: first-bundle step median 13.4 s (10.8 to 17.8 s; ten runs of 71 to 97 s waited on a cold Metro bundle). The change is kept only if the next warm-ups' median does not rise. It also starts the timed Android rerun goal P5 needs: the warm-up is part of every Android e2e test's cache key, and a dispatch of `e2e tests` is refused here (403 for the session's GitHub App, and the workflow is not on `main`), so this push reruns G1 to G3 with the VM sampler.
- Fix (19 Oct, CI run 39340494153 at `18e8db019`, the first sampled Android run): the VM samples held the host's load and MemAvailable but no device numbers, and the first sample of 22 of 23 units failed with "Interrupted system call (os error 4)". On the VM the device container `rdl` runs inside `agent-shell-dind`, which is what vm-ops-api's `/stats` lists, so the sampler now counts `agent-shell-dind` by default (image builds wait while the device is up, so it holds the device); and a sample whose call is interrupted by a signal is retried once. Numbers from that run: load peaked at 6.82 on 2 OCPUs, MemAvailable never below 2739 MB, every unit under 48 s.
- Correction (20 Oct, owner): the emulator runs only in CI. The close of the device-lab goal brought `rdl` up and down by hand (`wf android up`, the criteria, `wf android down`) so that `docs/goals/2026-10-13-device-test-process.md` a1 and a3 could ask a live device; that broke the rule. Rule added to the root instructions ("Tooling rules that bite"), followed rather than guarded (owner: no wf refusal, no test). The cause is removed: the CI warm-up (`e2e/warm-app.lua`) writes `wf android features --json` to `out/device-features.json` in its run bundle, and a1 and a3 read the newest one with the same conditions (`boot.completed`, `boot.activityService`, `restartProven`, `disabledTotal >= 40`). Spec for what follows: `docs/plans/after-device-lab/spec.md`.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab A2): CI wrote no `HEALTH_RPT.WF_USAGE` rows; every runner command said "usage row not recorded: WF_USAGE_ORDS_BASE_URL and HEALTH_REPORTS_ORDS_PASSWORD unset", because `usage::record` read only the environment and `~/.config/wf/.env`, and the runner holds only the deploy read token. When both places lack the keys, `wf` now reads them through the default backend (`secrets::load_values`, which serves the vault's sealed local copy, so a fresh copy costs no round trip). This drops the earlier "never waits on the vault" choice only where the keys are missing; a developer with them in the env file sees no change. Flow test: the usage row is posted with the keys only in a stub vault.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab A3): the flow run of 19 Oct counted 10 warnings; each is fixed where it starts, and a flow test that prints a warning now fails (`frontend/libraries/flow-test-harness/src/vitest/warningGate.ts`, installed by the harness setup, so CI's flow stage, which runs vitest directly, goes red too). Sources: `process.env.EXPO_OS` is now defined as `web` in the flow config, as babel-preset-expo inlines it for the web bundle (5 warnings); the design system's code block asked for `selectable` on a Text that renders a plain DOM node on web, and uses `userSelect` there (2); pressable SVG shapes (the uptime bars, the pie and line charts) made react-native-svg hand responder props to DOM elements through a mixin react-native-web calls deprecated, so a pnpm patch drops them on web, where the press is the onClick it already sets, and a flow test taps an uptime bar (1; a non-frozen `pnpm install` after the patch hoisted another react-native copy and broke the app's typecheck, a frozen one as CI runs it typechecks clean); the wheel picker asked react-native-web for the native animated driver (pnpm patch: the JS driver on web) (1); the WebView bridge test's deliberate throw is now caught by the page's error handler and asserted on both transports (1). The gate then surfaced warnings the report had not listed: the wheel item container spread a `key` and used the deprecated TouchableWithoutFeedback (now a Pressable), and three coding-agent flow tests let streamed updates land outside `act` (their waits are now inside it). A planted `console.warn` fails the stage (exit 1). App flow suite 78 of 78, coding-agent 28, settings 5, service-report 4, harness 63, all with zero warnings.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D, owner): oxlint 1.87.0 replaces ESLint for the React Native app, its five feature packages and the three linted frontend libraries, with one `frontend/.oxlintrc.json` and only the rules that catch what the typecheck and tests miss (keep list and what was dropped: `docs/plans/after-device-lab/spec.md` D0). The custom rules run as an oxlint JS plugin (the same `@lib/eslint-plugin-mobile-learn`, named through `meta.name`); the plugin lost 26 rules (among them `require-log-in-unit` and both `require-testid-*`) and gained `thin-route`, since oxlint has no `no-restricted-syntax`. Feature layer boundaries moved to dependency-cruiser (`frontend/feature-kit/depcruise.feature.cjs`): it resolves imports the ESLint boundaries plugin had silently skipped, and its first run showed the old default layers forbade `state -> data`, which the documented direction allows, so the default now allows it. The promise rules could not move: oxlint's type-aware mode needs TypeScript 7 and the repo is on 5.9. The pre-commit gate runs an oxlint package's own lint script instead of eslint over the staged files (flow test). D2 lint timing (ESLint against oxlint): the nine packages' `lint`, one at a time, cold cache, n=3: ESLint 232.7, 223.5, 229.5 s (median 229.5 s); oxlint plus dependency-cruiser 13.6, 13.1, 13.1 s (median 13.1 s, most of it pnpm start-up and the four dependency-cruiser runs); oxlint alone over the same files 1.4 s. Planted cases fail: each kept plugin rule (plugin test under oxlint), a restricted import, `console`, `vi.mock` in a flow test, a fat route, and a domain-to-ui import (dependency-cruiser).
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D3): ESLint is gone from the repository. The packages still on it (three backend packages, browser-client, the browser harness, wf-blackbox) held about 60 TypeScript files after the move to Rust, mostly tests, yet their cold lint took 179.5 s; their rules were largely about files that no longer exist (wf-blackbox's `src/` layers, the node-ts config's `src/**`). A root `.oxlintrc.json` lints everything outside `frontend/` (which keeps its own, nearest config): correctness, unused variables, `guarded-detached-spawn` on `clis/`, and the five test-quality rules on test files; its first run found 16 dead bits (unused imports, an unused parameter threaded through `stepsFor`, a dead `document` prop on the harness `Bezel`, needless escapes), all removed. Gone with ESLint: `eslint.config.mjs`, `@lib/eslint-config-node-ts`, wf-blackbox's config, the backend configs, eleven ESLint packages from the root and the catalog, five dead plugin dependencies (the Reanimated rules' Skia, Reanimated and redash, a scope manager, a parser), and the plugin rules `given-when-then-title` and `no-relative-imports`. The pre-commit gate runs `pnpm run --if-present lint` in each package that owns a staged file (flow test). Whole-workspace lint, one package at a time: 16.2, 14.7, 15.6 s with oxlint (the root config alone 1.5 s), against 229.5 s (frontend) plus 179.5 s (the rest) with ESLint. The plugin's oxlint test plants a case for all 14 rules it still carries.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab A5): the device harness joins the e2e cache key, reversing part of the 18 Oct lean keys. Each platform's tests now read the shared runner and Lua API (`clis/wf/src/run.rs`, `device/{mod,lua,golden}.rs`) and that platform's driver (`device/android.rs` and `android*`, or `device/browser.rs` and `browser*`); the rest of wf stays out, so a change to `main.rs` still replays every test. The Nx flow test checks both sides: the Android driver reruns the 4 Android tests and none of the browser ones, `lua.rs` reruns all 8, `main.rs` none. A commit trailer `E2E-Fresh: android` (or `browser`, `all`) runs that stage past the cache with `NX_SKIP_NX_CACHE`, read from the commit `wf test run --stage` runs at; the probe reports `fresh` so the job boots the device. It buys B3's and S0's n≥3 CI measurements without a hand run. Found on the way: Nx honours `.gitignore` only inside a git repository, so a workspace that becomes one hashes differently; the flow test commits before it seeds.
- Fix (20 Oct, after-device-lab A6): the sampler's interrupted first `/stats` call was a SIGCHLD meant for the run. glibc's `system()` (Lua's `os.execute`, which every G1 to G3 script calls at its start) and `posix_spawn` block signals in the calling thread, so a child that exits meanwhile has its SIGCHLD handed to another thread that takes it: the sampler, blocked in the `/stats` read with a timeout set (SO_RCVTIMEO), which then returns EINTR whatever the disposition. Reproduced in the sandbox with the real wf, a stub adb and a SOCKS-backed `/stats` that answers in 2 s: with the retry taken out, 6 of 6 runs of a script that shells out lost the first sample, 5 of 6 of the tamil-writing start, 2 of 6 of a script that only records; strace itself causes the same EINTR (every traced signal stops the thread), so it could only show the timing. The sampler thread now blocks SIGCHLD, SIGINT and SIGTERM (the run's main thread still takes the stop request), and the retry is gone: 0 of 18 runs. The harness flow test runs a script that shells out while a 1.5 s `/stats` is in flight; it fails without the mask (2 of 2) and passes with it (5 of 5).
- Baseline (20 Oct, after-device-lab B0): `docs/runbooks/.results/e2e-unit-timing-initial.json` holds each G1 to G3 unit's wall time per platform, the median of its newest three passing CI runs (`wf test results --query "test=editor-typing,tamil-writing,voice-edit platform=<p> since=14d"`, CI runs at `3002218c6`, `9fd10125b`, `c5fdc265e` and `95e494827`), 21 units on each platform; the three whole-test keys from before units existed are left out, since no run makes them again. It is written once and never changed: B3 compares each unit's newest three CI runs against it. Android ranges from 21.4 s (tamil consonant) to 39.8 s (tamil vowels 1-2), the browser from 13.8 s (tamil consonant) to 47.6 s (word-uyir-a).
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab B1): three wait verbs for what G1 to G3 polled for with fixed sleeps, each a probe like `appears` (true on the event, false once its seconds pass) that checks again as soon as the last check returns: `wait_keyboard(shown, secs)` (Android: the input method's mInputShown; the browser now answers `keyboard_shown` too, true while an editable element has focus, inside a WebView included), `wait_pixel(x, y, test, secs)` (one screen pixel, for what Android leaves out of the accessibility tree: the Reset dialog, the toast) and `wait_recording(secs)` (the recorder took the injected clip: Android sees the injector's `.swapped` marker newer than its start, the browser's fake microphone sees MediaRecorder start on its stream). The buffer-grown wait is the existing `wait_editor_longer`. One flow test drives all three on both drivers with stubs that change on the fourth look: each returns true on that look and the keyboard that never comes back is false at the end of its 0.3 s.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D4): the promise rules return. oxlint's type-aware mode (oxlint-tsgolint 7.0.2003, typescript-go inside) runs on TypeScript 5.9 once no tsconfig sets `baseUrl`; `no-floating-promises` (node:test's describe/it/test allowed), `no-misused-promises` and `await-thenable` are errors in both oxlint configs, 10 findings fixed, a planted case per rule in the plugin's oxlint test. The other 13 type-aware correctness rules stay off pending the usefulness review (D4b): with them on the workspace reported about 90 more findings, mostly `unbound-method` and `restrict-template-expressions`, and 253 floating node:test calls before the allowlist. A workspace override pins React to 19.2.3 (Expo SDK 57's renderer); auto-installed peers had resolved 19.2.7, the hoisted react-native flipped between installs, and with one copy the app's typed routes showed four dead routes in `@lib/app-config`. Baseline before D4 and D5, n=3 median (min to max): workspace typecheck 81.2 s (81.0 to 92.5), app typecheck 14.0 s (13.8 to 14.2), workspace lint 16.8 s (16.7 to 20.9), pre-commit gate on a one-line app edit 92.9 s (91.0 to 110.1); CI `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` on a push that touched most packages 277 s and 313 s.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D5): TypeScript 7.0.2 is `tsc` in every package that runs it (`@typescript/native` in the catalog), and `typescript` is the 6.0 API package (`npm:@typescript/typescript6@6.0.2`), because 7.0 ships no JavaScript API and Nx's project graph, dependency-cruiser and Tamagui's babel and metro plugins require one. The lint plugin resolves it too, so typescript 5.9.3 leaves the lockfile; the vm-ops-api image compiles with 7.0.2 like its CI gates, and a test holds the two pins together. dependency-cruiser refuses to run on a `typescript` of 7 or above, the failure the readiness trial found (0 modules cruised and a pass). Checked before landing: 27/27 typechecks, arch 335 modules and 617 dependencies, `nx show projects` (56) and `nx graph` (109 edges), type-aware lint clean, 813 workspace and 134 plugin unit tests, the app flow suite, `expo export --platform web`; three skeptic reviews (CI and images, typescript consumers, compiler semantics) found the vm-ops-api pin and the plugin's 5.9.3, both fixed. Timing in D6.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D4b, owner: port only the useful rules): each of the lint plugin's 14 rules went through a finder (files in scope, violations with its overrides lifted, history, overlap with the typecheck, oxlint built-ins, tests and guards) and a skeptic told to drop it unless it still caught a real defect nothing else catches (workflow wf_52083b83-54c, 28 agents). Kept: `no-hermes-unsupported-api` (Hermes lacks APIs that typecheck and pass in Node, jsdom and Chromium; `AbortSignal.timeout` crashed a phone on 29 Aug, and React Native 0.86's AbortSignal polyfill still lacks it), now off for test files, and `no-unhandled-catch` (13 swallowed catches with its overrides lifted, all justified today). Dropped: `no-styled-native-host`, `no-unguarded-web-global`, `no-unverified-dynamic-import`, `require-structured-log`, `no-direct-io-in-feature-ui` (dependency-cruiser's `ui-never-imports-data` carries its import half), `thin-route`, `guarded-detached-spawn` (nothing left in scope), and the five test-shape rules. The two rules are plain rule objects; their 15 cases run on oxlint's RuleTester (`oxlint/plugins-dev`), so `@typescript-eslint/*` and `eslint` leave the lockfile, and the root config loads no JS plugin.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab D4b, type-aware rules): the 13 type-aware rules in oxlint's correctness category other than the three promise rules went through a finder and a skeptic each (workflow wf_c3c9b386-4f6, 26 agents) and all stay off. 66 findings in total (unbound-method 26, no-base-to-string 14, restrict-template-expressions 13, require-array-sort-compare 4, no-unsafe-unary-minus 3, no-implied-eval, no-meaningless-void-operator and no-misused-spread 2 each, the rest 0), none a real defect: `unknown` narrowed to `{}` before a `String()` of a value that is always a string, labels inferred as unions in plain `.mjs`, unbound methods never called with `this`. no-array-delete and no-for-in-array were the finders' candidates as free insurance; the skeptics kept them off, since no line in the history ever deleted from or for-in'd an array and tsc 7 rejects the refactor that would create one.
- D6 TypeScript 7 and type-aware lint timing (20 Oct, n=3, median with min to max, same sandbox, nothing through Nx or a cache). Before is `b49358c95` (tsc 5.9.3, oxlint without type information, 14 plugin rules); after is `97d91b6ca` (tsc 7.0.2, type-aware promise rules, 2 plugin rules). Workspace typecheck (27 packages, one at a time): 81.2 s (81.0 to 92.5) to 20.0 s (19.7 to 20.1), 4.1 times faster. App typecheck: 14.0 s (13.8 to 14.2) to 4.6 s (4.6 to 4.8), 3.0 times. Workspace lint: 16.8 s (16.7 to 20.9) to 25.2 s (24.3 to 25.4); the same lint with type information off takes 12.0 s (11.6 to 12.0), so dropping 12 plugin rules saved 4.8 s and the type-aware pass costs 13.2 s, which is the price of the promise rules. Pre-commit gate on a one-line app edit: 92.9 s (91.0 to 110.1) to 90.0 s (89.5 to 99.3), unchanged, because the app's flow suite (about 90 s) dominates it, not the typecheck. CI `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` with every package's cache invalidated by the lockfile: 212 s at `05fe7ebd5`, against 277 s and 313 s on the last two pushes that touched most packages. Decision: keep both. The next lever on the local gate is the flow suite, not the compiler.
- Change (20 Oct, after-device-lab S2): CI publishes an EAS Update on branch `dev` after the service e2e tests whenever the app's package graph changed, and before the Android tests (which still run against the dev Metro until S0 decides). `wf ship eas` reports the update as one JSON line with its group id, an `https://expo.dev/.../updates/<group>` link and the export's source maps uploaded under `verification/eas-updates/<group>/`, so a stack from the device maps back to file:line (flow test: a stubbed publish, a fake bucket that receives the map). The runner's deploy read token resolves EXPO_TOKEN and the bucket's write link.
- Change (20 Oct, dead code, owner: clean up dead code): a repo-wide sweep (workflow wf_85787e5a-d18, 23 agents) found 123 candidates and a skeptic per candidate confirmed 96 dead. Applied by area with a re-check at HEAD (wf_67245cce-528): 91 removals across the frontend (stale stubs and scripts, 15 unused dependencies, unused exports and barrels), the other TypeScript (contracts modules, hook-runtime exports), clis (wf's unused rusqlite, reqwest, hyper and hyper-util, two cdp helpers), backend (unused workspace dependencies, ten dead functions) and repo config (dead catalog entries, scripts and build allowances). Skipped with reasons: deleting `@lib/registry-service` (it carries 21 tests), wf's `#[cfg(test)]` modules (tests), an opencode plugin the auto-mode guard would not let an agent delete, and two items already gone. A skeptic per area then found 7 problems, all fixed before landing: the root `@types/react` removal broke the app's JSX types, a stale comment in `test_plan.rs`, and line citations in five gap docs that the deleted functions moved. Checked: frozen install, 27/27 typechecks, lint, arch, every unit test, the app flow suite, `cargo check` of both Rust workspaces, `wf check --dead-refs`, `expo export --platform web`.
- Benchmark (21 Oct, harness API, agent proof): the same four-step two-sum task as the 14 Oct baseline (`docs/runbooks/.results/agent-proof.json`), against the Maestro-named API, with a frozen `wf` per stage against the local fixture export. The full `test --api` reference grew from 6,432 to about 17,000 bytes. Numbers, stage by stage, are in `docs/runbooks/.results/agent-proof-harness-api.json`, with a published report and recording for every run since `b8f07225c`.
  - At `7046c46ec`, as landed:
    - Sonnet n=1 passed: 12.2 s answer against 12.0 s; $0.045 against $0.028; 10,440 tokens against 6,114, because the prompt is larger.
    - opencode (MiniMax-M3, the ladder's first rung) n=3 passed 1 of 3.
    - Both failures read `scroll_until_visible`'s `direction` as the finger's way, because the reference printed signatures without what each option means.
    - In one of them, the sideways drag then started Chromium's overscroll history navigation, which took the device frame back a page. The next look failed with "Cannot find context".
    - The other also gated on `#device:get_logs() == 0`, which every load of the static export fails: gap `static-export-ssr-boundary-errors`.
  - At `b8f07225c`: the reference prints each option's doc and default, and the browser launches with `--disable-features=OverscrollHistoryNavigation`. opencode passed 2 of 3. The failure passed `from` a bounds string, because the doc said "the region" and the reference did not show the option's kind.
  - At `2da8aca09`: each option is printed as `name (kind): doc`, and `from` names an element.
    - opencode passed 3 of 3: answers in 48.8, 39.8 and 66.6 s (median 48.8 s against 85.4 s); $0.007 to $0.009 against $0.011; 22,856 to 24,846 tokens against 22,249.
    - Sonnet n=1 passed: 9.9 s answer, $0.041, 10,290 tokens.
    - Script run times were 5.5 to 5.9 s against 3.9 to 4.0 s: `tap_on`'s settle and the implicit waits.
  - Decision: keep the new API with the option lines.
    - The cheap model's pass rate, the measure that matters for delegated test writing, went from 1 of 3 to 3 of 3, and its answers came faster at a flat token count.
    - Sonnet pays about 4,200 more input tokens per answer for the longer reference.
    - Rejected: trimming the reference back. Both failures that the option lines fixed were missing meaning, not excess text.
- S0 benchmark (EAS Update against Metro) (21 Oct, after-device-lab S0, n=3 in CI): `e2e/update-vs-metro.lua` on the CI device, in the Android job, loads the latest `dev` update (group `27397722`, from `8a509fcaf`, both platforms) and the shared dev Metro, each from a cold Expo Go, then runs editor-typing two-sum part 2 on each. The runs: 39611387906 (`37bab6805`, Metro first), 39616095488 (`36b0d87a9`, update first), 39619152579 (`bf73e5c74`, Metro first), every stage green, the Android stage fresh in the last two (`E2E-Fresh: android`).
  - Cold load, open to the first frame: update 8.0, 7.8 and 7.4 s on the first open (median 7.8 s), 6.8, 14.2 and 7.5 s from Expo Go's cache (median 7.5 s); Metro 12.3, 14.7 and 12.2 s (median 12.3 s). The update's manifest and bundle take 2.4 to 3.2 s against Metro's 6.0 s, and its app start to the first frame 4.0 to 4.7 s against 6.2 s.
  - two-sum part 2: update 25.9, 19.4 and 16.7 s (median 19.4 s); Metro 24.9, 22.9 and 19.5 s (median 22.9 s).
  - From the runner, the update's 7.1 MB launch asset came in 0.11 s and its 32 assets (1.1 MB) in 1.9 s; Metro's dev bundle is 18.4 MB and came in 0.9 s. The CI publish (run 39585958890, `4b420170e`): export 219.5 s, upload 39.3 s, served 0.2 s after the upload, peak RSS 2514 MB, VM MemAvailable low 2347 MB.
  - Why it first failed: Expo Go sends `Expo-Updates-Environment: EXPO_DEVICE` from a device it takes for real hardware, and EAS answers an anonymous EXPO_DEVICE 403 "this project requires authentication. Sign in to Expo Go with an Expo account that is a member of the account that owns this project"; as `EXPO_SIMULATOR` it answers 200. The CI redroid passed Expo Go's emulator check (expo-modules-core `EmulatorUtilities`) as hardware; it now boots with `ro.product.name=wf_redroid_emulator` (`37bab6805`). Signing in would not have helped on Android 57.0.9: its loader reads the session from the wrong preferences file (expo/expo#50253, fixed in #50498, not yet released).
  - Decision: S3 goes ahead (owner, 21 Oct): the Android G1 to G3 units move to the update, which loads about 4.5 s faster per cold start and runs the unit no slower; the Android debugger goes with it, since its logpoints attach through the dev Metro's inspector.
- B3 round two (22 Oct, after-device-lab B3, harness levers, measured locally; the n=3 CI measurement follows): a per-verb split of 516 browser and 126 Android run bundles against the B0 baseline runs (`.agents/tmp/b3r2/deltas.json`) named where the units lost time. Every lever keeps the owner's rule: each user action stays, at up to twice a user's speed; no settle or wait bound is shortened; a lever only notices an event sooner or makes the same observation with fewer round trips. Browser runs at speed 2, published with recordings, against the local web export because the dev Metro was down (see the gap of 22 Oct).
  - L1 Tamil, kept: after a stroke the script waits for the score badge or the next stroke's guide, whichever comes first, within the same 2 s. letters=1-2 vowels 19.3/19.1 s to 13.5/13.8 s, the stroke-to-stroke gap 2.0 s to 54-86 ms, optional-wait misses 3 to 0; letters=5-6 vowels 15.1/15.0 s to 13.6/13.3 s.
  - L2 Android recorder stop, kept: one shell call polls for screenrecord's exit (bounded 5 s, the old 2 s sleep when the image has no pidof) instead of three calls and a fixed 2 s sleep. CI paid 2.0 s per unit on every unit.
  - L3 Android keyboard re-check, reverted: skipping the read between keys missed the app lowering the keyboard on its own; the test that showed it stays.
  - L5 Android visible waits, kept: the look that meets the wait answers it, one lookup instead of two (four adb calls to two on the dump path).
  - L6 Android buffer read, skipped: it already makes one objInfo; 0 fallbacks in 140 CI runs.
  - L7 browser settle in one page evaluation, kept: settle per tap 132.5 to 123.0 ms (34 taps per side).
  - L8 browser condition waits wake on a DOM change (carried mutation count, 50 ms floor) instead of a 200 ms sleep: 22 condition waits per side took 3.50 s to 1.62 s in all (mean 159 to 74 ms), and 3.17 s to 1.73 s in a second round.
  - L9 scroll: one page evaluation per check reads the key, and the row only while the key is short of its visibility; the row is measured fresh before every swipe. Finder evaluations 66 to 51 for 19 to 23 swipes.
  - L10 human strokes coalesce late touch moves as swipes do, on a monotonic clock, keeping the duration, the path's ends and every touchStart and touchEnd; the result reports how many moves were sent and coalesced. 247 samples 8.4 s to 4.4 s (34 to 18 ms per sample against 16.7 planned); letters 5 and 6 still 97-98 % on the line.
  - Decision: land L1, L2, L5, L7 to L10; measure n=3 fresh in CI once the dev Metro serves again; `b3.faster` decides.
- S3 stage 1, Android on the EAS Update (22 Oct, after-device-lab S3, owner's goal: no Metro on the VM; no new benchmark, S0's n=3 above stays the evidence and the first full Android job on the update is its check): every focused Android test (editor-typing, tamil-writing, voice-edit, stubbed-run-local) is `against: update`, and `e2e/update-vs-metro.lua` with its flow test is retired (S0 is decided and its `restore metro` forced a target switch). Decisions (owner, 22 Oct):
  - A push whose EAS publish failed or was cancelled errors its update units with the next step; there is no fall back to the dev Metro.
  - A stale update must not pass as current. `eas-update` runs whenever the app's package graph changed since the commit `latest-dev.json` names (`wf ship --changed`'s `easUpdate`, the rule that decides the Metro leg without the Metro runner's files), not only when the Metro deploy leg ran and succeeded, so a failed publish is repaired by the next push. The runner refuses a pointer that does not name the push's commit after a success, and, when the push published none, a pointer whose commit the app changed since (the same rule), with the publish command as the next step.
  - The cold start is keyed on the target (the Metro commit or the update group): a switch starts the app cold again, and a failed update start errors only the update units.
  - Review of the first implementation (22 Oct): a pnpm or git that fails is never "nothing changed" (the runner says it cannot tell, the planner publishes, a service leg's plan fails loudly; real pnpm 11.5.2 prints an error object on stdout and exits 1 for a commit it cannot filter by, and exits 1 with nothing on stdout for a workspace file it cannot read); the pointer's tailnet names count (a name `wf vm net resolve` no longer answers makes the update stale; a rotated token is not seen, publish with `--force`; the dev Metro had the same refresh rule, it resolved them when it launched); a pointer whose publish saw no 200 from EAS has its manifest asked; a pointer read is tried three times (a 404 once); `eas-update` stays off for a red deploy and keeps the old rule for the one push whose plan ran a wf without the output; a missing `against:` on an Android test is `update`; `--logpoint` on an update run is refused (Hermes is reached through the Metro's inspector until the Android debugger is replaced). Rejected: raising the 30 min timeout (the same units, and the same cold start after each failure, as under the Metro: the bound did not change) and a text test of deploy.yml (no flow test reads a workflow; actionlint and the `eas_update=` output pin cover it). Open, in the after-device-lab progress ledger (22 Oct S3 stage 1): no focused Android test is whole-app, so an app change none of them names publishes an update no Android run loads (the error probe of S3-12 is planned to be it), and the baked backend token is neither compared (the pointer holds names, never values) nor an Nx input (the bake is shell in deploy.yml).
  - Goldens: the update's production render may differ from the Metro dev build; the first update run decides, and a differing golden is taken from the run's published candidates (`wf test goldens <runId>`), never written by hand.
  - Not moved, by name: `metro-smoke` (its subject is the Metro itself) and `debugger-probe` (its logpoints attach through the dev Metro's inspector), both unfocused so CI never runs them; they stay `against: metro` until the Metro and the Android debugger go. `stop-probe` (unfocused) moved: it opens /settings and waits for an element that never appears.
  - The Android job's 30 min timeout stays: the cold starts stay at one per job (every focused test shares the update), and the benchmark's up to 10 min is gone.
- Decision (22 Oct, Metro retirement M1, rules and docs): no benchmark for the changed skills (cf-work, cf-lfg, runbook-automate, browser-cli) or the `expo start` hook reasons. Evidence: they swap a Metro link and `wf ship mla-tamagui` for the EAS Update link, `wf test export` and `wf ship eas`; no lever or hook behaviour changed (`guard.test.ts` pins the reasons), and the root file did not grow (`wf check --budget`: 74 of 150 lines). The two workflow edits are conditions, not levers: `deploy.yml`'s `eas-update` job lost its `eas_update == ''` fallback clause, and the deploy leg skips the retired `mla-tamagui` leg a previous-push plan can still list; the flow tests and `actionlint` are their evidence.
- Decision (22 Oct, Metro retirement M1, the code half, A1 to B1b): no benchmark. The harness changes delete a path (the one automatic retry of a unit a Metro deploy broke, the Metro warm-start key, the Metro `against:` arm, `wf ship mla-tamagui` and `--clear-cache`) or refuse one that nothing in CI reaches (`--logpoint` on an Android run, `wf test run` and `wf android test` alike), so there is no lever to measure; their evidence is the flow suite, with each new test shown failing on the code it replaces. `wf ship eas --latest` reads one pointer and publishes nothing. `debugger-probe` became a browser test (`e2e/debugger-probe.lua`, runbook `device-lab-debugger-probe-browser.md`) and has not been run since: its pass criteria (hits at 504 and 513) are the runbook's, unverified until a `wf test export` plus `wf test run debugger-probe` run records them.
- Change (22 Oct, vm-ops-api F3, `docs/plans/vm-ops-api-rs/plan.md`): the self-build moves from `docker build` plus `docker push` to `docker buildx build --push` on `agent-shell-builder` with the registry cache. Benchmark by the cheapest run that answers it: the deploy leg's own duration in CI, before and after, no extra runs and no live request. The self-build runs as an exec job in agent-shell (not as the `docker:26-cli` sibling, which only runs the swap), so it uses agent-shell's own `docker buildx`; nothing in the sibling image changes.
  - BEFORE (read-only `gh`, 23 Oct 2026, re-read after review): of the last 100 `deploy.yml` runs on dev, 15 have a green `deploy (vm-ops-api)` leg, counted by the leg's own conclusion whatever the run did afterwards: 83 s (run 39867682997), 274 s (39864759849), 306 s (39848840938), 398 s (39742423909), 61 s (39671871253), 61 s (39668965268), 62 s (39658083963), 94 s (39649727326), 102 s (39640200296), 62 s (39535538289), 315 s (39453273436), 318 s (39432942033), 326 s (39395319187), 295 s (39314824975), 386 s (39229471805). Median 274 s, range 61 to 398 s, two clusters: 61 to 102 s (7 legs, the old build's layers all cached) and 274 to 398 s (8 legs, the dependency layers rebuilt). The first reading counted only legs of runs that concluded green (9 legs, median 306 s) and biased the comparison point up. On those nine, the step that is nearly all of the leg is "Build, push, redeploy and wait until it serves the commit" (50 to 386 s). The leg waits 900 s for the commit to serve.
  - Cache evidence: `vm-ops-api:buildcache` on Docker Hub was last written 6 Oct 2026 (170 MB) and `watchdog:buildcache` the same day (82 MB), while `leetcode-backend:buildcache` and `remote-chat-agent:buildcache` were written on 22 Oct. Nothing writes the self images' cache today: `wf ship vm-ops-api` goes through the self-redeploy, `wf ship --build-only` never exports (`start_build` is `Mode::Push`; only `build_and_redeploy` starts `start_cache_export`), and the old build used no registry cache. So the self-build writes it itself, inline, with `ignore-error=true`, which puts the export on the critical path of the leg: wf starts it as its own job after the redeploy because the upload is minutes nothing waits for (`deploy_run.rs:246`), and here the swap waits for the build job to exit.
  - Decisions: the cache ref (`docker.io/<ns>/<image>:buildcache`), its export attributes (`mode=max,oci-mediatypes=true,compression=zstd,ignore-error=true`), the builder (`agent-shell-builder`, docker-container driver, created if missing, never removed), the login, the `--max-used-space 60GB` prune and the CPU shares are `clis/wf/src/build_image.rs`'s, and a unit test reads wf's source for the login, the create, the prune cap, the CPU shares and both cache flags, so the two writers share one cache per image. The prune names `--builder agent-shell-builder` because `buildx prune` acts on the current builder (wf runs `buildx use` first; the old script got it from docker-login.sh). `docker-login.sh` is no longer called: it prunes the builder to 25 % free disk before every build, which `wf ship` avoids because it empties the shared cache. Kept from it: the wait for the nested dockerd (up to 60 s of `docker info`, before the device gate), and the free-space floor, but only after a failed build and before its retry (`buildx prune --min-free-space` of a quarter of `df`'s disk size: a no-op unless the disk is nearly full, skipped when `df` prints no number). Reason: nothing else frees the nested builder's cache (`redeploy.ts` prunes the host's images at 70 %, docker-login.sh runs at agent-shell boot), and on a full disk both attempts would fail with no space left. Cost: a deterministic failure while the disk is under 25 % free empties the shared cache. Not kept: `docker image prune -af`, since a `--push` build loads no image into the nested daemon. A cache that is cold, missing or unreadable is a BuildKit warning; `ignore-error=true` keeps a failed export from failing the build; a build that still fails with the cache flags runs once more on the same builder without them, so a cache flag cannot keep vm-ops-api from redeploying (cost: a deterministic failure repeats its failing layers; the retry is logged as `retrying without it` and, just before the done line, as `built without it` with the image names, because the supervisor reads only the last 4000 bytes). A builder that cannot be created ends the build at once with docker's message, as in `wf ship`. Only the commit tag is pushed. Every docker call but the login pipe and the gate's `docker ps` goes through `run`, so a TERM takes it down. Accepted, not changed: the cache repositories are public and `mode=max` publishes the builder stages' sources (plan open question 9); wf has exported the same way since before F3.
  - Found by the group-signal cases: the trap's `kill -TERM "$child"` fails when the cancel's group signal killed the client first, and under `set -e` that ended the trap with status 1 before its `exit 143` (about 8 of 30 runs at a blocked `buildx inspect`). The build still stopped, but with the wrong exit code; the trap now ends that `kill` with `|| :`. The old script had the same trap.
  - Evidence in the tests: unit (`self-build-buildx.test.mjs`, the TERM cases of `job-cancel.test.mjs`, each docker step blocked in turn and signalled at the shell alone and at the whole group) and the integration cases of `self_build_script.rs`, which run the script the service renders against `docker`, `git`, `df` and `sleep` doubles; the cases were each shown failing on a mutant of the script (no `--push`, no retry, no `ignore-error`, a renamed cache ref, a dropped `--builder` on the build and on the prune, a second tag, the token on argv or an empty stdin, `docker-login.sh` back, an untracked `info`, `inspect`, `update`, `create`, `prune` or `build`, the retry's build and its free-space prune included, a swallowed `create` failure, no dockerd wait or one of 61 tries, no free-space prune, one before every build, a 75 % floor, no guard on an unreadable disk size, a retry that hides the second failure, no retry summary, and the trap's `kill` ending under `set -e`). Suites after review: unit 210 of 210 (186 before F3), integration 132 of 132 (124 before F3).
  - AFTER: TODO. The running service renders the script, so the push that lands F3 (P0) builds with the old one, and the first build with the new script is the push after it (P1). Judge each script on its own evidence: F1's swap script by the first push after 233f1b447 (`wf ship --status`: `redeploy.succeeded` with that push's tag), F3's by P1's build log; one push can be both. Before P1, warm the shared builder, because the first buildx build of these two images is expected cold (the old build ran on whichever builder was current; `watchdog`'s `watchdog-target` cargo cache mount is not in a registry cache, and a cold compile has run up to 52 min against the leg's 900 s wait): `wf ship --build-only vm-ops-api watchdog --ref dev`. Without it, a red P1 leg that waited out its 900 s means a cold builder, not a failed F3: the swap still happens when the build ends, but `service-e2e-tests`, `browser-e2e-tests`, `android-e2e-tests` and the EAS Update are skipped for that push. Record for P1 (warmed) and P2 (the push after it): the job's duration, the duration of its "Build, push, redeploy and wait until it serves the commit" step (the serving time), and the seconds of the build log's `exporting cache to registry` step per image, the one cost F3 puts on the swap's critical path. Compare with the 274 s median, whichever side of it they fall. Done when, on P1 or P2 after the warm-up: the leg is green (its own conclusion, which is the commit serving inside the 900 s wait) and `curl -fsS https://hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/nuesslerm/vm-ops-api/tags/buildcache | jq -r .last_updated` is later than that push (the same for `watchdog`), and the build log has no `retrying without it` line. If the warmed leg is still over its wait because of the export, take the export off the critical path (plan section 5, "Build time").
- B3 outcome so far (23 Oct, after-device-lab B3, read-only CI numbers; no new run): `b3.faster`'s own rule read from `wf test results --query "test=editor-typing,tamil-writing,voice-edit platform=<p> since=14d" --json`, the median of each unit's newest three passing runs against `docs/runbooks/.results/e2e-unit-timing-initial.json`. The runs are CI runs at `dd3534f5a`, `ed73e4198` and `0ca9ab744` (22 Oct; one Android unit also reads `7a266453d`). The levers are the 21 Oct pacing ones (`8e08e5bcc`, `7b646711e`, `c2f3df01e`) and round two (`c205f6620`).
  - Before (ledger, 21 Oct): 15 of 21 Android units and 5 of 21 browser units at or below the baseline; browser editor-typing 24 to 61 s against 19 to 39 s; on CI run 39640200296 (`7b646711e`) valid-palindrome part 2 took 38.0 s against 37.7 s, after 49.8 to 52.1 s without the levers.
  - Android: 21 of 21 units at or below the baseline. Medians, editor-typing 23.1 to 30.2 s (baseline 27.4 to 36.7 s), Tamil 16.5 to 34.6 s (21.4 to 39.8 s), voice edit chunks 16.6 to 21.6 s (27.0 to 36.0 s). These runs load the EAS Update, so part of the gain is S3's, not the levers'.
  - Browser: 20 of 21 units at or below the baseline. Medians, editor-typing 12.4 to 27.1 s (18.8 to 39.4 s), Tamil 6.3 to 26.8 s (13.8 to 47.6 s), voice edit 12.3 to 18.3 s (17.1 to 22.3 s). These runs load the web export (since `3a421b87b`), not the Metro. The one unit above is Tamil words `word-uyir-i`: 19.4, 26.8 and 65.4 s (median 26.8 s against 21.5 s); it ran 12.9 to 13.5 s at `7a266453d`, so the 65.4 s run (`dd3534f5a`) is an outlier whose cause was not looked into.
  - Decision: B3 stays unticked, 41 of 42 units pass and `b3.faster` needs all of them. No lever is reverted on this evidence; the per-lever attribution stays the local measurement above (the CI numbers mix the levers with S3 and the export). It closes when the newest three passing runs of `word-uyir-i` read at or below 21.5 s, which needs three more CI runs after the next push.
- S3 outcome (23 Oct, after-device-lab S3, read-only CI numbers; the benchmark is S0's n=3 above, nothing new was run): Android e2e ran against the EAS Update in CI at `7a266453d`, `dd3534f5a`, `ed73e4198` and `0ca9ab744` (22 Oct, 17:21Z to 23:13Z), every unit loading `exp://u.expo.dev/5d273b55-a8fc-4e4a-bae7-14d1a9f7eb57/group/<group>/--`: 21, 21, 20 and 21 units, all passing. The runs before it (`3a421b87b`, `c205f6620`) still loaded `exp://metro-runner-main:20000`. `s3.android-on-update`'s command exits 0 on the 21 newest Android units (all at `0ca9ab744`).
