Sizes: the 12 flow test binaries of the wf crates 402.5 MiB (largest `wf-harness` 53.4, `wf` 49.5, `wf-bench` 40.8) against the one 108.7 MiB flow binary before, the 11 thin binaries 504.4 MiB, debug `wf` 116.5 MiB against 246.0; the debug rows are not like for like because `[profile.dev] debug = "line-tables-only"` is new. Tests (flow, each crate through its thin binary with no `wf` on PATH, test names equal to the 162 of `census.tsv`, none lost, none returned early): `wf-secrets` 7, `wf-bucket` 3, `the ci crate` 6, `wf-vmops` 2, `wf-plan` 23, `wf-vm` 14, `offload` 10, `wf-ship` 18, `wf-bench` 5, `wf-login` 6, `wf-harness` 48, `wf` 20, 162 of 162 passed; the 142 tests of the 11 split crates passed again with `WF_BIN` set to the full debug `wf` (the parity oracle, now in post-land `rust-clis` for the whole workspace). Slowest tests (thread-seconds measured at W0, 1,037.7 in all): the two `nx_cache` contract tests of `wf-plan`, 199.6 s and 188.6 s, then `fresh_package_stage_runs_again_every_task_the_cache_would_replay` 71.0 s, `a_sandbox_pass_replays_on_ci` 65.7 s, `an_e2e_fresh_trailer_runs_the_stage_fresh` 55.4 s, `wf-ship`'s `deploy_legs` 43.3 s and `integration_scripts_are_planned_in_the_flow_stage...` 39.8 s; `wf-plan` holds 651 of the 1,038 s and its own task took 354 s wall here. Help: 382 of 384 re-run `--help`, `-h`, `help <cmd>` and `--version` outputs equal the pre-split binary byte for byte (the two `wf android list-devices` ones print the catalog's new path; 19 bare-noun captures were not re-run). Gates: `wf check --budget` passes with the new `wf-kit-lines` entry (1,044 of 1,200), `sync:check`, `arch:services`, `actionlint` and the secrets filter pass, `wf check --dead-refs` 0 on a scratch index of the tree (it reads `git ls-files`, so the untracked new crates read as dead until the commit: 314 findings on the bare working tree). Rerun estimate from the real crate graph (the rule Nx and `wf test plan` apply), replayed over the 1,295 commits of the last 30 days: a commit that touches `clis/wf` reruns 88.7 of 162 flow tests on average (54.7 %; the plan estimated 51 %), median 73, and 22.2 % rerun all 162 (estimated 22 %); over all commits the mean is 39.2 against 74.2 before (-47 %); the bounds, 88.2 to 108.6, depend on where the 754 touches of modules that no longer exist are placed. Flow tests that rerun for an edit in: `wf-kit`, `wf-secrets` or `wf-testkit` 162; `wf-vmops` 146; `wf-harness` 73; `wf-bucket` 70; `wf-plan` 61; `wf-ship` 38; `wf-vm` 34; `offload` 30; `the ci crate` and `wf-login` 26; `wf-bench` 25; `wf` 20; before the split every edit reran all 162 in the one `crate-wf` task. 79 of the 593 commits that rerun `crate-wf` today touch no file under `clis/` and now rerun `wf-plan` alone (70 commits, 23 tests) or with `wf-harness` (9); 84 commits rerun a test that reran none before, because the new `project.json` filesets list files the old task never hashed (`.github/**`, `mise.toml`, the secrets guards): holes closed, not a regression. Not measured, because nothing here may reach CI, the VM or a cloud: the `flow-tests` stage time of a docs-only, a `ci_comment`-only and a `wf-plan`-only commit, the musl build `install-wf` runs and its persistent-incremental path, the loaded VM and the `vnc-auth-session` image build; the replay script was a scratch file and is not committed. Decision: keep the split. The stop rule did not trigger (cold build 6.6 % below the baseline, a leaf edit 81 % below, the worst case 45.6 s still under 47.0 s). A kit, secrets or lock edit still reruns all 162 tests across 12 tasks at `--parallel 2` and costs about what the single task did, so revisit only if the first CI runs show that clearly worse, and measure the three commit types then.

The split commit's own pipeline failed once, at `deploy (reporting-service)`, and not because of the split: the watchdog read the sidecar unhealthy during the redeploy's compose stop and recreated it at the same moment as the redeploy ("container name already in use"); the failure comment `wf ci comment` posted named the failing step, the error line and the skipped jobs as designed. `21eb4e212` makes unhealthy count only on two sweeps in a row for the same container id. Sandbox to blob latency, read from the pre-commit gate's cache line (`wf: build cache`, 9 gets and 9 puts): get 1.15 s mean, 1.69 s slowest; put 0.96 s mean, 1.19 s slowest, against 60 to 240 ms from the VM.

## 2026-10-25: one Docker Hub token, vault-only secrets, loud build login

- Cause of the 60 min hang (run 40146009885): the VM held an expired Docker Hub PAT; the build script swallowed the `docker login` error.
- Decision: one `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` in the deploy vault project; the agent-shell-specific token name removed from the registry, groups, compose, scripts, goldens and docs. The agent-shell group requires it.
- Decision: an unset token throws everywhere it is read (compose `:?`, `${DOCKERHUB_TOKEN:?}` in the build scripts, `docker-login.sh` exits 1, a rejected login exits 1 before any build). No fallback to a build without a login.
- Decision: with a Bitwarden read token configured `wf secret` resolves from the vault alone (the shell and env files are never consulted, except the `BWS_*` bootstrap), and a vault error throws.
- Tests: wf-secrets flow (7), wf-ship flow, vm-ops-api core (284) and integration (125) pass; integration pins the failed login (exit 1, no image built).
- Done 25 Oct: agent-shell redeployed with the new name (VM check: set, Docker Hub login HTTP 200) and the old vault entry deleted. Owner-only: delete the stale `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` of the GH-CI project (it now holds the same working value, nothing reads it).

## 2026-10-25: one deploy flow (build, smoke test, swap, bounded health check)

- Decision: vm-ops-api no longer builds itself. `wf ship vm-ops-api --ref <commit>` runs the build as a waited job (`--build-only vm-ops-api-rs watchdog --tag sha-<8>`), then `POST /redeploy?ref=&tag=` proves the pulled image and swaps. Deleted: the build supervisor, the pressure sampler, the agent-shell job launcher, the self-build script and its goldens, `wf vm ops redeploy-self`, the 3600 s `wait_serving` loop.
- Decision: the smoke test is local to the throwaway container (probe from inside, spare port 18999 in the netns of the live container), not a signed request over the tailnet; the post-swap `/health` check over the tailnet stays. Default recorded, not asked: the spare port needs no ACL change this way.
- Decision: every app with an HTTP health gets the same smoke step in its redeploy script (leetcode-backend, remote-chat-agent, reporting-service); the others (no `/health`) have none.
- Decision: a failed pull is a failed redeploy: the pull is a line of its own, where the old `pull && down ; up` chain let the `;` mask it.
- Decision: no DB schema change from the agent: the test_runs table rejects `harness=integration` (check constraint `TEST_RUNS_HARNESS_CK`), so an integration run posts as harness `flow`, platform `integration`. Owner-only if wanted: widen the constraint.
- Limits (to be replaced by 3x the slowest of three live timings once measured): serving check 120 s, the build step 90 min (a cold dependency layer).

### CI findings of runs 40156279094 and 40159085189 (25 Oct)

- 40156279094, flow-tests: my vault-authoritative `wf secret` broke the wf-login and wf-vm fixtures (they passed secrets by env while selecting the bitwarden backend); the fixtures now serve them from the fake `bws` vault. Four wf-harness tests found no ffmpeg on a runner container recreated since the last run: flow-tests installs it like e2e-tests.yml does.
- 40159085189, flow-tests: only three wf-harness browser tests failed, on Chromium timeouts (`captureScreenshot` 5 s, `goto` 30 s) with the runner at load 7 on 2 OCPU: the change touched `wf-secrets`, so nearly every CLI crate re-ran at once. The other crates' passes are now in the Nx cache, so the next run replays them; `gh run rerun` and workflow dispatch answer 403 for this integration, so the next push is the re-run.
- Owner-only: widen `TEST_RUNS_HARNESS_CK` (integration rows post as `flow` until then).

- Live proof of fail-fast (run 40162454403, vm-ops-api leg, before agent-shell carried the new name): the leg failed 24 s after it started with `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN: DOCKERHUB_TOKEN is not set` and `build failed with exit code 2`, where the same fault had held the old leg for 60 minutes.

- Second hang found live (run 40162454403, deploy leetcode-auth-cookie-capture): the leg started its build job, then agent-shell was recreated; the job dir vanished with the container and `AGENT_SHELL_JOB_STATUS` printed no exit code for a dir that no longer existed, so the poll read `running` for over an hour. A job dir that is gone now reads as lost, exit 137 (unit tests: gone dir, live group). The run could not be cancelled from here (403) and ends at its 100 min job timeout.

- Third fault found live (run 40167125770): the bootstrap swap of the old vm-ops-api failed with `fatal: shallow file has changed since we read it` (exit 128), silently, because its self-build ran beside the registry cache export of the build before it, and both used one checkout. The cache export now has a checkout of its own (`expo-hello-world-app-2-cache`). The old service's failure never reached the leg: the new flow reports a build or smoke failure as the leg's own exit.

### 2026-10-25: the vm-ops-api smoke test does not compare GIT_COMMIT

The first three live `wf ship vm-ops-api --ref dd6ab6d6…` runs after the bootstrap all built in 14 to 16 s and were refused by the smoke test
with `vm-ops-api runs another commit than dd6ab6d6…; nothing was swapped`. The build had re-tagged the image of the same input hash
(`imagetools create`), and that image carries the `GIT_COMMIT` it was built at, so the check refused a healthy image. Decision: the tag is the
identity of an image, the commit comparison is deleted (script, integration case, golden). Evidence: core 250 and integration 113 tests pass
(unit, integration). The serving check still waits for a commit at or after the pushed one, so a manual re-ship of an unchanged vm-ops-api
ends at its bounded `/health` check; CI never does that, because `--changed` ships a service only when its input hash differs.

### 2026-10-25: deploy limits from live timings, and the secrets-fail-loudly record

Live phases of vm-ops-api (integration of the new flow, the first run on the serving service, CI run 40171455726, deploy leg): build 64 s (a
fresh image), smoke test 7 s, swap and serving 10 s. Other live builds: 54 s (CI leg) and 14 to 16 s (three manual re-tags). Limits are 3x the
slowest live phase, rounded up: the redeploy request (pull, smoke test) 30 s, the serving check 30 s. The build keeps its job timeout
(`deploy.yml`) and no tighter step limit: a cold dependency layer has taken 52 min on this VM, and a limit set from warm builds would fail the
one build that is slow for a known reason. Not yet timed live: the smoke test of the other five services (it runs from the next deploy of each).
Test type: flow (wf-ship, wf-vmops) and the CI deploy leg.

Secrets: what fails loudly today is the compose `:?` on required keys, the build scripts' `${DOCKERHUB_TOKEN:?}`, `docker-login.sh` exit 1,
`sync-env` refusing a partial `.env` on a missing required key, and vault-authoritative `wf secret` (a bitwarden backend with a read token
takes vault values only). Not built: `wf secret doctor --deployed` (a new read surface over deployed secret hashes for no failure the above
does not already end), a startup refusal in every service (each reads its own required keys from the compose `:?`), and a raw `env::var` lint
(`service_kit::secrets::load()` is the one reader). Decision: stop here; add a layer when a missing variable is found that none of these ends.

### 2026-10-25: the smoke test on the other services, three live timings, and its first catch

Nine manual `wf ship <app> --ref ccb90114…` runs (three rounds; images of unchanged inputs, so each build is a 13 to 16 s re-tag). Phase
"pull, smoke test and swap", seconds: leetcode-backend 7, 6, 5; remote-chat-agent 29, 11, 11; reporting-service refused all three. Builds of
fresh images: 54, 64, 53 (vm-ops-api, vnc-auth-session). vm-ops-api smoke 7 and swap 10 (CI run 40171455726). Limits at 3x the slowest of
three: the self-redeploy request 30 s (smoke 7 s x 3, rounded up) and its serving check 30 s (10 s x 3); an app redeploy is one synchronous
sibling bounded by the 300 s HTTP timeout of its request, because remote-chat-agent took 29 s once (3x = 87 s, so 300 s is not the limit
that follows from the timings; it stays only until a fourth app is timed, and is the one open value). The fresh-build limit stays the job
timeout: 3x 64 s would fail the cold dependency layer that has taken 52 min.

The smoke test caught a real defect before it reached CI: reporting-service named the compose service `reporting-service`, whose compose
file calls it `app` (`no such service`). Nothing was swapped, the 502 carried the error, and all three runs ended in 14 s. Fixed in
`smoke()`, with a unit test that reads each compose file and requires the service in it (unit; integration 113 and core 251 pass).

Default recorded, not asked: the probe runs inside the throwaway container's own netns (`<bin> --healthcheck` on port 18999), not as a
signed request over the tailnet, because the spare port is not in the tailnet ACL and opening it is an owner change. The tailnet check is
the bounded `/health` after the swap. leetcode-auth-cookie-capture, vnc-auth-session and agent-shell have no HTTP health, so they have no
smoke test (`smoke()` returns none); agent-shell is also deployed by hand.

### 2026-10-25: what has no smoke test, and why; the tailnet probe

The goal's own clause applies to the signed tailnet probe: the spare port is not in the tailnet ACL (`infra/tailscale/policy.hujson`, applied only on
a push to `main`), so the smoke test runs locally in the throwaway container's netns and the tailnet `/health` is the bounded check after
the swap. vnc-auth-session gets no smoke test by construction, not for want of a health route: its listener calls `serve::bind_route` at
start, so a throwaway copy would repoint the live tailnet route at its own gate. agent-shell is deployed by hand (the pipeline must not
restart its own runner), and its `app` service registers the runner, so a second copy is not a proof. The smoke set is therefore the five
apps that can be started twice without touching shared state. The stale `.claude/worktrees/wf_4baca7d6-b41-4` (662 uncommitted lines of
lockfile, tsconfig and dist noise from an earlier workflow) was stashed (`git stash list`, message "stale wf_4baca7d6 …") and removed, so no
file on disk names the deleted `AGENT_SHELL_DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` any more.

### 2026-10-25: correction, the tailnet ACL does not block the spare smoke port

The entries above say the smoke probe stays inside the container's netns because "the spare port is not in the tailnet ACL". That was
never tested and is wrong: `infra/tailscale/policy.hujson` has one grant, `src *, dst *, ip *`, so every port is open and no merge or ACL
change is needed. The throwaway shares the live node's netns (`network_mode: service:tailscale`), so a signed request over the tailnet to
`<node>:18999` reaches it. What is missing is the flow, not permission: today the smoke test runs inside one script on the VM and the swap
follows in the same script, so `wf` has no window to send its request. The signed probe needs the smoke step split in two (start and keep
the throwaway, then stop it after `wf`'s probe), with the failure still ending the leg with the container's log.

### 2026-10-25: the smoke test is a step of its own, and `wf` asks the throwaway container over the tailnet

Experiment first (round 1 live, c9b328b04): `POST /services/leetcode-backend/smoke` left the throwaway container running, and three
`GET http://leetcode-backend-prod:18999/health` over the tailnet answered `{"status":"ok"}`; the stop removed it. So the ACL was never the
blocker (see the correction above), and the container shares the live node's netns, so no `tailscale serve` route is needed either.
Decision: the smoke test leaves the redeploy script and becomes `POST /services/<app>/smoke` and `/smoke/stop` (vm-ops-api:
`POST /redeploy/smoke` and `/redeploy/smoke/stop`); `wf ship` runs build, smoke start, tailnet probe, stop (whatever the probe said),
swap, serving, each printing its seconds. The probe is the app's own `/health` on the spare port, a signed `/health` for vm-ops-api;
leetcode-auth-cookie-capture has no `/health` route, so its probe stays inside the container. Rolled out in two commits because the
running service must have the new routes before the `wf` that calls them ships it (c9b328b04 apps, edeab62b6 vm-ops-api, then this).
Deleted: the smoke block of the app redeploy script and of `POST /redeploy`, and the `keep` flag. Test types: core unit (252), service
flow (44), integration (113, goldens regenerated for the four app redeploy scripts), wf-ship flow (27, including a failed tailnet probe that
still stops the container and swaps nothing).

### 2026-10-25: `TEST_RUNS_HARNESS_CK` widened, integration rows post as their own harness

Done on the owner's go-ahead: through the ORDS REST SQL endpoint of the `HEALTH_RPT` schema (its own credentials, the table's owner), the check
constraint went from `harness IN ('flow','browser','android')` to `... , 'integration')`. Order of statements, so a constraint always exists: add
`TEST_RUNS_HARNESS_CK_V2` with the four values, drop the old one, add `TEST_RUNS_HARNESS_CK` with the four values, drop `_V2`; the query after
read back the one constraint with `'integration'`. `the ci crate` now posts an integration suite's row with `harness=integration` (it was `flow`
with platform `integration`); rows already stored keep their `flow` harness. Test type: flow (`the ci crate`, 7); the first live row is the next CI run
that executes the vm-ops-api integration suite. Supersedes the owner-only note above.

### 2026-10-25: vnc-auth-session gets a smoke test; every app is evaluated the same way

Decision (on the owner's ask): the one thing that made a second vnc-auth-session unsafe was the listener's `tailscale serve` route binding at start,
so the listener skips it when `VNC_AUTH_SMOKE` is set; the throwaway runs with `VNC_AUTH_SMOKE=1`, `VNC_AUTH_GATE_PORT=18998` and the listener on
18999, and the probe is any HTTP answer of the listener (it has no `/health`; 404 counts, as for the capture app). `Smoke` gained an `env` list for
this. Uniformity: a unit test requires every managed app to have a smoke test or a written `SMOKE_EXEMPT` entry (agent-shell only), and `wf ship`
ends every smoke gate with one line, `smoke: {"app","inside","tailnet"}`, each `passed`, `none` or `failed`. Test types: unit (core 253, wf-login 1),
service flow (45), wf-ship flow (26); the live proof is the vnc-auth-session leg of the next CI run.

### 2026-10-25: preflight of every deploy: secrets are checked for being usable, not only set

Decision (on the owner's ask, services and containers): `wf secret verify --for <app>` resolves the app's group (and `wf ship` adds
`DOCKERHUB_TOKEN`, which the build pushes with) from the vault and checks each value: hygiene for all (a trailing newline is the classic
silent breakage), a shape for `*_BASE_URL`, `OCI_*`, `TS_VM_AUTH_KEY`, `VNC_AUTH_OWNER_CHAT_ID`, `PAGERDUTY_INTEGRATION_KEY` and the Telegram
bot token, and one read-only provider call for `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` (the registry token endpoint, the call `docker login` makes), `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`
(`getMe`), `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `VM_PROVISION_GITHUB_TOKEN` (`/rate_limit`) and `GROQ_API_KEY` (`/models`). A rejected value (401/403/404) or a
malformed one fails with its key and reason (never the value); a provider outage or 5xx is `unchecked` and never blocks a deploy, so a
third party cannot stop yours. `wf ship` runs it before the build of every app and of vm-ops-api (`WF_PREFLIGHT=off` is the flow tests' seam
only). Not checked beyond presence, because a check would consume or mutate them: Tailscale auth keys (shape only), the OTLP and model-provider
keys with no known read-only endpoint, signing seeds. Run against the real vault the same day: all seven deploy groups pass, with live checks
passing for Docker Hub, both GitHub tokens and the Telegram bot. Test types: flow (wf-secrets 4 new, wf-ship 1 new); the live proof is the next CI deploy.

### CI findings of run 40203819776 (4d4cabd84, the preflight), three flow failures, three fixes

- `wf` surface test: `wf secret verify` had no section in `docs/reference/wf-cli.md`; added (the file has no generator in this tree, it is kept by hand).
- `wf-plan` nx_cache contract: `wf-secrets` now depends on `cli-http`, so a change to `clis/http` reruns its task too; the expected set names it.
- `wf-harness` browser test, `check-taps`: "0 of 1 taps show a dot" with the runner at load 7 (twelve CLI crates re-ran because `wf-secrets` changed).
  Root cause: the check wanted a frame within 300 ms after the tap, but the marker lingers 1800 ms (`lingerMs`), so a recorder that drops frames
  under load failed a tap it does show. The window is now 1500 ms, inside the linger; the negative cases of the test (a dot elsewhere, none) are unchanged.
  Test types: flow (wf-harness check-taps and the phone-framed cases, wf surface) run locally; nx_cache replays in CI.

### 2026-10-25: the checks moved into the registry, and every deploy secret has a decision

Decision (on the owner's suggestion, so nothing is duplicated): `wf secret verify` no longer carries a table of keys. Each registry entry holds
its own `check` (`shape`: url, pem, prefix or regex; `live`: one read-only HTTP call with `{value}`, `{OTHER_KEY|default}` placeholders, header or
basic auth and an optional body marker) or an `uncheckable` reason; `verify.rs` only interprets them. Of the 38 secrets any deploy group carries,
22 have a check (live: Docker Hub, Telegram, GitHub x2, Groq, Kimi, the ORDS database password; shape: URLs, OCI ids and key, Tailscale key, chat
ids, PagerDuty key and domain, BetterStack host, alert email) and 16 an `uncheckable` reason (shared secrets only the other side can judge, write-only
ingest tokens, an OAuth setup token and two model providers with no confirmed read-only endpoint, a key passphrase). A flow test fails when a
secret of any group has neither or both. Against the real vault all ten groups pass; the ORDS password reports `unchecked` where its
base URL is not in the group (remote-chat-agent). Test type: flow (wf-secrets, 12).

## CI findings of run 40206433066 (bfcf3c6db)

- Red: `wf-harness` flow `an_attached_session_reads_pages_and_manages_its_lifecycle`, `page.goto` 30 s timeout. Cause: the runner has 2 cores and `--parallel 2` plus in-process test threads launched several real Chromiums at once.
- Fix: one process-wide `chromium_gate()` mutex held for the life of a `BrowserSession` or `Attached` (`clis/wf-harness/src/testing/browser_session.rs`); the test passes alone in 1.65 s. No test skipped, no timeout raised.
- Also: `Entry.uncheckable` was never read (dead-code warning, fatal under `clippy -D warnings`); the registry parser still validates it, the field is gone.

## Flow tests with a real dependency move to the integration tier (owner, 25 Oct)

- Rule (CLAUDE.md): a flow test doubles every external dependency. Audit of `clis/*/tests/flow*`, `backend/*/rs/tests`, `frontend` flow tests found real Chromium (and ffmpeg) in four crates: `wf-harness` (13 journeys plus `check-taps` on a real ffmpeg video), `wf-bench` (the phone-framed memory/runtime/startup journey), `wf` (`check --visual`), `wf-login` (the listener driving a desktop Chromium through a login). Every other flow test doubles its browser, docker, adb, network and tools (`wf-secrets` runs the repo's own `node` guard script: a repo script, not an external service, left as is).
- Move: each crate gets `tests/integration.rs` beside `tests/flow.rs`; shared fixtures sit in a subdirectory module (`rig/`, `lab/`) because Cargo treats every `tests/*.rs` as a test target. The `chromium_gate` mutex stays, so one Chromium runs at a time inside a test process, and `wf-harness`'s flow `--test-threads=2` special case is gone (its flow tests are mock-only now).
- CI: `cargo-integration` Nx task per crate with `tests/integration.rs` (one test thread); `wf test plan` plans `test:integration` and the crates' integration in `service-e2e-tests` (after the CLIs are built and the services deployed), not `flow-tests`; the job installs Chromium and ffmpeg, publishes the integration run bundles, and its timeout grew from 10 to 35 minutes.
- Not proven: that the earlier Chromium timeouts came from parallel launches (the error only said `goto` did not finish in 30 s). Moving the tests removes real browsers from `flow-tests`; the first `service-e2e-tests` run measures them.

## `the ci crate` renamed `wf-report` (owner, 25 Oct)

- The crate holds `wf ci comment`, `wf test publish`, `wf test goldens` and the run-metrics rows: what a run leaves for people to read. `ci` named the pipeline, not the job. The `wf ci` verb, the PR-comment marker `ci-comment` (existing comments keep matching) and the archived plans keep the old name.
- Open: `wf test goldens` reads published bundles but belongs with `wf-harness`'s golden-image code; moving it is a follow-up.

## wf-harness split into harness-core, harness-browser, harness-android (plan round 2, 25 Oct)

- Step 1 (5ff4263e7): `Platform` trait in the run engine and the verb front end (`run.rs`, `device/cli.rs` named android and browser in 14 places); each driver implements it. Flow 36, integration 13, lib 38 passed before the move.
- Step 2: physical split. `harness-core` holds the Device command table, Lua API, run engine, goldens, reports, traces, spans, staticd, video check and the log capture that run reports render; `harness-browser` the browser verbs, daemon, tunnel, driver and `testing`; `harness-android` the Android verbs, adb and UI-server code, lease, lab, driver and stub server. `wf-harness` stays as the facade (`wf_harness::browser`, `::android`, `::device`... keep their paths), the thin binary `wf-harness` and the cross-platform flow and integration tests, because only a binary that serves both nouns can run `harness.rs`, `maestro.rs` and `surface.rs`.
- Moved with it: per-noun help tables (`HELP` is now two tables; `wf` lists both), `browser_session`/`android_serial` into their drivers, `utc_z` into the daemon (its only caller), the Nx e2e inputs (`tooling/nx/e2e-tests.js`) and the paths pinned by `wf-plan/tests/flow/nx_cache.rs`.
- Verified locally: clippy -D warnings (7 crates), unit (7, 4, 27), `wf-harness` flow 36 and integration 13, `wf-plan` 24 (one expectation rewritten: wf-harness now reaches a change in secrets through `wf-vmops`, and a change in the bucket through `harness-core`), `wf` surface 3, `wf-bench` flow 4. `cargo clippy --workspace` also trips on an unrelated `blob-cache` test lint (`unnecessary use of to_string`): not reached by CI's per-crate clippy, left alone.

## First CI runs of the integration tier (25 Oct)

- Runs 40233005896 (5ff4263e7) and 40235904669 (6ad8a4923): `service-e2e-tests` failed both times in `crate-wf-harness:cargo-integration`, the same two journeys (`an_attached_session_proxies_stubs_and_forwards_network_and_feeds_fake_media`, `an_attached_session_reads_pages_and_manages_its_lifecycle`) with `page.goto ... did not finish loading within 30 s`; the other 11 wf-harness journeys, the wf-bench journey and the wf visual journey passed. The two failures sat in the window where the wf-bench and wf binaries also ran a Chromium: Nx ran the three integration crates in parallel (`--parallel=4`) and the Chromium gate is per process.
- Fix, pushed in 6ad8a4923 and so planned only from the next push (the plan job runs the previous push's wf): the service-e2e stage runs the `integration` kind at `--parallel=1`. Not proven yet; if the same two journeys fail with the crates one at a time, the cause is not contention and the journeys need the page-load evidence the error does not carry.
- Flow-tests on 6ad8a4923 took 19 min (graph change, every dependent crate rebuilt and reran on the VM's single runner), typecheck-lint-unit-tests 6 min, clis 1 min.
- Round 4 audit finding: `wf flow run --update-goldens` painted frozen screens in a real Chromium inside `wf`'s flow tests. Moved to `harness-flow`'s integration binary; its flow tests double vitest and stop before a browser starts.

## harness-flow (plan round 4, 25 Oct)

It depends on `harness-core` only and publishes through the publisher the binary sets (`harness_core::run::publish`), so it stays below the pipeline.

## Run 815 and the tiers redefined (25 Oct)

- Run 40241664866 (26720f0fc), the first with the integration kind at `--parallel=1`: the crates ran one after another (wf-harness 176 s, then harness-flow, wf-bench, wf) and the same two wf-harness journeys failed again (`an_attached_session_proxies_stubs_and_forwards_network_and_feeds_fake_media`, `an_attached_session_reads_pages_and_manages_its_lifecycle`, `page.goto … did not finish loading within 30 s`). Contention is ruled out. browser-e2e and android-e2e passed on that run; flow-tests took 19 min, typecheck-lint-unit 9 min, clis 32 s.
- Fix for the evidence gap: the goto timeout of `cdp::attached` now reports `location.href`, `readyState` and the leaving marker, which tells a navigation that never committed from one that never loaded. The next red run names the cause.
- Owner direction (this session): integration tests are black-box contract tests of the built binary with every dependency mocked, run through the Nx cache right after the deploy and before any e2e; real dependencies belong only to e2e. The real-Chromium and ffmpeg journeys I had called integration are therefore e2e. They are now `tests/e2e.rs` (`cargo-e2e`, plan kind `e2e`, stage `cli-e2e-tests`); the stages run in the order flow, typecheck-lint-unit, deploy, integration-tests, cli-e2e-tests, service-e2e-tests, browser/android e2e. `integration-tests` keeps the packages' `test:integration` (vm-ops-api's black-box suite is the model) and a crate's `tests/integration.rs`, of which there is none yet.
- Service e2e tests were one logging check per service. Each now also checks a contract against the deployed service: reporting-service (health rows and commit, metrics series and the refused window, current/headroom/plans shapes, 404 for unknown paths and writes), leetcode-backend (health and commit, every `/api/leetcode` route refuses a missing or wrong bearer, 404), leetcode-auth-cookie-capture (the trigger refuses a missing or wrong secret and starts nothing, nothing else is served). They are read-only or refused calls: none needs a secret. `service_kit::e2e::assert_serves_commit` checks `/health` against `WF_EXPECTED_REVISIONS`.
- Open: black-box `test:integration` suites for reporting-service, leetcode-backend, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture and remote-chat-agent (only vm-ops-api has one).
- Round 5a: `wf-plan` and `wf-report` merged into `pipeline` (9c5d0df5e); wf-ship now depends on it by name; the nx_cache contract names it.

## Black-box integration suites of the services (26 Oct)

- `service_kit::blackbox::Running` (feature `blackbox`): a service's built binary on a free loopback port, in a temp dir, with a cleared environment (no vault, no telemetry endpoint, no secret) plus what the test gives it; killed on drop; a failure prints its stdout and stderr.
- reporting-service (4 tests: health with no rows, the metrics window and its refusal, a plan in a fake repository listed and served, 404 for unknown paths and writes), leetcode-auth-cookie-capture (4: the trigger refuses a missing, wrong or empty secret, the right secret is accepted, nothing else is served, the per-minute limit answers 429), leetcode-backend (5: health and commit, every route refuses a missing or wrong bearer even with the admin secret, no session answers signed out, a session stored with the admin secret is used as the cookie and csrf of the LeetCode request a fake LeetCode receives, a second login capture is a 409). Each is a package `test:integration` script, so the integration-tests stage runs it through the Nx cache. Passed locally: 4, 4, 5; backend clippy -D warnings clean.
- The black-box run corrected a guess: leetcode-backend answers `GET /user-status` without a session as 200 signed out, not 401.
- Open: remote-chat-agent has `tests/blackbox` but no `test:integration` script; vm-ops-api keeps its own suite.

## Silent Nx gap before the clippy tasks (26 Oct)

- Runs 40246994252 (timed out at the 20-min step limit) and 40251453496 (red on a real lint): in `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` the `cargo-clippy` call prints its banner and then nothing for 7.5 min (13 crates) before its first task starts; in run 815 the same call waited 6 min for 10 crates and `cargo-unit` 90 s for 5, while cached tasks (`existing outputs match the cache`) start at once and the tasks themselves finish in under a minute. Remote-cache latency is not it (16 gets, 1.5 s in total). Locally Nx starts the same tasks in 2 s. The gap grows with the number of tasks that miss, roughly with its square.
- The red of 40251453496 is real and unrelated to the gap: `crate-blob-cache`'s test `nx_server` had an unnecessary `to_string` that clippy `-D warnings` rejects; blob-cache entered the plan because the graph changed. Fixed.
- No fix to the gap yet: the stage now sets `WF_NX_TRACE=1`, which makes the Nx calls of `wf test run` verbose with perf logging, so the next run says what Nx does in that gap. The 20-minute limit stays as it is.

## Round 5b: the wf test and check verbs move into pipeline (2026-10-26)

- `visual`, `dead_refs`, `test_results`, `e2e_run`, `device_run` and `export_site` moved from `clis/wf/src` into `clis/pipeline/src`. The EAS read-side helpers (`served`, `latest_pointer`, `moved_hosts`, `config_field`, `children_peak_rss_mb`) were extracted from `wf-ship` into `pipeline::eas`, because `e2e_run` and `device_run` need them and `wf-ship` depends on `pipeline`, never the reverse (a move without the extraction would have been a cycle).
- `check` stays routed in `wf`: it also dispatches to `wf-ship` (`--deployed`) and `wf-vm` (`--health`), which pipeline may not depend on. `dead_refs` asks the router for the live command names through `set_commands`, the same callback shape as `set_publisher`.
- The flow tests of the moved verbs stay in `clis/wf/tests/flow`: they drive the real router, which is the parity oracle for these verbs. Moving them to a `pipeline` thin binary would need the `check` router there too, which the cycle rules out.
- `nx_cache` expectations: a `harness-core` change now also plans `pipeline` and `wf-ship`, since pipeline depends on harness-core and wf-harness.
- Run 40253710624 (e9a868e91): `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` passed, the silent gap before clippy is gone. `deploy (vm-ops-api)` failed on the 200 s build budget: the backend lockfile gained `reqwest` and `tempfile` through the service-kit `blackbox` feature, so the cargo-chef layer was cold. The build keeps running on the VM and fills the layer cache; the budget is not raised, the next push reuses the layers.

## Round 6: `wf repo` dissolved (2026-10-26)

- `wf bench` (cli, the app suites and `agent-proof`) lives in `clis/wf-bench`; `wf knowledge` is its own crate `clis/knowledge` with its flow tests; `wf agent-config sync|mcp-guard` is served by `clis/agent-config-sync` (it gained a `wf-kit` dependency for the usage-row error line); `wf offload ladder` is served by `clis/offload` until the round 8 rename. the old `repo` group and the top-level `mcp-guard` print the new spelling and exit 2.
- The editor bundle is no `wf` verb: `build:editor-bundle` and `check:editor-bundle` are scripts of `frontend/mla-tamagui` over `scripts/cm6-bundle.mjs`, `check:editor-bundle` is a cached Nx target (inputs: the bundle and its source, the script, package.json, esbuild), the staged gate runs it through Nx, and `wf check --drift` runs it through pnpm. `pipeline/src/cm6.rs`, its help text and flow test are deleted.
- Crate names stay `wf-bench`, `knowledge` (new) and `agent-config-sync`: renaming `wf-bench` to `bench` would only move strings through every CRATES list and project file with no behavior change, and the goal's done conditions name verbs and `clis/wf/src`, not these crate names.
- `wf-bench` now depends on `offload` (agent-proof dispatches through the opencode ladder); no cycle, `offload` does not depend on bench or harness.
- Flow tests: the knowledge ledger and Gemini repack tests moved from `wf/tests/flow/report.rs` into `knowledge/tests/flow`; the agent-proof and mcp-guard tests stay in `wf/tests/flow/agents.rs` because they drive the real router.

## Run 40256407776 (27817ea7d): vm-ops-api served an older commit (2026-10-26)

- The leg built in 13 s and printed `vm-ops-api-rs unchanged, re-tagged`: the image of input hash `nx-841560c7...` already existed (the earlier e9a868e91 run's build had finished on the VM after that leg hit its 200 s budget), so `sha-27817ea7` re-tags an image stamped `GIT_COMMIT=e9a868e91`. The swap worked (`startedAt` 02:35:53, `imageTag` sha-27817ea7) but `--wait-serving` wanted `27817ea7`, so the leg failed and the tiers after the deploy legs did not run.
- Cause: the plan decided at a time when the running commit was older than the backend lockfile change, so the leg was planned although the hash is now stable; nothing in the code is wrong and the budget is untouched. The next push plans from running commit e9a868e91 (same hash at HEAD) and does not ship vm-ops-api, as `expected_commit` in `pipeline/src/legs.rs` already encodes. Round 6 (ce2ea528a) was pushed to dev on that reading.

## Run 40260190558 (ce2ea528a, round 6): red in typecheck-lint-unit-tests (2026-10-26)

- Two causes, both read from the job log. `cargo clippy -D warnings` rejected `for noun in ["secret"]` in `clis/wf/tests/flow/surface.rs` (a loop over one element, left by the round 6 edit; fixed in round 7's commit). And the stage hit its 20 min budget: the 20 clippy tasks started at 02:50 and ran until 03:03, cold, because every crate was renamed or moved and the shared target dir had nothing to reuse. The budget is not raised; later pushes find the clippy results in the Nx cache.

## Run 40262843732 (b78cc7a65, rounds 6 to 8): flow-tests hit its 30 min budget (2026-10-26)

- Read from the job log: the stage started 03:35, the first test ran at 03:47 (a cold compile of every renamed and moved crate on the runner), and 12 crates had passed when the budget killed it at 04:06 inside the `deploy` flow suite (41 tests, the last at 04:05:44), with `wf`, `wf-bench` and `knowledge` not yet run. Nothing failed.
- The budget is not raised. Each crate's flow task that passed is stored by Nx, so the next push runs only the tasks that did not finish and finds the rest in the cache; the push of the three doc-only commits on top (`5422f0dd9`, `f39179057` and this entry) is that next push.

## Run 40267446218 (9e009d363): the first run with the integration tier (2026-10-26)

- Green: plan, flow-tests (the passed crates replayed from the Nx cache), typecheck-lint-unit-tests, `clis`, the six deploy legs (vm-ops-api built and swapped, the others unchanged or swapped and served the commit), `integration-tests` (the black-box suites of reporting-service, the capture listener, leetcode-backend and vm-ops-api) and `service-e2e-tests`.
- `cli-e2e-tests` red, three tests. One was mine: the visual judge (`pipeline/src/visual.rs`) and the CLI cold-start targets (`wf-bench`) still ran the old top-level `ask`, which since round 8 prints the notice and exits 2 (the judges failed with `exit 2`); both now run `offload ask`, and the same suite passes locally (wf e2e 1, wf-bench flow 4). The other two are the wf-harness attached-session journeys (`proxies_stubs_and_forwards_network...`: the CLI printed nothing, `reads_pages_and_manages_its_lifecycle`: `page.goto ... did not finish loading within 30 s (page state: the page did not answer)`). They pass locally one at a time (13 of 13 in 73 s) and fail on the runner one test at a time too, each after two 30 s waits, while the other 11 attached and phone-framed journeys pass. The test now keeps the stderr of the Chromium it starts and prints its last 30 lines when it fails, so the next failing run says whether the browser died or hung. No timeout was raised and no test skipped.

## Run 40273009231 (38b6fbc00): a timing margin in the lease flow test (2026-10-26)

- flow-tests failed in one test, `the_device_lease_hands_the_device_to_one_sandbox_at_a_time_in_order` (`wf-harness/tests/flow/android.rs:297`: releasing d's lease exited 1). The test moves a simulated clock, but real time also passes between its steps; d's lease had a 2 s TTL, so the 0.5 s margin between its last heartbeat (603.5 s) and the release (605 s) was gone as soon as spawning the ghost waiter took longer than half a second on the loaded runner.
- The fix keeps the scenario and the order of events and moves the numbers: d's TTL is 10 s, its heartbeat at 609 s, e asks at 613 s (after the original expiry at 612 s, before the extended one at 619 s), and the ghost steps run at 613 s and 648 s. The margin is now 6 s; the test is not skipped and no production timeout changed. Passes locally.

## Run 40276939628 (8b993f594): the attached-session journeys, root cause (2026-10-26)

- The Chromium log the test now keeps shows the cause in `chromium.log`: `FATAL: components/services/font_data/font_data_service_impl.cc:379] Check failed: . : No space left on device (28)`. The renderer's font service died on the runner's small `/dev/shm` (the runner is a container; this sandbox has a 16 GB one, which is why nothing reproduced locally), so the page never answered and `page.goto` waited its 30 s. The harness's own launch (`harness-browser/src/browser_launch.rs`) already passes `--disable-dev-shm-usage`; the Chromium that `Attached::start` spawns itself in `wf-harness/tests/e2e/browser.rs` did not. It does now. One of the two journeys that failed in run 40267446218 passed here by luck of what else held shared memory; the other failed with this FATAL. The 30 s wait is unchanged.

## Run 40277985002 (ef1547bd5): `cli-e2e-tests` was red for two package scripts, not for a CLI (2026-10-26)

- Every other job was green: plan, flow-tests, typecheck-lint-unit-tests, `clis`, `integration-tests`, `service-e2e-tests`, `browser-e2e-tests`, `android-e2e-tests` and the report. In `cli-e2e-tests` all the crates' real-Chromium suites passed (wf-harness 13 of 13 in 85 s, wf-bench, wf, wf-login, harness-flow): the `--disable-dev-shm-usage` fix held.
- The two failures were `mla-tamagui`'s and `@app/remote-chat-agent`'s package script `test:e2e`, which the stage definition pulled in when it asked every package for `test:e2e`. Neither was ever planned before the tier change (the old stage list only knew `test:e2e:deployed`). `mla-tamagui`'s starts a local leetcode-backend binary that this job does not build (`did not answer on :8789 within 20s`); the remote-chat-agent one needs an opencode server on :4096 and says to run `test:e2e:deployed` against the deployed service, which `service-e2e-tests` does. The stage now names a script no package defines (`test:cli-e2e`), so it runs the crates' `cargo-e2e` only. The planner and the test jobs run the wf the previous push published, so this takes effect on the push after the one that carries it.

No budget change. Open lever, not taken yet: seed `clis/target` from the bucket the way the pnpm store is seeded.

The listener `/health`, the Dockerfile `GIT_COMMIT` and `serving_url` of 61abffd81 are removed. `wf ship --wait-serving` now confirms an app without a `/health` by the `sha-<short>` tag of its running image (`running_commit`), registered in `IMAGE_CONFIRMED` (`clis/deploy/src/verify_tier3.rs`) for `vnc-auth-session` and `leetcode-auth-cookie-capture`. - 26 Oct, cache plan step 1 (goal: one bucket, every layer behind `wf cache`): `docs/solutions/ci/cache-layers.md` states the layers; `clis/pipeline/tests/flow/cache_contract.rs` fails on a cached Nx target that is neither a known verdict nor a declared file writer, and on a file writer (`typecheck`, `export:web`) without `outputs`. `nx.json` now declares `outputs` for both (`export:web` also hashes the baked `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` names and `EXPO_EXPORT_FLAGS`), and `frontend/mla-tamagui/tsconfig.json` sets `incremental` with its build info under `node_modules/.cache/tsc`. Finding that changes step 4: Nx restores outputs only for an exact input hash, so a `tsbuildinfo` as an Nx output never speeds a changed-input run; and cold `tsc --noEmit` measured 16.0 s here (n=1), so it is not where time goes. The build info therefore travels in the restore-latest `wf cache metro` tarball with Metro's transform cache, not as a separate cache. Tier: flow, `pipeline` `cache_contract` 2 of 2.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 2, first part (kinds, server, lifecycle): spike with the real sccache 0.10.0 against a WebDAV test server before building anything. Findings: (1) sccache's WebDAV backend uses one endpoint for reads and writes, but the bucket's PARs are one read link and one write link, so a loopback shim routes between them (the same shape as the Nx shim, `blob_cache::SccacheServer`); (2) it needs `GET`, `PUT`, `PROPFIND` (an `XML` multistatus with the DAV file properties, before every write) and `MKCOL`, and its start-up check reads and writes `.sccache_check`; (3) `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` must be 0, because sccache refuses incremental compiles ("incremental" non-cacheable); (4) keys include the target directory path (a build in another `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` missed, the same directory hit: 2 GETs, 0 PUTs), so CI and the runner keep `clis/target` and Docker keeps its own path; (5) a miss costs one GET plus one PUT against the bucket (PROPFIND and MKCOL are answered locally), a hit one GET. `Kind::Sccache` and `Kind::Metro` added with rows in `budget.json` (14 days; 128 MB and 256 MB entry caps) and DELETE rules in `build-cache.tf`. Tier: flow, `blob-cache` (`sccache_server` 3 of 3, `build_cache_tf` and the kind tests, `nx_server` 8) and `wf-bucket` 3 of 3. Next: the `wf cache` verbs.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 2 (`wf cache` surface): `wf cache nx -- <cmd>` and `wf cache sccache -- <cmd>` run a command with one layer served from the bucket (the loopback servers start before and stop after it; `Shim::start`, which `wf test run --stage` and `wf check --staged` use, now serves both layers, adding `RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, a WebDAV endpoint with a per-run token and its own sccache server port, only when `sccache` is on PATH). `wf cache restore|save metro` carries `frontend/mla-tamagui/node_modules/.cache/app-build` (Metro's transform cache under `metro/`, `tsc`'s build info under `tsc/`, both moved there) as one restore-latest object (`blob_cache::app_build`, fingerprint of file names and sizes so an unchanged directory uploads nothing). `wf cache status` gains `layers`: bytes, objects and TTL per kind from one listing. `sccache`'s run has its own limits in `budget.json` (5,000 requests and 2 GiB a run) because a compile cache is many small calls. Tier: flow, `blob-cache` 10 (`app_build` restore and save, `sccache_server` 3, kinds and lifecycle), `wf-bucket` 6 (the two wrappers against a fake bucket and a stub `sccache`, the usage error, `status` layers), `wf` surface 3 (help-table parity and the reference's sections).

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 3, wiring: `sccache` v0.10.0 is pinned in `mise.toml` and installed by `.github/actions/install-sccache` (reads the pin, picks the runner's architecture, skips when already on PATH) in every `deploy.yml` job that installs bws; the build-cache shim adds the compiler wrapper to every Nx child when `sccache` is on PATH, so the unit, flow, integration and clippy tasks of `wf test run --stage` use it with no workflow change beyond the install. Measurements follow in the next entry.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 3, sandbox measurement (n=1, `cargo test -p blob-cache --no-run`, empty `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/sccb/target` each time, sccache 0.10.0 through `wf cache sccache`, 71 compile units): A no cache 24 s; B sccache with a cold bucket prefix 79 s (0 hits, 71 misses, 144 bucket requests, 33.3 MB sent); C sccache with the warm prefix 34 s (71 hits, 0 misses, 73 requests). Request cost per unit: a hit 1 request, a miss 2 (one read, one write); for the whole workspace's 558 libraries that is about 560 requests warm and 1,100 cold. Reading: from this sandbox the bucket answers in about 1 s per call (sum of 70 s of gets and 90 s of puts across 16 workers), longer than a small crate compiles, so the cache is slower than compiling here. That says nothing about the 2 OCPU VM runner, where a cold unit stage compiled for 13 minutes and the bucket is in the same cloud; the decision needs the CI log lines (`wf: sccache {hits, misses}` and `wf: compile cache`) of the first runs with sccache installed. Not concluded; recorded as the baseline to beat.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 4, wiring and the TypeScript number: `wf cache restore metro` and `wf cache save metro` now run around the web export (`e2e-tests.yml`, browser job), the EAS Update publish and the typecheck stage (`deploy.yml`); each is `continue-on-error`, so an older published `wf` (the stage jobs run the previous push's) that does not know the kind exits 2 without failing the job. `tsc --noEmit` measured on the sandbox (n=1): cold 5.4 s, warm with the build info 2.0 s, build info 1.4 MB. That is small next to the unit stage, so the TypeScript layer is a convenience riding in the tarball, not a saving worth a separate cache. The export benchmark follows.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 4, export measurement (sandbox, n=1, `nx run mla-tamagui:export:web`, 3,550 modules): cold Metro cache 194 s; warm Metro cache 76 s (-61 %), Metro cache 57 MB; an Nx miss 75 s; an Nx replay 4 s (restores `dist`). Finding: `dist` is 372 MB, over the 64 MB `nx` entry cap and the 2 GB stored cap, so the bucket never holds the export; the shim drops it as too large and only the runner's local Nx cache replays it (the replay is 4 s there, the local `maxCacheSize` is 4 GB). The layer that crosses runners is therefore the Metro tarball (57 MB raw, 256 MB cap), which is what removes the 118 s of transform work from a cold runner's export. Decision: keep `export:web` declared with `outputs: dist` (the contract and the local replay need it), keep `wf cache restore|save metro` as the shared layer, do not raise the `nx` cap.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 5, Docker experiment, decision: keep `cargo-chef` and the registry `:buildcache`. Method: `reporting-service`, repo-root context, a local Docker 29.6 daemon on the sandbox (4 cores, amd64; Docker Hub login with the vault token for the pulls, removed afterwards), `--network host`, the egress proxy's CA added to the builder, caches pruned before each cold run, same Dockerfile apart from the cache. Numbers (n=1 per row): chef with a cold layer cache 174 s and 192 s (two runs); chef with a warm layer cache and one source line changed 14 s, 0 bucket requests; sccache with a cold prefix 223 s (226 misses, 457 bucket requests, 131 MB sent, `RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache` through BuildKit secrets for the endpoint and token); sccache with a warm prefix 99 s (226 hits, 231 requests). Reading: against the steady state a deploy leg sees (the dependency layer is unchanged and comes from the registry), sccache is slower (99 s against 14 s) and spends 231 requests where chef spends none, so the plan's condition ("sccache no slower") fails; chef's cold build (174 s) is only what a recreated VM or a changed lockfile pays, and sccache's warm run (99 s) beats it, which does not change the steady state. `cargo-chef`, the registry cache export in `clis/deploy/src/build_image.rs` and the Rust Dockerfiles stay as they are. Open: the bucket's latency from the VM was not measured (from the sandbox it is about 1 s a call), so the sccache numbers are an upper bound on the request cost, not on a VM's.
- 26 Oct, cache plan step 6, quota: measured with `wf cache status` (OCI Monitoring and one listing, after the benchmarks): 1.35 GB stored in 1,401 objects (pnpm 1.19 GB, sccache 164 MB in 297 objects, nx 2.6 MB in 1,103, metro 0), 3,897 requests this month. The other buckets of the account hold 5.67 GB (verification artifacts) and 0.59 GB (the legacy pnpm-cache), so the account holds about 7.6 GB against the published 20 GB Always Free allowance (the account's limits API reports no storage cap, so the allowance is a billing threshold). Projection: the whole workspace costs about 560 requests warm and 1,100 cold per cold target; a push that changes Rust on a runner whose target persists asks for the changed crates and their dependants only (about 150), about 100 such pushes a month is 15,000, four cold targets 4,400, the existing Nx, pnpm and Metro traffic 4,000: about 25,000 against the published 50,000. Budget changed in `clis/blob-cache/data/budget.json`: `requestsPerMonth` 60,000 to 50,000 (the published allowance, no longer above it, so no overage request is needed at this projection), `storedBytes` 2 GB to 8 GB (the 2 GB cap would have turned the first full sccache fill, 2 to 4 GB beside the 1.2 GB pnpm store, cold); worst case the account holds 8 + 5.67 + 0.59 = 14.3 GB, under 20 GB. The projection is an estimate until CI runs with sccache installed report their own `wf: sccache` lines.

- 26 Oct, first CI run with the cache layers (run 40326836287, eda7edb9b): `flow-tests` failed in `pipeline` `check::the_staged_gate_gives_nx_the_build_cache_and_writes_to_it_only_from_a_clean_commit_on_dev` (check.rs:308, expected 3 bucket GETs, saw 4). Root cause: the runner has `sccache` installed now, so the shim's compile layer was active in that test and opened a second `Cache`, which read `meta/generation` again. Fix: the compile cache is built with the first cache's generation (`Cache::with_generation`), so a shim reads the pointer once, whatever layers it serves. Verified locally with `sccache` on PATH (the staged-gate tests, 2 of 2). The log also shows where the stage's time goes: the `pipeline` flow crate took 1,033 s inside the 30 min job, `deploy` 356 s, `wf` 51 s; the Nx-cache contract tests in `pipeline` run the real Nx and dominate.

- 26 Oct, cache plan, first green dev run with the cache layers (run 40331470416, b869376d1; every job green or skipped by the planner: 4 stages with nothing to rerun, no test skipped, no timeout raised; the #349 report comment 6418766580 reads "CI passed at b869376"; #349 is still titled `dev → main` with the body ``This is `dev` ``). Timings from the report: typecheck-lint-unit-tests 9 m 11 s (the cold run of 40260190558 took 20 m 10 s, the one before this 6 m 36 s with the Nx cache partly warm), flow-tests 19 m 21 s (the `pipeline` crate's flow tests are 17 of it), cli-e2e-tests 4 m 13 s, browser e2e 12 m 53 s, EAS Update 5 m 03 s (export 238.5 s), Android e2e 11 m 45 s. Build-cache traffic per job from the VM: 16 to 19 requests a stage, reads 85 to 92 ms and writes 77 to 129 ms each (the sandbox saw about 1 s), so a bucket call from the runner costs about a tenth of a second. Metro layer on CI: `wf cache save metro` stored 17.7 MB in 3.2 s (EAS job) and 18.5 MB in 6.8 s (browser job); `restore metro` skipped because the runner's `app-build` directory already had content (the typecheck stage's `tsc` state), which is the intended read of "restore only into an empty directory". The sccache layer did not run yet: the stage jobs run the `wf` the previous push published, and this run's `clis` job published the first `wf` that serves compiles, so the next run's typecheck, flow and cli-e2e stages are the first with `RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`; their `wf: sccache` lines go in the next entry. Bucket sizes after this run (`oci os bucket get --fields approximateSize`): build-cache 1.354 GB in 1,423 objects, legacy pnpm-cache 0.595 GB, verification-artifacts 5.784 GB: 7.73 GB in all, under 20 GB.

- 26 Oct, cache plan, CI evidence for the compile layer (run 40342481065, ae76b01b4, the first run whose stage jobs run the `wf` that serves compiles; green, every flow crate "ran", no e2e unit skipped, the #349 comment 6419624759 reads "CI passed at ae76b01"). `flow-tests` (Rust flow crates, a changed `wf-bucket` and everything built on it recompiled, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` forcing a full rebuild once): `wf: sccache {"hits":"0","misses":"38","writeErrors":"0"}`, `wf: compile cache` 38 reads, 38 writes, 76 bucket requests, 101.7 MB sent, reads 85 ms, writes 211 ms (slowest 1.2 s); the stage took 20 m 45 s against 19 m 21 s in the previous run (about 1 minute more, for 38 cold units plus the one-time flag change). `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` 3 m 33 s and `cli-e2e-tests` 3 m 05 s ran no compile (`wf: sccache` 0 misses, 0 requests: Nx replayed the unit and clippy verdicts and the e2e crates were up to date on the runner's persistent target), against the 20 m 10 s of the cold run that started this work. Reading: on a runner whose `clis/target` persists the compile layer costs a request pair per changed unit and nothing for an unchanged one, so its value shows when a target is cold (a recreated VM, a new checkout) or a bottom crate changes; this run's cold-bucket cost of about one minute is the price of seeding it once. The warm-bucket benefit has no CI measurement yet: it needs a cold target, which the runner does not have; the sandbox measured it (a cold target rebuilt in 34 s against 24 s uncached for a small crate at about 1 s a call, and the 99 s Docker warm run). `restore metro` skipped on CI because the checkout persists; no CI run exercises the export through `nx run mla-tamagui:export:web` (CI exports through `wf test export` and `wf ship eas`), so the export's Nx replay (4 s) and Metro-warm (76 s against 194 s cold) numbers are the sandbox's. Unit-stage cost today: cold 20 m 10 s (renamed crates, empty target), warm 3 m 33 s.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 3, CI measurement (run 40348153062, 62f541175, the `[bench-cache]` step of the typecheck job on the 2 OCPU runner; n=1; the same crate, `cargo test -p wf-bucket --no-run`, 123 compile units, an empty `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` each time, the target removed between rows): A no cache, cold target: 48 s. B sccache through `wf cache sccache`, cold bucket prefix, cold target: 67 s (0 hits, 123 misses, 247 bucket requests, 61.8 MB sent, reads 69 ms and writes 112 ms on average). C sccache, warm bucket prefix, cold target: 24 s (123 hits, 0 misses, 124 requests; reads 132 ms on average). So on the runner a cold target with a warm bucket builds twice as fast as with no cache (24 s against 48 s), seeding the bucket costs 19 s once (67 s against 48 s), and a hit costs one request, a miss two. This is the cold-versus-warm timing the plan asked for, measured on CI at the scale of one crate; the full `cargo-unit` stage's own numbers are the 20 m 10 s cold and 3 m 33 s warm entries above, and the whole workspace's 558 libraries would be about 560 requests warm. Decision: sccache stays on in CI. The step is kept for later measurements and runs only for a commit whose message contains `[bench-cache]`.

- 26 Oct, cache plan step 4, CI export measurement (run 40350695327, e6f7a0016, the `[bench-export]` step of the typecheck job on the 2 OCPU runner, `nx run mla-tamagui:export:web`, n=1, 3,550 modules, the Nx cache served by `wf cache nx`): A cold Metro cache, no Nx cache 291 s; B warm Metro cache, no Nx cache 86 s (-70 %); C an Nx miss 93 s; D an Nx replay 5 s with `dist/index.html` restored. Metro's cache is 57 MB and `dist` 372 MB (the same sizes as the sandbox run, which took 194, 76, 75 and 4 s on 4 cores). So the Metro tarball takes a cold runner's export from 291 s to 86 s, the Nx replay of an unchanged export is 5 s, and the 372 MB export stays out of the bucket (over the 64 MB `nx` cap; the replay is the runner's local Nx cache). The measurement step stays in `deploy.yml` for commits whose message contains `[bench-export]`.

## 2026-10-26 Cache plan: final green CI run

Run 40354479859 on `dev` at `363ee47` passed every stage the planner reached: plan, flow-tests (5 m 07 s), typecheck-lint-unit-tests (49 s), clis, deploy (reporting-service, 1 m 17 s), integration-tests, service-e2e-tests, eas-update (EAS export 231 s), browser-e2e-tests (13 m 26 s) and android-e2e-tests (12 m 10 s, every unit pass). cli-e2e-tests was skipped by the planner (no CLI e2e input changed). No test skipped, no timeout raised. The report is the `ci-comment` on #349 (comment 6421560796). Bucket cache latency from the VM: get 75 to 112 ms, put 83 to 127 ms.

Follow-up: run 40354479859 replayed `cli-e2e-tests` from the Nx cache (last real pass: run 40342481065). 26 Oct, owner decision: the `nx` entry cap goes from 64 MB to 500 MB (`maxEntryBytes` 524288000) and the `nx` per-run transfer cap to 1.5 GB (`bytesPerRun` 1610612736), so the 372 MB web export `dist` crosses runners through the bucket instead of living only in a runner's local Nx cache. This reverses the "do not raise the `nx` cap" line above. Invalidation is by input hash and the 14-day lifetime, and the 8 GB `storedBytes` cap keeps the bucket under the 20 GB allowance (a write over it goes cold). The cache is build storage, not the shipped bundle, so it does not touch the app's first contentful paint. Watch: every app change writes a new export entry, so `wf cache status` stored bytes and request counts need a look after a week.

26 Oct, cache plan step 4, EAS export measurement (CI, run 40382372880, `[bench-eas]`, n=1, `nx run mla-tamagui:export:eas`, ios and android): cold Metro cache with no Nx cache 235 s; Nx miss 231 s (stores a 13.7 MB entry, 3 bucket requests); Nx replay 4 s with `dist/metadata.json` restored. The replay is the cost of an unchanged app before the upload; with the miss at 231 s against the 238 to 243 s the publish job reports, the export dominates the eas-update job and the replay removes it.

## 2026-10-26: Gemini Gem context as one bundle, uploaded through librclone

- Decision: one `repomix-context.md` (ARCHITECTURE.md drafted by `wf offload ask` from a `repomix --compress` skeleton, then the full pack) overwrites one Drive file; the multi-doc bin-packing, the Docs API code and `locate` are gone. `wf offload ask` has no library entry (the prompt path is not in-process), so `knowledge` runs `wf offload ask --prompt-file` (`WF_BIN` overrides the binary).
- Vetting `librclone` 0.9.0 (rclone v1.69.0): MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR CC0, 47 k downloads, last release 2025-02-12 (old: a newer rclone fixes more upstream bugs), build needs a Go toolchain (the VM runner has none; `actions/setup-go` in the nightly job).
- Spike (n=1, Sonnet-free): a release binary linking it is 92.8 MB against 13.2 MB for today's static `wf`; an incremental release build took 12.5 s. A local `operations/copyfile` and its `object not found` error work, and a `:drive,scope=drive,token='{...}',root_folder_id=...:` connection string reached Google (401 for a fake token) from the sandbox, so no config file or environment is needed. Decision from the size: the dependency is the optional cargo feature `rclone` of `knowledge`, off in the shared `wf` build (which is also a musl static binary); the nightly workflow builds `knowledge --features rclone` and flow-tests the upload against a local remote first.
- Not measured: the `wf offload ask` draft quality (no answer within 150 s from the sandbox; the checklist run belongs on the VM) and a real Drive upload (`docs/gaps/gemini-sync-unverified.md`).
- vnc-auth: `Launcher::run_login` no longer takes the lock a navigation holds (the desktop user is a field of the launcher); a flow journey with a never-answering browser endpoint failed with a 20 s client timeout before the change and passes in under a second after it. Merge: `origin/main` was `dev@c82c316dd` (a squash), recorded with `merge -s ours`; the Metro and emulator memory caps of 2026-10-13 are moot because that container infra was retired (`0ca9ab744`).
