## 2026-10-27: viewer lag, and the credential reaches the sandbox

- Measured idle from the sandbox: `/frame` 0.65 s each (0.40 s of it the tunnel, so ~0.25 s a headed software-rendered screenshot, 47 KB), sequential at 160 ms spacing: 1-2 fps, and the full JPEG was sent for an unchanged page. Not measured: the multi-second freezes the owner saw (the host was idle afterwards; during the CI run it had load ~17 and `vnc-auth` had the default CPU weight).
- Change: one capture thread per listener takes numbered frames and stores one only when the JPEG differs; `GET /frame?after=<n>` long-polls (200 with `x-frame-seq`, or 204 after 8 s of no change); taps, scrolls and keys use their own CDP connection so they never wait behind a screenshot; the capture runs fast for 2 s after an input and while the page changes, slow (400 ms) when it holds still, and pauses 20 s after the last viewer request. `cpu_shares: 2048` for `vnc-auth`. Not native `Page.startScreencast`: the `cdp` crate only reads events during a call, so a push stream would need a reader thread first; the poll-with-change-detection keeps the properties that matter (no traffic for a still page, no input stall). Not re-measured on the phone.
- Credential: `wf secret login <tool> --remote` waited for the file to appear on the machine that runs it, which only happens in agent-shell (the listener relays there). It now also asks the listener (`POST /auth/credential {sessionId}`: `ready` plus base64 files, same secret and owner gate, never logged), writes the files under its own home (0600), closes the session, and for gcloud selects the copied account; the wait defaults to the 12 h link life instead of 5 min. The sandbox presets `CLOUDSDK_AUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN` (a placeholder that overrides a local login), so gcloud must run without it; `knowledge`'s token call drops it.
- 2026-10-27, one login command: `wf secret login <tool>` always logs in through the VM's browser and waits until the listener's own validity check passes (so a stale credential on the caller never counts); `--remote` is gone (refused with the reason) and so is the local native-browser branch. A descriptor lists the files a caller may fetch under `copy` (`~/`-rooted, no `..`, validated in `Descriptor::parse`; set for claude, codex, opencode, gemini, gcloud), and `POST /auth/credential {sessionId, copy}` reads only those; `--copy` writes them under the caller's home (0600) and, for gcloud, selects the account with the sandbox's placeholder token dropped. Without `--copy` nothing leaves the VM. Flow journeys: finished/not finished/timeout, `--copy` writes 0600 and selects the account, undeclared and escaping paths are refused; the viewer e2e with real Chromium still passes.
- 2026-10-27, placeholder and `--clear`: nothing in the repo uses `bq`, `gsutil` or the sandbox's placeholder `CLOUDSDK_AUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN` (only my two workarounds did), and an empty value lets gcloud use the stored login (`CLOUDSDK_AUTH_ACCESS_TOKEN= gcloud auth print-access-token` returned a real `ya29.` token). The Claude settings contract now takes an `env` map and `.agents/config/claude-settings.json` blanks the variable for every session in this repo (from the next session start); both workarounds are gone. `wf secret login <tool> --clear` starts from nothing: it deletes this machine's declared `copy` files (gcloud: unsets the account) and calls the listener's `POST /auth/logout {tool}`, which revokes where the tool can (gcloud `auth revoke --all`), deletes the descriptor's credential files, clears the login browser's cookies and cache over CDP and ends the open session; the login then proceeds with a fresh link. agent-shell's relayed copy stays until the new login overwrites it.
- 2026-10-27, relay bug found by `--clear`: the listener relayed a credential file to agent-shell as soon as it EXISTED, and any gcloud command in the login container (my logout's revoke, the validity check) creates an empty `credentials.db`, so agent-shell's good gcloud login was overwritten by an empty file at 11:14 (its `gcloud auth list` is empty now) and the real login that followed was never relayed (the relay fires once). It now relays only when the tool's own validity check passes (`is_authenticated`), with a journey that proves an empty file is never relayed. `/auth/credential` also returns `check` (the validity command's first 200 chars) when a login is not ready, and the client prints it on timeout. The sandbox's background waiter was killed by a worker restart, so a copy needs the foreground command.
- 2026-10-27, why the second sign-in never became "ready": the browser showed "You are now authenticated with the gcloud CLI!" yet the listener's check stayed false. The listener (root) ran gcloud for its validity check and for the logout revoke, and the login runs as `abc`; a root-run gcloud creates `~/.config/gcloud` and its files root-owned, which `abc`'s login then cannot write (the first login, before any root-run gcloud, worked). Not observed inside the container (no exec there), so it is the leading explanation, not proven. Change: the validity check, its diagnostic output and the revoke run through `s6-setuidgid <login user>`, the relay uses the same check, and logout removes the whole `~/.config/gcloud` so root-owned leftovers cannot survive; a journey asserts every one of those commands ran as the login user. One `wf secret login gcloud --clear` is needed after this deploys to wipe what root left behind.
- 2026-10-27, first Gem run: it failed at once because the nightly job runs in `gh-runner`, a different container from agent-shell, with no gcloud and no login. Owner's choice: the login goes into the vault. New registry key `GCLOUD_DRIVE_CREDENTIALS_DB_B64` (base64 of gcloud's credentials.db, created in the deploy-secrets project with `wf secret create --value-stdin --apply`); the job installs gcloud from Google's apt repo, restores the file from the vault, selects the account and carries on. Costs: the refresh token now also sits in the vault and on the runner, and a re-login (`--clear` revokes the old token) means updating that secret by hand. The sandbox's own attempt to upload the repo pack to Drive was refused by the sandbox as exfiltration, so the first upload has to come from the runner.
- 2026-10-27, run 2 of the Gem sync found a hole in the sealed vault copy: the runner's copy was fresh (1 h) and predated the new secret, so the new key read as "not found" although it was in the vault. A key missing from a fresh copy is now looked up once against the live vault (the copy is rewritten) before it is reported missing; a journey proves it (it failed with "not found" without the change).
- 2026-10-27, no hand-edited secret: a descriptor may name a `vault` secret that mirrors one of its `copy` files (gcloud: `GCLOUD_DRIVE_CREDENTIALS_DB_B64` for `credentials.db`). When a login finishes, the client asks the listener for that file and sets the secret (base64, through `update_secret_value`, the vault write token the sandbox has); `--clear` first sets it to a base64 "logged-out" value (the vault takes no empty value and that decodes to no login). Without the write token the login still succeeds and says why. The old value goes through the tool's existing rotation history. Journey: set on login, emptied then set again on `--clear`, a warning without the token, and a spec whose vault file is not a `copy` path is refused.
- 2026-10-27, runs 3 and 4 of the Gem sync still read the key as missing: it had been created in the CI-secrets project (`b9781a83`), and the runner holds only the deploy read token, which reads `mobile-learning-deploy-secrets` (`65501031`). Moved with `wf secret move` (id kept); the deploy read token lists it now. Lesson for the next vault key: a key a runner or VM reads belongs in the deploy-secrets project (`wf secret move --help` says so).
- 2026-10-27, Gem sync run 8: all four ladder models failed in under a second: opencode takes the prompt as one command-line argument (128 KB per argument on Linux) and the draft prompt inlined CLAUDE.md, CONCEPTS.md and a 600 KB skeleton. The prompt now names the three files to read; the skeleton is limited to manifests, entry points, barrels and area guides (162 files, 333 KB, about 92 k tokens measured with repomix here), and the flow stub fails a prompt over 100 KB. Job timing, from the runs: 1.5 to 2.5 min once started (the first run 3 min with the rclone build), the wait before it starts is the shared CI queue (2 to 4 min).

## 2026-10-27: every ask runs on the rca service; the cooldowns are one shared table

Decision: `wf offload ask` (auto, `--no-auto`, `--batch`, the plan worker's implementer, the vision judge, the Gem draft), `--stats`, `--savings` and `ladder clear-cooldown` go through the rca service (remote-chat-agent, `POST /runs`, `GET /runs/log`, `POST /runs/cooldowns/clear`), with no local opencode fallback. The service has no checkout, so the files a prompt reads go in (`--files`, binary as base64) and the files a run changed, created or removed come back in `changes` and are written into the repository. The pool cooldowns live in `HEALTH_RPT.POOL_COOLDOWNS` (one row per pool, ORDS auto-REST) behind rca-ladder's `CooldownStore` port, replacing the per-caller `pool-cooldowns.json` and the vm-ops-api mirror (`cooldown_mirror.rs`, deleted). rca-core moved to `libraries/rca-core`; the ladder is its `plugins/ladder` crate `rca-ladder`.

Why: the gh runner is a different container from agent-shell and had to install opencode and the provider keys itself, and a cap seen by one caller cost every other caller a failed attempt (about 3 minutes each in the first Gem run).

Evidence: flow tests (offload 9, rca-service 45, rca-ladder 5, knowledge 5) and the Chromium visual e2e on a fake service; not yet measured on the VM. Still local: `--runbook` (its agent drives a browser and a device from a checkout, which the service does not have) and the old `/cooldowns` endpoint of vm-ops-api, now unused. Open: `--stats` of the service's log has no per-model token counts for remote rows, because `POST /runs` does not return usage yet.

## 2026-10-28: `wf offload ask` takes opencode's own options and continues sessions

Decision: the ask flags are opencode's: `--auto` (edit and run commands; without it the run is read-only, as in opencode, and `--no-auto` is kept as a no-op), `-f/--files`, `-s/--session`, `-c/--continue`, `--agent`, `--variant`, `--title`. `POST /runs` takes `session`, `agent`, `variant`, `title` and `auto`, and answers with the `session` id and the token `usage`. A read-only run creates its session with deny rules for `edit` and `bash`. A session's staged directory is kept (marked `.rca-session`, `RCA_SESSION_TTL_HOURS`, a day by default) so a later turn finds its files, and only that turn's changes come back; the ladder can switch model between turns because a session is not tied to one. The client saves the last session id in `.agents/tmp/rca-last-session` for `-c`, and sends `.opencode/agents/<agent>.md` with an agent run, since opencode reads project agents from the session's directory. The default flipped to read-only, so the plan worker, `/cheap` and cf-dispatch pass `--auto`.

Open: `fork` is not done. A run that needs the repository itself (`/cheap` on code the caller did not name, the cf researcher and reviewer agents, runbooks) has only the files it is sent; the fix is a checkout the service runs in (a worktree of a pushed ref, which the service's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can clone), tracked in `docs/gaps/rca-runbook-and-remote-run-usage.md`. Token usage now comes back with each run, which closes the zero-token rows noted there.

2026-10-29, the options of `wf offload ask` held to `opencode run`: the 24 options of `opencode run --help` (1.19.0) are recorded in `clis/offload/tests/flow/fixtures/opencode-run-options.json`, each `same`, `unsupported` or `not-applicable` with a reason. A flow journey runs every `same` option (long and short form) against a fake service and checks it reaches the request, refuses `--fork` with why, and checks every `not-applicable` option is rejected and sends nothing. It caught two differences, both fixed: `-p` was `--prompt` where opencode's is `--password` (now not accepted), and `--dir` did not resolve files against the directory the way `opencode run --dir` does. A by-hand test (`cargo test -p offload --test e2e -- --ignored`) compares the table with the installed opencode after an upgrade. Real-model checks: three e2e journeys of the CLI (`-c` continuing a session and `--format json`, read-only without `--auto` and editing with it plus `--variant`, a repository-defined agent) pass against the deployed service in 22 s from this sandbox, and the service's own deployed e2e (`e2e_deployed.rs`: session, read-only, changes, agent, usage) are written and run by the service-e2e stage, not from a sandbox that has no tailnet route for reqwest.

2026-10-29, CI for `fa89e485c` was fully green (cli-e2e, service-e2e, all deploy legs), and the drift test (`cargo test -p offload --test e2e -- --ignored`) passes against opencode 1.19.0. `--title` live: `wf offload ask --no-auto --title <t> --format json` against the deployed service returned `ok` with a session id, so the service's session-create body with `title` is accepted by opencode. Reading the title back is not possible from this sandbox (the opencode server listens on the VM container's localhost only), so the title's visibility rests on the flow journey asserting the request body, not on a live read.

2026-10-29, integration tier for the rca service (`backend/remote-chat-agent/rs/tests/integration.rs`, `test:integration`): the built `rca-service` binary on a free port, a fake opencode (session, SSE events, prompt) and a fake ORDS table of the test, nine journeys over real HTTP, one per `wf offload ask` option as it reaches `POST /runs`: secret gate, `auto` false (edit and bash denied) and true (changes returned), `-m`/models, `--shape` ladder pick with pool, `-f` text and base64 files, path escapes refused, `--agent` plus `--variant` plus `--title`, `-s`/`-c` session continuation, `caller` in the run log. 9 pass in 0.7 s locally with no model call, so no benchmark was needed. It showed that the API's `auto` defaults to true when absent; the CLI always sends it. `wf offload ask` itself is covered by its flow journeys (fake service over HTTP, real binary) and by its e2e against the deployed service; it has no separate integration tier.

2026-10-29, `opencode run` against `wf offload ask` side by side (`clis/offload/tests/e2e/parity.rs`, on demand: `cargo test -p offload --test e2e parity -- --ignored`; needs the opencode binary, the vault key and the tailnet). Nine journeys, same pinned model (`opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash`), same prompts in same-shaped checkouts: plain answer, `-f`, `-c`, `-s`, `--agent`, `--variant`, `--format json`, `--auto`, a refused option. All nine pass, about 55 s and a few cents. One real difference found: without `--auto`, `opencode run`'s build agent still edits files (`--auto` only approves what opencode would ask), while `ask` denies edits and commands (read-only). The test pins that as the known difference; the option table's note now says so. Whether `ask` should follow opencode here is open: the Gem draft and the cf researcher/reviewer agents rely on `--no-auto` being read-only. Also found: opencode resolves `-f` and `--agent` files against `$PWD`, not its working directory, so a harness must set `PWD` (the test does).

2026-10-29, no on-demand tests: the parity e2e and the option-drift test of `wf offload ask` lost their `#[ignore]` and run with the CLI e2e tests of every push that reaches the crate (the `cargo-e2e` task, replayed by Nx while the crate, the option table and the test files are unchanged); the cli-e2e job installs `opencode-ai@1.19.0` for them and reads `OPENCODE_GO_API_KEY` from the vault. The remaining `#[ignore]` attributes are the `e2e_deployed.rs` files of the four Rust services: they run on every CI push through `test:e2e:deployed` (`-- --ignored`), the attribute only keeps a bare `cargo test` from calling a deployed service. Nothing else in the repository is a test that waits for someone to run it.

2026-10-30, `wf offload ask` made to behave as `opencode run` where a remote run can, after the parity e2e found where it did not. (1) `--auto`: opencode's build agent edits and runs commands with or without it; the flag only approves what opencode would ask (env files, other directories, repeated calls). The service now creates the session with those asks approved (`auto`) or rejected (not), a run's changes always come back, and `--no-auto` is refused with the way to a read-only run: `--agent read-only` (`.opencode/agents/read-only.md`, which the Gem draft and the visual judge use now). The default shape is `document`; `--shape implementer:unit` is explicit for the plan worker, the batch and `/cheap`. (2) Output: stdout is each finished text part and a newline, the header and tool trace (read, write, edit, bash, glob, grep as opencode prints them, byte for byte in a unit test) are on stderr, and `--format json` prints the turn's events one per line, built by the service from opencode's own parts. (3) `-f` also carries the file as a file part of the message (`attach`), so the model sees it without a tool call. Not matched: unknown-option exit code (wf's 2, opencode's 1) and the directory itself (the service has no checkout; `--files` names what a search may see). Evidence against a local service and opencode with the real model, 18 e2e tests of the CLI pass: 8 option journeys beside `opencode run` (plain, `-f`, `-c`, `-s`, `--agent`, `--variant`, JSON event shapes, tool sequences), the permission mapping (an outside file is refused without `--auto` and read with it, on both), a read-only agent, a three-turn session that writes, appends and counts a file, two interleaved sessions taken up by id, a file changed between turns, and a task that greps, writes and runs bash. Service integration 9, flow 24 and e2e_deployed (3 run locally) pass. Cost: about 190 s and a few cents a run of the suite.

2026-10-30, the capabilities of opencode's docs (config, rules, agents, commands, permissions, skills, tools, custom tools, plugins, MCP, formatters, models, sessions) put through `opencode run` and `wf offload ask` side by side, 62 e2e tests of the CLI in all (`clis/offload/tests/e2e/parity*.rs`, run by the cli-e2e job with opencode 1.19.0 installed) with a real model, plus 13 integration tests of the service binary and 14 flow tests of the CLI. They found and fixed: the service's session did not carry what `opencode run` creates it with (question, plan_enter and plan_exit denied) and answered nothing when opencode asked a permission, so a run that asked stalled to its idle limit (now `permission.asked` is replied: approved with `--auto`, rejected without); the project's `opencode.json`, `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, `.opencode` agents, commands, skills, plugins and tools, the skills of `.claude` and `.agents`, and the files they name were not sent (now sent, with the local MCP servers that install a package dropped); `--command` was not carried (now it is, through opencode's command endpoint); an unknown agent was an error, not a fallback; the first request to a directory with plugins timed out while opencode installed their dependencies (180 s now); `node_modules` and install files of that were returned as the run's changes and used up the reply budget before the real files (excluded, and pruned from the kept session directory); a file a background job made after a turn was never delivered (the next turn on the session returns it); `-c` with no earlier session errored (now starts one); the message words are quoted and joined, and a pipe appended, as opencode does; a failed run prints one error event in `--format json`. The server image is now opencode 1.19.0, the version the tests pin. Each paragraph of the docs that has a counterpart on the gateway has a test; what has none is listed in `docs/gaps/rca-runbook-and-remote-run-usage.md`. Cost: about 15 minutes and a few cents a full run of the suite.

2026-10-30, the CI budget (`wf ci budget`) and a preflight job (`wf ci preflight`). First version judged each job and the wall time against the 75th percentile of the last ten green `dev` runs with a 25 % + 60 s slack; replayed on the 58 real push runs (15 green) it flagged 5 of 11 judgeable green runs, because green wall times run from 2 minutes to 98 minutes with the cache (cold against replayed), so it was made report-only and replaced: each Nx task is now judged against itself in a ledger (HEALTH_RPT.CI_TASKS, CI_JOBS; Nx's own `run.json` gives task, hash, ran or replayed, ms). A task that ran although its hash was produced before (and the task has been replayed once, entry under 13 days old) fails the run; a hash that moved with no affected project, and the same work under one hash taking 50 % and 30 s longer, are reported. The time limits that do not depend on the cache were set from the same real runs (14 green runs: idle time between jobs 8 to 47 s except the 30 Oct stall at 590 s; runner wait not explained by another run's interval 2 to 526 s except the stall at 1489 s): idle at least 120 s and 5 %, wait over 600 s. Those two flag only the stall. `enforce` stays false for the soft checks until about 20 runs have filled the ledger; the cache-refused-write and should-have-hit checks fail the run from the start. Evidence: ledger round trip against the real database (`cargo test -p pipeline --lib live_ledger -- --ignored`), preflight run live from the sandbox (cache probe written and read back, database and tailnet services up, vault secrets usable; the sandbox's own full disk and missing docker were the failures it reported), 22 flow tests of the verbs against a fake GitHub API and a ledger file. Not yet seen: the budget and preflight jobs in a real CI run.

2026-10-31, the VM's memory and disk, measured (reporting-service samples in the Autonomous Database, `wf vm exec` into agent-shell, vm-ops-api `/stats`). Memory: 7.0 of 11.9 GB in use with nothing running but CI, of which `remote-chat-agent-opencode` held 4.3 to 4.8 GB: its history climbs from 0.35 GB after the 18:00 redeploy to 4.8 GB by 22:00 while CI's `cli-e2e-tests` ran 62 real-model sessions against it, and it gives none back (opencode keeps every session in memory; the container had no memory limit). Disk: 122 to 131 GB used of 145 to 150 GB (81 to 85 %), flat across the day rather than growing; the agent-shell workspace holds only 9.2 GB of it (6.9 GB of kept ask directories), `agent-shell-dind` has written 53 GB, and no compose file limited a container's log size, so the host's Docker data (images, build cache, json logs) is the rest; the host's Docker could not be listed from agent-shell. Changes: the opencode container is capped at 3 GB (the kernel restarts it, its sessions are on the workspace volume), the service deletes an ask session from opencode when its kept directory expires and keeps those directories 3 hours instead of 24 (`RCA_SESSION_TTL_HOURS`), every prod compose service rotates its log (3 x 20 MB), the redeploy script's prune at 70 % now also trims the build cache to 6 GB and removes stopped containers, and `wf ci tidy` frees the runner volume (rust incremental state, old wf installs, week-old cargo targets) above 70 % and prints the biggest directories into the job summary so the next full disk is explained by the data. Not measured yet: the split of the host's 112 GB outside the workspace; the runner volume's breakdown arrives with the next run's summary.

2026-10-31, the flow-tests job (21 minutes of a 25 minute run) measured test by test (`cargo test -p pipeline --test flow -- -Z unstable-options --report-time`, RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1). Of the pipeline crate's flow tests, ten are the Nx cache contract suite, which runs the real Nx (not the stubbed `nx` the other flow tests use) over a copy of the repository dozens of times each: 208, 168, 92, 77, 59, 56, 53, 32, 20 and 17 seconds, about 780 s of the suite; every other test in the file takes under 10 s. The suite was rerun on every change to the crate, including edits to the CI report that cannot touch the contract. It is now its own test binary (`tests/nx_contract.rs`) and its own Nx task (`cargo-contract`, inputs: the crate's sources and nx.json, tooling/nx, every project.json and package.json), and `wf test plan` selects it only when the plan is not a change, the crate is reached through a dependency or the Nx graph, or one of the crate's own changed files can move the contract (everything except `ci_*.rs`, `nx_ledger.rs`, the help texts and the report's flow test, so a new file runs it until it is classified). Expected effect: a change to the CI report runs the pipeline crate's flow tests in about a minute instead of fourteen; a change to the Nx modules, `nx.json`, `tooling/nx` or any manifest still runs the contract. The weekly cache audit and the forced cold run still run everything.

## 2026-10-31: the VM has 2 cores; where a run's time goes (run 12acfbb, measured)

- `nproc` on the VM is 2 and `CPU_OCPUS` is 2.0. A container's CPU is docker's cores x 100, the host's is a share of both cores: the runner's 135% is 1.35 of 2 cores. The earlier "60% unattributed" read the 2 cores as 4. The host sample now also stores steal and iowait; the report splits the busy cores into containers, hypervisor and the rest.
- Warm Nx stages are cheap: flow-tests 1m40s, typecheck-lint-unit-tests 1m32s (`--parallel 2`, one cargo target dir per workspace, `outputs: []` on every cargo target, one `--all-targets` clippy per crate).
- The same run's deploy legs took vm-ops-api 4m53s (failed on the build-silence guard), remote-chat-agent 7m23s, leetcode-backend 4m04s: the Cargo profile edit changed every backend image's input hash and the cargo-chef dependency layer. A run that touches no service hashes deploys nothing.
- Decision: no CARGO_BUILD_JOBS cap (it slows a stage that runs alone); no linker or tmpfs change (CPU-bound, not I/O-bound). Next measure: the per-stage CPU seconds and bytes written that `wf test run` now prints (`wf: nx cost`).

## 2026-10-31: the parity e2e suite no longer runs for a dependency-only change

- Run 4e55c57 (measured): flow-tests 22m 17s, typecheck-lint-unit-tests 11m 09s, and cli-e2e-tests still running after 30 min. The same `pipeline` rebuild takes 11 s on the 4-core sandbox. cli-e2e-tests is the `offload` crate's e2e suite: about 60 tests, 40 of them parity tests that run a live model through rca beside opencode, one at a time (600 s timeouts, a `sleep 70` test). It is model wait, not VM CPU, and a change in `wf-vmops` reached it through the dependency graph.
- Change: a crate reached only through a dependency or the Nx graph runs `cargo-e2e-smoke` (the same suite `--skip parity`); `cargo-e2e` with the parity tests runs for a full plan (a forced dispatch, the weekly cold run) and when a file of the crate itself changed. `parity_selected` in `test_plan.rs`, the Nx target in `tooling/nx/cargo-crates.js`, the target in the drift list.
- Also found: the vm-ops-api "promoting latest failed" on every push was the workflow asking for `sha-<7>` where the registry's tags are `sha-<8>` (fixed in deploy.yml).
- Not measured yet: the smoke run's duration (first run that reaches it).

- Measured in the 4-core sandbox, `--skip parity`: wf 23 s, harness-flow 14 s, wf-bench 23 s, wf-harness 64 s, wf-login 17 s (2.4 min for the five). The 19-31 min `cli-e2e-tests` of runs 4e55c57, da1d256 and 4fd61b8 is therefore the `offload` crate's `ask::` tests (live models through rca) that the smoke split still ran; with opencode's 3 GB cap, rca was also OOM-killed during them (reporting-service `oom` events at 04:17, 05:08, 05:26). Decision: a crate with `tests/e2e/parity.rs` runs no e2e for a dependency-only change; opencode's cap is 5 GB (applied by a hand `wf ship remote-chat-agent --ref dev`, since a compose-only change does not move the image input hash).
- Attribution: the report's container CPU averaged the minute before the run and each container's own sweeps, so "not in any container" read 38-44%; on the corrected average the first sample was 2.00 busy = 1.95 containers + 0.08 hypervisor. The "I/O wait" row is the kernel's I/O pressure (PSI, peaks 39-45%); the CPU's own I/O wait, the gate's measure, peaked at 4-9%.

- `wf ci tidy` ran on every run's preflight and walked the runner's whole work volume to size its candidates and list the biggest directories: 19-26 s and about 800 MB of reads each time (CPU I/O wait 17%, PSI 39% on the preflight sample of run 39548ec). It now measures nothing while the disk is under its high-water mark.

- Correction to the spawn counts above: `strace -e execve` also logs each failed `PATH` lookup (ENOENT) of a spawn, so 111k / 80k / 75k overcounted. - Ratchet (31 Oct, about 16 judged ledger runs): the one false flag of the window was the `cpu-iowait` peak of 5929744, "15% (limit 15%)": the peak was 15.x and the test compared the unrounded value with the limit. It is now judged on the whole percent the report shows (a 15.4 passes, a 15.6 breaches; unit test). With that, the budget job gave "no waste found" on every other judged run (no `cpu-seconds`, `should-have-hit`, `key-drift`, `slower-same-work`, `runner-wait`, `idle` or `io-wait` breach in the last eight; the earlier ones were the `tidy` walk and cold-cache runs, both fixed), so `.github/ci-budget.json` is set to `"enforce": true`: the soft breaches now fail the run's last step too. The ceilings: `cpuSecondsPerRun` 1000 (executing runs 860, 895, 787), `cpuIowaitPeak` 15 (10 would flag two of ten), `ioWaitPeak` 60. To revert, set `enforce` back to false; the hard breaches (`should-have-hit`, `cache-read-only`) fail the run either way.

## 2026-10-31: the Gem sync's trigger and its proof (no run yet)

- Plan agreed with the owner: the manual dispatch becomes an empty `[gem-sync]` commit to `dev` that must not start the CI; the schedule becomes every ten minutes (test phase, `--no-summary`, queueing in the existing `gemini-context` concurrency group) and returns to nightly after the queueing and the in-place overwrite have been seen. `deploy.yml` gets `paths: ['**', '.*', '.*/**']` on its push trigger so an empty commit starts nothing; whether GitHub honours that for an empty commit is checked on the first tagged push (a `deploy.yml` run for that SHA means the fallback in the plan: a separate concurrency group and an `if` on the tag).
- `knowledge index --gemini` reads the Drive folder back after the upload and fails on a missing, duplicated, wrong-sized or stale file; a Drive destination without a login now fails before the draft, not after minutes of model time. Local tiers with the rclone feature (Go 1.24, libclang): 3 unit, 5 flow, 5 integration pass, clippy clean with and without the feature; disabling the early login check makes the integration test for it fail (mutation check).

- Gem sync, live: the empty `[gem-sync]` commit starts no `deploy.yml` run (two commits, 0 runs each) and, through the `gate` job, runs the sync: run 41220136392 green, 24 minutes from the push to the end including the wait for the runner behind the CI run of the previous commit, one upload of 15,881,840 bytes (1,279 files) read back with a fresh modified time. The first version of the job-level condition did not work: GitHub gives `null`, not `[]`, for the file lists of an empty commit.

- `main` merged into `dev` (strategy `ours`) to clear the conflicts of the standing dev → main PR #351: `main` held one commit `dev` lacked, 56f0d54 (the merge of #349), whose tree is identical to that of dev's a41975a, so nothing on `main` was dropped; the conflicts came from the old common ancestor (30fc841, #348) that the merge of #349 left, and recording `main` as an ancestor of `dev` repairs it. Merging a standing dev → main PR with a merge commit keeps that ancestry; a squash cuts it again.

## 2026-10-31 night: all CI crons removed

- GitHub starts scheduled workflows hours late in this private repo (the Tailscale watch `0 6 * * 1` fired at 14:20, 12:01 and 12:12 UTC on its last three Mondays; the Nx audit `17 3 * * 1` at 09:03; the ten-minute Gem cron produced no run in 63 minutes after it reached `main`), so the owner had every `schedule` trigger removed: `repomix-gemini-context.yml`, `cold-ci.yml`, `nx-cache-audit.yml`, `tailscale-auth-key-expiry.yml`, `ci-budget.yml`. The workflows keep `workflow_dispatch` (the Gem sync: the tagged empty commit). `ci-budget.yml`'s dispatch now takes a true/false value, because the monthly reset was its only way to set the flag back to false.
- Consequences, accepted: no weekly cold run (the `cold-slower` check of `wf ci budget` and the backstop for the contract suite's selection have no fresh cold runs unless someone dispatches Cold CI), no cache audit, no warning before the Tailscale auth key expires, no automatic resume of CI after a budget flag, no nightly Gem refresh.

## 2026-10-31 night: the crons are Claude scheduled tasks

- The GitHub `schedule` triggers stay removed (they start hours late here). Each former cron is now a Claude scheduled task that pushes an EMPTY commit to `dev` whose message carries a tag, and the workflow runs for that push, from `dev`'s copy of the file (a push needs no merge to `main`): Gem sync nightly 02:47 UTC `[gem-sync]`, cold CI Sunday 04:17 `[cold-ci]`, Nx cache audit Monday 05:17 `[nx-audit]`, Tailscale key watch Monday 06:07 `[ts-key-watch]`, CI budget reset on the 1st at 00:07 `[ci-budget-reset]`. All five use the shared reusable workflow `tag-gate.yml`, which starts only for a tagged commit that changes no file (read from the commits API: the push event's file lists are null for every commit, which is why the first gate, built on them, let a commit through whose message only mentioned the tag). `deploy.yml` starts only when a push changes a file, so these commits start no CI run.
- Cost: each fire is one short Claude session (the failed test fire cost about $0.10), a few dozen a month.

- The clocks run through a hub session. A scheduled task that starts a fresh session cannot push: that session has no repository and no `add_repo` tool (two test fires, both "No such tool available"). So a long-lived session, "CI clock hub" (`session_01MdgrcfKcdme9NVXNuJ2fnB`, created in the "No holds barred" environment from `dev`), holds the checkout, and the five scheduled tasks are bound to it (`persistent_session_id`): Gem sync nightly 02:47 UTC (`trig_011Nc1zs…`), CI budget reset on the 1st 00:07 (`trig_016HVkev…`), cold CI Sunday 04:17 (`trig_01R9nkAm…`), Nx cache audit Monday 05:17 (`trig_01PxJpeb…`), Tailscale key watch Monday 06:07 (`trig_01AHDWgG…`). A task's session cannot be changed in place, so changing a binding means delete and create.
- Proof of the chain (test fire of the CI budget clock): the hub pushed an empty commit with the tag (9b4b4a3, 0 files); `ci-budget.yml` ran, `gate` (tag-gate.yml) succeeded, the Gem workflow's run was skipped, `deploy.yml` started no run. The last job, "Set CI_BUDGET_LOW", failed with `HTTP 403: Resource not accessible by integration` from `gh variable set`: the workflow's built-in token cannot write repository variables, whatever `permissions:` says. That predates the clocks (the old monthly cron would have failed the same way) and is unfixed: it needs a token with the Variables permission as a secret, which the monthly reset cannot work without. The flag only gates the hosted-minutes workflows (`ci.yml`, `ci-gate.yml`), not the VM pipeline.

## Gem knowledge as several Google Docs, no rclone (2026-10-31)

- Decision (user): the Gem's knowledge is several Google Docs (a Drive-linked Doc refreshes inside a Gem; a markdown file does not), up to ~9M chars.
- Measured: the full compressed pack is 13.6M chars, of which 8.1M is generated data (stroke tables 5.9M, three golden snapshots 3.1M, key registry 0.1M). With `PACK_IGNORE` the uncompressed pack is 6.66M chars, 1,273 files, so 8 parts of at most 900k plus the architecture Doc make 9 of the Gem's 10 files. Over 10 Docs the run fails.
- Dropped librclone (Go, libclang, an 80 MB archive): plain Drive REST over `cli-http` creates a Doc or `PATCH`es media over the Doc of the same name, then reads it back (mime type, exported chars within 2%, modified within 15 min). A fake Drive on loopback now runs in the integration tests, which the rclone build could not do.
- Evidence tier: unit (splitter, verdict), flow, integration (fake Drive). Unproven until the first live run: whether Drive converts a `text/plain` media `PATCH` over an existing Doc in place, and whether the Gem refreshes a linked Doc (docs/gaps/gemini-sync-unverified.md stays open).

1 Nov, hexagonal-rings CI plan, step 1 baseline (read-only; the 74 completed `dev` runs of 27 to 31 Oct that one `gh run list` page returns, cancelled runs excluded; a stage counts as run when its job took at least 120 s, shorter ones are Nx replays; change class from the commit's file list):

| Stage | Run | Skipped by plan | Median | Max | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| flow-tests | 42 | 21 | 723 s | 1515 s | 28,429 s |
| cli-e2e-tests | 17 | 29 | 2,169 s | 3,471 s | 31,804 s |
| typecheck-lint-unit-tests | 13 | 2 | 460 s | 921 s | 6,589 s |
| deploy | 15 | 54 | 238 s | 403 s | 3,544 s |
| service-e2e, browser e2e, Android e2e | 1 each | 55, 10, 10 | 217, 587, 729 s | | |

Findings: (1) 81 % of the stage seconds (58,338 of 71,899) sit in pushes that touch `clis/`; `cli-e2e-tests` and `flow-tests` alone are 60,233 s of them. (2) Pushes touching only `docs/` ran a real stage 2 times in 17. Both are catch-up, not ring leakage: the one inspected (`4e55c57`, a decision-log edit) deployed four services and ran cli-e2e because the running images and the published `wf` were behind after earlier cancelled runs, which the plan's input-hash rule does by design. (3) Backend pushes are few (9 of 74) and their stages follow the plan's package graph. Decision rule of the plan: no stage reran, more than once, on a change class the rings say it should ignore, so step 3 (ring-scoped levers for backend or the app) is not built. The standout is the CLI crate graph, where `wf-kit` sits at the bottom and every crate above it reruns its flow and e2e when it changes; that is the `wf-crate-split` question, not a ring-scoping one, and needs per-crate attribution of `cli-e2e-tests` (which crate's e2e ran on which change) before any change. Limits: one `gh` page (the proxy refuses page 2), so earlier runs are missing; job durations are wall time on a 2-core VM shared with other jobs.

1 Nov, hexagonal-rings CI plan, step 2 (core purity): the workspace `tokio` in `backend/Cargo.toml` now enables only `macros`, `rt`, `sync` and `time`; each crate that does I/O (`service-kit` and the six `rs` crates) names `fs`, `io-util`, `net`, `process`, `rt-multi-thread` and `signal` itself. New `backend-core-io-violations` budget (`clis/pipeline/src/core_purity.rs`, in `wf check --budget`, so on every commit and in the staged gate) counts a core that depends on an I/O crate or builds tokio with an I/O feature, and the workspace tokio carrying one; limit 0, gated. Planted-violation unit tests in the module and a flow scenario in `clis/pipeline/tests/flow/check.rs`. The real repo reads 0. `cargo check --locked --workspace --all-targets` on `backend/` passes (1 m 13 s warm). Correction to the plan's context: `vm-ops-api`'s core does have unit tests (its `test:flow` runs `-p vm-ops-api-core`); what the plan got right is that no check kept the cores' tokio free of I/O. Still open: `remote-chat-agent`'s core domain reads the clock and makes UUIDs (`domain/ids.rs`), which this check does not see; recorded in the architecture doc's holes.
- Cleanup (user): each sync trashes (never permanently deletes) files in the Gem folder whose name starts with the Doc prefix and that no part replaced, e.g. the old `repomix-context.md`. Files of other names and other folders are untouched. Fake-Drive integration test covers both.


1 Nov, owner: "the whole point of the sandbox is that you do everything you can locally so CI can skip the already executed lints and tests". Finding: the staged gate (`wf check --staged`) only gated TypeScript files (`is_code`: ts, tsx, js, mjs, cjs, json), so a Rust-only or `Cargo.toml` change ran no Nx task at all, and I had run the backend with direct `cargo check` and `cargo test`, which Nx cannot replay. Enforcement added: `wf check --pushed` (`clis/pipeline/src/pushed.rs`) is a lefthook pre-push job that plans `--changed --base origin/dev` and runs CI's `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` and `flow-tests` stages through `wf test run --stage`, i.e. the same Nx tasks, crates included. A push to dev (read from the ref lines git gives the hook, `use_stdin`) runs with `Scope::Pushed`, so its passes are written to the shared cache from a clean tree; any other push only reads. `git push --no-verify` is denied for the agent (`deny.json`, tested). The backend packages' flow tests were also run through `wf cache nx -- nx run-many -t test:flow`: reporting-service and vm-ops-api passed (2 misses, 2 puts to the bucket), service-kit passed. Not covered by the pre-push: deploys, integration and service e2e (they need the VM or the deployed services), browser and Android e2e, and the Docker image builds.

1 Nov, owner: local runs can cover integration and browser e2e. `wf check --pushed` now also runs `integration-tests` (fakes and loopback only; it ran after the deploy in CI by ordering, not by need) and `browser-e2e-tests`. A browser test that would run (the stage's `--probe`) needs the export CI's job builds, so the gate runs the same prerequisites: `wf vm net up`, `wf vm ops probe` of the services the tests call, the tailnet addresses and the vault's `VM_GATEWAY_AUTH_SEED` (plus the BetterStack pair when both exist) baked into `wf test export`, and stops the push naming what is missing; an all-replay stage needs none of it. Service e2e (`test:e2e:deployed`) stays in CI: it checks that the deployed service serves the pushed commit, which exists only after the deploy, and an unchanged service already replays. Android e2e and the image builds stay in CI (the emulator and Docker run only there). The same prerequisites still live as bash in `e2e-tests.yml`'s "Export the web app" step: moving them into `wf test export` so CI and the gate share one implementation is the follow-up. Also learned: the `uname -sm` input was already removed from the browser key (`cdb9fc0ec`), and the sandbox disk (3.6 GB free of the session's allowance) is the practical limit on cold Rust builds here; `backend/target` (3.6 GB) was deleted to make room.

1 Nov, renames and corrections (owner). (1) `wf test export` is now `wf test prepare --web`: it prepares what the browser e2e tests run against and is not a test; `wf test export` answers with the new name and exit 2. (2) The `test:e2e:deployed` suites check that a deployed service is healthy, refuses what it should, runs the pushed commit and logs to BetterStack, so they are deployment verification, not e2e: `test:verify:deployed`, `tests/deployed_verification.rs`, the plan kind and CI stage `deployment-verification-tests` (the job and its `deployment_verification_tests` plan output were `service-e2e-tests`; a branch-protection rule naming the old job needs the new name). They stay in CI: they test the deployed commit, which exists only after the deploy. (3) Correction: I said results that depend on live services should not be replayed from the Nx cache. Wrong. Nx keys on a task's inputs, and a sandbox pass replays on CI when they match; the browser e2e tests already work that way against the VM backends. A future service-behaviour suite against real dependencies is an Nx target keyed on the service and what it calls, run by the pre-push gate and replayed by CI. It does not exist yet: `integration.rs` uses fakes and the deployed suites verify a deployment. (4) `wf check --pushed` is covered by a flow test of its orchestration (`WF_PUSHED_EXE` stands in for `wf`): it plans against origin/dev, runs the four stages in CI's order, does not skip the rest after a red one, writes to the cache only for a push to dev, asks for no export when the browser stage is all replays, and stops the push naming the failed stage.

Now `Clock` and `IdSource` are traits in `domain/ids.rs`; `Engine::new` and `Translator::new` take them; the service implements them in `rs/src/infrastructure/system.rs` and wires them in `api/app.rs`; the core ships `testing.rs` (`FixedClock`, `CountingIds`, feature `testing`) and a unit test proves a translator's events now carry stub ids and a stopped instant, which was not testable before. - 1 Nov, why sandbox passes did not replay on the VM (run 41271777485: 1 of 10 unit tasks and 0 of 15 clippy tasks replayed, 17 min typecheck stage, 94 min run). Evidence: `wf test drift --against` of a table printed by the runner (probe step, run 41273980879) and the sandbox. For `crate-harness-core:cargo-unit` the only differing input is `AllExternalDependencies`: Nx hashes every npm package into a task with no resolved dependency, and the npm graph names differ by CPU (`@esbuild/linux-arm64@0.27.7` on the aarch64 runner, `linux-x64` in the x86_64 sandbox). Fix: every `cargo-*` target names `{ externalDependencies: [] }` (tooling/nx/cargo-crates.js), so a Rust task's key no longer holds the npm graph; verified locally that the input is gone. Still open: package tasks that really use esbuild or vitest (for example `@lib/hook-runtime:test:unit`) hash the arch-specific optional packages and will not replay across CPUs; run 41273980879 showed in-CI replay is 100 % (10/10 unit, 15/15 clippy, 13 s typecheck stage). The other 94 min were not cache misses of the sandbox kind: a workspace Cargo.toml change rebuilt all six service images (28 min) and the flow stage ran 29 min cold.

- 1 Nov, result of the Rust-key fix (run 41278408462, b0bf9a8b5): the pre-push gate ran typecheck, lint, unit, flow, integration and browser stages cold in the sandbox once (new keys) and wrote them; CI then took 4 min 50 s, green: flow-tests 37 s, typecheck-lint-unit-tests 46 s, cli-e2e 39 s, browser-e2e 26 s, no deploy leg (no service image input changed). Against the 94 min of run 41271777485 (same refactor, keys that did not match). The next Rust service refactor is the test of the rule: gate, push, CI under 20 min.

- 1 Nov, service refactor under the 20 min target (reporting-service sampler times Docker through its `Clock` port, 04ce30a70; gate then CI run 41278872927): the gate passed all four sandbox stages and wrote them; CI took about 6 min 15 s end to end, green: flow-tests 46 s, `deploy (reporting-service)` 1 min 37 s (the one service whose image input changed), integration 25 s, deployment verification 29 s, typecheck stage skipped (nothing the stage selects changed). Together with the remote-chat-agent core/rs refactor (94 min on the run whose keys did not match; the fix is the entry above) the rule holds: a service refactor gated in the sandbox costs CI minutes, not tens of minutes.

Not yet fixed: package tasks that use esbuild or vitest hash arch-specific optional packages (see the entry above), so a JS-package change still reruns on the VM after a sandbox pass.

- 1 Nov, package-task keys across CPUs fixed (`supportedArchitectures: {os: [linux, darwin], cpu: [x64, arm64]}` in pnpm-workspace.yaml, e5e704b64; run 41280702853): Nx reads the hoisted versions from node_modules, and the x86_64 sandbox and the aarch64 runner installed different optional packages (esbuild, lightningcss), so the npm node names and every package task key differed. With both CPUs' packages installed on both sides, the sandbox gate (cold once, new keys) wrote the package stages and CI took 8 min 26 s, green: typecheck-lint-unit-tests 30 s (a replay), browser-e2e 28 s, flow/integration/cli-e2e skipped (nothing they select changed), eas-update 5 min (the app's own export). The gap file `nx-js-keys-differ-by-cpu` is closed by this entry. Still true: a workspace-manifest change rebuilds every service image, as it should: their image input hash changed.

- 1 Nov, correction to the entry above: the package-task keys are not fixed. The probe table of run 41280702853 against the sandbox's (`wf test drift --against`) shows `crate-harness-core:cargo-unit` identical (the Rust fix holds) and `@lib/hook-runtime:test:unit` still different in 17 inputs: the hoisted version Nx names differs (`jsdom` 30.0.1 on the runner, 27.4.0 in the sandbox), so `supportedArchitectures` was not the whole cause. The 8 min 26 s of run 41280702853 is real but shows only that a push with nothing else to run is fast, not that package tasks replay across the two machines; the gap file is open again with the evidence. `supportedArchitectures` stays (harmless; it removes the CPU-specific part).

- 1 Nov, cause of the package-task key drift found and fixed. Nx names the hoisted version of each npm package from `node_modules`, but caches its project graph (`.nx/workspace-data`) by the lockfile alone: the sandbox's graph had been built under an earlier install (jsdom 30.0.1 named as the hoisted one) while the installed tree hoisted 27.4.0, so every package task keyed differently from the runner's, whose graph matched its tree. Not the CPU, not the runner's install (a fresh install there changed nothing; run 41281860202 reverted), not `supportedArchitectures` (kept). After deleting `.nx/workspace-data` in the sandbox, `wf test drift --against` of the runner's table (run 41282369160, probe step) reports `ok: true`, 2 of 2 compared tasks the same (`@lib/hook-runtime:test:unit` and `crate-harness-core:cargo-unit`). Fix: `wf check --pushed` drops the cached graph whenever the hash of `node_modules/.modules.yaml` differs from the one it was built under (`drop_stale_graph`, unit tested), so the gate's keys always follow the installed tree. This supersedes the two gap entries above; `docs/gaps/nx-js-keys-differ-by-cpu.md` is closed. The CI runner can have the same stale graph after a reinstall; its table matched the sandbox's fresh graph today.

- 1 Nov, backend dependency decoupling, step 0 spike and first service (plan `declarative-knitting-hummingbird`: one Cargo workspace per service). Spike (sandbox, n=1, cold target dir, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, reporting-service `cargo test -p reporting-service-rs --test flow`): shared backend workspace 80 s, peak RSS 417 MB, target 637 MB; the service's own workspace 62 s, 418 MB, 636 MB (4 tests pass in both). `cargo metadata --offline --locked` works from `backend/reporting-service/Cargo.toml`; its lockfile is cut from the shared one: 270 of 387 packages, every version unchanged. cargo-chef 0.1.78 from the service workspace: `prepare --bin` writes a recipe of the service's three manifests only, `cook --profile deploy` 90 s, the final `cargo build --locked` 10 s, binary 7.1 MB; `service-kit` and `clis/telemetry` are skeleton path dependencies (the Dockerfile's existing mechanism for telemetry and rca-core) and the build needs `clis/secrets/src/data/registry.json` as before. Docker itself was not run (no daemon in the sandbox); the Dockerfile edit is the `COPY` lines and the workdir. Change: `backend/service-kit/Cargo.toml` states its dependency ranges itself (as `clis/telemetry` already does, because two workspaces build it); `backend/reporting-service/{Cargo.toml,Cargo.lock,.cargo/config.toml}` (own workspace, `target-dir = "../target"` shared); the shared `backend/Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` lose the service (2 packages pruned, no version moved); the Dockerfile copies the service's manifest and lockfile as files (the image hash reads files, and drops a copied directory whose manifest is outside the crate closure), no sibling service, `service-kit` and `telemetry` as skeleton path dependencies; `clis/pipeline/src/image_hash.rs` finds the lockfile of the workspace a service belongs to instead of the fixed `backend/Cargo.lock`; `core_purity.rs` checks every workspace under `backend/`; the deploy-legs flow test asserts that the shared manifest and lockfile no longer move reporting-service and that its own lockfile moves it alone. The first push of this change redeploys all services once, because `service-kit`'s manifest changed and every image copies it; after it a dependency edit in reporting-service redeploys that service alone.

- 1 Nov, per-service workspace, measured in CI. (1) The change itself (a434bfb79, run 41313505704): every functional job green, including the real Docker build of reporting-service from its own workspace (deploy leg 4 min 03 s) and its deployment verification; it redeployed every service once, as predicted, because `service-kit`'s manifest changed and every image copies it (deploy legs 9 min 32 s vm-ops-api, 6 min 02 s remote-chat-agent, 3 min 57 s leetcode-backend, 3 min 34 s leetcode-auth-cookie-capture, 4 min 03 s reporting-service; replay stages typecheck 35 s, flow 2 min 17 s); the CI budget job flagged the CPU of that one rebuild (3935 CPU-seconds against 1000, soft). (2) The rehearsal (c73d3e09c, run 41316783162): `tokio` 1.53.2 to 1.54.1 in `backend/reporting-service/Cargo.lock` only. `wf ship --changed` listed `reporting-service` alone before the push, and CI ran one deploy leg: preflight 20 s, plan 30 s, typecheck 35 s, flow 29 s, `deploy (reporting-service)` 3 min 43 s, integration 21 s, cli-e2e 20 s, deployment verification 1 min 00 s, browser e2e; the run took 9 min 20 s, green, and the other four Rust services kept their images and stayed on 1.53.2. Before the split the same one-line dependency edit rebuilt all six images (28 min of deploys, the 94 min run). Bug found by the gate on the way: the core-purity budget did not match a service workspace's member `"core"` (only `".../core"`), so that service's core was uncounted; fixed with a planted-service test. Next: move the other services (watchdog, leetcode-backend, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture, remote-chat-agent, vm-ops-api) one at a time; each move edits the shared `backend/Cargo.toml` (its `members`), which the services still in it hash, so the services left in the shared workspace redeploy once per move: moving the last five in one push costs one rebuild of five instead of five of four.

- 1 Nov, two more services in workspaces of their own (leetcode-backend, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture; reporting-service was first), after the run that redeployed every service once (35 min) showed the cost of a shared edit. To keep that from recurring per move, the image hash of a Rust image now takes from the shared workspace manifest only what its crates use (`workspace_part` in `clis/pipeline/src/image_hash.rs`): the file re-serialised without `members` and without the `[workspace.dependencies]` entries no crate of the image's closure inherits, with profiles, the resolver and the entries it uses still counted. Flow tests derived from the repo's own manifests (`clis/deploy/tests/flow/deploy_legs.rs`): a dependency only the agent service's crates inherit ships remote-chat-agent alone; an unused entry plus a new `members` line ship nothing; a profile edit ships every service still in the shared workspace and none of the three with their own; a blank line ships nothing; each own-workspace lockfile moving `tokio` ships that service alone. Moved by `migrate.py` (scratchpad, not committed): per-service `Cargo.toml` with the entries its crates inherit and the shared profiles, `Cargo.lock` cut from the shared one (versions unchanged), `.cargo/config.toml` with the shared `target-dir`, `package.json` scripts on `Cargo.toml`, the Dockerfile with no sibling service. Left in the shared workspace: vm-ops-api (with its integration crate), watchdog, remote-chat-agent.

- 1 Nov, CI for the two further workspace splits and the narrowed image hash (6058f6d6e, run 41318760941): 19 min 53 s from the first job to the last (preflight 49 s, plan 30 s, flow-tests 2 min 24 s, typecheck 42 s, four deploy legs, integration 21 s, cli-e2e 57 s, deployment verification 33 s, browser e2e 28 s), every functional job green; only the soft CPU budget job failed. Deploy legs: leetcode-backend 3 min 44 s and leetcode-auth-cookie-capture 3 min 32 s (the two moved services, built from their own workspaces), plus vm-ops-api 1 min 50 s and remote-chat-agent 2 min 16 s: those two did not change, but the hash definition did (the manifest is now hashed by the part their crates use), so their `nx-<hash>` tags were new and the images rebuilt from the cached layers. That cost is paid once; the next push that touches one service ships one leg (9 min 20 s in the tokio rehearsal). Three of the six Rust services now have a workspace of their own (reporting-service, leetcode-backend, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture); vm-ops-api (with its integration crate), watchdog and remote-chat-agent are still in the shared one.

### 1 Nov: vm-ops-api and watchdog in workspaces of their own, CI run 41322534773 (n=1)

- Wall time 30 min 43 s, one deploy leg (vm-ops-api, which ships the watchdog). `flow-tests` took 981 s because both crates' Nx keys and target directory were new (a one-time cold compile); `deploy (vm-ops-api)` 562 s; every other job under 60 s. The CPU-seconds budget job soft-failed on the rebuild.
- Decision: accepted as the one-time cost of the move; the next edit of either service replays the warm keys. remote-chat-agent moves in the next push, which leaves the shared `backend/Cargo.toml` as service-kit's own workspace only.

### 1 Nov: remote-chat-agent in a workspace of its own, CI run 41326333726 (n=1)

- Wall time 15 min 51 s, one deploy leg (remote-chat-agent); every job green except the soft CPU-seconds budget. All six Rust services now have a workspace, manifest and lockfile of their own; `backend/Cargo.toml` holds only service-kit.
- Runs of the split: 35 min (first, all-service redeploy), 19 min 53 s, 30 min 43 s (cold keys), 15 min 51 s. A service update after the one-time cold keys is the 6 to 9 min rehearsal (run 41316783162).

### 1 Nov: why CI re-ran what the gate had passed (runs 41322534773, 41326333726)

- Same Nx hashes on both sides (`@backend/vm-ops-api:test:flow` 4360480774046488316, `@infra/watchdog:test:flow` 595337641252581187, `@app/remote-chat-agent:test:flow` 10011553193497661492), so it was not key drift. Two causes: (1) Nx writes the shared cache only for a task it ran, and the sandbox's own cache already held these passes, so they were local hits and never uploaded; (2) in run 1 the watchdog's `cargo test` was started from `infra/oracle-cloud/watchdog`, where the service's `.cargo/config.toml` does not apply, and wrote 633 MB to `backend/watchdog/target`, an untracked path that made the tree dirty, so every gate write was skipped (`puts:0`) and CI ran the 725 s contract suite and all flow tasks cold (flow-tests 981 s).
- Fix: a push to `dev` runs the gate with a run-scoped `NX_CACHE_DIRECTORY` (tasks hit the shared cache or run and upload; the browser probe uses the same directory), the watchdog flow script sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=../../../backend/target`, and `.gitignore` lists `backend/*/target*/`. Flow test: the gate gives each stage of a push to dev a fresh Nx cache directory and a push elsewhere keeps the shared one.

### 1 Nov: the pre-push gate now fails a push whose passes did not reach the shared cache

- A gate that passes without seeding the cache defeats its purpose (it did exactly that twice: a dirty tree made every write skip, and local Nx hits never uploaded). For a push to `dev`, `wf check --pushed` reads each stage's `wf: build cache` stderr and refuses the push when a stage says the run only read the cache (`dirty`, `not-on-dev`, `tools-unpinned`) or went cold (`degraded`), naming the stage. Together with the run-scoped local Nx cache (a task either replays from the shared cache or runs and uploads), a green gate now means CI replays.
- Flow test: a stage that passes but reports a read-only cache refuses the push (`check::the_pushed_gate_plans_the_push_runs_every_sandbox_stage_and_stops_the_push_on_a_red_one`).

### 1 Nov: CI after the runner recreate and the seeding fix (n=1 each)

- Run 41335113598 (96694285b, gate and flow changes): 8 min 5 s wall, green. flow-tests 74 s (replayed), typecheck-lint-unit-tests 74 s, cli-e2e-tests 80 s.
- Run 41337986675 (77301e0a0, a real change to reporting-service, the watchdog and the runner compose file): 7 min 56 s wall, green, two deploy legs (`deploy (vm-ops-api)` 89 s, `deploy (reporting-service)` 86 s). A changed service deploys in under 10 minutes with the cache seeded by the pre-push gate.
- The earlier slow runs had a cause each: cold keys because the gate wrote nothing (a dirty tree and local Nx hits), then browser-e2e (12.8 min) and android-e2e (11.9 min) running serially for a push that changed e2e files and `wf`; run 41330346677 was 29 min of work plus 45 min queued behind a runner stranded by a tailscale restart (runner healthcheck and 15 s health polling added in 77301e0a0).

### 1 Nov: the one run over 20 minutes, and why (run 41343650449, bc8f52bc1)

- 55 min wall (19:49 to 20:44), every job green except the soft budget job. Cause: the commit carried the Better Stack metrics sink in `clis/telemetry`, a crate `service-kit` and so all six services build, so every image hash moved and all services redeployed serially (vm-ops-api 9.5 min, remote-chat-agent 6.4, leetcode-backend 3.9, and the rest) after a 9.5-minute cold flow-tests and a 5-minute typecheck for the new crates.
- This is the global-deploy cost of editing a shared library that the versioned-libraries plan removes: the `wf lib` slice is built and the registry buckets and secrets exist; migrating `telemetry` and `service-kit` to pinned versions is the next step, after which a library release redeploys only the services that bump.
- Until then, an edit to `clis/telemetry` or `backend/service-kit` is expected to redeploy every service and will exceed 20 minutes; it is not a regression of the per-service split.

## Run 41358035572 (e3df89ff5): 35 min, not under 20 (2026-11-01)

- Wall clock 22:37:07 to 23:11:57. `flow-tests` (13 m 04 s, of which 3 m 11 s was `Save the pnpm store`) held the one runner, so `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` waited 13 m 08 s for it (the budget job's `runner-wait`, the only breach). That job then took 9 m 37 s with 32 of 32 Nx lookups hits.
- In that job the log is silent for exactly 6 m 00 s between `hashMultipleTasks` (22:52:59.4) and the first replayed `cargo-unit` task (22:58:59.7); hashing itself takes 2 s (`wf test drift`), and the same `nx run-many` on a local cache runs the tasks in 17 s. It is the same gap as the 26 Oct entry, and it also cost run 1024 (a plan-doc-only push, 9 m 36 s in that job). Not yet explained: the cache shim reports 32 gets, 4.9 s in total.
- Two follow-ups, neither done: (1) find the 6 min wait between hashing and the first replay with the `WF_NX_TRACE` output (it prints to stderr of the Nx child; the job log shows none of it); (2) stop `flow-tests` and `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` queueing behind each other, by a second runner or by dropping the 3 m pnpm store save when nothing changed.

## Run 41365175856 (ade70b455): 16 m 42 s, red on the first libs-proxy deploy (2026-11-02)

- A wf-crates-only push (trace flags in `pipeline` and `blob-cache`): `flow-tests` 5 m 02 s, `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` 8 m 07 s (the silent Nx gap is still in it; the jobs run the wf the previous push published, so the new `WF_NX_TRACE` output exists only from the next run), `clis` 7 s, then `deploy (libs-proxy)` failed after 44 s and skipped every later tier. No queueing this time: the runner was free, so the wall clock is the sum of the jobs.
- Cause of the red: the smoke test runs the pulled `app` image in the network namespace of its `tailscale` sidecar, and a first deploy has no sidecar (`cannot share network namespace with service tailscale: container missing`). Fix: the app smoke script starts the compose services other than the app, with `--no-deps`, before the throwaway container; an existing unchanged sidecar is left as it is. The script snapshot `app-smoke.sh` carries the new line.

## Run 41366982272 (11b23f779): green in 10 min 36 s (2026-11-02)

- The vm-ops-api smoke fix: `vm-ops-api` and the first `libs-proxy` deploy both green (1 m 30 s and 45 s), `integration-tests`, `cli-e2e-tests` (3 m 37 s), browser and Android e2e green; the budget job found nothing. `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` took 35 s and `flow-tests` 35 s, all Nx lookups replayed, no queueing.
- So the 6 min silence of runs 41358035572 and 41365175856 did not recur on an identical stage with warm caches; those two runs were the ones that followed a new lockfile (pnpm store save, 3 m 11 s) and a rebuilt wf, which points at a cold-cache cost on the first run after such a change, not a fixed Nx wait. The `WF_NX_TRACE` request log and 30 s process table stay in, so the next occurrence names itself.

## Registry dependencies in CI, and the 90 s Nx stall (2026-11-02)

- Run 41387049219 (8e1e0a03e, the library migration): `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` hit its 20 min step limit with every Nx lookup a cache hit. The `WF_NX_TRACE` request log showed the cache requests arriving in bursts exactly 90 s apart (04:20:31, 04:22:01, 04:23:31, 04:25:01), the idle timeout of a pooled client connection; the shim now answers one request per connection (`Connection: close`) and logs each answer time. Run 41392534745 (8446c9d08): the same stage took 2 m 22 s.
- That run also resolved `service-kit 0.1.1` and `rca-ladder 0.1.1` from `libs-proxy` inside Docker builds (BuildKit RUN steps reach the tailnet name), and the migrated services built and deployed in 3 to 4 min each (remote-chat-agent 6 m 17 s, leetcode-backend 4 m 08 s, leetcode-auth-cookie-capture 3 m 54 s, reporting-service 3 m 44 s, vnc-auth-session 3 m 57 s).
- `deploy (vm-ops-api)` failed in both runs, for two different reasons. 41392534745: the vm-ops-api and watchdog builds ran together on one shared cargo registry cache mount and both unpacked service-kit (`failed to open .cargo-ok: File exists`); every Dockerfile now has a cache of its own (`id=cargo-registry-<image>`). 41396448439: the cold build (every dependency compiled once, 14 min) printed nothing for 200 s, so the client gave up while the VM job finished with exit 0 and pushed the image; the next push finds it cached.
- The wf-harness flow test `a_browser_script_drives_the_daemon_waits_by_observer_and_reports` read a 982 ms wait slice against a 990 to 1000 ms window on a loaded sandbox; the window is 900 to 1000 ms now.
- Still open: a run with all six services redeploying takes about 50 min in total on one runner because the legs are serial (about 4 min each warm, 14 min for a cold build); the next push should be the first run of this change set under 20 min.

## Run 41405214719 (a97dea1a6): the offload e2e suite is the 20 min problem now (2026-11-02)

- Stages: preflight 31 s, plan 30 s, flow-tests 30 s, typecheck-lint-unit-tests 39 s, clis 8 s, deploy (vm-ops-api, image already built) 1 m 28 s, integration-tests 1 m 46 s, then `cli-e2e-tests` 41 m 47 s and red, deployment-verification 1 m 46 s, browser e2e 9 m 57 s. Everything before the e2e tiers took 7 min.
- `cli-e2e-tests` is `crate-offload`'s `tests/e2e`: 62 tests in 2227 s that drive the real opencode with a live model (parity_features: each test 14 to 90 s). It reran because the library migration changed `clis/Cargo.lock`, an input of every clis crate's tasks: this was the first run since then that got past the deploy legs (`cargo tree` shows `offload` does not depend on `blob-cache`). One test, `a_subagent_is_called_through_the_task_tool_with_its_own_session_and_its_own_rules_on_both`, failed because the model did not say the word the test waits for: a live-model assertion, not a code failure.
- Consequence: a lockfile or `offload` source change costs about 37 min of live-model tests in CI, and one model whim turns the run red; a push that changes neither replays the result from the Nx cache once this suite has passed once. Next steps, not done: cut the e2e Nx inputs of `offload` to the crates that change its behaviour, or move the parity suite to its own scheduled workflow (it needs a decision: it is the proof that the opencode and rca paths behave alike).
- Decided with the owner: the red offload test is fixed first, and `wf check --pushed` can seed the VM cache with the live-model e2e. The subagent parity test no longer asserts the model's relay of the pirate's answer (`both_say("ARRR")`): the task result carrying ARRR in a session of its own is asserted for both, what the model then says is its wording. `WF_PUSH_CLI_E2E=1` adds `cli-e2e-tests` to the pre-push stages (flow test in `clis/pipeline/tests/flow/check.rs`), so a clean-tree push to dev writes its passes to the shared Nx cache and CI replays the suite.

## Offload e2e split into one Nx task per group (2026-11-02)

- Before: one `crate-offload:cargo-e2e` task (62 live-model tests, about 37 min) keyed by the whole crate (`rust`), so a lockfile change or any offload source edit, including the batch, runbook, pricing and worker code no test drives, reran all of it.
- Now: four tasks, `cargo-e2e-ask` (5 tests), `cargo-e2e-parity`, `cargo-e2e-parity_features`, `cargo-e2e-parity_capabilities`, declared in `clis/offload/Cargo.toml` `[package.metadata.e2e]`. Each is keyed by its own test file, `tests/e2e.rs`, `tests/e2e/parity.rs` (the helpers), `tests/flow/support/**`, the read-only agent, the opencode Dockerfile (the pinned opencode version), the `src` files an ask runs through, the lockfile and `^rustProduction`.
- Rerun scope: the test file of one group reruns that group only; `parity.rs`, `e2e.rs`, the shared support, `dispatch*.rs`/`rca_client.rs`/`run_format.rs`/help, a dependency crate, `clis/Cargo.lock`, the read-only agent or the opencode pin rerun all four (about 37 min, as before); `worker_*.rs`, `dispatch_batch.rs`, `runbook.rs`, `pricing_sync.rs`, the flow tests and docs rerun none. An edit to one group's test file now reruns that group's share of the 37 min, not all of it (per-group times to be read off the next CI run's nx ledger).
- Proof: `nx_cache.rs` (the `cargo-contract` task of pipeline) runs the real Nx over the four group tasks with `cargo` doubled and asserts the rerun set for 16 edits, and the plan test asserts one entry per group. Flow/contract tier only: no live model ran.

- Owner decision: the pre-push gate runs the live-model e2e by default (`cli-e2e-tests` after the four sandbox stages), skipped with a note when the vault has no `OPENCODE_GO_API_KEY` for the machine, `WF_PUSH_CLI_E2E=0` to leave it out. The first push after this change runs every group the Nx cache has no pass for (about 40 min once), then each later push replays or reruns only the groups its edits reach.
- The gate that pushed the default (d6431d1a3) did not run the e2e stage: the pre-push hook runs the installed release `wf`, which was built before the change. A change to the gate itself takes effect from the push after the one that carries it, once `wf` is rebuilt (`cargo build --release -p wf`); the next push is the first with `cli-e2e-tests` in its stages.
- Owner decision, after run 41416164234 went red on a second, different live-model test (`parity_capabilities::continuing_without_a_session_...`, the earlier one was the subagent test; 52 min): each live-model test runs up to three times (`crate::parity::retrying`, 60 of the 62 tests; the two `--help` tests call no model), and only three misses in a row fail it, every failed attempt printed with its number. A green suite is cached, so the one-time cost is the first local run through the gate. The retry helper has two flow-tier tests (no model). Nothing from a live model ran for this change.

## Run 41441721246 (a10440d6e): the seeded e2e replays, 5 min 5 s (2026-11-02)

- The pre-push gate (76 min, once) ran the live-model suite locally and wrote its passes to the shared Nx cache. CI replayed it: `cli-e2e-tests` 25 s (it was 42 to 52 min, red twice), `flow-tests` 27 s, `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` 44 s, `clis` 8 s, browser e2e 29 s, Android e2e 24 s, budget green. Wall clock 12:04:22 to 12:09:27 = 5 min 5 s, the whole pipeline, nothing redeployed.
- So the sandbox seeds the `cargo-e2e` keys and CI computes the same ones: no arch or hash desync for those tasks.
- Runs 796590e5b (the one before, green) and the e2e-heavy runs ahead of it show the other cost: a run queued behind a long one waits, since there is one runner; a newer push replaces a run that is still waiting.
