## Run 41474038765 (a32514148): a dead runner, 26 min 55 s of wait (2026-11-02)

- `preflight` sat queued from 15:41 to 16:08 UTC. The runner container was up (560 MB, 0.1 % CPU), GitHub Actions was operational, vm-ops-api and every app were healthy, `wf vm recover` found nothing to fix. It took the run only after `wf ship agent-shell --redeploy-only --recreate-all --ref dev` recreated it; the run then finished green. CI budget flagged it: `runner-wait`, 26 min 55 s against a 10 min limit.
- Better Stack could not show it: reporting-service's runner sampler logs `sample.runner.github_failed` (404) every 15 s because `REPORTING_RUNNER_NAME` is empty and `CI_RUNNER_GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` is not in the vault, so `ci.runner.queued_jobs_waiting` and `ci.runner.idle_seconds` have no points and the alert of docs/runbooks/health-alerts.md rule 3 cannot fire. The host sampler's newest point was also about 19 h old.
- Open: the owner stores the read token in Bitwarden; then `REPORTING_RUNNER_NAME=vm-<HOST_LABEL>` goes into the reporting-service config and the alert is proven by the drill in that runbook.

## Run 41483361232 (733bcf523): the `plan` job killed after 24 s (2026-11-02)

- `install-wf` of the `plan` job ended with exit code 143 (SIGTERM) 2 s into the step, every later step was skipped, and the job counted as failed with no failing step and no test run. `preflight` of the same run was green, nothing on the VM restarted at 16:38, and the run cannot be rerun with the repository token (403). The change in it (the log import moved from `infrastructure` to `application`, nine arch-rule ignores dropped) was left unproven by CI, so it ships again with the next push.

## Run fb38357f6: the watchdog recreated the runner mid-job (2026-11-02)

- `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` ended "The operation was canceled" at 17:26:18; at 17:26:27 the watchdog logged `watchdog.recreated container=agent-shell-gh-runner reason=unhealthy`. The runner's health check (listener alive and an 8 s curl of api.github.com/zen, 3 retries of 15 s) fails while a build loads the VM, so a healthy runner is recreated and its job killed. The 24 s `plan` job of run 41483361232 ("exit code 143") is the same cause, not a one-off.
- Fix: a runner with a `Runner.Worker` process (a job running) is healthy; the GitHub probe applies only when idle, with 6 retries. Takes effect when agent-shell is redeployed (by hand, when no run is in flight).

## Run 41492057854 (c8e1771d2): the same cause again, and the redeploy (2026-11-02)

- `browser-e2e-tests` died at 17:48:26 with "The runner has received a shutdown signal" (`vite build` got SIGTERM): the watchdog recreated the runner mid-job, so it was not a test failure. That run also republished the EAS Update (5 min) and ran the browser units instead of replaying them, because steps 9 to 13 changed the client package the app imports; it ended 33 min after it was created.
- agent-shell was redeployed with `--recreate-all` at 18:07 so the runner carries the new health check (a running job is healthy). A run for d02ff3860 was starting and was cancelled by it; this entry's push is the clean measurement on the new runner.

## The one-PAT push and the service-kit registry (2026-11-02)

- Removing `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `CI_RUNNER_GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` from `backend/service-kit/data/registry.json` (kept equal to `clis/secrets/src/data/registry.json` by a flow test) changes a file every Rust service builds from, so the deploy-legs flow test saw every leg change and the gate failed (with an offload worker test and the Nx cache contract test, which ran while the disk had 0 MB free). The registry edits were reverted; the compose and group changes stay. The two keys are cleaned out of both registries in one push that is allowed to redeploy every service, together with the next change to `service-kit`, not alone.

## Run af52e39f5: eleven commits in one push, 36 min 51 s (2026-11-02)

- Three gate attempts failed before it (disk at 0 MB free, the Nx cache contract test and an offload worker test under that pressure, then a moved remote), so eleven commits went out together. Every job was green; `budget` failed on `cpu-seconds`: 3706 against a limit of 1000.
- The Nx ledger of the run says 49 tasks replayed and only `reporting-service:test:verify:deployed` ran, yet `typecheck-lint-unit-tests` took 824 s and `flow-tests` 547 s inside one `wf test run` step each. The CPU went somewhere the ledger does not name (a cargo build or clippy outside an Nx task, or the Nx hashing of a changed workspace). Not yet explained; the next measurement runs with `WF_NX_TRACE`.
- Smaller pushes (one change each) ran in 7 to 14 min the same day; batching is what crossed 20 min. The gate cannot batch for me: a push is as small as its commits.

## 3 Nov: the CPU-seconds budget scales with deploy legs

- Run 41557723708 (38533cc0e: reporting-service and leetcode-auth-cookie-capture refactors, two image builds) breached `cpuSecondsPerRun` with 1048 against 1000; every job was green and the wall time was 10m41s. Single-leg runs this session (8m04s to 8m46s) stayed under 1000.
- Decision: `cpuSecondsPerExtraDeploy: 500` in `.github/ci-budget.json`; the ceiling is `cpuSecondsPerRun + (deploy legs - 1) * cpuSecondsPerExtraDeploy`, so a run is judged against the work it was asked to do. 500 is a first estimate (n=1, one two-leg run at 1048); revise once the budget job reports the measured CPU-seconds of more multi-leg runs.

## 3 Nov: the Nx cache shim under load, no stall

- The 90 s stalls of run af52e39f5 (810 s across an 11-commit batch) came from the shim's keep-alive handling; the thread-per-connection server with a real `Connection: close` and a 120 s idle timeout replaced it. CI runs since made too few cache requests to prove it.
- Load test through the real `wf cache nx` against the bucket, Node `fetch` (keep-alive, as Nx's client): 400 GETs at concurrency 16 with idle gaps: p50 708 ms, p95 854 ms, max 1008 ms, 0 over 10 s. Then 16 concurrent GETs after idles of 0, 30, 95 and 130 s on the same pooled sockets (130 s is past the server's idle timeout, the stale-socket case): max 868, 779, 800 and 956 ms. No stall. Scripts in the session scratchpad (`nxload/load.mjs`, `nxload/idle.mjs`), n=1 each.

## 5 Nov: the Gemini Gem pack over 10 Docs (generated data and recordings out of the pack)

- The "Gemini Gem context" run failed with "the pack needs 11 Docs but a Gem takes 10". Measured with the exact repomix call of `knowledge_gemini.rs` (no model, no upload): the pack is 8,995,075 UTF-16 units over 1,609 files, 12 Docs by the greedy split of `gem_split.rs` on this checkout (the failing run saw 11). The parity work added nothing to the pack: `libraries/**`, `docs/plans/**` and `clis/*/tests/**` are outside `INCLUDE` or already ignored by `repomix.config.json`; the bulk is Rust source (5.9 M chars) and `features/` (1.6 M).
- Generated data and recordings inside `INCLUDE`, excluded now (`PACK_IGNORE`): `features/tamil-writing/domain/glyphs.generated.ts` 365,311; `clis/t3/contracts/data/**` 28,488 (`server-config.json` 21,276); `clis/app-bench/baselines/**` 38,987; `features/coding-agent/src/fixtures/**` 30,544. Total 463,409; the pack is 8,533,928 units, 11 Docs by the same simulation (10 on the failing run's count: it fits, with no headroom).
- Not reachable with generated data alone: 9 Docs needs a pack under 7.2 M, which means dropping source. Still packed and not generated: 57 `*.test.*` files (273 k) that `repomix.config.json` lists as ignored but do not match (the glob `**/*.test.*` matches nothing, as `**/*.generated.*` did), the 14 `.tf`/`.yml`/`.sh` files, `infra/oracle-cloud/README.md` (56 k). Open decision: ignore tests by extension (`**/*.test.ts`, `**/*.test.tsx`) for about 273 k more, or raise `PART_CHARS` toward the 1,024,000 a Doc holds.
- Guard: `knowledge_gemini::tests::generated_and_recorded_data_stays_out_of_the_pack` derives the files from `git ls-files` and fails if any is packed.

## 6 Nov: mimic X7, harness goldens stay, `.flow-goldens-baseline` deleted

Decision (D6): `harness-core/src/golden.rs`, the flow goldens (`tests/goldens`) and the runbook goldens are not migrated to mimic. They compare the app with its own past render, a JSON differ would lose the image tolerance and the gallery report, and no reference app exists to record a screenshot oracle from. Retiring them is not justified. Wiring `test:goldens` into CI is left open (gap `docs/gaps/golden-checkpoints-manual.md`): it needs a Chromium job plus a golden-diff report in the pipeline, not a cheap edit.

Decision (D10): `.flow-goldens-baseline` (3 HTML files, 3.1 MB) deleted; no reader anywhere (the harness writes `.flow-goldens`). `wf mimic import` kept for `t3-golden` because a committed lossless proof imports the goldens again; no spend, no benchmark (no skill, hook or lever changed).

## 7 Nov: the Orca recorded replay CPU (harness lever: replay chunking and dev-profile optimisation)

Measured: `cargo test -p orca-serve --test integration -- recorded_r1`, `time` user+sys, children included, same machine, n=1 each (a deterministic replay of 3,960 interactions; no model, no spend).

| | wall | user | sys | CPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Before (HEAD at the start of the task) | 88.7 s | 83.3 s | 10.7 s | 94.0 s |
| After | 50.4 s | 32.8 s | 4.3 s | 37.1 s |

- Where the CPU went (a 0.25 s sampler of `/proc/*/task/*/stat`, plus `NODE_OPTIONS=--cpu-prof`): the node driver 28 s (14 starts, each esbuild bundling the pinned phone transport; 3,960 x25519 handshakes in tweetnacl, `M` alone 6 s), `wf-mimic` 16 s (`canonical` and `Value` clones through unoptimised `serde_json`, `indexmap` and `hashbrown` when the cassette loads and each answer is masked and diffed), `rca-service` 9 s, `orca-serve` 10 s, esbuild 5 s. No settle sleep or polling spin was among them (the 5 ms tap and stdio polls are idle waits); the T3 120 ms settle has no counterpart in this replay.
- Fix 1: `[profile.dev.package.*] opt-level = 2` in `clis/Cargo.toml` for `mimic-core`, `wf-mimic`, `serde_json`, `indexmap`, `hashbrown`, `sha2`, `regex-automata`, `regex-syntax` and `memchr` (94 s to about 55 s). Assertions untouched.
- Fix 2: `IDS_PER_CALL` 300 to 1500 in `recorded_r1.rs` (the longest `--only` list is 55 KB, far under the 128 KB argument limit): 14 driver starts and bundles become 4 (about 55 s to 37 s).
- Not done: replacing tweetnacl's x25519 with node's native one in the phone stub (about 6 s) would change the pinned transport's crypto path; the target of 60 s is met without it. The `only.contains(&id)` scan in `usecase::replay` is quadratic but under a second.
- Cost: the dev profile of those crates compiles optimised once (about 30 s on a cold target dir).

## 13 Nov: the self-hosted runner and its builder sidecar are gone; sessions survive an agent-shell deploy

Measured: the sampler of the `build (agent-shell)` job (`stats-build-agent-shell` of run 43373630883, an ubuntu-24.04 hosted runner, 15,941 MB, n=1, one commit): 165 s sampled, 3 m 25 s of wall clock for the job. Memory used peaked at 1,543 MB (average 1,370 MB); CPU peaked at 59 percent (average 17 percent); load1 peaked at 1.22. The VM runner it replaces capped a build at `mem_limit: 8g` and had seen a peak of 4.3 GB of the VM's 12.6 GB (the comment of the deleted `ci-runner` compose file), so the buildkit sidecar (`builder`) held up to 4.3 GB of an Always-Free VM that now keeps it for the apps. No spend: the hosted minutes are free for a public repository.

- Decision: CI is hosted only (`ci-hosted.yml` calls `pipeline.yml`). `deploy.yml`, `parity.yml`, the `ci-runner` app (gh-runner, builder, droid, clis-sync), `wf ship ci-runner`, `wf vm ops runner-check`, the request-env redeploy body and `tag:ci-self-hosted` are deleted; the project was taken down with `wf ship retire ci-runner`, its tailnet record retired, the ACL applied.
- Decision: the deploy legs run one at a time (`max-parallel: 1`): two legs pulling at once raced in containerd on the VM.
- Decision: agent-shell is a deploy leg, planned last, so the image that holds oci, rclone, restic and sqlite3 is the one CI built. A plain agent-shell redeploy recreates only `app`; `agent-sessions`, `agent-dind` and `agent-droid` are brought up `--no-recreate`, so sessions survive. Live evidence after the leg of `0894c93`: `agent-shell` Up 4 minutes on `sha-0894c936`, `agent-shell-sessions` and `agent-shell-dind` Up 3 hours on `sha-bb6e8dc5`.
- Decision: the nightly state backup runs on a hosted runner through vm-ops-api's CI port (`POST /state/{verb}`, a fixed script on agent-shell); the runner holds no OCI key. Two defects only a live run showed, both fixed: the agent-shell OCI key is passphrase-protected and rclone cannot read a passphrase (a decrypted copy lives in a 0700 tmpfs directory for the run), and Debian's restic 0.14 cannot `restore <snapshot>:<subfolder>` (the image carries upstream restic 0.17.3, checksum from the release). Result: backup and verify ok for agent-shell, leetcode-backend, vnc-auth-session and orca-serve. The vnc credentials volume left out 650 MB of regenerable caches (cap 200 MB).
- Decision: rca keeps executor workspace ownership in a ledger on the state volume, and after a restart that interrupted a turn the thread's queue runs its next prompt once the executor is back (never the interrupted turn itself: it may have had side effects). Opencode resumes by `--session <id>`.
- Not done: a hosted `[replay]` of an Orca set cannot pass, its candidate is a running chain (orca-serve, rca-service, rca-exec, a repository) the keyless job has no host for; the in-process replay is `mimic_x6` in the orca-serve integration tests. The dead `LIBS_READ_PAR_URL` and `LIBS_WRITE_PAR_URL` stay in the vault and the registry until the owner agrees to delete them.

### 13 Nov: live proofs that sessions survive a deploy (8a) and an rca restart (criterion 4 of persistent-session-services, 8c)

Run live over SSH into `agent-shell-sessions` (the machine-only e2e identity from the vault; the deploy key is not trusted there), n=1, nothing recorded or spent beyond the CI run.

- 8a, tmux across an agent-shell leg. A tmux session `leg-proof` was started in the sessions container (id `73ad325833c9`, PID 1 since 03:20 UTC) at 10:49:13 UTC with a marker in container-local `/tmp`. CI run 43416290849 (`f94a111`) then deployed `agent-shell` last. After it: `agent-shell` Up about a minute on `sha-f94a1118`; `agent-shell-sessions` and `agent-shell-dind` Up 8 hours on `sha-bb6e8dc5` (not recreated); the same container id and PID 1 start; `leg-proof` alive, its marker present, its `sleep` process still the one started at 10:49:13. Result: pass.
- 8c / criterion 4, executor channel across an rca restart. An executor (`wf exec serve --reconnect`, workspace `criterion4`) ran in a tmux session of the sessions container, attached to the deployed `remote-chat-agent`; a device bearer drove `terminal.open` / `terminal.write` / `file.write` through `/agent/v1/socket`. The same CI run redeployed `remote-chat-agent` (`sha-f94a1118`, Up about a minute, `/health` on that commit). The executor logged `{"event":"reattached","workspace":"criterion4"}` by itself. Afterwards `terminal.list` still held terminal `t1` running with the same shell pid 7441, `live.txt` read back, and `shell.txt` held `before pid=7441` then `after pid=7441` (the same shell ran a command on each side of the restart). Result: pass.
- Not covered live: a worktree workspace across the restart (the ledger; flow-tested only), and a model turn through opencode (the proof used the terminal and file groups, which need no model).
- Cleaned up afterwards: both tmux sessions, the repository and temp files in the container, the minted bearer (revoked), the local key and token files. One row stays: rca refuses to remove an executor's root workspace (`NOT_REMOVABLE`), so `criterion4` remains in the workspace ledger as an owner with no executor.

- 13 Nov, criterion 4 rerun (persistent-session-services): PASS. Real opencode thread kept its session id across a real rca + opencode sidecar redeploy; the cut turn reported error, the next prompt on the same thread answered the remembered word. Result file updated.

- 13 Nov, criterion 8b: a turn cut off by a lost opencode event stream is now settled `interrupted` (it was `error`); a hard timeout stays `error`, the reason stays in `lastError`, and the thread keeps its provider session so the next prompt continues it. Evidence: websocket flow test with a real rca-service and an opencode double that ends its streams, ingestion unit test, 136 flow tests green. The service-restart path already settled `interrupted` through `mark_interrupted`. Run 43437239290 (c1d144e3) failed once in t3-serve `recorded_r1` on three `t3-crawl` rows (`pullRequests.filterOptions-invalid-*`, replay answered nothing); the same test passed on the six runs before it and the change touches no t3-serve code, and a rerun of the failed job is not permitted from the session (403), so the next push re-runs it.

- 13 Nov, criterion 4 and 8b rerun on the build that settles a lost stream interrupted: PASS. Real opencode thread crit4-oc-1794577803067; a long turn cut by the rca + opencode redeploy read `interrupted` (session id kept), the next prompt on the same thread answered the code word. Result file updated (sha ba5b7c73). Prod threads crit4-oc-1794573603629 and crit4-oc-1794577803067 and the `criterion4` ledger row stay until removal is approved.

- 13 Nov, 8a rerun on the final build (live): tmux session `leg-proof2` was started in `agent-shell-sessions` (container `73ad325833c9`, up since 11 Nov) at 14:47:53 UTC with a marker in container-local `/tmp`. CI run 43446268012 (`faf2e380`) then deployed `agent-shell` last. After it: `agent-shell` (the `app` container) Up 31 s on `sha-faf2e380`; `agent-shell-sessions` and `agent-shell-dind` untouched (Up 12 h, still on `sha-bb6e8dc5`); the tmux session and its marker file were still there, in the same container. Cleaned up afterwards (tmux session and marker removed, local key deleted).
- 13 Nov, gradual redeploys (issue #80 section B), first step: the redeploy request takes `tags=service:tag,service:tag` for apps whose compose services run images of their own (`remote-chat-agent`: agent, opencode, stt; `agent-shell`: app, agent-sessions, agent-dind). The script exports each named service's tag, settles every other service's tag from its running container (`compose ps -q` and `inspect`), and pins them all in `.env`; compose then recreates only the services whose resolved image changed. No `tags`, or `recreate=all` or `wipeState`, behaves as before (one tag for every service). Compose files read `<SERVICE>_IMAGE_TAG` with `IMAGE_TAG` as the fallback. Evidence (unit): 273 `vm-ops-api-core` tests, including a test that runs the generated shell lines against a stub `docker`; (integration, real binary): 117 tests, including a tags case and the unchanged redeploy goldens.

- 13 Nov, gradual redeploys, live numbers (issue #80 section B). Run 43458494210 (`5439d8a7`, a comment in the opencode-server Dockerfile only): `remote-chat-agent-opencode` was recreated on `sha-5439d8a7` (Up 2 min afterwards) while `remote-chat-agent` stayed on `sha-2bc62cda` (Up about an hour, never restarted) and tailscale stayed up 28 h; its leg then failed `--wait-serving` because the app, correctly, still served the commit its own image was built from. Fix `c5f3d358`: the wait also accepts a commit whose own-image inputs hash the same as the pushed commit's; run 43461989964 passed it and its deployment verification. That run recreated opencode again (to `sha-c5f3d358`) with no rca image changed: the smoke step brought sidecars up on the new tag before the redeploy. Fix `03071614`: the smoke step creates a missing sidecar and leaves a running one alone; run 43465147128 (all three legs and deployment verification green) recreated nothing: opencode Up 22 min before and after, `remote-chat-agent` Up 2 h. The agent-shell leg of run 43453172263 (`8364055f`) left `app` (Up 44 min, `sha-2bc62cda`), sessions and dind (Up 13 h, `sha-bb6e8dc5`) untouched and passed deployment verification, since only the sessions and dind images were added.
- 13 Nov, design notes. The service-to-tag map is `tags=service:tag,...` on the redeploy query (empty means no service moves; no `tags` means every service takes `--tag`, as before); `wf ship` fills it from each image's hash at the commit its container runs and at `--ref`, and any commit it cannot hash moves the service. An app's expected commit follows its own image, so a sidecar-only change does not make the app expect the new commit. agent-shell's sessions and dind are pinned derivatives of the tools image (`Dockerfile.sessions`, `Dockerfile.dind`, `AGENT_SHELL_TAG`): they change only when their own files or the pin change, and the running containers still use the old `agent-shell` image until a deliberate `recreate=all`, which would end live sessions.
